[1]. [*Non-Monotonic Exciton Transport Efficiency in Multi-layer Ring-shaped Quantum Networks inspired by Photosystem I*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13949 "Non-Monotonic Exciton Transport Efficiency in Multi-layer Ring-shaped Quantum Networks inspired by Photosystem I")

Yi Li, Rui Li, Kai-Ya Zhang, Ming-Zi Jin, Tao Liu, Qing Ai

arXiv:2610.13949v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Photosystem I (PSI) is one of the most efficient natural photosynthetic complexes and serves as a structural prototype for understanding excitation energy transfer (EET) and designing artificial light-harvesting systems. Here, we construct a multilayer elliptical-conical quantum network inspired by PSI to investigate how antenna architecture affects EET. Excitation dynamics are simulated using the quantum jump approach combined with coherent modified Redfield theory (QJA-CMRT). The transfer efficiency exhibits a pronounced nonmonotonic dependence on pigment density, reaching a maximum of 0.86 at x=6 in the five-layer network. The optimal configuration contains 91 pigments, close to the number of core antenna pigments in natural PSI. Our combined density-of-states and kinetic analyses reveal that this nonmonotonic dependence originates from a trade-off: at low pigment densities, enhanced band overlap promotes energy transfer, whereas at high densities, excessive exciton delocalization compromises funnel directionality and reduces efficiency; the optimal configuration is consequently achieved at x=6. These findings provide insights into PSI antenna organization and offer guidelines for designing efficient artificial light-harvesting systems.



[2]. [*Real-Space Interference from Quantum Metric*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14048 "Real-Space Interference from Quantum Metric")

Bo-Qing Wu, Xing-Lei Ma, Jin-Xin Hu, Jinchao Zhao, K. T. Law

arXiv:2610.14048v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Real-space interference of wave packets is governed by their central momentum separation and momentum width, which set the oscillation wavelength and spatial envelope, respectively. Here, we show that in crystalline systems, quantum metric distribution in an isolated band can similarly give rise to real-space interference through the evanescent tails of a localized bound state. The location and width of a quantum metric hot spot in the momentum space set a complex wavevector as one evanescent plane wave component of a bound state. Consequently, with multiple quantum metric hot spots, the density distribution of a bound state or the total density of two spatially overlapped bound states exhibits an interference pattern, where the oscillation period and long-range decay length are determined by the momentum-space separation of the hot spots and the width of the narrowest hot spots, respectively. This quantum-geometric interference is particularly pronounced in (nearly) flat bands, where competing scales from band dispersion are suppressed. Furthermore, we demonstrate that using a Josephson STM the real-space interference can be experimentally observed through the tip-position dependence of the Josephson critical current. Our results reveal a direct correspondence between momentum-space quantum geometry and real-space interference, showing how bound-state profiles encode the momentum-resolved quantum metric.



[3]. [*Domain of Applicability of the Distorted Wave Born Approximation (DWBA): A Critical Analysis for X-ray and Visible-Light Interface Scattering*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14054 "Domain of Applicability of the Distorted Wave Born Approximation (DWBA): A Critical Analysis for X-ray and Visible-Light Interface Scattering")

Reinhard Sigel

arXiv:2610.14054v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The Distorted Wave Born Approximation (DWBA) is established for X-ray interface scattering, yet its domain of applicability has never been stated explicitly. The DWBA implicitly rests on the small refractive index deviation (SRID) condition - satisfied by X-rays ($|n-1| \sim 10^{-6}$) but violated by visible light ($|n-1| \sim 0.5$). The DWBA mixes microscopic and macroscopic Maxwell equations, whereas a rigorous optical theory must be built on the macroscopic Maxwell equations and constitutive relations. Rigorous alternatives for two specific inconsistencies in the standard DWBA are discussed. The Extended Transfer Matrix Approach (ETMA) for visible-light interface scattering is outlined.



[4]. [*Hierarchical Multi-Agent Reasoning Systems for Knowledge-Grounded Material Substitution*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14123 "Hierarchical Multi-Agent Reasoning Systems for Knowledge-Grounded Material Substitution")

Tarjei Paule Hage, Subhadeep Pal, Wei Lu, Yu-Chuan Hsu, Gayla Lyon, Jiezhu Jin, Markus J. Buehler

arXiv:2610.14123v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: AI-assisted scientific discovery increasingly seeks to move beyond generating plausible ideas toward reproducible, constraint-aware workflows that can support practical engineering decisions. We present MARS, a hierarchical Multi-Agent Reasoning System for knowledge-grounded material substitution under scientific and manufacturing constraints. We apply MARS to replace per- and polyfluoroalkyl substances (PFAS), a demanding materials-design problem in which substitutes must satisfy functional performance, environmental, experimental, and processing requirements. MARS decomposes substitution into a closed-loop workflow comprising requirement analysis, grounded candidate generation, and manufacturability assessment with process design. The system integrates retrieval-augmented generation, knowledge-graph reasoning, a database of lab-available materials, persistent memory of intermediate results, and rejection-aware iterative feedback. In a PFAS-substitution case study, MARS generates structured material requirements, identifies an evidence-grounded substitute candidate, and develops a lab-scale manufacturing route. In a multi-seed ablation evaluation, MARS achieves the highest overall performance across all three subsystems, with gains that persist when the backbone model is held fixed. These results suggest that hierarchical decomposition, knowledge grounding, and manufacturability-aware refinement improve material-substitution reasoning beyond model capability alone.



[5]. [*IceCube data for astrophysical neutrinos exhibit q-statistical behavior*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14129 "IceCube data for astrophysical neutrinos exhibit q-statistical behavior")

Constantino Tsallis, Ugur Tirnakli

arXiv:2610.14129v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Relevant IceCube recent data for astrophysical neutrinos are analyzed. Similarly to cosmic rays and proton-proton LHC collisions, the corresponding spectra are satisfactorily described, for the entire interval of measured energies, through q-Gaussians. This fact reinforces the already available evidence that nonadditive entropies (either of the $S_q$ class or of the $S_{delta}$ one, or even alternative ones) appear to play a crucial role in systems such as black holes and related cosmological phenomena (e.g., dark energy). The IceCube data yield sensibly high values for the momenta-distribution nonadditive index (q \simeq 1.6), in contrast with the cosmic rays values (q \simeq 1.2) and the LHC values (q \simeq 1.1). This fact neatly indicates very-long-ranged space-time correlations. The detailed associated physical mechanisms remain, at the present stage, as an instigating open issue.



[6]. [*On physical implementability of reinforcement-learning-driven reasoning*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14131 "On physical implementability of reinforcement-learning-driven reasoning")

Eiji Konishi

arXiv:2610.14131v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In the machine learning community, the reinforcement-learning-driven reasoning process has been intensively studied since around 2025. In this article, we present a negative result on the hardware implementation of this reinforcement-learning-driven reasoning process in a physical neural network model in the presence of a reward external potential energy for a given reward. In this hardware implementation, the existence of a chain of thought in dynamics contradicts the Lyapunov stability of the system.



[7]. [*Electric-dipole-induced nuclear electric resonance in silicon*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14139 "Electric-dipole-induced nuclear electric resonance in silicon")

Jiyun Su, Shihang Zhang, Peihao Huang

arXiv:2610.14139v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The nuclear spin of a phosphorus atom in silicon is considered as a promising quantum information carrier for quantum computing due to its long coherence time. Coherent control of the nuclear spin is typically realized using nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR). In this work, we theoretically propose that with the aid of hyperfine interactions and magnetic field gradients, fast electric-dipole-induced nuclear electric resonance (EDNER) can be achieved. The stronger EDNER (compared with NMR) arises from the combined effects of hyperfine interaction between the nuclear and electron spins and coupling between the electron spin and charge degrees of freedom mediated by magnetic field gradients in this scheme. By operating at specific working points, the nuclear-spin splitting becomes less sensitive to electrical noise, enabling long coherence times and high-fidelity quantum operations. All elctrical, addressable control of nuclear spins paves the way for scalable silicon-based quantum computing.



[8]. [*In-Situ TEM Observation of Solid-State Phase Evolution in High-Entropy Alloy Nanoparticles*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14147 "In-Situ TEM Observation of Solid-State Phase Evolution in High-Entropy Alloy Nanoparticles")

Nirmal Kumar Katiyar, Noorbasha Bhavani Sai, Guilherme Gurian Dariani, Douglas S. Galvao, Pulickel M. Ajayan, Pedro Alves da Silva Autreto, Sriram Vijayan, Chandra Sekhar Tiwary

arXiv:2610.14147v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The emergence of new materials has gone beyond conventional design principles. Among them are high entropy alloys (HEAs), in which multiple metallic elements coexist within a single unit cell. In this case, each atomic position in a solid material has a different chemical environment, leading to configurational entropy as high as that in the fully random or melted state, which has been frozen to a solid solution. However, the behaviour of such alloy systems at pre-melting temperatures is largely unknown, where atoms gain sufficient mobility and time to rearrange, leading to an enthalpic rather than an entropic interplay. This study demonstrated the phase transformation of HEA nanoclusters (CoCrFeMnNi) under high-resolution transmission electron microscopy (HRTEM) at different temperatures, including room temperature (RT), 550 {\deg}C, and 850 {\deg}C. The clustered nanoparticles coarsen into larger clusters, accompanied by compositional heterogeneity due to surface and lattice diffusion. This experimental finding of atomic mobility was further corroborated using molecular dynamics, which provides a detailed mechanistic description.



[9]. [*Experimental observation of Higgs oscillations and their power-law decay across pairing regimes in a cavity QED quantum simulator*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14171 "Experimental observation of Higgs oscillations and their power-law decay across pairing regimes in a cavity QED quantum simulator")

Chitose Maruko, Haoqing Zhang, Eliot A. Bohr, Leah Huzjak, Anjun Chu, Victor Gurarie, Chengyi Luo, Ana Maria Rey, James K. Thompson

arXiv:2610.14171v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Understanding how pairing correlations in superconductors evolve out of equilibrium is a central challenge in quantum many-body physics. In systems with $s$-wave pairing, the character of Cooper pairing evolves continuously from a BCS regime of weakly bound, overlapping pairs to a BEC regime of tightly bound molecules, each with distinct excitation spectra. Here we realize and probe pairing dynamics across these regimes using cold, non-degenerate $^{87}$Rb atoms coupled to an optical cavity with Floquet dressing. Our cavity-QED platform emulates fermionic superfluids through a mapping onto Anderson pseudospins, while optical and microwave control tunes the ratio of interactions to single-particle energies, providing a flexible approach complementary to ultracold Feshbach and ultrafast solid-state platforms. Following a quench, we observe persistent pairing and transient oscillations of the order parameter associated with a collective amplitude (Higgs) mode. We find that the decay of these oscillations sharply distinguishes weak- and strong-pairing regimes: in the weak-pairing regime, oscillations decay with a power-law exponent $|\alpha|=0.65(8)$, whereas in the strong-pairing regime the decay is substantially faster, with $|\alpha|>1$. The oscillation frequency is set by the minimum quasiparticle excitation energy, directly linking nonequilibrium dynamics to the underlying excitation spectrum. Our results establish nonequilibrium dynamics as a direct probe of pairing character and collective excitations in interacting quantum systems.



[10]. [*Exact SU($N$) symmetry resolution of bosonic Hubbard models: general boson-fermion mappings and flat-band singlet-cluster states*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14183 "Exact SU($N$) symmetry resolution of bosonic Hubbard models: general boson-fermion mappings and flat-band singlet-cluster states")

Lo\"ic Herviou

arXiv:2610.14183v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We show how to perform exact diagonalization of SU($N$)-symmetric Bose-Hubbard models separately in each irreducible representation of SU($N$), by exploiting the Howe duality between U($L$) and U($N$). Three structural consequences follow for any lattice and any Hamiltonian built from the U($L$) generators: (i) the spectrum in a given orbital sector is independent of $N$ and of the particle statistics, (ii) the canonical partition functions for bosons and fermions obey the exact identity $Z_{\mathrm{b}}(-N)=(-1)^{N_\mathrm{b}}Z_{\mathrm{f}}(N)$, (iii) with unfrustrated hopping the groundstate is always a color ferromagnet, so that the color-resolved method we introduce is useful to study excited sectors, frustrated lattices and finite temperatures. As applications, we first derive the finite-temperature phase diagram of attractive SU($N$) bosons on few-site rings at large $N$ for arbitrary filling. We then show the emergence of singlet-cluster states from frustrated hopping, the counterpart of fermionic ferromagnetism, with the example of the sawtooth and kagome lattice.



[11]. [*Precursor stoichiometry controls dopant structure, defect chemistry, and valence in Mn-doped ZnGa$_2$O$_4$ nanocrystals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14195 "Precursor stoichiometry controls dopant structure, defect chemistry, and valence in Mn-doped ZnGa$_2$O$_4$ nanocrystals")

M\'aty\'as M. Rudolf, G\'abor Bortel, Nikoletta Jegeny\'es, \'Arp\'ad Jakab, L\'aszl\'o Bencs, Ferenc Simon, Adam Gali, D\'avid Beke

arXiv:2610.14195v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The luminescence of doped nanocrystals is usually optimised through the activator; here we show that the host cation ratio is an equally decisive, and largely overlooked, design variable. Mn-doped ZnGa$_2$O$_4$ nanocrystals (Mn:Zn = $4\times10^{-4}$) were grown hydrothermally at nominal Zn/Ga ratios between 0.30 and 0.75 (stoichiometric: 0.50) and post-annealed at 400--1100$^\circ$C, and the resulting composition--temperature matrix was mapped by X-ray- and UV-excited luminescence, EPR, Rietveld-refined X-ray diffraction, Raman spectroscopy and SEM. The as-grown crystals are phase-pure spinel at every composition and accommodate their off-stoichiometry internally as a point-defect inventory, reflected in a 0.4\% variation of the lattice parameter; on annealing, the excess gallium is exsolved as $\beta$-Ga$_2$O$_3$ in amounts set by the solution composition. The Mn$^{2+}$ emission separates into two optima, near-stoichiometric crystals annealed at 400--600$^\circ$C and Zn-excess crystals annealed at 1100$^\circ$C, the latter being twice as bright under X-ray excitation. Under X-ray and 365~nm excitation the two optima are separated by a minimum at 800$^\circ$C that coincides with a collapse of the Mn$^{2+}$ EPR population and has no counterpart in the Raman linewidths, identifying transient, defect-compensated oxidation of the dopant rather than lattice degradation as its origin. The most Zn-rich composition shows neither Mn$^{2+}$ nor Mn$^{4+}$ emission at any annealing stage, whereas its trace Cr$^{3+}$ emission is strong, indicating that Mn is rejected already during growth. Solution stoichiometry therefore acts on two levels, dopant capture and native-defect inventory, while annealing controls dopant valence and lattice order. The brightest high-temperature phosphor is thus not the nominally stoichiometric one, but the composition whose excess is consumed on its way to stoichiometry.



[12]. [*Anisotropic pressure response of thermal conductivity in crystalline polytetrafluoroethylene*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14220 "Anisotropic pressure response of thermal conductivity in crystalline polytetrafluoroethylene")

Maissa Adi, Jamal Abou Haibeh, Samuel Huberman

arXiv:2610.14220v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Compression can change thermal conductivity differently along polymer chains and across them by altering phonon velocities and lifetimes. We examine this directional response in orthorhombic Form III polytetrafluoroethylene (PTFE) at calculated pressures of 1.503, 3.452, and 5.056 GPa, using first-principles harmonic and cubic force constants within the single-mode relaxation-time approximation (SMRTA). At 100, 200, and 300 K, the chain-axis conductivity decreases at every pressure step. Between 3.452 and 5.056 GPa, both transverse components recover: at 300 K they increase by 23.7% and 13.7%, while the longitudinal conductivity decreases by 14.5%. An exact factorization separates the conductivity into a heat-capacity--velocity factor and a weighted effective lifetime. The transverse recovery follows from larger heat-capacity-weighted squared velocities, consistent with stiffer interchain restoring forces. Along the chains, shorter effective lifetimes dominate, and both conductivity-weighted Normal and Umklapp rates increase at 300 K. Comparing the same cubic force-constant blocks at all three pressures, we find that interchain coupling is already substantial at 1.503 GPa and increases under compression.



[13]. [*Emergence of quantum transport in SnTe films upon structural refinement*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14233 "Emergence of quantum transport in SnTe films upon structural refinement")

Shrinkhala Sharma, Rabindra Basnet, Thomas Parker, Afolabi Akinfaderin, Alexander Samokhvalov, Rajeswari Kolagani, Ramesh C. Budhani

arXiv:2610.14233v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Topological crystalline insulators are distinguished from the conventional Z2 topological insulators by crystalline symmetry protection of the surface states. Quantitative characterization of these states requires magneto-transport measurements from which the phase coherence length and number of independent coherent channels can be extracted. Here we report such measurements on textured thin films of SnTe grown on Bi2Te3 buffered c-plane sapphire (Al2O3) by pulsed laser ablation. A post deposition annealing protocol was implemented to control the crystallographic texture, which reveals improved crystallinity and enhanced epitaxial alignment along the [111] direction in films annealed at elevated temperatures. The higher carrier density and lower resistivity seen in films annealed at 100 oC are suggestive of Sn vacancies which act as native acceptor defects, while films annealed at 200 oC approach the 1:1 stoichiometry. The magnetoresistance of all samples at T < 10 K characterized by a cusp centered around zero-field is suggestive of weak antilocalization and interpreted in the framework of the Hikami-Larkin-Nagaoka theory in the strong spin-orbit coupling limit. At T > 20 K, the MR is small and positive, and deviates from conventional Kohler scaling. An approximate collapse of the MR curves between 50 and 250 K is obtained using an empirical temperature-dependent scaling factor, indicating that changes in the Hall-derived carrier density alone cannot account for the observed scaling behavior. These results demonstrate that post growth annealing strongly influences the interplay between stoichiometry, crystallinity, and coherent transport in SnTe thin films.



[14]. [*Benchmarking machine-learning interatomic potentials using classical force fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14239 "Benchmarking machine-learning interatomic potentials using classical force fields")

Johannes Sandberg, Genevi\`eve Dusson, Julien Lam

arXiv:2610.14239v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We propose a rational comparison between several well-known machine-learning interatomic potentials (linear atomic cluster expansion, the Behler-Parrinello high-dimensional neural network potential, and the graph neural network-based MACE potential) by testing their performance for fitting classical potentials such as the Lennard-Jones and Stillinger-Weber potential. With this we assess their advantages and limitations regarding several aspects: varying training dataset size, robustness to noise, carbon footprint, as well as the ability to reproduce the radial distribution function as an example observable. Overall the MACE models are shown to most accurately reproduce the potential energy surface, with little sign of overfitting, but at a significant computational cost, highlighting important questions regarding the choice of models in computational materials science.



[15]. [*Partitioning the magnetization raises the stability limit of ferromagnetic colloids*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14247 "Partitioning the magnetization raises the stability limit of ferromagnetic colloids")

Paramita Modak, Priyadharshini M, Ankur Jaiswar, Ambarish Ghosh

arXiv:2610.14247v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Magnetic actuation scales with a particle's magnetic moment, yet practical colloidal suspensions remain superparamagnetic or weakly magnetized, a permanent dipole drives a near contact attraction growing as the square of the moment, binding particles irreversibly. Here we show that this constraint depends not only on how much magnetic material a particle carries, but on where it sits. Partitioning a fixed moment M into N internal elements leaves the far field interaction, and every actuation force, unchanged while suppressing the near-field pair energy by 1/N. As we show in our experiments across 57 samples and three geometries, stability collapses onto a single $\sim M^{2}/N$ boundary, raising the accessible dipolar energy scale nearly 150-fold. A liquid-phase analogue of magnetic force microscopy images the engineered energy landscape directly. The resulting colloids outrun size-matched superparamagnetic beads in weak fields far from a magnetic source, suggesting their applicability in hard to reach places. Together, these results open a new operating regime for colloidal manipulation, with impactful implications in microfluidics and drug delivery.



[16]. [*Explicit Solutions in a Probabilistic Cellular Automaton Model for Self-Assembled Monolayer Degradation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14256 "Explicit Solutions in a Probabilistic Cellular Automaton Model for Self-Assembled Monolayer Degradation")

Wes Caldwell, Ryan Evans, Jacob Majikes, Anthony Kearsley

arXiv:2610.14256v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Self-assembled monolayers (SAMs), formed by the adsorption of a monomolecular layer of adsorbate onto a surface, have emerged as leading candidates for plat- forms for electrochemical biosensors. In order to characterize the working age of a SAM-based biosensor, it is necessary to understand the long-term degradation of the monolayer, with particular emphasis on the intermolecular forces present in the self-assembly process. In this work, discrete- and continuous-time proba- bilistic cellular automaton (PCA) models for the degradation of SAMs involving adsorbate-adsorbate interactions are proposed in which adsorbate molecules desorb at a rate dependent only on the number of adsorbed neighbors. Impor- tantly, we make no assumptions on the relationship between degradation rate and adsorbed neighbors. Using techniques from generating function theory and complex analysis, the expected degradation curve is solved for explicitly in one dimension and shown to be a sigmoid curve related to the Gompertz function.



[17]. [*Mn--Cr dopant association in dilute co-doped $\mathrm{ZnAl_2O_4}$ revealed by combined EPR and optical spectroscopy*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14262 "Mn--Cr dopant association in dilute co-doped $\mathrm{ZnAl_2O_4}$ revealed by combined EPR and optical spectroscopy")

Apoko S. Omondi, M\'aty\'as M. Rudolf, Nikoletta Jegeny\'es, \'Arp\'ad Jakab, G\'abor Bortel, Bence G. M\'arkus, Adam Gali, D\'avid Beke

arXiv:2610.14262v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Efficient and durable phosphors made from abundant elements underpin energy-saving solid-state lighting, near-infrared light sources for sensing, and X-ray and biomedical imaging. Co-doping is a standard route to brighter phosphors, yet the fate of the co-dopants is rarely established. We combine quantitative electron paramagnetic resonance (EPR) spin counting with photoluminescence, photoluminescence excitation, and X-ray-excited spectroscopy across two dilute Mn/Cr co-doping series of hydrothermally grown $\mathrm{ZnAl_2O_4}$ nanoparticles and show that the two ions can form an exchange-coupled, edge-sharing Cr--Mn defect complex, with $\mathrm{Mn^{2+/4+}}$ entering an octahedral site next to $\mathrm{Cr^{3+}}$. The octahedral Mn lifts the inversion symmetry of the $\mathrm{Cr^{3+}}$ site and strengthens its parity-forbidden absorption, enhancing the $\mathrm{Cr^{3+}}$ emission without energy transfer. The population of the defect complex follows the law of mass action, and above a threshold Mn concentration, Mn re-partitions into EPR-silent octahedral configurations that quench both emissions. Concentration optima in co-doped phosphors can thus reflect defect speciation, not only energy-transfer efficiency, which opens a structural design handle for brighter emitters.



[18]. [*Establishing the role of thickness in LaOBr; an alternative gate dielectric for vdW heterostructures*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14272 "Establishing the role of thickness in LaOBr; an alternative gate dielectric for vdW heterostructures")

Isaac Antoine Lagaud, Justin Boddison-Chouinard, Antoine Labb\'e, Ayse Melis Aygar, Alex Bogan, Li-Lin Tay, Takashi Taniguchi, Kenji Watanabe, Aljoscha S\"oll, Zden\v{e}k Sofer, Adina Luican-Mayer, Louis Gaudreau

arXiv:2610.14272v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In electronic devices, the quality of the dielectric material is determined by figures of merit such as the band gap, leakage onset field, breakdown field and the number of built-in defects, which can all limit device performance. An additional and important metric to consider is the strength of the dielectric constant. Hexagonal boron nitride (hBN) is the benchmark gate dielectric for two-dimensional van der Waals heterostructures, owing to its wide bandgap, and atomically flat interface. At the same time, its dielectric constant (2--4) highlights the opportunity to explore alternative higher-permittivity van der Waals dielectrics that provide stronger electrostatic control. Previous work established lanthanum oxybromide (LaOBr), a layered dielectric with a predicted dielectric constant of 12.5, as a potential dielectric for van der Waals heterostructures. Using graphene field-effect devices with LaOBr, we show that the leakage-onset and breakdown fields decrease systematically with thickness. Dual-gate capacitance measurements combined with a dead-layer model reveal a bulk dielectric constant of $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bulk}} = 12.2$, in agreement with theory, while interfacial dead-layers cause the effective dielectric constant to vary from 6 to 11 across multiple thicknesses. Benchmarked against established gate dielectrics, LaOBr combines breakdown fields comparable to hBN with dielectric constants 2 to 3 times larger. By establishing the operating regime of devices with various thicknesses, we further confirm the advantage of LaOBr over hBN for high-capacitance gate-dielectric operation. These results validate LaOBr as a promising dielectric for integration into two-dimensional heterostructures, and establish thickness-dependent benchmarking as essential for evaluating emerging layered dielectric materials.



[19]. [*Revealing New Leakage Paths in GAAFETs using Predictive First-Principles Simulations*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14298 "Revealing New Leakage Paths in GAAFETs using Predictive First-Principles Simulations")

Denis Mamaluy, Juan P. Mendez, Coleman Cariker, Michael Titze, Alex A. Belianinov

arXiv:2610.14298v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Gate-All-Around Field-Effect Transistors (GAAFETs) are emerging as successors to Fin Field-Effect Transistors (FinFETs) and are central to continued scaling at advanced semiconductor nodes. Despite their improved electrostatic control, recent measurements show that the drain current in GAAFETs deviates strongly from thermionic behavior at negative gate voltages, increasing as the gate voltage becomes more negative. This leakage has been attributed to direct gate-drain tunneling through the high-$k$ dielectric, enhanced by drain-induced dielectric barrier lowering (DIDBL). Here, we use predictive, charge-self-consistent Non-Equilibrium Green Function (NEGF) simulations based on the Contact Block Reduction (CBR) method to spatially resolve this leakage pathway in GAAFETs. Unlike approaches based primarily on phenomenological leakage models, our first-principles, multi-terminal quantum-transport framework treats the source, drain, and gate as current-carrying quantum-mechanical contacts and resolves the local current density throughout the device. The spatially resolved current density shows that gate-injected carriers tunnel through the high-$k$ dielectric preferentially near the drain side of the gate, where the dielectric barrier is most strongly lowered by the drain bias, and are collected by the drain. This provides a direct real-space picture of the DIDBL-driven gate-drain leakage mechanism.



[20]. [*A Solution-Processed Ti/Pt/NiOx/Ag RRAM Device with Multiple Forming-Free Non-Volatile Intermediate States*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14322 "A Solution-Processed Ti/Pt/NiOx/Ag RRAM Device with Multiple Forming-Free Non-Volatile Intermediate States")

Abhrajit Roy, Chopali Chanchal Sahu, Rudraksh Sangore, Raman Hissariya, Poulomi Chakrabarty, Harsh Roniyar, Merlyne De Souza, Debanjan Bhowmik

arXiv:2610.14322v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Artificial neural networks (ANN) implemented on crossbar arrays of non-volatile synaptic devices are known to consume much less energy compared to that implemented on conventional computing units. Solution-processed resistive random-access memory (RRAM) devices based on transition-metal oxides are suitable as such synaptic devices because of their cheap and easy fabrication method, stability, and suitable memristive properties. Here, in NiO-based RRAM devices with Ti/Pt as the bottom electrode and Ag as the top electrode (NiO layer deposited through solution processing), we experimentally report forming-free binary switching between a high resistance state (HRS) and a low resistance state (LRS) as well as forming-free pulse-driven modulation (long-term potentiation (LTP), long-term depression (LTD)) through 30 intermediate synaptic states between HRS and LRS (around 5-bit synaptic weight resolution). Many of these states are measured to be stable at least up to 1000 seconds confirming their non-volatility and their utility in storage of synaptic weights for ANN crossbar arrays. On the contrary, most prior experimental reports (including reports both on solution-processing-based and sputtering-based RRAM devices) require forming to exhibit non-volatility. Avoiding forming reduces variability, eliminates the need for high voltage pulses, and makes the crossbar array design simpler. Further, we confirm the presence of oxygen vacancies in our materials stack using X-ray photo-electron spectroscopy (XPS). Finally, we show successful data classification on MNIST and CIFAR-10 data sets using a multi-layer perceptron (MLP) model and a convolution neural network (CNN) model respectively, where the synaptic weight updates of these ANN models are calibrated against the experimentally measured LTP-LTD characteristics of the NiO RRAM devices.



[21]. [*Competitive scaling of small-subsystem-optimization heuristics for LABS*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14361 "Competitive scaling of small-subsystem-optimization heuristics for LABS")

Ginger E. Lau, Adil A. Gangat

arXiv:2610.14361v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The low autocorrelation binary sequences (LABS) problem, often formulated as a spin-lattice energy-minimization problem, is a combinatorial optimization problem with a very rugged energy landscape. We demonstrate that iterative optimization of very small subsystems of n spins that are decoupled from the rest of the system achieves global optimization of LABS with a time-to-solution scaling that is competitive with heuristics that optimize the global system of N spins directly. Our isolated-subsystem heuristic with n = 4 achieves a time scaling of O(1.333N ) on the interval N = [27, 60], while the current state-of-the-art classical and quantum scalings are respectively O(1.34N ) and O(1.21N ). Our results suggest that specialized optimization hardware with only small numbers of logical spins may be of practical relevance.



[22]. [*Explicit Solvents Reverse Temperature Dependence of Phase Behavior in Charged Fluids*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14366 "Explicit Solvents Reverse Temperature Dependence of Phase Behavior in Charged Fluids")

[23]. [*Direct Observation of Chirality-Dependent Berry Curvature Dipole via Cross-Axial Nonlinear Hall Effect in Trigonal Te*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14367 "Direct Observation of Chirality-Dependent Berry Curvature Dipole via Cross-Axial Nonlinear Hall Effect in Trigonal Te")

[24]. [*Atoms that Matter: Largest Materials Datasets Are 99% Redundant, and Local Chemistry Can Guide the Required 1%*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14392 "Atoms that Matter: Largest Materials Datasets Are 99% Redundant, and Local Chemistry Can Guide the Required 1%")

[25]. [*Biomolecular condensates and their pathologies: A colloidal, interfacial, and transport perspective*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14398 "Biomolecular condensates and their pathologies: A colloidal, interfacial, and transport perspective")

Abstract: Biomolecular condensates are micron-scale, membraneless compartments that concentrate proteins and nucleic acids by liquid-liquid phase separation (LLPS). Condensates play critical roles in transcription, mRNA metabolism, stress responses, and signaling across the cell, with their dysregulation increasingly correlated with several pathologies spanning neurodegeneration, cancer, cardiometabolic disease, and viral infections. The emergent biology of condensates can be cohesively viewed through the framework of colloidal, interfacial, and transport phenomena. Here we review the condensate literature through this joint lens, treating each condensate as a biological colloid whose behavior is captured by four measurable physical quantities: a Flory--Huggins interaction parameter that determines whether the dense phase forms at all, a partition coefficient that dictates the degree of client molecule accumulation, a viscoelastic relaxation time that sets whether the dense phase responds as a liquid or a solid, and an interfacial tension that governs droplet shape and interfacial dynamics. Each quantity is traced to its physical origin, its measured range in biological condensates, and the techniques that quantify it, for both three-dimensional droplets and two-dimensional membrane-tethered condensates. Interpreting disease as the displacement of these physical quantities from their functional ranges, we reorganize condensate pathologies into a small set of physical failure modes --- saturation concentration dysregulation, aberrant aging and liquid-to-solid transitions, disrupted partitioning, interfacial aggregation, and pathogen hijacking --- and show how the same parameters become design variables for condensate-targeted drugs and engineered coacervate delivery vehicles. The review concludes with open questions at the interface of condensate biology and colloidal and interfacial science.

The dimer structure makes the two doped holes (electrons) bound up and form a singlet which is also polarized. Another type of dopant in cuprates is oxygen which behaves similarly. So, we propose that, for all oxide high TC superconductors, the holes (electrons) are pre-paired (Z-pairs) during the preparation of the superconductor. The spontaneously formed Z-pairs are conceptually different from not only the Cooper pairs, but also the Zhang-Rice singlets. The attraction force in a Z-pair originates basically from the strong antiferromagnetic exchange energy. We also found that the on-site Coulomb repulsion (U) is no longer a constant due to doping. Around a doping site, U can reduce its value discontinuously, which plays a key role in stabilizing the Z-pairs against isolated holes (electrons). A Z-pair is a p-wave like localized state which breaks the symmetry of the CuO2 plain and attracts each other along the direction perpendicular to the polarization. The Z-pairs may be popular in all of the unconventional superconductors and the periodic assembly can result in the emergence of coherence states and superconductivity.

[28]. [*Large Out-of-plane Polarization in Two-dimensional Bi2O3 Monolayers Predicted by an AI-driven Inverse-design Workflow*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14429 "Large Out-of-plane Polarization in Two-dimensional Bi2O3 Monolayers Predicted by an AI-driven Inverse-design Workflow")

[30]. [*Soft-to-Rigid: Shape Continuation as an Alternative Search for Packing Congruent Shapes*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14463 "Soft-to-Rigid: Shape Continuation as an Alternative Search for Packing Congruent Shapes")

Abstract: Searches for dense packings of congruent shapes move rigid pieces. Hardening starts each piece as its inscribed disk and deforms it into the target shape while the container is compressed. We ask whether it is a search worth running alongside existing ones, and test it at matched budget and comparable tuning against rigid starts, growth, simulated annealing and perturbation--compression on seven families of squares, triangles, hexagons and disks in squares, triangles and circles (188 instances), scoring each method by the lowest container size it reaches. Hardening is not a better default: tuned rigid starts reach the lowest size most often. It is a different search. On squares in a square it reaches packings the other four methods miss, including Trump's n = 11 and Wainwright's n = 19 in 32 runs per method, and in a targeted follow-up campaign at ten times the budget (16 runs per method) it is the only one to reach the best known packings for n = 17 and n = 26, while annealing alone reaches n = 27. In exploratory ablations, compressing disks and then switching to polygons at once, or growing rigid polygons along hardening's area schedule, shows no detectable difference from rigid starts, and hardening's unique packings contain more tilted pieces. The bias costs elsewhere: hardening is worse on triangles and on four families held out from all development, where the short-pilot policy we tested showed no detected advantage over always starting rigid. Shape continuation is an alternative search that reaches different packings, worth keeping beside rigid ones; we did not show that splitting a fixed budget between them pays, and we have no test that predicts where it helps. Search campaigns were planned before they ran, with amendments, exploratory analyses and post-hoc checks identified separately; every run is replayable and every number is generated from an event log.



[31]. [*Quantum Geometry and Pair Breaking in Intra- and Intervalley Superconductivity of Rhombohedral Graphene*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14480 "Quantum Geometry and Pair Breaking in Intra- and Intervalley Superconductivity of Rhombohedral Graphene")

Abstract: The observation of superconductivity emerging from a spin- and valley-polarized normal state in rhombohedral graphene raises a basic puzzle: intravalley Cooper pairs involve states that are not generically degenerate because of trigonal warping, and therefore lack the particle--particle nesting underlying the conventional Cooper instability. We separate the superconducting instability into three contributions: the symmetry-resolved form-factor-dressed interaction, the Cooper-pair phase space determined by the symmetrized pair dispersion, and the energy mismatch generated by trigonal warping. We find that the Bloch form factors strongly favor intravalley pairing, with a leading predominantly $p_x-ip_y$ instability and a substantially larger maximum $T_c$ than the intervalley reference state. At the same time, trigonal-warping-induced mismatch cuts off the Cooper logarithm and confines intravalley superconductivity to a much narrower region of the phase diagram. The dominant instability occurs near pair-Lifshitz transitions and primarily in reconstructed annular or three-lobed Fermi-surface regimes. [32]. [*Exotic metallic phase in a heterotic Kondo lattice: violation of Luttinger's theorem and absence of Kondo gap*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14556 "Exotic metallic phase in a heterotic Kondo lattice: violation of Luttinger's theorem and absence of Kondo gap")

We examine this issue within the paramagnetic phase of a Kondo lattice system with a heterotic layered structure. In the weak Kondo-coupling regime, the system exhibits a fractional Dirac liquid involving the massless or massive Dirac fermions and a charge neutral spinon Fermi surface. In the strong Kondo-coupling regime, the spinons hybridize with the Dirac fermions resulting in an intermediate nearly-flat hybridizing band together with the conduction and valence heavy fermion bands, yielding a large electric Fermi surface. Absence of the Kondo gap between these bands is observed depending on the stacking configurations of the layered structure. In particular, the system remains metallic even at the exact half filling in this regime and Luttinger's theorem is violated across the critical point. Implications of these results on the van der Waals quantum heterostructures are briefly discussed.

Abstract: Self-propelled Janus particles are promising active agents for microscale manipulation and microrobotic applications. Here, we present an optoelectronic (OE) strategy for simultaneously and independently controlling electrically driven, self-propelled metallo-dielectric Janus particles (JPs) using spatially programmable virtual electrodes. These electrodes are generated by projecting a single predefined illumination pattern from a digital micromirror device onto a photoconductive surface. The resulting confinement landscape enables multiple JPs to follow distinct trajectories without continuous monitoring, feedback, or pattern reconfiguration. We demonstrate controlled pairing of JPs with passive polystyrene particles and biological cells. For automated multi-agent operation, an image-based assignment framework identifies optimal agent-target pairs, excludes intersecting trajectories, and generates a single composite pattern for simultaneous, selective pair formation without real-time updating. Finally, we demonstrate independent manipulation of multiple magnetically rolling JPs under a globally applied magnetic field. Overall, this programmable open-loop strategy provides a versatile framework for multi-agent microrobotic manipulation, with potential applications in in-vitro micromanipulation and microfluidic systems.

[40]. [*Finite-temperature adiabatic-connection reduced density-matrix functional theory: a unified framework for i-DMFT and TAO-DFT*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14706 "Finite-temperature adiabatic-connection reduced density-matrix functional theory: a unified framework for i-DMFT and TAO-DFT")

[50]. [53]. [*Time-temperature-composition equivalence of the viscoelastic behavior through the glass transition of digitally mixed 3D-printed polymers*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14986 "Time-temperature-composition equivalence of the viscoelastic behavior through the glass transition of digitally mixed 3D-printed polymers")

Abstract: The linear viscoelastic behavior of a polymer shows an equivalence of loading frequency and temperature, the time-temperature equivalence (TTE). Horizontal shifting extends moduli measured over limited ranges to much wider ranges, through the glass transition. Composition shifts the location and breadth of the glass transition and increases the rubbery modulus. Here we extend TTE to a time-temperature-composition equivalence (TTCE). Across all measured temperatures, frequencies, and compositions, the storage modulus collapses onto one master curve. Composition translates the transition and stretches its breadth, both linearly. [55]. [*DiffGA: Steering Frozen Diffusion Models for Materials Design via Evolutionary Search*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14999 "DiffGA: Steering Frozen Diffusion Models for Materials Design via Evolutionary Search")

Abstract: Steering candidate generation toward user-defined objectives is central to inverse materials design, yet remains difficult when objectives are black-box, changing, or non-differentiable, as in many simulation- and experiment-derived properties. Generative models offer a solution but typically require retraining or fine-tuning for each new objective, demanding labeled data and substantial compute, or rely on differentiable guidance that is brittle to gradient noise and undefined for many practical targets. We present DiffGA, a retraining-free framework that injects evolutionary search into the sampling trajectory of a retrained diffusion model, evolving partially denoised states under an arbitrary black-box oracle. Because oracle evaluation often dominates campaign cost, we couple DiffGA with an on-the-fly Bayesian surrogate that reuses representations from state-of-the-art machine-learned force fields (MLFFs). A Gaussian process is fit during the campaign on the force fields's graph embeddings and, once it demonstrably ranks an unseen generation, replaces expensive property evaluations under an uncertainty-aware acquisition rule, reducing oracle cost with negligible loss of accuracy. Across three crystal-design tasks using a frozen MatterGen backbone, DiffGA outperforms diffusion-only, compute-matched, purely evolutionary, and fine-tuned baselines in oracle-assessed rates of stable, unique, and novel hits. Rather than proposing scientific compounds, we use density functional theory to quantify MLFF oracle fidelity, showing that it is a faithful proxy in key regimes but systematically biased in others, delineating when proxy-driven generative discovery can be trusted. On a representative sample, DiffGA's top hits survive first principles re-evaluation as physically realistic hits, while the surrogate-accelerated pipeline preserves this fidelity at a fraction of the cost.

[57]. [*Role of defect trapping in the enhanced corrosion resistance of Ni-Mo alloys with yttrium and yttrium oxide additions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15089 "Role of defect trapping in the enhanced corrosion resistance of Ni-Mo alloys with yttrium and yttrium oxide additions")

[58]. [*Contact-mediated asymmetric delay and rate memory in snapping beam pairs*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15119 "Contact-mediated asymmetric delay and rate memory in snapping beam pairs")

Abstract: Slender structures in dense environments interact through contact, which alters how and when they become unstable. Here we show that contact makes snap-through history dependent, so that when and whether a beam pair snaps depends on how long the contact is loaded. Under cyclic compression, the pair snaps on loading, snaps on unloading, or stays packed, with the loading and unloading rates selecting among the three. Upon contact, one beam rolls along the other without slip, and faster driving lets it roll further before it slides and snaps: to larger compression on loading and to smaller compression on unloading. This delay is asymmetric between loading and unloading as the unloading branch carries a memory of the loading rate. [59]. [*Spectral Mapping Theorem between SU(N) and SU(Ns/N) Heisenberg Models and Exact Diagonalization of SU(4) and SU(7) Long-Range Ordered Phases on the Triangular Lattice*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15162 "Spectral Mapping Theorem between SU(N) and SU(Ns/N) Heisenberg Models and Exact Diagonalization of SU(4) and SU(7) Long-Range Ordered Phases on the Triangular Lattice")

arXiv:2610.15162v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We first show that, for the SU(N) Heisenberg model with isotropic two-site interactions on an arbitrary lattice of Ns sites, the full spectrum in its singlet sector (when N divides Ns) is the negative of the full spectrum in its singlet sector of the SU(Ns/N) Heisenberg model defined on the same lattice. This theorem, valid for models with the fundamental irreducible representation (irrep) at each lattice site, follows naturally from a transposition operation in the basis of Standard Young Tableaux (SYTs). Second, this basis -- previously recognized as particularly convenient for exact diagonalization (ED) of SU(N) Heisenberg models -- admits an efficient, memory-saving implementation, thereby enabling large-scale diagonalizations for unprecedented system sizes Ns and numbers of colors N>3. We apply these ideas to an ED study of the SU(4) and SU(7) Heisenberg models on the triangular lattice (TL), extending the calculations up to Ns=28 sites. In particular, starting from the SU(4) case, whose nearest-neighbor ground state remains debated, we confirm that next-nearest-neighbor couplings stabilize four-sublattice long-range order. We further find that increasing the number of colors from N=4 to N=7 provides another route toward a N\'eel-like ground state, even without such additional couplings. N = 4 to N = 7 provides another route toward a N\'eel-like ground state, even without such additional couplings.



[60]. [*Interfacial Coupling Reshapes Nonlinear Polarization in a Spin-Defect van der Waals Heterostructure*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15189 "Interfacial Coupling Reshapes Nonlinear Polarization in a Spin-Defect van der Waals Heterostructure")

[61]. [*Reparameterizing GFN1-xTB for Ionic Liquids*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15190 "Reparameterizing GFN1-xTB for Ionic Liquids")

IL-xTB follows a hierarchical parameterization strategy including different cation and anion families and substantially reduces the systematic errors of GFN1-xTB across the chemical space considered, providing a route for developing xTB parameterizations with broader applicability to ionic liquids. [62]. [*Nucleation at the GST--oxide interface and beam-driven ordering in amorphous Ge--Sb--Te: HAADF, EDS and electron pair distribution functions across a 4D-STEM dose series*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15196 "Nucleation at the GST--oxide interface and beam-driven ordering in amorphous Ge--Sb--Te: HAADF, EDS and electron pair distribution functions across a 4D-STEM dose series")

Abstract: Where crystal nuclei form in amorphous phase-change materials decides how they switch. Nucleation sites do not differ in composition or number density: the nuclei lie on the same robust HAADF-EDS relation as the matrix, and at fixed HAADF the spread of the summed EDS signal equals its Poisson counting noise (ratio 1.03 +- 0.05). The nuclei are nevertheless crystalline and stand out in the 4D-STEM max-intensity virtual image (median z = 11.0) but not in HAADF (z = 1.3) or EDS (z = 0.3). All bulk classes share one short-range structure (first shell 2.70-2.74 {\AA}); towards the interface the first sharp diffraction peak (FSDP) moves to lower q, and every method isolates a thin interface-edge environment with a shorter or missing first shell. The I(q) classification isolates, in every scan, an interface environment that holds 62-100% of the nucleus pixels (1.2-2.5-fold above depth) and persists when the nucleus pixels are removed before classification. The interface, rather than a distinct composition or density of the nucleating volume, therefore marks where nuclei form. Over 4.7e3 e/{\AA}^2 the HAADF signal falls by 17%, the first shell shortens by 0.03 {\AA} and the FSDP narrows by 9%, apparently ordering the film as it damages it. All analyses used PySTEMLab, an interactive 4D-STEM analysis environment developed for this work.

Abstract: The discovery of integer and fractional Chern insulators in rhombohedral graphene, following their discovery in the isolated topological flat band in twisted MoTe$_2$, motivates a search for the conditions under which an electronic band can host a topological correlated state. [66]. [*Dispersion of low-energy optical excitations in NiPS$_3$ explained by singlet polaron theory*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15230 "Dispersion of low-energy optical excitations in NiPS$_3$ explained by singlet polaron theory")

[67]. [*Framework for an AI-Ready ARPES Community Database*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15236 "Framework for an AI-Ready ARPES Community Database")

Zhi-Xun Shen, Jin Gene Wong, Karen Lei, Leon Garcia, Felix Baumberger, Sergey Borisenko, Aaron Bostwick, Ting Cao, Tanny Chavez, Yulin Chen, Monika Choudhary, Andrea Damascelli, Dan Dessau, Thomas P. Devereaux, Sandy A. Ekahana, Alexei Fedorov, Atsushi Fujimori, Yucheng Guo, M. Zahid Hasan, Makoto Hashimoto, Yu He, Philip Hofmann, Zahid Hussain, Harold Y. Hwang, Zhurun Ji, Chunjing Jia, Timur K. Kim, Phil D. C. King, Takeshi Kondo, Alessandra Lanzara, Chun Lin, Donghui Lu, Matteo Michiardi, Sung-Kwan Mo, Brian Moritz, Laurenz Rettig, Eli Rotenberg, Takafumi Sato, Kyle M. Shen, Jonathan A. Sobota, Inna Vishik, Shuolong Yang, Ming Yi, Timothy Zaklama

arXiv:2610.15236v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Condensed matter stands to gain profoundly from widespread adoption of artificial intelligence (AI). However, its current means of data organization impedes this endeavor. As a premier measure of electronic structure underlying material properties, Angle-Resolved Photoemission Spectroscopy (ARPES) has accumulated over four decades of spectra, yet the community's publication-centered workflow preserves only a highly selected fraction of its experimental record. A shared data ecosystem could transform these fragmented measurements and their contexts into a persistent, collective scientific memory, creating immediate value for reproducibility and reuse while laying the foundation for AI-enabled discovery. In this Perspective, we report outcomes of a recent community workshop and propose a practical path toward such an ecosystem. Its benefits unfold along three timescales. In the near term, standardized, searchable, and citable datasets strengthen analysis pipelines independently of AI. In the intermediate future, community-validated tools operating across laboratories could enable broader reuse and increasingly closed-loop automated experiments. In the long term, a diverse corpus of measured electronic spectra could support foundation models for quantum matter. Looking forward, a diverse corpus of measured electronic spectra could support foundation models for quantum matter and new discoveries. We discuss lessons from existing scientific databases, the current state of AI in ARPES, as well as areas of community convergence on data organization, access, credit, governance, and a staged pilot program.



[68]. [*Valley-resolved high harmonics probe symmetry breaking beyond the Dirac cone*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15251 "Valley-resolved high harmonics probe symmetry breaking beyond the Dirac cone")

arXiv:2610.15251v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Low-energy electrons in graphene obey an emergent, rotationally invariant Dirac equation, while the honeycomb lattice enters only through higher-order, symmetry-breaking corrections, the leading one being trigonal warping. This provides a unique solid-state arena to address a cornerstone challenge in quantum gravity: how to detect ultraviolet symmetry-breaking granular corrections, suppressed by a fundamental scale, atop an emergent infrared relativistic continuum. We show that intense counter-rotating $\omega$-$2\omega$ fields and valley-resolved high-harmonic generation (HHG) provide such a probe. Within a family of models that interpolates continuously between the isotropic Dirac cone and nearest-neighbour graphene at fixed Fermi velocity, the valley contrast of the harmonic yield vanishes identically in the symmetric theory, grows linearly with the warping coupling, and its valley-even counterpart appears only at second order, as expected from effective-field-theory power counting. The stronger the laser, the farther it drives electrons from the Dirac points, so the laser intensity plays the role of the collision energy. For the fourth harmonic, the leading warping correction alone accounts for the valley contrast of real graphene up to fundamental intensities of about $10^{11}$ W/cm$^2$, below the damage threshold of monolayer graphene, whereas the fifth harmonic already reveals higher-order lattice corrections at intensities around $10^{10}$ W/cm$^2$. The two harmonics, which rotate in opposite directions, are emitted by opposite valleys. Valley-resolved harmonics are far more sensitive than the residual valley populations, making them a practical detector of lattice-scale physics hidden beneath an emergent Dirac theory.



[69]. [*Three-dimensional reconstruction of weakly scattering objects from single-defocus 4D-STEM data*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15303 "Three-dimensional reconstruction of weakly scattering objects from single-defocus 4D-STEM data")

In this study, we propose a 3D-structure linear inversion (3D LINV) STEM algorithm, which enables a fast and direct reconstruction of 3D structures from a single-defocus 4D-STEM dataset. This method is based on solving linear equations relating the measured 4D-STEM data and the unknown 3D specimen potential under the thick weak phase object approximation. [70]. [*Weyl's theorem and the theoretical origin of Boson peak in two-dimensional scalar spring network*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15318 "Weyl's theorem and the theoretical origin of Boson peak in two-dimensional scalar spring network")

arXiv:2610.15318v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The origin of the anomalous low-frequency vibrational density of states (VDOS) universally observed in amorphous solids has been debated for decades. In this work, we model amorphous solids as a zero-temperature scalar spring network, the simplest model that captures both the amorphous structure and the features of the anomalous VDOS. Using this scalar network, we mathematically demonstrate that disorder can lead to a shift of the VDOS toward low frequencies, thereby accumulating the anomalous part there. Disorder is defined through the topological connectivity of the network. The validity and universality of the theoretical framework are general and valuable for real-world scenarios because it rests only on Weyl's theorem for Hermitian matrices and is free of perturbation and phenomenological assumptions. This work strips away complex factors and reveals, at the level of mathematical principles, a profound and clean mechanism for the origin of the anomalous VDOS in amorphous solids.



[71]. [*Programmable memristive circuits for reconfigurable collective pseudomagnetic ordering, transport, and learning*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15346 "Programmable memristive circuits for reconfigurable collective pseudomagnetic ordering, transport, and learning")

Sichang Qiu, Yuxin Jiang, Yongchao Wu, Peng Han, Xinxin Gao, Zekun Huang, Ce Shang, Ronny Thomale, Tie Jun Cui, Shuo Liu

Abstract: Programmable hardware typically uses fixed architectures, whereas complex physical systems obtain diverse behavior by connecting reusable local elements through different interaction rules. Memristive devices combine writable states with nonlinear dynamics, but translating these local properties into reconfigurable collective order and symmetry-governed transport remains challenging. Here we develop modular circuits configured through programmable connections between identical memristive units. Experiments establish binary conductance states as Ising-type pseudospins and show that signed feedback between units selects uniform or staggered pseudospin order. Circuit-model simulations then examine controllable domain-wall motion and pattern recovery. Although the antiferromagnetic and altermagnetic encodings share a compensated pattern, their distinct voltage spreading illustrates how programmed circuit symmetry affects transport. Device-calibrated simulations show that memristive dynamics generates history-dependent features for reservoir computing. By separating local memory from programmable interactions, symmetry encoding and task-specific readout, this architecture provides a modular route from device dynamics to collective order, symmetry-dependent transport, and learning.



[72]. [*Microscopic foundation for orbitronics: orbital exchange interactions, orbital-dependent transport, and orbital switching*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15374 "Microscopic foundation for orbitronics: orbital exchange interactions, orbital-dependent transport, and orbital switching")

Abstract: Recent experiments highlighted the essential role of the orbital degrees of freedom underlying current-induced magnetic dynamics. However, a microscopic picture of how orbital angular momenta are transferred between itinerant and localized electrons, and the way it feeds back into transport, remains elusive. Here we develop a theoretical framework for the orbital exchange interactions by generalizing the Anderson-Kondo model to incorporate the orbitals of conduction bands. Illustrating with the $p$-orbitals, we determine the way electrons acquire orbital polarization and its influence on the transport properties, uncovering the orbital counterparts of phenomena analogous to spin filtering and tunnel magnetoresistance. Reciprocally, we demonstrate that orbital-polarized currents can exert torques on localized magnetic moments without having to convert into spin currents, which enables electrical switching of magnetization even when spin polarization becomes inactive. These striking discoveries lay a microscopic foundation for orbitronics and reveal critical mechanisms for orbital manipulations of magnetic order.



[73]. [*Matrix-product-state GPLHR for non-Hermitian many-body eigenproblems*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15387 "Matrix-product-state GPLHR for non-Hermitian many-body eigenproblems")

Abstract: Computing multiple eigenstates of non-Hermitian many-body operators plays an important role in investigating excitation spectra, spectral properties, and relaxation dynamics in interacting and open quantum systems. Here, we formulate the generalized preconditioned locally harmonic residual (GPLHR) method in a matrix product state (MPS) framework to efficiently compute multiple targeted right eigenstates whose eigenvalues lie near a specified shift in the complex plane. The results demonstrate that GPLHR combined with MPS provides an efficient and accurate approach for computing multiple eigenstates of non-Hermitian many-body operators.



[74]. [*Extended-range percolation on lattices with cubic and non-compact neighborhoods in three dimensions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15441 "Extended-range percolation on lattices with cubic and non-compact neighborhoods in three dimensions")

Linear fitting leads to $zp_c\sim2.5987(1)$ for large $z$, predicting a value of continuum percolation threshold of aligned cubes $\eta_c=0.324838(12)$ using $zp_c=2^{d}\eta_c$. We then examine site percolation for non-compact neighborhoods extending to the fifth coordination zone on SC, FCC, and BCC lattices. Through a comparison of the three indices of $z$, $\xi=\sum_iz_ir_i^2/i$, and $\zeta=\sum_iz_ir_i$, we find that $\zeta$ is effective in removing the degeneracy. The number $K$ indicates lattice connectivity.



[75]. [*Non-Hermitian Two-Kink Control of Corner States in Twisted Chern Bilayers*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15470 "Non-Hermitian Two-Kink Control of Corner States in Twisted Chern Bilayers")

Abstract: Corner states are bound by boundary-mass defects, but this geometric anchoring restricts their spatial and internal control. The mass-phase kink creates the parent corner pole and fixes its energy and intrinsic decay, while directed interlayer tunneling encodes its layer polarization. The imaginary-momentum texture instead reshapes the closed-boundary envelope and redistributes stationary right-state weight between corners. A twisted Qi--Wu--Zhang bilayer verifies this division on arbitrary, including concave, boundaries. The two kinks therefore cooperate or compete while keeping spectral ancestry, spatial weight, and layer character distinct, thereby establishing a unified framework for controlling mass-defect corner states across disparate boundary geometries and mass textures.



[76]. [*A Solvable Model in the Half-filled Landau Level*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15471 "A Solvable Model in the Half-filled Landau Level")

arXiv:2610.15471v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: A solvable microscopic model of interacting electrons in a half-filled Landau level is presented. The Hamiltonian is a well-defined limit of the electron-electron interaction. The low energy sector consists of a set of independent, spatially interlaced, one-dimensional systems of non-interacting fermionic dipoles, each system with a Fermi sea ground state. The total number of dipoles in the set of Fermi seas forming the two-dimensional ground state equals the number of electrons in the system and the density of states at the Fermi level is that of a two-dimensional Fermi gas. The model is obtained by generalizing, or rather adopting, the existing exact solution on a thin, but not too thin, torus to the two-dimensional bulk case.



[77]. [*Generalized Constraint-Controlled Rheological Model: Two Constitutive Pathways under Sinusoidal Periodic Shear Deformation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15538 "Generalized Constraint-Controlled Rheological Model: Two Constitutive Pathways under Sinusoidal Periodic Shear Deformation")

Abstract: We formulate the Generalized Constraint-Controlled Rheological Model (GCCRM) for entangled linear and long-arm-branched polymer melts under sinusoidal periodic shear deformation (SPSD), spanning the linear and strongly nonlinear regimes. This state feeds two time-domain constitutive pathways (a configurational stress and a dissipative internal-variable stress) which are recombined before Fourier analysis. For branched polymers, the architecture is described by an entire backbone of molecular weight $M_{bb}$ and $N_a$ long arms of molecular weight $M_a$, with a compliance-weighted representative-constitutive-cell partition. The linear limit is normalized so that the configurational moduli sum to the entanglement modulus, while dynamic tube dilation and stretch drive distinct contributions to dissipative activation. Under reduced closures, GCCRM generates a smooth nonlinear elastic response with an onset of nonlinearity, an approximately logarithmic growth regime, an architecture-dependent maximum, and distinct high-deformation asymptotes. Fourier harmonics, ELR, VLR, and Lissajous curves are treated as post-processing observables of the total stress. The framework establishes a consistent constitutive architecture whose quantitative validation requires one parameter set per material across SPSD and, prospectively, transient shear histories.

arXiv:2610.15546v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The local Hilbert space dimension is a fundamental parameter in quantum many-body systems, but its role in controlling the ergodicity-breaking transition remains less explored. We systematically study this question by introducing toy models of qudits in zero dimensions that exhibit an ergodicity-breaking transition. Using the hybridization condition, we analytically obtain the dependence of the critical value of the interactions on the local Hilbert space dimension. Numerical studies of spectral correlation measures show agreement with the theoretical predictions, hence confirming the existence of the ergodicity-breaking transition in these models. We further introduce a distinct higher spin version of the model and numerically obtain the same critical behavior, demonstrating that the observed critical point is independent of the microscopic structure of interactions considered.



[79]. [*Unlocking the potential of polymer networks for high strength through topology*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15552 "Unlocking the potential of polymer networks for high strength through topology")

Zheqi Chen, Yu Zhou, Chenghai Li, Ho Cheung Shum, Zhigang Suo

arXiv:2610.15552v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: A covalent polymer network is orders of magnitude weaker than individual covalent bonds. This large gap reveals an unrealized potential. The weakness of a network comes not from individual covalent bonds, but from how they are connected. Here we unlock this potential by identifying a topological criterion, involving optimally sparse crosslinks, long chains, and dense entanglements. Optimizing each topological variable alone is insufficient for high strength, yet exceptional strength emerges when they are combined within a narrow regime. We describe the physics of this synergy, and use the criterion to strengthen networks widely used but known to be weak. For example, we prepare a polyacrylamide hydrogel of 90 wt% water, amplifying strength from a commonly reported value of ~0.1 MPa to ~6.5 MPa. The exceptional strength is achieved without sacrificing other properties, such as softness, toughness, and elasticity. The criterion establishes topology as a general design principle for strong polymer networks.



[80]. [*Dynamical chiral symmetry breaking in rotating QED$_3$: critical flavor number and the occupation-reweighting channel*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15568 "Dynamical chiral symmetry breaking in rotating QED$_3$: critical flavor number and the occupation-reweighting channel")

At finite temperature, rotation suppresses the condensate through a dominant occupation-reweighting channel, $\hat{\kappa}_{\rm occ}=-1.55$, accounting for $\ge 99.995\%$ of the linearized total response at the reference parameters, with self-consistent feedback amplifying it by $26.5$. [81]. The experimental spectra were satisfactorily reproduced, demonstrating the potential applications of our method to HX-PES spectra of many compounds.



[82]. [*Single-Particle Spectroscopy and Cross-Barrier Dynamics in Inhomogeneous Luttinger Liquids via Unified Field Bosonization Technique*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15608 "Single-Particle Spectroscopy and Cross-Barrier Dynamics in Inhomogeneous Luttinger Liquids via Unified Field Bosonization Technique")

arXiv:2610.15608v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The Unified Field Bosonization Technique (UFBT) is employed to investigate the single-particle properties of inhomogeneous Luttinger liquids containing localized clusters of impurities in the presence of forward-scattering interactions. Within this framework, fermionic two-point correlation functions are used to derive the leading-order singular contributions to the dynamical density of states (DDOS) and the cross-barrier tunneling conductance. The results reveal distinct power-law behaviours of the DDOS near the impurity cluster and in the far-field regime. The tunneling conductance exhibits a power-law temperature dependence, decreasing with decreasing temperature for repulsive interactions and increasing for attractive interactions, while remaining temperature-independent in the noninteracting limit. The impurity cluster modifies the amplitudes and spatial structure of these observables, whereas their characteristic power-law exponents are determined solely by the interaction parameters. The formulation accommodates arbitrary impurity strengths without requiring perturbative expansions in the impurity or interaction strengths. The characteristic scaling behaviour emerges directly from the fermionic correlation functions, without an explicit renormalization-group (RG) treatment of the impurity strength, while the weak-interaction limit recovers the leading perturbative correction to the tunneling response. These findings establish UFBT as a unified framework for describing local spectral properties and cross-barrier transport in inhomogeneous Luttinger liquids.



[83]. [*Enhancement of local magnetic ordering temperature due to non-magnetic disorder in anti-Heusler alloy Al$_2$MnCu*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15622 "Enhancement of local magnetic ordering temperature due to non-magnetic disorder in anti-Heusler alloy Al$_2$MnCu")

Soumya Bhowmik, Suvayan Saha, Saikat Debnath, Arka Patra, Biswarup Satpati, Maxim Avdeev, Shishir Kumar Pandey, Chandan Mazumdar

arXiv:2610.15622v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Structural disorder is generally regarded as detrimental to magnetism. Since magnetic properties are governed by both the arrangement of magnetic atoms and the crystal environment, including non-magnetic atoms that mediate exchange interactions, the distinct role of magnetic and non-magnetic disorder remains less understood. The recently reported anti-Heusler alloy, Al$_2$MnCu, forming with $\sim$50\% intra-symmetry site disorder (B2) involving the magnetic Mn atom and $\sim$5\% inter-symmetry site disorder associated with non-magnetic Al and Cu atoms, offers a good platform to disentangle the individual roles of magnetic and non-magnetic atomic disorder. Our magnetic critical analysis, in the vicinity of the Curie temperature ($T_{\rm C}$), does not lead to any conventional single universality class expected for a standard ferromagnet; instead, the non-monotonic temperature dependence of effective critical exponents points towards more complex magnetic interactions, akin to inhomogeneous ferromagnets. Theoretical analysis establishes that the enhancement of the local magnetic ordering temperature arises directly from the strengthening of exchange interactions due to the small degree of non-magnetic disorder, whereas $T_{\rm C}$ is hardly influenced by the much larger level of magnetic-atom disorder. The combined experimental and theoretical investigations on the anti-Heusler alloy Al$_2$MnCu reveal and explain the unusual observation of nonmagnetic disorder-induced elevation of $T_{\rm C}$, providing a paradigm for the counterintuitive role of disorder in magnetic materials.



[84]. [*Hidden Spin-Valley Hall Effect and Gate-Switchable Electric Hall Effect in 2D Altermagnets*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15630 "Hidden Spin-Valley Hall Effect and Gate-Switchable Electric Hall Effect in 2D Altermagnets")

arXiv:2610.15630v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Hidden beneath the vanishing charge Hall response of altermagnets are spin-up and spin-down Hall currents of opposite sign---concealed, not absent---whose cancellation is enforced by symmetry. When these spin states are locked to distinct valleys, the hidden currents constitute a spin-valley Hall effect. Here we show that in 2D altermagnets with spin-valley-layer coupling (SVLC), a gate field unveils this hidden spin-valley Hall response.~By lifting the symmetry-enforced compensation between valleys with opposite layer polarizations, the field converts the hidden response into a net charge Hall signal, yielding a gate-switchable electric Hall effect (EHE) enabled by SVLC. Using collinear spin layer group (cSLG) theory, we classify all 158 altermagnetic cSLGs, identify 32 that support SVLC, and predict monolayer Mg$_2$Os$_2$(SO$_5$)$_2$ and the Ca(CoN)$_2$-family as candidate materials. First-principles calculations for Mg$_2$Os$_2$(SO$_5$)$_2$ reveal reversible valley splitting, a gate-switchable Hall response, and a Kerr rotation exceeding 1 mrad whose sign reverses together with the EHE signal. These results establish a symmetry-based route to all-electrical valley-spin control and identify altermagnets as promising platforms for high-performance valley transport and electrically switchable magneto-optics.



[85]. [*Indirect prediction and screening of frustrated magnetic materials using machine learning*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15683 "Indirect prediction and screening of frustrated magnetic materials using machine learning")

Regina Burganova, Irina Piyanzina, Ruslan Akhmerov, Alexander Sigov, Maxim Fedorov

arXiv:2610.15683v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Magnetic frustration is commonly quantified by the frustration index, calculated from the Curie-Weiss and Neel temperatures. Because both quantities are not simultaneously available for most materials, direct prediction of the magnetic frustration index remains challenging. Here, we propose a machine learning workflow for indirect prediction of the frustration index and consequent screening of potential frustrated magnetic compounds through independent prediction of Curie-Weiss and Neel temperatures. In our work, composition-based models implemented via the CatBoost algorithm, trained on experimental data were optimized using chemically informed dataset subdivision. The highest predictive accuracy was achieved for the rare earth-oxide model in predicting Neel and for the rare earth model in predicting Curie-Weiss. Ensemble uncertainty was incorporated through a conservative frustration index to improve the reliability of candidate selection. Screening of approximately 67000 Materials Project compounds identified 20 high-confidence frustrated magnetic candidates, including several experimentally known frustrated magnets and previously unexplored oxide systems potential for further investigation.



[86]. [*Field Response of the Orbital Moment Probed by Integrated Magnetic Circular Dichroism*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15684 "Field Response of the Orbital Moment Probed by Integrated Magnetic Circular Dichroism")

Qian-Sheng Shi, Ziqiang Wang, Jian Kang, Yi Zhang

arXiv:2610.15684v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Magnetic circular dichroism (MCD) is an optical probe of orbital time reversal symmetry breaking. Although frequency integrated MCD is related to the self-rotation contribution to orbital magnetization at zero field, its magnetic response generally differs from the thermodynamic orbital susceptibility. Here, we define the MCD susceptibility and derive general expressions for multiband systems using an electromagnetic $U(1)$ gauge invariant approach. For insulators, the response contains contributions from the non-Abelian quantum metric, Berry curvature, and field induced interband mixing. For metals, a finite temperature Green function formulation separates the smooth response into Fermi sea and Fermi surface contributions.
