An independent Landau level calculation confirms the expressions for the zero-field MCD moment and its linear magnetic response. In a continuum model for twisted bilayer graphene, the MCD susceptibility vanishes within the charge neutrality gap, while the thermodynamic orbital susceptibility remains markedly diamagnetic. Near the gap edges, the orbital moment term dominates the response. Features near the van Hove energies are associated with density of states enhancement. These results clarify how the two susceptibilities reflect different orbital processes even for the same electronic structure and thus establish the MCD susceptibility as a distinct probe of orbital magnetic response.



[87]. [*Developing the Statistical Mechanics of Evolving Assembly Spaces*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15695 "Developing the Statistical Mechanics of Evolving Assembly Spaces")

Anisha K. Yeddanapudi, Abhishek Sharma, Ian Seet, Leroy Cronin

arXiv:2610.15695v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: A gap exists in connecting biological evolution within the framework of statistical mechanics. It has been hoped that statistical mechanics would provide us with a general predictive principle for the emergence of novelty and complexity. However, the emergence of novel functional features initiated by stochastic processes remains difficult to describe, because these processes commonly arise outside near-equilibrium or linear-response regimes. To understand how environmental influences generate diverse, open-ended, complex objects by driving physical systems to specific regions of phase space over long spatial and temporal scales, a new approach to non-equilibrium thermodynamics is necessary. Here, we present the coupling of assembly theory (AT) with statistical mechanics as a framework that works within the laws of thermodynamics, but can contextualise the processes of novelty, complexity, and evolution within these laws. We map construction processes from physical space to assembly space (AS) and introduce the concept of an assembler, a machine that constructs objects contingent on previous states and is represented by bitwise operations. Using this approach, we apply principles from stochastic thermodynamics with AT to define selection within large combinatorial spaces1 and estimate the work required to create novel and complex objects. This approach allows us to represent complex processes, such as selection, within large combinatorial spaces as a set of rules for a memory-feedback loop followed by the assembler. This framework concretely defines the relationships between thermodynamics and information theory by introducing a novel aspect of physics that uses history and causal contingency to influence the probabilistic time evolution of complex systems.



[88]. [*Reversible intrinsic-extrinsic crossover of the anomalous Hall effect in PrAlSi via Al/Si ratio tuning*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15726 "Reversible intrinsic-extrinsic crossover of the anomalous Hall effect in PrAlSi via Al/Si ratio tuning")

Huiyang Yang, Xiaohua Luo, Hongwei You, Wei Ren, Yuntao Gao, Changcai Chen, Zhishuo Zhang, Chunsheng Fang, Shengcan Ma

arXiv:2610.15726v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The anomalous Hall effect (AHE) in magnetic Weyl semimetals arises from distinct microscopic origins-intrinsic Berry curvature and extrinsic scattering mechanisms-yet reversible switching between these regimes within a single material platform remains elusive. Here, we demonstrate that off-stoichiometric tuning of the Al/Si ratio (from ~1.05 to ~0.75) reversibly drives an intrinsic-extrinsic-intrinsic crossover of the AHE in PrAlSi single crystals. Across four compositions spanning a narrow Si variation (PrAl0.93Si0.89 to PrAl0.79Si1.06), the anomalous Hall conductivity (AHC) exhibits a non monotonic arch (170-1413- 507-164 {\Omega}-1cm-1). The intermediate composition PrAl0.90Si0.94 (Al/Si ~0.96) exhibits high carrier mobility, multi carrier transport, yielding a giant AHC of 1413 {\Omega}-1cm-1 dominated by extrinsic skew-scattering. The Si-poor and Si-rich compositions exhibit lower AHC values and intrinsic Berry-curvature-dominated AHE, establishing a composition-driven intrinsic-extrinsic-intrinsic crossover. Deviating from the intermediate composition suppresses mobility and reverses carrier type from hole-like to electron-like. The Al/Si ratio thus serves as a chemical knob that primarily modulates the Fermi level via compositional band filling, while off-stoichiometry-induced chemical disorder plays a subordinate role in determining the AHE mechanism. These results position off-stoichiometric PrAlSi as a tunable platform where subtle compositional variation reversibly switches the AHE mechanism.



[89]. [*Coexistence of Multiple Distinct Corner-State Classes via Nearest-Neighbor Couplings Alone*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15727 "Coexistence of Multiple Distinct Corner-State Classes via Nearest-Neighbor Couplings Alone")

Luhong Su, Dongyi Wang, Shenglong Guo, Xinjiang Chen, Lei Zhou, Shaojie Ma, Mengyao Li

arXiv:2610.15727v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The coexistence of multiple classes of localized states, including different types of topological corner states, is highly desired; yet its realization typically requires sufficiently strong long-range couplings, bringing challenges to experimental realizations across a wide range of systems. Here, we demonstrate the emergence of multiple localized corner modes evolution under purely nearest-neighbor coupling conditions without long-range couplings, not even next-nearest-neighbour couplings, and experimentally confirm their coexistence in a single lattice. Our platform simultaneously hosts type-I and type-II corner states, corner localized bound states in the continuum, flat-band states, and edge states. We experimentally realize such lattice using topolectrical circuits, a system that can strictly exclude long-range coupling by design, enabling direct characterizations of both the energy-band spectrum and the spatial profiles of the localized eigenmode. This work demonstrates that solely modifying nearest-neighbor couplings can replace the role of long-range interactions in realizing multiple types of corner states, providing a deterministic and widely accessible route to multiple corner-state classes and rich localized states, with immediate potential for mode-division multiplexing and multimode topological devices.



[90]. [*Spatial interference changes the symmetry class of a Josephson element*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15746 "Spatial interference changes the symmetry class of a Josephson element")

Takayuki Kubo

arXiv:2610.15746v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Spatial interference can break the inversion symmetry of an effective Josephson potential even in a laterally homogeneous junction with inversion-symmetric local energy. We derive a general criterion based on harmonic-dependent complex spatial form factors: a single local harmonic remains a shifted cosine, whereas multiple harmonics can acquire phases incompatible with any common inversion center, permitting cubic interactions and three-wave mixing. Phase reflection reverses all odd-order expansion coefficients about corresponding minima while preserving the even-order ones. For a capacitively shunted junction at fixed charging energy and arbitrary offset charge, it also preserves the complete quantum spectrum, enabling reversal of the odd nonlinear sector without spectral retuning. A centered cross junction with multiple local harmonics is one concrete example. Within the imposed-field model, a three-component magnetic field can generate an inversion-asymmetric effective potential, and reversing an odd number of field components exactly reflects it without changing the quantum spectrum of the capacitively shunted junction.



[91]. [*Variational Minimization of Almost Strong Zero Modes in Parafermionic Wires*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15748 "Variational Minimization of Almost Strong Zero Modes in Parafermionic Wires")

Niklas Tausendpfund, Matteo Rizzi

arXiv:2610.15748v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Strongly interacting fermionic chains obeying a $Z_n$ symmetry can host topological phases with highly degenerate ground-state manifolds. The different ground states are connected by strongly bound fractionalized edge modes called parafermionic zero modes. While exactly preserved under time evolution at zero temperature, interactions lead to a destabilization of these modes at finite energy densities, and, given a generic Hamiltonian, obtaining the lifetime of these modes is often impossible as it requires knowledge of the full spectrum of the Hamiltonian or long time evolutions. Here, we propose a novel quantum-inspired algorithm that can be used to variationally search for these almost strong zero modes in generic parafermionic chain models. We demonstrate analytically and numerically that our ansatz faithfully reproduces the exact results and finds strongly bound parafermionic operators in the non-integrable Kitaev-Hubbard chain and chiral Potts model. Our results further allow us to verify the existence of a true strong zero mode in the symmetrical limit of the chiral Potts model for $Z_3$-parafermions.



[92]. [*Approaching Irrational Quantization*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15753 "Approaching Irrational Quantization")

Zhihong Wu, Zhimou Chen, Siyuan Zhang, Jinyang Chen, L. N. Pfeiffer, K. W. West, Yang Liu, Xi Lin

arXiv:2610.15753v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The integer and fractional quantum Hall effects reveal that quantized physical responses can take integer and fractional values, respectively. This naturally raises the question of whether quantization can extend beyond rational numbers to an irrational value. Here we show that such a quantized response can emerge from the limiting behavior of a finite, experimentally realizable geometric network. We develop a method based on infinite geometric structures, in which a sequence of finite networks rapidly converges toward an infinite limit. Following this approach, we identify a planar network whose normalized resistance converges to the irrational value $\sqrt{3}$, and realize a finite approximation with high-precision geometric control. The measured normalized resistance is $1.735 \pm 0.001\, h/e^2$ for a fully occupied Landau level in the designed pattern. Our experimental results extend the concept of quantization beyond rational values and demonstrate that an irrational quantized response can be approached through a finite physical system.



[93]. [*Magpie: A Graphical Magnon Software*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15790 "Magpie: A Graphical Magnon Software")

Tobias Weber

arXiv:2610.15790v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Magpie is a fully graphical linear spin-wave software which has its main focus on 3-d visualisation of magnetic models and magnon dispersion relations. The software is distributed under an open-source license and is written in high-performance, portable C++. Its modular architecture allows for a rapid development of new calculation modules and permits the inclusion into external applications. Included code generators ensure seamless interoperability with other linear spin-wave libraries.



[94]. [*Ionomer-free Iridium Anodes with Low Loading for Zero-gap Electrolyzers via Direct Atomic Layer Deposition*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15798 "Ionomer-free Iridium Anodes with Low Loading for Zero-gap Electrolyzers via Direct Atomic Layer Deposition")

Peter M. Piechulla, Iris Blommestein, Anne van Gerven, Henri Pelzer, Ezgi Demir\"oz, Jonas Englhard, Mingliang Chen, Julien Bachmann, J. Ruud van Ommen

arXiv:2610.15798v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Proton exchange membrane water electrolysis (PEMWE) is among the most promising techniques to provide hydrogen as both energy carrier and feedstock for the chemical industry, due to its compatibility with intermittent renewable electricity sources. However, a major bottleneck in scaling up PEMWE is the limited supply of the anode catalyst Ir. This work shows a novel approach to tailoring ionomer-free catalyst layers based on custom-made microporous substrates and two different atomic layer deposition (ALD) processes, both using Ir(EtCp)(CHD) precursor. Thermal ALD using O3 as counter-reactant yields mostly metallic Ir/IrOx and an electrode Ir loading of 0.21 mg/cm2. The PEMWE cell-level performance is comparable to a commercial catalyst-coated membrane anode despite 7x lower loading, and is maintained over an accelerated durability test equivalent to 600 h of intermittent operation. Moreover, using a low-loading ALD-made cathode, the total platinum group metal loading is only 0.24 mg/cm2. Plasma-enhanced ALD reduces the Ir loading to 0.04 mg/cm2 by tailoring the catalyst distribution, however, yields lower-activity IrO2. Finally, direct ALD catalyst deposition needs no ionomer-bearing ink. The anodes are therefore compatible with various membrane materials, as demonstrated in a CO2 electrolysis experiment with an anion exchange membrane.



[95]. [*Latent Heat and Raman Spectrum Change on Phase Transition of Water Including Calcium Chloride*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15821 "Latent Heat and Raman Spectrum Change on Phase Transition of Water Including Calcium Chloride")

Dongha Kim, Changyoun Lee

arXiv:2610.15821v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: This study investigates how Raman O-H stretching signatures may be able to track latent-heat-related energetics during water's phase transitions. Prior work on latent heat and Raman analysis of phase change is reviewed to establish theoretical approach. Building on a framework that links O-H frequency shifts to hydrogen-bond strength, we relate entropy/enthalpy variations to temperature-dependent changes in peak position and integrated intensity. Experiments on pure water and impurity-doped samples, specifically CaCl$_2$, were performed under controlled temperatures, and spectra were deconvoluted and integrated over defined bands. The Raman metrics exhibit reproducible temperature dependence that captures the transition window, while CaCl$_2$ induces systematic shifts and measurable modifications in the apparent latent-heat signature. These results highlight Raman spectroscopy as a quantitative probe of thermodynamic evolution and impurity effects in water and related systems.



[96]. [*Emergence of Active Foams in a Minimal Cognitive Flocking Model*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15835 "Emergence of Active Foams in a Minimal Cognitive Flocking Model")

Aryan Dwivedi, Diganta Bhaskar, Anil Kumar Dasanna

arXiv:2610.15835v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigate a minimal visual perception-driven model of self-propelled particles that interact through purely position-based cohesive steering within a finite vision cone, in the absence of velocity alignment and physical volume exclusion. Building on a previously proposed minimal cognitive model, we extend the interaction range from the previously studied short-range and distance-independent limits to an intermediate regime, enabling a systematic investigation of how a finite vision range influences collective behavior. We find that this regime exhibits a rich state diagram that combines characteristics of both limiting cases while giving rise to qualitatively new collective states. Besides the gas, nematic-band, and polar file states, we uncover the emergence of \emph{active foams} -- system-spanning fractal-like networks sustained by dynamically evolving particle streams. These structures arise solely from non-reciprocal vision-based interactions and are absent in both the short and long vision-range limits. For larger vision angles, the system self-organizes into a variety of aggregate morphologies, including condensates, rings, and multi-twist filaments, revealing a strong interplay between interaction range and anisotropic perception in shaping collective organization.



[97]. [*Eigenstate transitions and critical transport in 3D Aubry-Andr\'e model*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15842 "Eigenstate transitions and critical transport in 3D Aubry-Andr\'e model")

Pragna Das, Lev Vidmar, Miroslav Hopjan

arXiv:2610.15842v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We numerically study spectral and wave-function properties, as well as time evolution, in finite-size commensurate approximations of the 3D Aubry-Andr\'e model. We explicitly show, through scaling analysis, the existence of two critical points. One of the critical points is a localization-delocalization transition in real-space, similar to the critical point in the 3D Anderson model. The other is a localization-delocalization transition in a dual momentum space, which does not have any analog in the 3D Anderson model. We estimate their values from spectral measures, such as the gap ratio and the spectral form factor, as well as from wave-function measures, such as the inverse participation ratio and the participation entropy. Additionally, we demonstrate that both critical points exhibit hallmarks of critical points between quantum ergodicity and localization. First, the eigenstates and the eigenspectrum at the critical point fulfill the mutual universal relation between the eigenspectrum compressibility $\chi$ and the eigenstate fractal dimension $d^{(1)}$ [S. Jiricek et al., Proc. Nat. Acad. Sci. 123, e2518027123 (2026)]. Second, the dynamics of the wave packet at the critical point is governed by the critical dynamical exponent $z$, characterized by the spectral fractal dimension and the dimensionality of the lattice [M. Hopjan and L. Vidmar, Phys. Rev. Lett. 135, 060401 (2025)]. Thus, similarly to the 3D Anderson model, the critical dynamical exponent is sub-diffusive with the value of $z=3$.



[98]. [*Epitaxial Oxide Thin Films with High Entropy and Anomalous Crystallinity*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15861 "Epitaxial Oxide Thin Films with High Entropy and Anomalous Crystallinity")

Joseph Petruska, Christina M. Rost, Jon-Paul Maria, Saeed S. I. Almishal

arXiv:2610.15861v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Pulsed laser deposition (PLD) expands the compositional and structural possibilities of high-entropy oxide (HEO) thin films through far-from-equilibrium growth and kinetic arrest of metastable configurations. This review connects growth kinetics and epitaxial constraints to the roles of individual cations in shaping the resulting films. We focus primarily on rock salt MgCoNiCuZnO and its derivatives, with selected fluorite, spinel, and perovskite examples illustrating broader applicability. In MgCoNiCuZnO, Co valence and Cu coordination illustrate how chemistry and growth conditions jointly enable lattice tuning and coherent nanostructure formation, while aliovalent and size-misfit additions expand the accessible defect and compositional landscapes. These coupled degrees of freedom underpin anomalous crystallinity, the coexistence of extensive chemical disorder with exceptional crystalline quality and epitaxial order, including full coherence in selected systems, particularly rock salts grown on MgO. This combination opens opportunities to discover and engineer functional configurations in high-quality single-layer films and heterostructures.



[99]. [*Quantum Localization in Geometric Complex Networks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15874 "Quantum Localization in Geometric Complex Networks")

Jan Lewenstein-Sanpera, Tobias Grass, M. \`Angeles Serrano, Bruno Juli\'a-D\'iaz

arXiv:2610.15874v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Quantum localization, the phenomenon by which wave diffusion is halted, here understood as the superposition of propagating waves, can have many different origins, such as onsite random potentials in Anderson localization. However, some systems exhibit complex geometries and could potentially reproduce similar results without external potentials. In this work, we study how localization of single particle states is affected by the underlying topology of a simple but highly predictive complex network model: the geometric soft configuration model $\mathbb S^1$. By tuning the model's temperature and heterogeneity parameters, we are able to generate different structural patterns and focus on the transition from a small-world to a non-small-world regime. Our study suggests that small-world networks tend to have extended states, which spread the whole system's size, in contrast to non-small-world networks which tend to localize, showing how complex connectivity can play the role assigned to random energetic disorder.



[100]. [*Probing the wave-vector gap in nanoscale magnonics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15945 "Probing the wave-vector gap in nanoscale magnonics")

Ond\v{r}ej Wojewoda, Samer Kurdi, Toeno van der Sar, Daniel Schick, Sebastian Wintz, Alexei Maznev

arXiv:2610.15945v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Short-wavelength spin waves are central to miniaturizing magnonic devices and to studying exchange-dominated magnetization dynamics. Yet the wave-vector range between roughly 30 and 300 radians per micrometre has remained difficult to access experimentally, posing challenges for both excitation and detection. This Perspective reviews recent advances in emerging measurement techniques and discusses prospects for their future development. Optical and microwave techniques are restricted to the vicinity of the Brillouin-zone centre. Inelastic neutron, electron and resonant hard x-ray scattering cover the full Brillouin zone but lack the energy resolution needed for low-frequency magnons at intermediate wave vectors. This Perspective examines five emerging approaches that are beginning to close this gap: Mie-enhanced Brillouin light scattering, colour-centre magnetometry, magnon momentum microscopy, extreme-ultraviolet transient-grating spectroscopy, and time-resolved scanning transmission x-ray microscopy. For each, we discuss the physical principle, demonstrated capabilities, current limitations and prospects for development. Compared side by side, the techniques give complementary access to magnon frequencies, local stray fields, real-space dynamics and momentum-space distributions, and they differ in their sensitivity to coherent and incoherent excitations. We therefore discuss the main advantages and use cases of each technique and consider the practical requirements for addressing different scientific questions. We highlight opportunities for further development, including higher sensitivity, improved spatial and temporal resolution, and broader frequency and wave-vector coverage. Together, these advances could enable, for example, systematic studies of nonlinear magnon interactions at nanoscale wavelengths and investigations of emerging magnetic materials such as altermagnets.



[101]. [*Spreading the Damage: How Heterogeneity Delocalizes Fracture in Polymer Networks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15962 "Spreading the Damage: How Heterogeneity Delocalizes Fracture in Polymer Networks")

Saleh Assadi, Zachary White, Radhika Joshi, Franck J. Vernerey

arXiv:2610.15962v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Fracture in polymer networks is strongly influenced by how network architecture and topology organize damage in space. Although these materials can sustain large deformations under homogeneous loading, they often fail in a brittle and flaw-sensitive manner once defects are present. Here we show that network heterogeneity is one key architectural parameter controlling this transition. Using a discrete mesoscale network model that explicitly resolves strand-level prestretch, force transmission, and irreversible bond scission, we demonstrate that heterogeneity in prestretch and strand lengths broadens crack-induced deformation, delocalizes bond rupture, and enlarges the fracture process zone. In polydisperse networks, increasing contour-length disorder broadens the prestretch distribution and systematically increases both toughness and the intrinsic length scale governing flaw sensitivity. Bimodal networks amplify this mechanism further through combined contrasts in prestretch and strand lengths, producing the largest damage zones and the highest toughness among the architectures studied. Inspired by the results, we derive a scaling estimate relating the emergent fracture-active length to the spatial range of crack-induced deformation and the logarithmic spread of strands' remaining stretch to rupture.



[102]. [*Molecular-Level Evaluation of the Thermodynamic Properties of Charged Solutions from Submolar to Molar Ion Concentrations*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15985 "Molecular-Level Evaluation of the Thermodynamic Properties of Charged Solutions from Submolar to Molar Ion Concentrations")

Sahin Buyukdagli

arXiv:2610.15985v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The accurate estimation of the ionic activity coefficients is paramount for the characterization of the electrochemical reactions driving energy storage processes as well as the biological mechanisms involving ATP hydrolysis. The numerical evaluation of the ionic activity coefficients by computational methods is rendered difficult by the infinite dilution limit involved in the integral identity between the Helmholtz free energy and the equation of state of the electrolyte. By combining a recently introduced self-consistent Debye-Huckel formalism and its analytically tractable low density limit, we develop a computationally efficient approach enabling the accurate calculation of the free energy and the related thermodynamic functions of aqueous electrolytes over a broad range of ionic concentration and electrostatic coupling strength. Via comparison with numerical simulations and experimental data, it is shown that our approach can predict the salt dependence of the Helmholtz free energy, the entropy, and the ionic activity coefficients from the electrostatically driven submolar ion concentrations to the hard-core dominated molar density regime.



[103]. [*Extreme anharmonicity determines the superionic transition in the prototypical antifluorite Li$_2$O*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15986 "Extreme anharmonicity determines the superionic transition in the prototypical antifluorite Li$_2$O")

Lorenzo Bastonero, Chiara Cignarella, Nicola Marzari, Lorenzo Monacelli

arXiv:2610.15986v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Superionic conductors are promising electrolyte candidates for all-solid-state batteries, thanks to their liquid-like ionic mobility. In the prototypical lithium-ion oxide superionic conductor with antifluorite structure, the high-temperature transition to the fast-ion regime has long been attributed to volume-driven harmonic phonon softening. However, this quasi-harmonic picture neglects the strong lattice anharmonicity inherent to the fast-ion regime, leaving the microscopic origin of the transition unresolved. Here, we apply non-perturbative lattice dynamics combined with a first-principles-accurate machine-learning interatomic potential to study the phase transition in Li$_2$O. We demonstrate that anharmonicity, rather than volume expansion alone as commonly assumed, plays the decisive role in determining the transition temperature and the underlying mechanism. Finally, we analyze the spectral properties using the time-dependent self-consistent harmonic approximation, revealing the breakdown of the quasi-particle picture in this material. The spectral function and the infrared response provide signtures of the phase transition that can be investigated experimentally using inelastic neutron or X-ray scattering and infrared spectroscopy. All these results establish the extreme anharmonicity of Li$_2$O near the superionic regime and provide a lattice-dynamic framework for understanding and engineering the fast-ion transition in this type of materials.



[104]. [*An empirical model for deriving information on ion dynamics from $\mu$SR data via the conventional Kubo-Toyabe function analysis*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16006 "An empirical model for deriving information on ion dynamics from $\mu$SR data via the conventional Kubo-Toyabe function analysis")

Ryosuke Kadono

arXiv:2610.16006v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We present an empirical model that analytically relates the parameters of the extended Kubo-Toyabe (KT) function $G_z^{\rm EA}(t;\Delta,\nu_{\rm i},Q)$, which describes muon spin relaxation ($\mu$SR) induced by jump diffusion of ions using the Edwards-Anderson parameter $Q$, to those of the conventional KT function $G_z^{\rm KT}(t;\Delta_{\rm KT},\nu_{\rm KT})$ that serves as an approximation of $G_z^{\rm EA}(t)$. The proposed formulae successfully reproduce the anomalous temperature dependences of the linewidth $\Delta_{\rm KT}(T)$ and the fluctuation rate $\nu_{\rm KT}(T)$ which are artifacts observed upon analyzing $\mu$SR data on ion diffusion using $G_z^{\rm KT}(t)$. Applying this model to $\Delta_{\rm KT}$ and $\nu_{\rm KT}$ published in the literature of battery materials yields activation energy $E_{\rm i}$ and pre-exponential factor $\nu_{\rm i0}$ in the Arrhenius equation for ion diffusion, $\nu_{\rm i}(T)=\nu_{\rm i0} e^{-E_{\rm i}/kT}$, that are in excellent agreement with those deduced using other techniques, transforming peculiar artifacts into a powerful diagnostic tool.



[105]. [*Deformation-induced spectral softening drives phase separation on non-spherical membranes*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16037 "Deformation-induced spectral softening drives phase separation on non-spherical membranes")

Avraham Moriel, Howard A. Stone

arXiv:2610.16037v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Models of curvature-driven membrane sorting couple composition directly to membrane bending elasticity. Here we show that shape deformations can destabilize a homogeneous state without this composition-dependent mechanical coupling. We analyze the eigenspectrum of the Laplace-Beltrami operator on deformed spheres analytically, and verify it numerically. As the deformation amplitude increases, the spectral degeneracy breaks, and the lowest nonzero eigenvalue decreases substantially. Using Cahn-Hilliard dynamics, we show that this eigenvalue softening provides a purely geometric route to phase separation.



[106]. [*Nonrelativistic Optically-Induced Spin Torque in Noncollinear Magnets*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16052 "Nonrelativistic Optically-Induced Spin Torque in Noncollinear Magnets")

Zhiyuan Li, Jian Zhou

arXiv:2610.16052v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Manipulating spin using contactless and non-invasive optical approaches is one of the forefront topic in the modern spintronic science and technology. Prior studies on optically-induced spin reorientation usually scales with spin-orbit coupling strength, setting a limitation on composing heavy elements. In this work, we extend such a limit into nonrelativistic regime by exploring noncollinear magnetic configuration. Using a general spin group-theoretical investigation, we show how the symmetric operators in both spin and lattice spaces control the spin torque and quantum geometry of Bloch wavefunction--including both quantum metric and Berry curvature. We describe their site-resolved spin symmetries and unravel their selection rules. Furthermore, we adopt a Kagom\'e lattice model and a realistic material family to showcase the local spin torque under light irradiation. Since the nonrelativistic spin splitting usually spans on the order of electron-volt, significantly larger than typical spin-orbit induced splitting, the nonrelativistic light-induced spin torque is concomitantly much stronger. This work extends the previous opto-spintronics from relativistic to nonrelativistic limit, significantly broadening its realization platform.



[107]. [*Electronic Structure of a Delafossite Oxide CuAlO$_2$ in Comparison with CuCrO$_2$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16061 "Electronic Structure of a Delafossite Oxide CuAlO$_2$ in Comparison with CuCrO$_2$")

Kenta Takahashi, Ryo Kato, Mario Okawa, Tetsuji Okuda, Akira Yasui, Eiji Ikenaga, Kanta Ono, Noriaki Hamada, Tomohiko Saitoh

arXiv:2610.16061v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigated the electronic structure of a delafossite oxide CuAlO$_2$ in comparison with CuCrO$_2$, using vacuum ultraviolet (VUV) and hard x-ray (HX) photoemission spectroscopy (PES) and band structure calculations within the local-density approximation +$U$ scheme. Cu 3$p\! -\! 3d$ resonant PES and Cu $2p$ core-level HX-PES give concrete evidence that the Cu valence in CuAlO$_2$ is monovalent, which makes a sharp contrast to CuCrO$_2$ that showed contradictory results depending upon probing methods [Yokobori \textit{et al.}, Phys. Rev. B \textbf{87}, 195124 (2013)]. We further noticed weak but detectable satellite structures due to Cu$^{2+}$ state in the Cu $2p$ HX-PES spectrum of CuCrO$_2$, surprisingly, without an observable main peak due to the Cu$^{2+}$ state. Comparisons between the valence-band HX-PES spectra and the band structure calculations revealed a significant amount of the Cu $4s$ and Al $3s$/Cr $4s$ states in the valence band. The above results can reasonably be explained by the Cu $3d, 4s-$Cr $3d$ charge transfer model that we previously proposed in the above paper, thereby demonstrating the critical role of empty $3d$ orbitals in the electronic structure of the copper delafossite oxides.



[108]. [*High-mobility spin transport mediated by indirect excitons in a van der Waals heterostructure*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16089 "High-mobility spin transport mediated by indirect excitons in a van der Waals heterostructure")

Zhiwen Zhou, W. J. Brunner, Luc M. Nguyen, C. A. Lozano, G. Mercado, L. H. Fowler-Gerace, L. V. Butov

arXiv:2610.16089v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Efficient spin transport with high spin mobility is of both fundamental and practical significance for spintronics. Spin can be transferred by moving particles. However, particle scattering limits spin transfer and prevents high spin mobilities. In this work, we study the kinetics of spin transport mediated by a quantum system of indirect excitons (IXs) in a van der Waals heterostructure composed of atomically thin transition-metal dichalcogenides (TMDs). This spin transfer mechanism allows for the realization of efficient spin transport characterized by a high spin mobility reaching 10$^5$ cm$^2$/(eV$\cdot$s), which is orders of magnitude higher than that achieved via standard diffusive spin transport in TMD materials. The suppression of IX scattering enables high-mobility spin transport.



[109]. [*Vibrational Fingerprints of Competing Structural and Polar Phases in CuInP2S6*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16091 "Vibrational Fingerprints of Competing Structural and Polar Phases in CuInP2S6")

Pegah Mohammadi, Sobhit Singh

arXiv:2610.16091v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Layered van der Waals CuInP2S6 crystals exhibit a rich sequence of temperature- and pressure-driven phase transitions, giving rise to multiple structural phases with distinct physical properties. Despite extensive experimental and theoretical efforts, unambiguous identification of these phases remains challenging because of their structural similarities and complex vibrational responses. In this work, we employ first-principles density functional theory calculations to systematically investigate the vibrational properties of the reference paraelectric C2/c and P-31c phases and their corresponding dynamically stable ferroelectric Cc and P31c phases, as well as the antiferroelectric P-1 and P-3 phases. From calculated phonon spectra and symmetry analysis, we determine the Raman- and infrared-active modes of each candidate structure and identify characteristic vibrational fingerprints that enable their phase determination through vibrational spectroscopy. Furthermore, using Porto notation, we establish polarization- and crystal-orientation-dependent Raman selection rules for different backscattering geometries and identify the modes that are expected to emerge or vanish under specific experimental configurations. Thus, our results provide a comprehensive theoretical framework for interpreting Raman and infrared measurements of CuInP2S6 and for reliably identifying its competing structural, ferroelectric, and antiferroelectric phases across temperature- and pressure-driven phase transitions.



[110]. [*Few-Magnon Universality beyond Contact Interactions in Long-Range Spin Chains*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16126 "Few-Magnon Universality beyond Contact Interactions in Long-Range Spin Chains")

Yiran Huo, Pengfei Zhang

arXiv:2610.16126v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Quantum few-body systems exhibit a rich variety of phenomena that provide fundamental insights into universality. A paradigmatic example is the Efimov effect, in which an infinite tower of three-body bound states emerges with binding energies forming a geometric sequence. Motivated by advances in trapped-ion systems, recent theoretical work has predicted that the Efimov effect can arise in certain long-range quantum spin chains, where magnons exhibit a general dispersion $\epsilon_k=u|k|^z$ with $z\in(0.52,0.88)$ and interact through a contact potential. Nevertheless, whether Efimov universality persists in the presence of experimentally relevant long-range interactions between magnons remains unexplored. In this work, we address this issue by investigating the two- and three-magnon problems with a model interaction potential $V(x)=V_0/(1+|x|^\beta)$. Our results demonstrate that few-body universality persists for any $\beta>1$: In the two-magnon sector, the low-energy expansion of the scattering matrix near a two-body resonance agrees with the universal form predicted by contact-interaction theory. The three-magnon bound-state spectrum exhibits Efimov scaling, with a scaling factor consistent with the theory. We further highlight the experimental relevance of our model, which can be readily realized in trapped-ion systems. Our results pave the way for quantum simulations of universal few-body physics beyond the traditional paradigm of nonrelativistic particles.



[111]. [*Length-scale dependence of collective time reparameterization in swap dynamics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16168 "Length-scale dependence of collective time reparameterization in swap dynamics")

Massimo Pica Ciamarra

arXiv:2610.16168v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Swap Monte Carlo dramatically accelerates the equilibration of deeply supercooled liquids while preserving the equilibrium Boltzmann distribution. Recent work has argued that swap and other accelerated algorithms act by reparameterizing time: different dynamics follow the same slow relaxation process, but with different clocks. Here we test this idea in a continuously polydisperse glass former. We confirm that mean collective overlaps satisfy an approximate time reparameterization, in agreement with earlier results. However, the associated clock depends systematically on the collective probe length and on the relaxation stage. Thus the observed collective collapse does not define a unique, observable-independent material time. Over the numerically accessible $\alpha$-relaxation regime, swap changes not only the rate of relaxation, but also its length-scale dependence. The resulting collective reparameterization is therefore only approximate and does not establish an exact dynamical equivalence between swap and reference relaxation.



[112]. [*Chirally pinned flat bands, helical minibands, and bias-controlled topology of Jackiw-Rebbi states in a periodically modulated Dirac mass texture*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16183 "Chirally pinned flat bands, helical minibands, and bias-controlled topology of Jackiw-Rebbi states in a periodically modulated Dirac mass texture")

Tao Zhou

arXiv:2610.16183v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Flat bands amplify interactions into correlated phases, and topological bound states are robust by principle; combining the two in a model that is both exactly analyzable and experimentally accessible is therefore especially desirable. Here we show that a square-lattice model with a slowly modulated Dirac mass texture, $m(x)=m+m_0\cos(2\pi x/L)$ with $L$ up to hundreds of sites, provides such a setting, without engineered geometries, lattice-scale textures, or long-range hoppings. Exact nesting reduces the problem, independently at each transverse momentum $k_y$, to a $1+1$ dimensional Dirac Hamiltonian with a position-dependent mass: every simple zero of $m(x)$ binds Jackiw-Rebbi zero modes, and the two walls of a period form an effective Su-Schrieffer-Heeger ring. The model interpolates between a chirally pinned flat band at large $L$, a helical miniband whose nodes are enforced by a boundary phase of the ring, and a bias-dimerized regime in which the sign of the bias sets a winding number. A central result is that the miniband bandwidth is not a single scale: a max-min measurement conflates three widths of different origin, the helical height $W_{\rm JR}$ exponential in the period, the zone-edge upturn $W_{\rm edge}\propto L^{-2/3}$ dominating at large $L$, and a bias bump exponential in $L$, so the flat band is robust against bias in the thermodynamic limit. We analyze two lattice realizations, a two-orbital model with modulated inter-orbital hybridization and a single-orbital bond-order texture sharing the nodal physics while leaving the van Hove points gapless, and propose experiments on cold-atom superlattices, Kekule-ordered graphene, STM designer lattices, and microwave arrays.



[113]. [*Self-Avoidance Governs Re-entrant Coil-Globule Transition of Inertial Active Brownian Ring Polymers*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16188 "Self-Avoidance Governs Re-entrant Coil-Globule Transition of Inertial Active Brownian Ring Polymers")

Rakesh Palariya, Arindam Panda, Roland G. Winkler, Sunil P Singh

arXiv:2610.16188v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Active matter systems composed of self-propelled entities exhibit a wide range of out-of-equilibrium phase behaviors and collective dynamics. Assemblies of active particles, e.g., in a linear structure, are particularly interesting, as they exhibit an intriguing interplay between activity and geometrical restrictions due to self-avoidance and entanglements. Using Langevin dynamics simulations of an active Brownian ring polymer with translational and rotational inertia, we uncover an inertia-driven re-entrant coil-globule transition in the absence of any attractive interactions. We show that rotational inertia plays a paramount role, leading, in the globular state, to spontaneous, nonzero, radially outward polarization of the active forces. Self-avoidance and entanglements break the rotational symmetry of active beads and effectively stabilize the globular states. The transition becomes increasingly sharp with increasing rotational inertia, yielding highly compact globular conformations at intermediate activities, whereas at high activity the polymer re-expands into a coil state. Scaling of the radius of gyration reveals crossovers from self-avoiding, phantom-like to compact-globular structures. Further, rotational inertia enhances transport properties, with an intermediate activity-induced Rouse-type bead diffusion and a long-time diffusion coefficient displaying a power-law dependence on the moment of inertia. Our study establishes a purely inertia-induced mechanism for controlling the collapse of active polymers, arising from spontaneous active-force polarization and self-avoidance.



[114]. [*Field-driven phase transition crossover in kagome antiferromagnet Mn$_{3-x}$Co$_x$Sn*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16189 "Field-driven phase transition crossover in kagome antiferromagnet Mn$_{3-x}$Co$_x$Sn")

Shubhankar Paul, Chanchal Sow

arXiv:2610.16189v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Mn$_3$Sn exhibits a complex magnetic phase diagram with multiple symmetry-breaking phenomena. Minute electron doping at the Mn site breaks the kagome lattice symmetry in Mn$_3$Sn. In this work, we exploited this to tune the order of the magnetic phase transition using magnetocaloric effect in Mn$_{3-x}$Co$_x$Sn single crystals. Pristine Mn$_3$Sn shows metallic behaviour with a spin-reorientation transition at 278 K, which shifts to 175 K upon Co doping. Additionally, Co doping also induces a weak-ferromagnetic transition near $\approx 210$ K. The effect of Co doping results inverse to conventional magnetocaloric effect, with enhanced magnetic entropy change. Further, the scaled entropy change reveals a field-driven magnetic phase transition crossover from first order to second order in Mn$_{2.75}$Co$_{0.25}$Sn crystal. Based on experimental data, a new phase diagram is constructed, depicting the field evolution of various magnetic orders.



[115]. [*Orbital cancellation and reentrant localization in a quasiperiodic trimer ladder*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16197 "Orbital cancellation and reentrant localization in a quasiperiodic trimer ladder")

Xin Wang, Pei-Jie Chang, Quanfeng Lu, Muxi Zheng, Dong Ruan, Gui-Lu long

arXiv:2610.16197v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Uniform orbital detuning can restore spatial extent even as quasiperiodic modulation strengthens. In a trimer ladder with fixed hoppings and orbital modulation signs, increasing detuning and modulation amplitude together drives successive avoided crossings that redistribute orbital weights, suppressing the projected onsite potential in both lower molecular bands while leaving the third strongly modulated. Complete-band participation scaling supports two reentrant high-participation windows, with wave-packet propagation corroborating spatial recovery at representative controls over the studied sizes and times. These diagonal cancellations leave residual interband modulation, which generates a self-energy whose magnitude depends on the molecular level separation. Gaussian quadrature improves convergence of closed-walk determinant estimates used to cross-check energy-resolved attenuation. Comparing the coupled and decoupled ladders under the same finite-size participation criterion shows that restoring the full off-diagonal modulation narrows both sampled windows, with a larger absolute contraction of the second.



[116]. [*Defining Thixotropy*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16237 "Defining Thixotropy")

Yogesh M. Joshi, Ronald G. Larson

arXiv:2610.16237v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Originally coined 100 years ago to represent a shear-reversible sol-gel transition, the range of the term "thixotropy" has gradually expanded to encompass time-dependent changes in viscosity. Since this risks confusing "thixotropy" with ordinary nonlinear viscoelastic behavior, in this work we seek simple, historically faithful, quantifiable, and experimentally testable definitions of "thixotropy" that avoid this confusion. By analysing both historical tradition and published experimental data, we arrive at three complementary definitions. Definition 0 provides an idealized, purely inelastic reference useful primarily for recognizing thixotropy in a constitutive equation. Definitions 1 and 2, which are more practical, require, respectively, unusually large strains to reach steady state, and unbounded growth of the material relaxation time during rest. Definition 1 can apply in either shear-rate or stress-controlled modes, with the latter encompassing the so-called "viscosity bifurcation." These criteria are tested across diverse materials that we identify as thixotropic based on their responses clearly exceeding their viscoelastic reference scales. The framework is deliberately phenomenological and does not require specific microscopic mechanisms, allowing diverse materials to be described within a common rheological language, as is typical for rheological terminology. We believe that these definitions provide a practical framework for assigning thixotropy a more definite place in rheological taxonomy.



[117]. [*Wilson-loop formulations of the spin polarization and the spin Chern number*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16246 "Wilson-loop formulations of the spin polarization and the spin Chern number")

Kiminori Hattori, Ryuki Uchida

arXiv:2610.16246v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In this paper, we derive the spin polarization and the spin Chern number in momentum and real spaces in terms of the Wilson loop in each spin sector. For these derivations, the spin matrix in the subspace of occupied or unoccupied states is generalized by the unitary rotation to prevent the spectral gap from closing. In the presence of a finite spin gap, the spin eigenvectors recompose the eigenstates of the Hamiltonian in two spin sectors. A dimensional reduction leads to the charge polarization in each spin sector in terms of the Wilson loop along the effective Brillouin zone (BZ) in one dimension (1D). A linear combination of the charge polarizations defines the spin polarization, which naturally quantifies the spin Chern number in momentum space. It is also shown that the spin Chern number is directly derived from the local Wilson loops on a discrete 2D BZ. The theoretical treatment is further generalized for real space in a supercell framework, which can deal with ordered and disordered systems in a unified manner. In this framework, the spin Chern number in real space is formulated in terms of the Wilson loop around the BZ boundary. These formulations are used to numerically evaluate topological phases in the Bernevig-Hughes-Zhang model. Numerical results show an equivalence of the spin Chern number and the ${\mathbb{Z}_2}$ index.



[118]. [*Symmetry Condition and Mechanism for the Anomalous Hall Effect in Odd-Parity Magnets*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16247 "Symmetry Condition and Mechanism for the Anomalous Hall Effect in Odd-Parity Magnets")

Xun-Jiang Luo, Su-Qi Zhang, Yi-Dong Liu, Yong-Ting Shi, Ding-Fu Shao, Mingliang Tian

arXiv:2610.16247v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The anomalous Hall effect (AHE) in unconventional magnets has attracted considerable attention. A giant AHE has recently been observed in the candidate $p$-wave magnet Gd$_3$(Ru$_{1-\delta}$Rh$_{\delta}$)$_4$Al$_{12}$ [Nature 646, 837(2025)], while its theoretical interpretation invokes a spontaneous magnetization to break time-reversal symmetry ($\mathcal{T}$), although the magnetic order itself already breaks $\mathcal{T}$. Consequently, the microscopic origin of the AHE in odd-parity magnets (OPMs) remains an open question. Here we establish the symmetry condition and microscopic mechanism for the AHE in OPMs. Since the OPM order is classified by the spin group whereas the AHE is governed by the magnetic group, the AHE is allowed only when the magnetic point group is ferromagnetic; conversely, it is forbidden whenever the effective time reversal $\mathcal{T}\tau$ (with $\tau$ a fractional translation), which is essential to the realization of the OPM, is preserved. Microscopically, OPMs generically host a hidden Zeeman field (HZF) rooted in this intrinsic $\mathcal{T}$ breaking, and its interplay with spin-orbit coupling (SOC) generates the AHE without any external field. We demonstrate this picture with models on the square, honeycomb, and kagome lattices. In particular, a square-lattice helimagnet with sublattice-resolved SOC maps exactly onto an anisotropic Rashba--Zeeman model, whose Zeeman term is nothing but the HZF and directly yields the AHE. Our results establish the symmetry condition for the AHE in OPMs and identify the HZF--SOC interplay as a general route to realize it.



[119]. [*Pressure Evolution of the Superconducting Order Parameter of Kagome Superconductor CsV3Sb5*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16253 "Pressure Evolution of the Superconducting Order Parameter of Kagome Superconductor CsV3Sb5")

Haiyan Zuo, Wangju Yang, Zhifan Wu, Yuqing Zhao, Qiuxia Chen, Weiming Lao, Yangpeng Qi, Hechang Lei, Haizi Cong, Cong Ren

arXiv:2610.16253v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Kagome superconductors AV3Sb5 (A = K, Rb, Cs) offer an outstanding platform for exploring the interplay among charge-density-wave (CDW) order, superconductivity (SC), and nontrivial topological electronic states. For CsV3Sb5, moderate hydrostatic pressure induces a double-dome superconducting phase diagram while monotonically suppressing the CDW phase, which signals an unconventional coupling between SC and CDW. Nevertheless, the microscopic nature of the pressure-modulated superconducting state and its relationship with CDW order remain poorly understood. Here we employ soft directional point-contact spectroscopy (SDPCS), combined with superconducting resistive measurements, to probe the superconducting order parameter of CsV3Sb5 under hydrostatic compression. Our anisotropic PCAR measurements resolve two distinct s-wave superconducting gaps along the c-axis and a strongly anisotropic gap within the ab plane. Two-band Blonder-Tinkham-Klapwijk (BTK) fits to the c-axis spectra yield a large gap satisfying 2Delta1/kBTc > 3.52 and a small gap with 2Deltas/kBTc < 3.52. The spectral-weight-averaged coupling ratio 2Deltaavg/kBTc is enhanced in the pressure window between P1 and P2, suggesting that pressure-induced modifications of the low-energy electronic structure may contribute to the evolution of superconductivity. For ab-plane junctions, the superconducting gap anisotropy is gradually suppressed above P1, accompanied by the emergence of a pronounced peak-dip-hump structure suggestive of enhanced coupling between superconducting quasiparticles and a bosonic excitation. These results shed new light on the intricate entanglement between CDW and SC in kagome superconductivity.



[120]. [*Universal Boolean Computation in Negatively Coupled Circle Map Lattices*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16290 "Universal Boolean Computation in Negatively Coupled Circle Map Lattices")

Priyanka D. Bhoyar, Prashant M. Gade

arXiv:2610.16290v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigate the computational capabilities of a one-dimensional lattice of negatively
  coupled circle maps in its traveling wave regime. We interpret propagating waves as information carriers and exploit their interactions to implement Boolean logic. The logical states are encoded by the propagation direction of the traveling waves, while the presence or absence of a wave at a designated output location represents the logical output. Using controlled wave collisions, we demonstrate the realization of NAND and NOR gates along with other basic gates. Since NAND and NOR operations are functionally complete, the results establish that the traveling wave regime of the negatively coupled circle-map lattice supports universal Boolean computation in principle. The demonstrated logical operations remain valid over a finite range of the coupling strength \epsilon and map parameter \omega, indicating robustness of the computational scheme to parameter variations. Our results therefore demonstrate that computational functionality can emerge from the collective nonlinear dynamics of a coupled map lattice, providing a con- nection between the dynamical properties of traveling wave states and collision based information processing.



[121]. [*Dynamical-state switching controls active microrod navigation in porous media*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16331 "Dynamical-state switching controls active microrod navigation in porous media")

Ana Cazorla, Maria L. Jimenez, Raul A. Rica-Alarcon, Carla Fernandez-Rico, Laura Alvarez

arXiv:2610.16331v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Adaptive search and travel strategies enable biological swimmers to explore complex environments, but remain difficult to encode in minimal synthetic microswimmers. Here, we unveil multiple dynamical and orientational states of rod-like Janus microswimmers actuated via electric fields: planar active, oriented chiral and persistent rods. We discover a hidden dynamical state after pre-orienting them, which allows us to switch on demand from strong chiral to persistent motion. We harness this phenomenological orientation and chirality switch for exploration in microstructured porous arrays. Here, strong chirality promotes local scanning but drives circular trapping and poor net displacement, whereas the pre-aligned dynamical state enables efficient obstacle sorting, and trap escape. Comparing experiments with simulations of active Brownian particles in the very same experimentally-extracted obstacle geometries, we quantify navigation efficiency via first-passage time statistics, revealing how chirality tuning optimizes the trade-off between local exploration and long-range transport.



[122]. [*Derjaguin-corrected Kelvin equation for finite open slits*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16367 "Derjaguin-corrected Kelvin equation for finite open slits")

Alexandr Malijevsk\'y

arXiv:2610.16367v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We formulate a Derjaguin-corrected Kelvin equation for phase transitions in finite open slits. In such pores, capillary condensation is modified by the finite wall length because the condensed phase is bounded by two terminal menisci pinned at the slit ends. Starting from the macroscopic generalized Kelvin equation, we introduce a scaling approximation which replaces the implicit edge-contact-angle condition by a closed analytic expression while preserving the exact limiting behaviours. We then incorporate mesoscopic wetting-layer contributions into the finite-slit free-energy balance. The single-wall contribution naturally separates into a planar Derjaguin term and a residual finite-length correction controlled by the competition between the wall length $H$ and the parallel correlation length $\xi_\parallel$. The resulting equation reduces to the standard Derjaguin-Kelvin equation for infinitely long slits and to the macroscopic finite-slit Kelvin equation when adsorbed layers are absent. Explicit forms are derived for long-ranged dispersion forces and for short-ranged forces. The theory is tested using classical density functional theory for finite slits with attractive Lennard-Jones walls and hard walls, corresponding respectively to capillary condensation and its drying analogue, capillary evaporation. The Derjaguin-corrected theory substantially improves on the purely macroscopic prediction, capturing both the correct infinite-slit limit and the observed dependence on wall length. For the short-ranged hard-wall system, the agreement with DFT remains nearly quantitative even for very narrow slits.



[123]. [*Angle-Resolved Photoemission as a Sensitive Probe of Surface Quality: Sputtered versus Annealed Cu(001)*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16421 "Angle-Resolved Photoemission as a Sensitive Probe of Surface Quality: Sputtered versus Annealed Cu(001)")

Hyeonhee Na, Jeonghwan Oh, Jinju Lee, Hanjun Sim, Myeongun Go, Jimin Sohn, Jeonggu Kang, Byungmin Sohn

arXiv:2610.16421v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES) requires a well-ordered surface, whose quality is routinely judged from low-energy electron diffraction (LEED) and x-ray photoelectron spectroscopy (XPS). We compare LEED, XPS, and ARPES of a Cu(001) single crystal measured in situ at four stages of surface preparation: as-prepared, after aging for 24 h at 6 K, after Ar$^+$ sputtering, and after annealing. The sputtered and annealed surfaces show nearly identical $(1\times1)$ LEED patterns and Cu 2$p$ core levels, but the sputtered surface yields a momentum-independent ARPES spectrum, and the band dispersion recovers after annealing. We attribute this contrast to how each probe responds to sputtering-induced defects: LEED reflects the average lattice, which remains periodic, whereas ARPES reflects the electronic states, whose crystal momentum is no longer well defined. Our results indicate that a sharp LEED pattern and clean core-level spectra do not guarantee a surface suitable for ARPES.



[124]. [*Thermal softening of the yield stress in glasses with different stability*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16424 "Thermal softening of the yield stress in glasses with different stability")

Tianze Wan, Ludovic Berthier

arXiv:2610.16424v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The yielding of amorphous solids has been extensively studied in the athermal limit, but the influence of thermal fluctuations is much less understood, in particular for glasses of different stability. Using molecular dynamics simulations of a model glass where the swap Monte Carlo algorithm can be employed, we study the yield stress at finite temperature and shear rate, both in steady flow and at shear start-up, over a broad range of glass stability. Thermal fluctuations soften both the dynamic yield stress in steady state and the start-up yield stress. In all cases, the temperature dependence is compatible with the $T^{2/3}$ law expected for thermally activated yielding near a saddle-node instability, with no systematic dependence of the exponent on stability or shear rate. Extrapolations to zero temperature recover the athermal yield stresses. The amplitude of the softening at start-up, by contrast, depends strongly on stability: highly stable glasses are much less sensitive to temperature than poorly annealed ones. A description of yielding at finite temperature must therefore account explicitly for the stability of the glass.



[125]. [*Decoupling Trap States in Amorphous Oxide Semiconductor Transistor Using a Conductive-Semiconducting-Conductive MOS Structure*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16438 "Decoupling Trap States in Amorphous Oxide Semiconductor Transistor Using a Conductive-Semiconducting-Conductive MOS Structure")

Kai Chen, Wei Zhang, Xuanyao Fong, Shengman Li, Yunlong Li

arXiv:2610.16438v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We propose a novel amorphous oxide semiconductor (AOS)-based conductive-semiconducting-conductive (CSC) MOS test structure to effectively sense, discriminate, and analyze distinct trap-induced conductance losses in AOS transistors. In conventional test structures, non-ideal metal-AOS contacts convolute the conductance loss generated by various trap states. The CSC architecture mitigates these contact-related artifacts, enabling the decoupling of interface states and shallow states. Furthermore, the contributions from leakage, series resistance, and device-geometry effects are identified and calibrated. Our results yield bias-resolved trap profiles and reveal three distinct regimes: (1) In the depletion region, interface states dominate the conductance loss. These states exhibit sub-microsecond time constants and their density increases slightly with deeper depletion, which is associated with donor-like states. (2) From weak to moderate accumulation, shallow states become the primary contributor, displaying a Gaussian-like volumetric density-of-states distribution with response times spanning from microseconds to nanoseconds. (3) In the strong accumulation regime, interface states regain dominance with an increased state quantity, while their time constants remain invariant. These bias-resolved profiles clarify how different trap populations impact AOS transistors under distinct operating conditions, providing intuitive metrics for understanding reliability degradation. Consequently, the CSC structure offers a simple, scalable, and robust platform for trap characterization in AOS transistors.



[126]. [*Thermally Enhanced Pseudo-Goldstone Spin Gap in the Isotropic Helimagnet ZnCr$_2$Se$_4$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16439 "Thermally Enhanced Pseudo-Goldstone Spin Gap in the Isotropic Helimagnet ZnCr$_2$Se$_4$")

N. D. Andriushin, A. Sanders, J. Reuther, D. C. Peets, P. Steffens, A. Hiess, J. Lass, L. Prodan, V. Tsurkan, V. Felea, D. S. Inosov

arXiv:2610.16439v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In magnetically ordered systems, the spin gap in the magnon spectrum is normally expected to decrease on warming and close at the transition out of the long-range order. The presence of a pseudo-Goldstone gap is commonly associated with nonlinear terms in spin-wave theory. However, its precise origin and detailed properties can vary and remain under debate. Here we show by means of inelastic neutron scattering that the pseudo-Goldstone gap in the Heisenberg helimagnet ZnCr$_2$Se$_4$ exhibits an atypical, nonmonotonic temperature dependence: the gap increases over much of the ordered phase before closing near $T_\mathrm{N}$. These results align with recent spin-dynamic simulations based on an effective quartic potential in an isotropic system~[Phys. Rev. B \textbf{113}, L220411 (2026)]. As the pseudo-Goldstone spin gap in ZnCr$_2$Se$_4$ is dominated by quantum effects at subkelvin temperatures, its characteristic growth on warming is caused by thermal fluctuations.



[127]. [*Photon echo from Maki-Thompson interference in a fluctuating s-wave superconductor*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16462 "Photon echo from Maki-Thompson interference in a fluctuating s-wave superconductor")

Hui Zhou, Yuan Wan

arXiv:2610.16462v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We study theoretically the nonlinear optical response of a fluctuating s-wave superconductor driven by two successive terahertz pulses. We show that the response exhibits a photon echo signal that arises from the quantum interference between the time-reversed electron and hole trajectories in the Maki-Thompson process. By contrast, the transport of the fluctuating Cooper pairs responsible for the Aslamazov-Larkin conductivity does not generate an echo. The photon echo signal can be readily measured using terahertz two-dimensional coherent spectroscopy, highlighting its potential to distinguish different fluctuation conductivity mechanisms.



[128]. [*Modelling the dynamics of the martensitic transformation in Ni$_2$MnGa with machine-learning force-fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16467 "Modelling the dynamics of the martensitic transformation in Ni$_2$MnGa with machine-learning force-fields")

Markus E. Gruner, Yulia Klunnikova, Mike J. Bruckhoff, Konstatin P. Skokov, Olga N. Miroshkina

arXiv:2610.16467v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The martensitic transformations in stoichiometric Ni$_2$MnGa and related Heusler alloys are governed by a delicate interplay between electronic structure, magnetism, and lattice dynamics. While density functional theory (DFT) has successfully mapped the energy landscape of modulated martensites, it remains computationally prohibitive for capturing finite-temperature dynamics across relevant length and time scales. Here, we bridge this gap by constructing DFT-trained machine-learning force-fields (ML-FF) interpolated from ab initio molecular dynamics. We demonstrate that ML-FF trained on DFT data can reproduce key features of Ni$_2$MnGa, including the soft TA$_2$ phonon and a preference for nanotwinned martensites. Using classical molecular dynamics simulations, we explore the impact of adaptive nanotwinning on the austenite-martensite transition, which facilitates the dynamics of both, forward and backward transformation. We demonstrate, that the negative twin boundary energy along the entire transformations path predicted by DFT for $T=0$, supports the formation of ordered modulated martensites at finite temperatures. Our results pave the way for large-scale simulations of magnetic shape memory alloys with near-DFT accuracy.



[129]. [*Deterministic positioning accuracy of B centers in hBN by electron irradiation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16468 "Deterministic positioning accuracy of B centers in hBN by electron irradiation")

A. N\'u\~nez Marcos, C. Arnold, J. Barjon, S. Buil, J. -P. Hermier, A. Delteil

arXiv:2610.16468v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Hexagonal boron nitride (hBN) is a promising material platform for quantum technologies, enabling both the realization of nanophotonic devices and the hosting of point defects that act as high-quality single-photon emitters (SPEs). Among these defects, B centers, which reproducibly emit at 436 nm, can be generated individually at predefined positions using electron beam irradiation, a crucial capability for top-down quantum photonics. Yet, the spatial accuracy of emitter creation strongly conditions the potential applications. Here, we generate arrays of individual B centers in a scanning electron microscope (SEM) with in-situ cathodoluminescence (CL) monitoring. We characterize the sources of random deviations from the target positions to identify the limitations to their deterministic positioning. In particular, we measure the distance between the electron beam and the emitter at the moment the emitter is created based on CL mapping. This approach provides a direct estimation of the spatial extent of the beam-emitter interaction. We find this contribution to be the main constraint to the emitter fabrication accuracy, yielding a standard deviation of 27 nm. Additional contributions, primarily from the finite density of UV-emitting carbon dimer precursors, increase the overall positional standard deviation to 40 nm. Our results highlight the possible improvements to the B center creation technique to reach sub-10 nm accuracy.



[130]. [*Molecule-intercalated van der Waals Josephson junctions uncover complex multiband superconductivity*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16483 "Molecule-intercalated van der Waals Josephson junctions uncover complex multiband superconductivity")

Daniel Margineda, David Caldevilla-Asenjo, Covadonga \'Alvarez-Garc\'ia, Daniel Tezze, Raul Arenal, Maxim Ilyn, Celia Rogero, Luis E. Hueso, Marco Gobbi

arXiv:2610.16483v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Intercalation of molecular species provides a route to tailor the electronic structure in van der Waals superconductors, yet intercalated compounds have rarely been integrated into phase-sensitive quantum devices. Here, we realize hybrid molecular Josephson junctions based on TaS2/NbSe2 heterostructures. Selective intercalation of the TaS2 electrode induces monolayer-like Ising superconductivity, while the junctions exhibit asymmetric Fraunhofer interference, field-induced and spontaneous Josephson diode effects. The zero-field diode disappears upon reversing the electrode stacking and re-emerges after intercalating the lower NbSe2 electrode. These findings indicate that the hybrid superconducting electrode hosts unconventional superconductivity underlying the nonreciprocal transport, while molecular intercalation acts as a tuning knob for its internal phase structure. This behavior is consistent with a time-reversal-symmetry-breaking multiband superconducting state characterized by a nontrivial relative phase between superconducting condensates. Our results establish hybrid molecular Josephson junctions as a quantum-device platform for phase-sensitive investigation and integration of molecularly engineered superconductors, opening opportunities to uncover and engineer unconventional superconducting phases.



[131]. [*Emergence of Nonlocal Hydrodynamics in Many-Body Radiative Heat Transport*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16488 "Emergence of Nonlocal Hydrodynamics in Many-Body Radiative Heat Transport")

Mauricio Gomez Viloria, Florian Herz, Riccardo Messina, Philippe Ben-Abdallah

arXiv:2610.16488v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We develop a nonlocal hydrodynamic theory of radiative heat transport in many-body systems by coarse graining their microscopic electrodynamic interactions. Long-range radiative exchange naturally generates a spatially nonlocal constitutive relation in which the heat current at a given position depends on temperature gradients over an extended region, while energy conservation retains its local conservative form. Fourier transport emerges as a controlled long-wavelength limit when the interaction kernel has a finite second spatial moment. When this moment fails to converge, spatial nonlocality survives coarse graining and gives rise to non-Fourier transport. The resulting framework establishes a systematic bridge between discrete many-body fluctuational electrodynamics and a continuous description of radiative heat transport.



[132]. [*Scalable Quantum Monte Carlo for Shannon-R\'enyi Entropy: An Experiment-visitable Probe of Quantum Phases and Criticality*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16494 "Scalable Quantum Monte Carlo for Shannon-R\'enyi Entropy: An Experiment-visitable Probe of Quantum Phases and Criticality")

Dong-Xu Liu, Zhe Wang, Zenan Liu Yi-Ming Ding, Zheng Yan

arXiv:2610.16494v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The Shannon-R\'enyi (SR) entropy characterizes the probability distribution of many-body wave functions in a chosen basis and is directly accessible from single-shot measurement snapshots in cold-atom experiments, yet its theoretical evaluation in interacting systems carries exponential cost. Here we construct a field-theoretic spacetime manifold for the SR entropy in which $q$ replicas are glued at the imaginary-time boundaries, recasting it as a ratio of generalized partition functions amenable to large-scale quantum Monte Carlo simulation. On this basis we develop a reweight-annealing framework with two complementary strategies: a virtual-parameter scheme interpolating between unglued and fully glued limits via binomial-weighted partial gluing, and a real-parameter scheme using a physical coupling constant as the annealing variable within stochastic series expansion. Both reduce the computational cost from exponential to polynomial. Most importantly, we establish for the first time a finite-size scaling theory for the SR entropy near a quantum critical point, $S_q=a(g)L^2+S_q^0\left((g-g_c)L^{1/\nu}\right)$; data collapse of its first derivative yields the correlation-length exponent $\nu$ for both the O(3) and Ising universality classes, realized in the spin-$\frac{1}{2}$ columnar dimerized Heisenberg model and the bilayer Heisenberg-Ising model. The same simulations extract the Goldstone-mode count $N_G=2$ and the $Z_2$ ground-state degeneracy $\ln 2$ from subleading terms. These results elevate the SR entropy to a standalone, order-parameter-free diagnostic of quantum criticality with the same scaling status as the entanglement entropy, and close the loop between theory and quantum simulation by enabling quantitative benchmarking of experimental snapshots from state-of-the-art quantum simulators.



[133]. [*Superconductivity from altermagnetic fluctuations: the crucial role of sublattice polarization*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16507 "Superconductivity from altermagnetic fluctuations: the crucial role of sublattice polarization")

Mats Barkman, Andreas Kreisel, Brian M. Andersen

arXiv:2610.16507v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Unconventional superconductivity in correlated systems often appears in the vicinity of a magnetically ordered state. This situation is also relevant for altermagnets, characterized by a nonrelativistic spin-split electronic structure in the presence of N\'eel order. Many proposed altermagnetic candidates, however, never cross the magnetic instability. We study superconductivity arising from altermagnetic fluctuations in such systems and show that the sublattice polarization (SLP) of the normal-state bands plays a pivotal role in determining the superconducting state. SLP, an inherent property of altermagnetic band structures that persists even without magnetic order, prevents Cooper pairs from forming between different sublattices. This suppresses pairing mediated by the nearest-neighbor antiferromagnetic exchange, which favors spin-singlet superconductivity, and leaves longer-range same-sublattice bonds dominant. When the next-nearest-neighbor exchange is ferromagnetic, as expected for frustration-free N\'eel order, this results in spin-triplet superconductivity. This mechanism is fundamentally different from that in altermagnets with long-range N\'eel order, where the spin-split Fermi surfaces favor equal-spin pairing. Finally, when longer-range interactions in the disordered phase instead favor singlet pairing, SLP leads to singlet states with characteristic properties distinct from nearest-neighbor pairing states.



[134]. [*Optically driven ultrafast lattice contraction in a topological crystalline insulator*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16513 "Optically driven ultrafast lattice contraction in a topological crystalline insulator")

M. H. Berntsen, W. Chen, D. Phuyal, J. Wu, A. Grubi\v{s}i\'{c}-\v{C}abo, M. Dendzik, Q. Guo, A. Orthaber, C. Li, R. M. Geilhufe, J. Weissenrieder, O. Tjernberg

arXiv:2610.16513v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The ability to dynamically control topological order remains a central challenge in the exploration of quantum materials. Here, we investigate the lattice and electronic response of the topological crystalline insulator (TCI) Pb$_{1-x}$Sn$_x$Se to ultrafast optical excitation using time- and angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (tr-ARPES) in combination with X-ray diffraction (XRD) and first-principles calculations. Static XRD measurements establish the lattice constant as the key control parameter for the topological transition, with a critical value $a_\mathrm{c}=6.073$ \AA separating the normal-insulator and topological phases. Despite generating electronic temperatures far above the topological-to-normal transition temperature $T_\mathrm{c}$, optical excitation in the TCI phase does not induce a phase transition. Instead, it drives an ultrafast lattice contraction of $\Delta a \approx -0.45$ pm within less than 1 ps that counteracts thermal expansion and prevents the system from reaching $a_\mathrm{c}$. The optical excitation therefore pushes the system deeper into the topological phase, providing a direct explanation for the absence of an optically driven transition. Total-energy calculations reveal that this behavior originates from the band inversion in the topological regime, which reverses the character of the photoinduced charge transfer and transiently strengthens atomic bonding. These findings identify band inversion as a mechanism that fundamentally alters the nonequilibrium lattice response, with general implications for ultrafast optical control of topological materials.



[135]. [*Theoretical Investigation of Oxygen-Vacancy Diffusion in SrTiO$_3$ using Neural Network Potentials*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16521 "Theoretical Investigation of Oxygen-Vacancy Diffusion in SrTiO$_3$ using Neural Network Potentials")

Kazutaka Nishiguchi, Ryota Yamamoto, Seiichiro Ten-no

arXiv:2610.16521v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Oxygen-vacancy formation and diffusion play important roles in determining the electronic and atomic transport properties of SrTiO$_3$ (STO). Accurate analysis of such point defects requires sufficiently large-scale and long-time molecular dynamics (MD) simulations; however, direct investigations of oxygen-vacancy dynamics using {\it ab initio} MD (AIMD) based on density functional theory (DFT) are limited to relatively small systems and short time scales because of their high computational cost. In this study, we develop a neural network potential (NNP) for pristine and oxygen-deficient STO using a reference dataset generated from AIMD simulations. The trained NNP model accurately reproduces the DFT reference energies, atomic forces, and virials for both pristine STO and STO containing an oxygen vacancy. The NNP also reproduces the supercell-size dependence of the total energies and oxygen-vacancy formation energies, including the formation energy extrapolated to the dilute limit. Large-scale and long-time MD simulations using the NNP (NNP-MD) are then performed to investigate oxygen-vacancy diffusion. The resulting oxygen-vacancy diffusion coefficients show substantially better agreement with experimental data than those obtained from conventional classical MD simulations. An Arrhenius analysis yields an oxygen-vacancy migration enthalpy of 0.64 eV, in close agreement with the experimental value of 0.67 eV.
