These results demonstrate that NNPs trained on first-principles data provide an accurate and computationally efficient framework for investigating defect formation and long-time vacancy transport in perovskite oxides.



[136]. [*Bogoliubov excitations of a Bose-Einstein condensate in the presence of the non-Hermitian skin effect*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16525 "Bogoliubov excitations of a Bose-Einstein condensate in the presence of the non-Hermitian skin effect")

Carlo Panu, Ulf Peschel, Oleg A. Egorov, Elena A. Ostrovskaya, Eliezer Estrecho

arXiv:2610.16525v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The non-Hermitian skin effect is one of the most remarkable topological features of non-conservative physics. It causes a macroscopic accumulation of states at the boundaries of a confined system, opening new ways for controlling and directing waves in non-Hermitian settings. This effect has recently been observed in experiments with Bose-Einstein condensates of exciton polaritons and ultracold bosonic quantum gases. The underlying physics is captured by a mean-field Hamiltonian with a trapping potential and an imaginary gauge field. While prior work has examined the effect of the imaginary gauge field term on the condensate itself, its effect on the collective excitations on top of the condensate remained unexplored. Here, we develop a new theory for the Bogoliubov excitations in a non-Hermitian framework and systematically study their spectrum across different interaction regimes. By analysing the position expectation values, we find that while the repulsive interactions oppose the localisation of the condensate due to the skin effect, the excitations remain strongly affected by the imaginary gauge field. As a consequence, in the presence of harmonic confinement, we predict a strong shift in the dipole oscillation frequency, which can be observed in experiments. This is in contrast to Hermitian systems, where this frequency is fixed by the trap dimensions. Furthermore, we develop a consistent second-quantised field theory of the collective excitations in this non-Hermitian system. Strikingly, we find that a second-quantised treatment removes the shift of the dipole oscillation frequency entirely. This intriguing discrepancy between the two theoretical approaches to the treatment of elementary excitations in a non-Hermitian system can be resolved in experiments.



[137]. [*Interlayer Registry Controls Polar Topology in Twisted Bilayer h-BN*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16528 "Interlayer Registry Controls Polar Topology in Twisted Bilayer h-BN")

Di Fan, Changming Ke, Shi Liu

arXiv:2610.16528v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Twisting van der Waals bilayers creates a moir'e stacking field with periodically varying local interlayer registry, enabling spatially patterned polarization. However, a systematic understanding of how this registry-controlled polar topology evolves with twist angle, temperature, and mechanical strain is still lacking. Here, using first-principles-trained deep-potential molecular dynamics simulations, we show that spatially varying interlayer registry governs the polarization texture and can be directly tuned by twist and relative layer deformation. As the twist angle decreases, the moir'e polarization texture evolves from compact, nearly circular vortex-like patterns to extended triangular AB/BA domains. This crossover arises from enhanced lattice relaxation at small twist angles, which expands low-energy, oppositely polarized AB/BA regions and confines in-plane polarization mainly to domain boundaries. Despite this reconstruction, the local in-plane winding remains tied to interlayer registry and persists up to 1000 K, indicating robust topology against thermal fluctuations. We further show that the mechanical response is dictated by whether deformation changes the relative registry: homogeneous deformation of both layers produces only weak distortions, whereas layer-selective deformation strongly reconstructs the moir\'e polarization texture. A mismatch-matrix registry-mapping model is developed to capture this response, explaining deformation-induced changes in vortex radii at larger twist angles and triangular-domain geometry at smaller twist angles. These results identify interlayer registry as the central control parameter for forming and tuning moir'e polar topology in twisted bilayer h-BN.



[138]. [*Real-space obstruction control of the shift current in two-dimensional Quantum Spin Hall insulators*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16544 "Real-space obstruction control of the shift current in two-dimensional Quantum Spin Hall insulators")

Michele Bagaglini, Mattia Iannetti, Jonas Erhardt, J\"org Sch\"afer, Ralph Claessen, Giorgio Sangiovanni, Gianni Profeta

arXiv:2610.16544v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The search for materials exhibiting a large bulk photovoltaic effect has recently expanded to low-band-gap systems capable of operating in the infrared regime, with potential applications in optoelectronics. Among the microscopic mechanisms contributing to this phenomenon, the shift current is one of the dominant sources of photocurrent generation. Owing to its geometric origin, the shift current is not only determined by the optical transition probability but also by the real-space displacement associated with the optical excitation, making non-centrosymmetric materials with obstructed electronic wave functions particularly promising candidates. In this work, using a tight-binding model of a quantum spin Hall insulator on a triangular lattice, we investigate the nonlinear photocurrent across trivial, quantum spin Hall, and higher-order topological insulating phases. We show that obstructed topological phases exhibit a substantially enhanced shift-current response, despite the similar electronic band structure. We identify the origin of this enhancement in optical transitions between states belonging to distinct Wannier-center sectors, which allow for a large real-space displacement upon photoexcitation and, depending on the topological phase, control the sign of the shift current. Our results extend the search for controllable bulk photovoltaic effects to materials characterized by real-space electronic obstruction and identify indenene, a monolayer of In on SiC(0001), as a promising candidate for realizing this phenomenon.



[139]. [*Activity-Induced Structural and Dynamical Heterogeneity in Colloidal Gels*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16553 "Activity-Induced Structural and Dynamical Heterogeneity in Colloidal Gels")

Santu Nath, Sudipta Mandal, Smarajit Karmakar

arXiv:2610.16553v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Active gels are intrinsically non-equilibrium materials whose structural and dynamical heterogeneity emerges from the interplay of attractive interactions, thermal fluctuations, and self-propulsion. We investigate a model active gel, simulated using large-scale Brownian dynamics simulations, to explore the effects of temperature $T$, active force strength $f_0$, persistence timescale $\tau_p$, and active particle concentration $\rho_a$. We characterize the structure of the evolving gel network through morphological analysis, while dynamical changes are quantified by the relaxation timescale and dynamical heterogeneity (DH). We show that an optimal choice of activity parameters yields a percolating gel network with faster gelation and larger voids and clusters, enhancing mechanical stability. Conversely, the structural relaxation timescale and DH decrease with increasing activity, indicating enhanced particle rearrangements and reduced spatial correlations of mobile and immobile regions. An effective temperature ($T_\text{eff}$) framework for colloidal gels maps structural quantities and relaxation timescales in an active gel onto those of a passive gel. More interestingly, the growth of DH with increasing swim force can also be described by the same effective-temperature framework, in stark contrast to observations in active glasses. Our findings show that, in colloidal gels, temperature and active force compete, giving rise to similar structural ordering and, consequently, similar DH at the same $T_\text{eff}$. These findings highlight the crucial role of structural ordering in governing DH in active colloidal gels, as compared to active glasses.



[140]. [*Probing quantum nonlinear phononics in multilayer graphene with time-resolved Raman scattering*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16566 "Probing quantum nonlinear phononics in multilayer graphene with time-resolved Raman scattering")

Jakob Dolgner, Michael R\"ubhausen, Dirk Manske

arXiv:2610.16566v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Coherently driven phonons are conventionally described classically. Nonlinear phonon-phonon interactions, however, allow an infrared drive to reshape the fluctuation spectrum and generate substantial nonthermal enhancement of phonon fluctuations, motivating a description beyond the coherent displacement alone. We benchmark classical and semiclassical approximations against the full quantum theory and find that the semiclassical treatment substantially improves agreement with the quantum dynamics. We propose nonequilibrium spontaneous Raman scattering as a sensitive probe of these effects and apply our framework to recent experiments suggesting quantum nonlinear phononics involving the $\mathrm{E}_{1u}$ and $\mathrm{E}_{2g}$ modes in multilayer graphene. At very strong pump fields, driving the $\mathrm{E}_{1u}$ mode significantly modifies the $\mathrm{E}_{2g}$ Raman spectrum; however, reproducing the reported response at experimental field strengths requires accounting for pump-induced Raman matrix element dynamics or alternative energy pathways into the $\mathrm{E}_{2g}$ mode. Our findings extend nonlinear phononics beyond average atomic displacements to the dynamics of lattice fluctuations, highlighting the interplay between coherent motion and quantum correlations in driven materials.



[141]. [*Tailored VxOy Metal--Insulator-Transition Oxides for Temperature-Triggered Smart Insulation in Superconducting Coils*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16581 "Tailored VxOy Metal--Insulator-Transition Oxides for Temperature-Triggered Smart Insulation in Superconducting Coils")

Mattia Ortino (GREEN), Davide Uglietti (SPC), Andrea Testino (PSI), Nicola Casati (PSI), Alexander Steppke (PSI), Wiktoria Wnetkowska (PSI), Andrea Masi (ENEA), Kamil Sedlak (SPC)

arXiv:2610.16581v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Smart insulation is a turn-to-turn layer that insulates in normal operation and conducts only near a hot spot. It could combine the fast ramping of insulated coils with the passive energy dump of non-insulated coils. Metal-insulator transition (MIT) oxides are natural candidates, but the only one tested in a coil so far, V2O3, switches at 150-170 K, far above the 30-70 K window useful for REBCO magnets. We explore the vanadium-oxygen system to lower the transition. TGA redox processing produced multi-phase powders with an effective transition near 127 K, about 25 K below V2O3. Hydrothermal V3O7, hydrated or annealed, was far too resistive and showed no usable transition. V2O3-epoxy specimens provided a robust reference and an acceptance criterion. Cryogenic measurements revealed a reproducible thermal hysteresis, and the measured specific heat of V2O3 corrects earlier model inputs by close to two orders of magnitude. A first electro-thermal model reproduces current sharing through the smart-insulation layer. Doped V2O3 and V8O15 are identified as the next targets.



[142]. [*Admittance extraction of quantum dot arrays for charge sensing*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16590 "Admittance extraction of quantum dot arrays for charge sensing")

Conor Power, Mathieu Moras, Andrii Sokolov, Robert Bogdan Staszewski, Elena Blokhina

arXiv:2610.16590v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: This work demonstrates admittance extraction of quantum dot arrays using a rate equation frame- work matching experimental reflectometry measurements. Using a semiclassical rate equation, we consider a perturbation of the source/drain chemical potential to model reflectometry readout in a quantum dot array. This is a time-dependent extension of the rate equation which has been employed in literature for pumped arrays of quantum dots and capacitance extractions. Here we extend this to a double quantum dot for admittance extraction. The simulations are calibrated us- ing Fermi-Hubbard parameters extracted from experimental data, which it is then shown to closely approximate to.



[143]. [*Solubility of poorly soluble species in bulk and nanopores from grand-canonical Wang-Landau sampling*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16599 "Solubility of poorly soluble species in bulk and nanopores from grand-canonical Wang-Landau sampling")

Russell Kajouri, Adyant Agrawal, Philipp St\"ark, Alexander Schlaich, Christian Holm

arXiv:2610.16599v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Poorly soluble species can have mean populations below one in small systems such as nanopores. In this regime, the grand-potential minimum, which identifies the most probable particle number, differs from the ensemble-averaged solute loading. We use grand-canonical Wang-Landau sampling (GCWLS) to calculate the density of states $\Omega(N_\mathrm{A}, N_\mathrm{B})$ for a binary Lennard-Jones mixture of solute B in solvent A, both in bulk and in slit pores. One set of GCWLS simulations gives ensemble averages over a range of chemical potentials, whereas conventional grand-canonical Monte Carlo (GCMC) simulation requires a separate simulation at each state point. Independent GCMC simulations confirm the GCWLS ensemble averages. The solvent number distribution is nearly Gaussian, whereas the dilute solute distribution is skewed and Poisson-like. The most probable solute number therefore lies below the ensemble-averaged loading $\langle N_\mathrm{B}\rangle$ and is zero whenever $\langle N_\mathrm{B}\rangle<1$. Reading the solute loading from the grand-potential minimum underestimates it and can falsely indicate that no solute is present. Defining the oversolubility as $\mathcal{O}_\mathrm{B}=x_\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{conf}}/x_\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{bulk}}$, where $x_\mathrm{B}$ is the solute mole fraction, we find $\mathcal{O}_\mathrm{B}\approx12$ in a pore of 3.5 particle diameters width when both wall interactions are repulsive. Adding wall-solute attraction moderately raises it to $\approx13$, whereas wall-solvent attraction lowers it to $\approx3.4$. This comparison shows that the wall-solvent interaction controls most of the enhancement. As the pore widens, $\mathcal{O}_\mathrm{B}$ decreases toward unity. In bulk and in nanopores alike, the solubility of poorly soluble species should be obtained from ensemble averages rather than from grand-potential minima.



[144]. [*Nontrivial topological properties from the cooperation of static perturbations and Floquet fields in type-II semi-Dirac semimetals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16659 "Nontrivial topological properties from the cooperation of static perturbations and Floquet fields in type-II semi-Dirac semimetals")

Wen-Hao Bian, Jing Wang

arXiv:2610.16659v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In this work, we present a comprehensive theoretical study for the topological and anomalous transport properties of type-II SDSMs under three scenarios, i.e., static symmetry-breaking perturbations, periodic Floquet driving by two-frequency orthogonally polarized light (TFOPL), and their cooperative effects in the hybrid scenario. We first show that a static mass $m_z$ opens a bulk gap and drives the system into a Chern insulator with Chern number $C^{\rm per}=\pm 2\,{\rm sgn}(m_z)$. In comparison, TFOPL alone only shifts the positions of Dirac points and cannot open a full gap. Nevertheless, it produces a Berry curvature odd in the $x$-direction, yielding a finite Berry curvature dipole (BCD) along $x$ proportional to the sine of the initial phase, while the $y$-component vanishes. The hybrid scenario, by contrast, exhibits a remarkably rich phenomenology. Specifically, its gap-closure criterion becomes cubic and thus admits up to three real roots when the effective parameter along the $x$-direction is negative, thereby producing multiple light-tunable gap-closing points and a series of topological phase transitions. In particular, the BCD exhibits a double-peak structure with strong enhancement near gap-closing manifolds and synchronized sign reversal of the full vector under external tuning. We further analyze the anomalous Hall conductivity (AHC), anomalous Nernst conductivity (ANC), and anomalous thermal Hall conductivity (ATHC). The AHC and ATHC exhibit quantized plateaus that serve as signatures of Chern-insulator phases, while the ANC, being a Fermi-surface probe, provides complementary local information and can identify light-induced gaps even when global topological invariants remain unchanged. Taken together, these results offer useful insights into the topological properties of 2D type-II SDSMs and related systems.



[145]. [*Topological Chiral Superconductivity from Doping Correlated Charge Transfer Insulator with Orbital Magnetism*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16674 "Topological Chiral Superconductivity from Doping Correlated Charge Transfer Insulator with Orbital Magnetism")

Wen Sun, Yuan Yang, Shou-Shu Gong, Wen Huang

arXiv:2610.16674v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Topological chiral superconductivity has been extensively studied from the perspective of electronic structures in momentum space, while its emergence from real-space processes in strongly interacting systems remains largely unexplored. Here, we study a doped charge-transfer insulator in a strongly repulsive spinless-fermion model with three sublattices and $2\pi$ flux per unit cell. Combining perturbative analysis, exact diagonalization, and density matrix renormalization group simulations, we establish a topological superconducting ground state with $p+ip$ symmetry. In the large charge-transfer gap limit, chiral charge-transfer excitations generate a correlated-hopping process that provides a microscopic mechanism for electron pairing and its chiral structure. The chiral superconductivity persists into the smaller-gap regime, where chiral excitonic two-particle bound state can emerge. Our results establish a concrete real-space route to topological chiral superconductivity upon doping strongly interacting charge-transfer insulators with orbital magnetism.



[146]. [*Confinement-deconfinement transitions in the dimer-loop models on two-dimensional non-bipartite lattices*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16676 "Confinement-deconfinement transitions in the dimer-loop models on two-dimensional non-bipartite lattices")

Jay Pandey, Souvik Kundu, Kedar Damle

arXiv:2610.16676v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Recent work [Kundu, Damle, Phys. Rev. X 15, 011018 (2025)] has demonstrated that a class of $S=1$ kagome lattice antiferromagnets with competing anisotropies can exhibit unusual low-temperature behavior that is best described in terms of the statistical mechanics of an interesting interpolation between the fully-packed dimer model and the fully-packed loop model on the bipartite honeycomb lattice. On this and other bipartite lattices in two and three dimension, such fully-packed dimer-loop models generically have two distinct power-law correlated Coulomb phases, both of which admit a low-energy description in terms of a fluctuating divergence-free polarization vector field [Phys. Rev. X 15, 011018 (2025); arXiv:2510.11607]. The transition between these phases corresponds to a confinement transition for the $Z_2$ part of the corresponding $U(1)$ flux [Phys. Rev. X 15, 011018 (2025); arXiv:2510.11607]. Here we demonstrate that a variant of such a topological confinement transition can also exist in dimer-loop models on non-bipartite lattices such as the kagome and triangular lattices although the low-energy description does not feature any conserved $U(1)$ flux. In these non-bipartite dimer-loop models, both phases are short-range correlated liquids, and the topological distinction between them is in terms of a $Z_2$-valued winding number instead of the flux of a divergence-free polarization field. In spite of this key difference, this transition appears to be in the same Ising universality class as the analogous transition of the bipartite dimer-loop models. These kagome and triangular lattice dimer-loop models correspond respectively to the low-energy limit of a class of $S=1$ antiferromagnets with competing anisotropies on the ruby lattice comprising a corner-sharing arrangement of fully-frustrated squares and a lattice of corner-sharing fully-frustrated hexagons.



[147]. [*Domain-wall-to-domain interconversion in a polar conducting oxide*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16681 "Domain-wall-to-domain interconversion in a polar conducting oxide")

Martin F. Sarott, Majid Ahmadi, Johanna van Gent Gonz\'{a}lez, Ewout van der Veer, Mads C. Weber, Jacopo Pinna, Daniel A. Chaney, Arijit Sinha, Hamideh Hassani, Horatio R. J. Cox, Thomas Perrault, Amir Sabet Ghorabaei, Maria A. Loi, Philippe Ghosez, Bart J. Kooi, Beatriz Noheda

arXiv:2610.16681v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Domain walls in ferroic crystals are topological defects with a symmetry distinct from the domain interior, allowing them to display functionalities that are otherwise forbidden in the host material. Despite their unique properties, domain walls are strictly confined to two-dimensional interfaces, which hinders unveiling their true nature and limits their applicability. Here, we demonstrate reversible dimensionality control by expanding nanoscale domain walls into three-dimensional functional regions in polar epitaxial tungsten trioxide ($\text{WO}_3$). This domain-wall-to-domain interconversion is enabled by the conducting, non-polar domain walls adopting the parent-phase structure. An alternating electric field applied through a scanning probe tip induces localized heating, which expands this domain-wall phase into the adjacent polar domains. This approach enables reversible spatial control to switch between mutually exclusive properties of domains (polarity) and domain walls (conductivity), offering a new paradigm for reconfigurable oxide electronics.



[148]. [*Destructive Interference of a Charge Density Wave in the Skyrmion Magnet GdRu2Si2*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16682 "Destructive Interference of a Charge Density Wave in the Skyrmion Magnet GdRu2Si2")

G. D. A. Wood, D. A. Mayoh, F. Orlandi, A. Bombardi, D. D. Khalyavin, P. Manuel, S. H. Moody, P. D. Hatton, R. D. Johnson, T. W. Chamberlain, O. A. Petrenko, M. R. Lees, G. Balakrishnan

arXiv:2610.16682v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In skyrmion magnets, charge density waves induce higher-order exchange interactions that can select multi-Q ground states. Using resonant X-ray scattering and neutron diffraction, we measure temperature-dependent spin-charge coupling in GdRu2Si2. A minimal Landau model with one critical exponent and two ordering temperatures reproduces all six observed order parameters. Spin-density wave components of the multi-Q ground state drive charge ordering, but the onset of a magnetic helix suppresses it via destructive interference.



[149]. [*Colloid-Colloid Interactions Impact Network Morphology of Confined Colloidal Drying Patterns*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16695 "Colloid-Colloid Interactions Impact Network Morphology of Confined Colloidal Drying Patterns")

Andreas Andersen Hennig, Natalia Kizilova, Ilaria Beechey-Newman, Erika Eiser

arXiv:2610.16695v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Colloidal suspensions that are confined between two closely spaced plates and left to evaporate slowly form maze-like labyrinth patterns in the final stage of evaporation. The fingers in these patterns form bifurcations and occasionally loops, which we may quantify with a skeletonization method. We compare drying patterns formed with two different salt concentrations in the droplet which changes the screening length and therefore the effective colloid interactions. We find that the junctions generally follow Murray's law for optimal transport networks, and that the typical bifurcation angles peak around 53. Screened droplets additionally show more loopy networks, and is characterized by bands of transversal fingers before merging into a central non-fingering colloidal aggregate. Our observations suggest that colloid-colloid interactions can strongly influence the large-scale topology of the pattern while leaving the local geometry of the individual fingers unchanged.



[150]. [*1/f frequency-noise reduction in nanomechanical resonators by dissipation dilution*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16711 "1/f frequency-noise reduction in nanomechanical resonators by dissipation dilution")

Kostas Kanellopulos, Silvan Schmid

arXiv:2610.16711v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The frequency noise of a resonant nanomechanical sensor is well understood down to its thermomechanical limit, but the longest useful averaging time is set by a flicker, or $1/f$, frequency-noise floor whose origin has remained unknown. We show that this floor is the thermal noise of the tension. By the fluctuation-dissipation theorem, the tension of a resonator with fixed ends fluctuates by an amount set by the loss angle of its material; for structural damping, these fluctuations produce $1/f$ frequency noise with a flicker coefficient $h_{-1}=E k_{\mathrm{B}} T/(2\pi V\sigma_0^2 Q_{\mathrm{el}})$, with $Q_{\mathrm{el}}$ the intrinsic loss under elongation, that contains no free parameter and is diluted by the tensile stress $\sigma_0$ in the same way as damping is in dissipation dilution. Silicon nitride strings and membranes from 30 to 340~nm thickness follow the predicted volume scaling, the predicted $\sigma_0^{-2}$ dilution across resonators and within stress-engineered strings, and the predicted thickness dependence, which places noise-active two-level systems in the volume and at the surfaces of the resonators, all at the absolute level set by the independently measured intrinsic loss. The same relation accounts for published data between 78 and 296~K; below 78~K, the loss measured at the resonance no longer represents the loss probed by the noise, marking the limit of the structural-damping assumption. Dissipation dilution therefore lowers the $1/f$ floor, and the longest useful integration time of a resonant sensor is set by its prestress, volume, temperature, and intrinsic loss angle.



[151]. [*Emergent Spinorial Structure in Mesoscopic Irreversible Thermodynamics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16738 "Emergent Spinorial Structure in Mesoscopic Irreversible Thermodynamics")

Giorgio Sonnino

arXiv:2610.16738v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We reconstruct a geometry of thermodynamic coordinates from transport coefficients within a mesoscopic canonical model. Self-adjoint flux observables determine a compatible evolution generator containing symmetric and antisymmetric transport coefficients. When the Hermitian transport tensor is positive, an exact graded first-order factorization reproduces this generator on its scalar sector. Its commutators define a spectral distance whose metric depends only on the symmetric tensor. Gradients of the antisymmetric tensor can change the evolution while leaving the metric unchanged. The construction allows coordinate-dependent coefficients. In the constant Onsager regime, a Clifford representation gives the same distance and an algebra fixed by the transport matrix. Published transport data for a $\mathrm{Bi_2Te_3}$ film give a principal-distance ratio of approximately $2.12$ in normalized coordinates at $ZT\simeq0.68$. We also examine a separate quadratic entropy-production observable and distinguish its discrete spectrum from the spectrum of the evolution generator. The spinorial representation adds no physical spin degrees of freedom.



[152]. [*Impact of shear and dilational membrane viscosities on capsule shape and dynamics under shear flow: a theoretical study*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16739 "Impact of shear and dilational membrane viscosities on capsule shape and dynamics under shear flow: a theoretical study")

Paul Regazzi, Marc Leonetti

arXiv:2610.16739v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In artificial (micro)capsules or cells such as red blood cells for example, the two-dimensional (2D) rheology of the membrane (or the shell) is viscoelastic and not only elastic. The deformation and inclination of such a capsule are theoretically studied under shear and extensional flows. The membrane mechanics is described by the Kelvin-Voigt model: the 2D dissipation with the Boussinesq-Scriven law associated to specific dilational and shear Boussinesq numbers $Bq_d$ and $Bq_d$ respectively while the elastic constitutive law is either Hookean, strain-softening (neoHookean) or strain-hardening (Skalak). The dynamics depends on the elastic capillary number $Ca$, the Poisson ratio (or $C$ in Skalak law), the viscosity ratio $\lambda$, the nonlinearities included in the constitutive law and both boussinesq numbers. Three dynamics are identified in the limit of weak deformations: a tank-treading motion in the limit of small elastic capillary number with a characteristic time depending on $\lambda$, $Bq_s$ and $Bq_d$, an oscillation without any elasticity and a damped oscillation leading to a tank-treading motion in the limit of large Boussinesq numbers. In all cases, both shear and dilational 2D dissipations contribute differently leading to non-trivial results. In the case of a Skalak law with $C=1$, $Bq=Bq_s=Bq_d$ and $Bq>>\lambda$, the deformations in the limits $Ca<<1$, $Bq=O(1)$ and $Ca=O(1)$, $Bq>>1$ determined in the seminal work of \cite{barthes1985role} are recovered.



[153]. [*Transport Determines Spectral Distance in Mesoscopic Thermodynamics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16752 "Transport Determines Spectral Distance in Mesoscopic Thermodynamics")

Giorgio Sonnino

arXiv:2610.16752v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We derive a distance between thermodynamic states from a positive Hermitian transport tensor within a mesoscopic canonical model. An exact first-order factorization of the evolution generator gives a commutator norm and the metric $G=2g^{-1}$, where $g$ denotes the symmetric transport tensor. Gradients of the antisymmetric tensor can change the evolution, but they leave this distance unchanged. The construction allows coordinate-dependent coefficients. For constant Onsager coefficients, a Clifford representation gives the same metric. In a two-channel thermoelectric system, the ratio of normalized principal distances is $\sqrt{1+ZT}+\sqrt{ZT}$. Transport coupling thus fixes the anisotropy of this metric. The construction requires neither an independent metric nor a physical spin variable.



[154]. [*Engineering Ferroelectric Phases in BaTiO$_3$ Films via Buffer-Layer Induced Misfit Strain Reversal*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16766 "Engineering Ferroelectric Phases in BaTiO$_3$ Films via Buffer-Layer Induced Misfit Strain Reversal")

A. S. Mikheykin (Southern Federal University), N. V. Lyanguzov (Southern Federal University), A. S. Konstantinov (Southern Federal University), V. B. Shirokov (Southern Federal University, Federal Research Centre The Southern Scientific Centre of the Russian Academy of Sciences), E. D. Linnik (Southern Federal University), T. Bondar (Southern Federal University), A. G. Razumnaya (Jo\v{z}ef Stefan Institute)

arXiv:2610.16766v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Controlling ferroelectric phase symmetry through strain engineering is crucial for tailoring polarization topology and functional properties in oxide-based devices. Here, we show that a thin conductive buffer layer can override substrate-induced strain in BaTiO$_3$ (BTO) films on MgO. Although the lattice parameter of MgO(001) would be expected to induce tensile misfit strain, in 500~nm BaTiO$_3$ films the thermal-expansion mismatch dominates, resulting in effective compressive stress upon cooling. Inserting a 50-nm La$_{0.67}$Sr$_{0.33}$MnO$_3$ (LSMO) buffer layer reverses the effective strain state from compressive to tensile. Using temperature-dependent polarized Raman spectroscopy, X-ray diffraction, and piezoresponse force microscopy, we reveal an unconventional phase sequence upon cooling: paraelectric $\rightarrow$ orthorhombic ($\textit{aa}$-phase, $\sim$407 K) $\rightarrow$ monoclinic ($\textit{r}$-phase, $\sim$300-345 K), characterized in-plane and tilted polarization components. The LSMO layer not only serves as a bottom electrode but also modifies the strain state, while interfacial charge redistribution induces a built-in electric field, evidenced by self-polarization and asymmetric PFM hysteresis. These findings highlight the critical role of conductive buffer layers in controlling ferroelectric symmetry and enabling oxide heterostructures with tailored polarization textures for low-power spintronics, tunable microwave devices, and neuromorphic computing.



[155]. [*Quantum Ising transition on the Cayley tree: boundary criticality, universality, and an effective Bethe line*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16774 "Quantum Ising transition on the Cayley tree: boundary criticality, universality, and an effective Bethe line")

Pietro Richelli, Natalia Chepiga

arXiv:2610.16774v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigate quantum criticality in the transverse-field Ising model on finite Cayley trees and demonstrate that their extensive boundary fundamentally changes the nature of the quantum phase transition compared with the infinite, boundary-free Bethe lattice. Using tree tensor-network simulations for systems with up to $N=12286$ sites, we identify a boundary-driven continuous transition at $h_x^c\approx1.76$, distinct from the mean-field Bethe-lattice transition at $h_x^c\approx2.23$. We characterize this transition through finite-size scaling of the magnetization and magnetic susceptibility and extract a consistent set of critical exponents. By introducing next-nearest-neighbor interactions, we demonstrate the robustness of the extracted critical exponents, providing numerical argument for the Ising boundary universality class on the Cayley tree. Furthermore, we show that the physics of the boundary-free Bethe lattice can be efficiently recovered on a finite Cayley tree by fine-tuning a local boundary field. The resulting \textit{effective Bethe line}, whose existence is tied to the mean-field nature of the bulk transition, effectively screens the extensive boundary and restores both the critical point and the mean-field critical scaling of the infinite Bethe lattice.



[156]. [*Information Entropy as a Sensitive Diagnostic of Relaxation Dynamics in a Quasiperiodic Mott Insulator*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16788 "Information Entropy as a Sensitive Diagnostic of Relaxation Dynamics in a Quasiperiodic Mott Insulator")

N. D. Chavda, Ant\^onio M. S. Mac\^edo, Robin P Sagar, Barnali Chakrabarti

arXiv:2610.16788v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We study the nonequilibrium dynamics of a bosonic Mott insulator following a sudden quench from a periodic to a quasiperiodic lattice. Using ab initio multiconfigurational (MCTDHB) continuum many-body dynamics, we characterize the evolution through momentum distributions, fragmenta- tion, correlation functions, and information entropy. We find that melting of Mott correlations does not necessarily imply relaxation of the many-body state. In the weakly interacting regime, the initial Mott-like correlation undergo recurrent melting and partial recovery, while the information entropy displays large, intermittent fluctuations within the simulated time window. In contrast, a strongly interacting and fully fragmented Mott insulator retains its characteristic Mott correla- tions, up to partial distortions, while the many-body information entropy approaches a well-defined stationary regime plateau with comparatively small fluctuations. These results indicate that Mott- correlation dynamics and many-body relaxation can be dissociated in this finite system and support the use of information entropy, evaluated in the multiconfigurational representation, as a sensitive complementary diagnostic of nonequilibrium relaxation.



[157]. [*Self-phoresis on a one-dimensional periodic domain*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16791 "Self-phoresis on a one-dimensional periodic domain")

Leah Anderson, David S. Dean

arXiv:2610.16791v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We study the spontaneous motion and collective dynamics of self-phoretic particles on a one-dimensional periodic domain. The particles act as localized chemical sources (of a particle-dependent strength) of a field and the particles themselves respond to the gradients of the field they generate (with a particle-dependent phoretic mobility). The chemical field itself diffuses and evaporates. For a single particle, we derive the exact threshold for spontaneous propulsion and establish the emergence of stable constant-velocity states near onset through a supercritical pitchfork bifurcation. We then consider an active source coupled to a non-emitting tracer and determine the conditions for slipping and stable phase-locked motion. For two mutually coupled sources, the stationary antipodal configuration can lose stability through either a pitchfork or a Hopf bifurcation, depending on the relative source strengths and phoretic mobilities. We determine the competing instability thresholds and compare them with direct numerical simulations of the coupled particle-field equations. Beyond onset, the resulting translating and oscillatory states provide qualitative counterparts to modes of motion observed experimentally for camphor disks in annular water channels. These results connect the onset of individual propulsion, tracer entrainment, and collective motion within a common analytically tractable framework.



[158]. [*Molecular Precursor Chemistry Shapes Early-Stage Reaction Pathways in Nitrogen-Doped Carbon Nanotube Synthesis*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16831 "Molecular Precursor Chemistry Shapes Early-Stage Reaction Pathways in Nitrogen-Doped Carbon Nanotube Synthesis")

Narayan N. Som, Lukasz Nowicki, Tomasz Gizewski, Karolina Z. Milowska, Anna Wroblewska, Caghan Tuncer, Maciej Trzaskowski, Olga Iwasinska-Kowalska, Tomasz Plocinski, Mariusz Zdrojek, Krzysztof K. Koziol, Malgorzata Jakubowska, Agnieszka Lekawa-Raus

arXiv:2610.16831v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The structural characteristics of carbon nanotubes are usually discussed in terms of processes occurring during nanotube nucleation and subsequent growth from an already formed catalyst nanoparticle. Here, we show that their structural trajectory can begin much earlier, within the first nanoseconds of precursor decomposition and catalyst formation. We combine experiment and reactive molecular dynamics to compare nitrogen-free CNT synthesis with two closely related aromatic nitrogen precursors, pyridine and pyrazine, containing one and two nitrogen atoms per molecule, respectively. Experimentally, both nitrogen-containing precursors promote bamboo-like CNT formation and produce substantially more structurally disordered CNTs than the nitrogen-free reference. Notably, nitrogen incorporation does not scale simply with the amount of nitrogen supplied, demonstrating a strong contribution from precursor chemistry. Reactive molecular dynamics reveals that nitrogen-containing precursors significantly modify local Fe-carbon and Fe-SiO2 interactions during catalyst evolution, while pyridine and pyrazine exhibit distinct decomposition pathways, with pyrazine showing significantly greater decomposition within the simulated timescale. These results demonstrate that precursor chemistry influences the local chemical environment and early catalyst evolution, highlighting its potential role in the initial stages of CNT growth.



[159]. [*Trust-Limited Machine-Learning-Accelerated Nudged Elastic Band Calculations for Small-Polaron Hopping in Rutile TiO2*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16850 "Trust-Limited Machine-Learning-Accelerated Nudged Elastic Band Calculations for Small-Polaron Hopping in Rutile TiO2")

Ashish Kumar Singh, Irina V. Lebedeva

arXiv:2610.16850v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Nudged elastic band (NEB) calculations of small-polaron hopping are demanding because nuclear motion is coupled to a self-consistent localized electronic state. Near the charge-transfer region, slow self-consistent-field convergence, localization switching, and competing electronic solutions complicate surrogate-assisted optimization. We develop a Gaussian-process-assisted NEB workflow for this regime and demonstrate electron small-polaron hopping in rutile TiO2 using spin-polarized density functional theory with a Hubbard U correction (DFT+U ) with SIESTA. A joint energy-force Gaussian process (GP) with a Mat\'ern covariance model advances the elastic band, while active-atom root-mean-square and maximum-atom displacement limits constrain surrogate motion relative to evaluated configurations. Reaching these limits triggers first-principles sampling without requiring full surrogate convergence. Three independently DFT-verified 96-atom [001] calculations using 11, 13, and 15 images and different surrogate settings and GP parameterizations yield barriers of 0.1645, 0.1637, and 0.1640 eV, respectively, spanning 0.793 meV. In the 15-image calculation, repeated central-image sampling activates a separately calibrated local GP during surrogate optimization. Final full-band DFT verification gives a maximum projected NEB force of 0.0346 eV/{\AA}. A 324-atom calculation yields a lower barrier of 0.1302 eV, indicating supercell sensitivity and demonstrating feasibility at larger GP observation dimension. The workflow combines controlled surrogate extrapolation, targeted charge-transfer-region interpolation, and independent DFT verification to obtain polaron migration pathways, emphasizing reproducible barriers and full-band force convergence.



[160]. [*Magnetic properties and excitation spectrum of the S = 1/2 three-legged spin ladder, LiMoOP2O7*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16856 "Magnetic properties and excitation spectrum of the S = 1/2 three-legged spin ladder, LiMoOP2O7")

Aly H Abdeldaim, Alexander A. Tsirlin, Jacques Ollivier, Clemens Ritter, Robin S. Perry, Lucy Clark, G{\o}ran J. Nilsen

arXiv:2610.16856v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We report a comprehensive experimental study including density functional theory calculations detailing the magnetic ground state of the $S = 1/2$ Mo$^{5+}$ pyrophosphate, LiMoOP2O7, and its magnetic excitations. Neutron powder diffraction measurements confirm the $P2_1/n$ structural model which is comprised of a three-legged ladder of distorted Mo$^{5+}$ octahedra. Magnetometry and specific heat reveal a long-range magnetic ordering transition at $T_\textrm{N} = 3.5$~K and a field-induced transition at $\mu_{0}$H$_\textrm{c}$ = 1 T. This zero-field long-range magnetic order was determined to be a commensurate $\kappa = 0$ order with an ordered moment that is reduced from the full $S = 1/2$ magnetic moment by 30%. Through inelastic neutron scattering measurements and density functional theory calculations, we define the minimal microscopic model and resolve the spin Hamiltonian up to the seventh leading exchange parameter, resulting in a model with dominant antiferromagnetic leg interactions, weaker ferromagnetic rung interactions, and a significant interlayer ferromagnetic coupling.



[161]. [*Mutual linearity from classical response theory*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16894 "Mutual linearity from classical response theory")

Timur Aslyamov, Massimiliano Esposito

arXiv:2610.16894v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We unify known mutual-linearity relations for Markov jump processes and chemical reaction networks within a common framework based on classical finite-response theory. In a Markov jump process, an exact finite-response identity shows that all stationary probability changes share a common factor when the rates of one edge change. Eliminating this factor gives the relations between probabilities, observables, and currents, even far from equilibrium. We also explain how the same argument applies when one coefficient of a linear system changes and recovers the known relations in Laplace space and between stationary complex activities in chemical reaction networks.



[162]. [*Mutual information across the commensurate--incommensurate crossover and near the Kosterlitz--Thouless point of the spin-$\tfrac12$ $J_1$--$J_2$ Heisenberg chain*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16915 "Mutual information across the commensurate--incommensurate crossover and near the Kosterlitz--Thouless point of the spin-$\tfrac12$ $J_1$--$J_2$ Heisenberg chain")

Wuttichai Pankeaw, Pruet Kalasuwan

arXiv:2610.16915v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: What quantum mutual information adds to conventional correlation functions is often unclear. We study the spin-1/2 $J_1$-$J_2$ Heisenberg chain, using DMRG on infinite chains and periodic rings and an SU(2) decomposition of the mutual information into correlation channels. For singlet ground states the two-site mutual information is an exact function of the spin correlator, so any additional content must come from blocks. In the dimerized phase, blocks resolve bond-energy correlations in the SU(2)-scalar channel without an independent asymptotic length: singlet and triplet correlation lengths agree to about 1%. The scalar channel dominates block mutual information at large distances in the commensurate regime up to $J_2/J_1=0.40$, but falls to a few percent beyond the disorder point, which coincides numerically with the Majumdar-Ghosh point $J_2/J_1=1/2$; the structure-factor peak splits only at the Lifshitz point $J_2/J_1=0.520622(2)$. On the gapless side we compare block mutual information on rings extrapolated to infinite size with the two-interval prediction of SU(2)$_1$ conformal field theory across the Kosterlitz-Thouless point $J_2/J_1=0.241167$. There, for block lengths $l\le6$, the correction decays as $l^{-p}$ with $p=1.96$-$2.07$, and any logarithmic term is at least 60 times smaller than in the Heisenberg chain. Expanding in the marginal coupling (from level spectroscopy) the first-order coefficient is consistent with $l^{-2}$ scaling and the second-order coefficient is positive for $x\le1/4$, suggesting that the mutual information relative to the field-theory value becomes stationary at the KT point for large blocks. The scalar-channel share approaches 1/4 at small cross ratio $x$, as expected from SO(4) symmetry; under two stated assumptions its first-order response to the coupling is $(3/8)\ln x-5/8$ at small $x$, matched by the $l=4$ data within 1-3% at $x\le1/9$.



[163]. [*Microscopic Modeling of Interlayer Tunneling in Twisted Bilayer Cuprates*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16930 "Microscopic Modeling of Interlayer Tunneling in Twisted Bilayer Cuprates")

Siddhant Panda, Andreas Kreisel, Laura Fanfarillo, P. J. Hirschfeld

arXiv:2610.16930v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The field of twistronics, recently extended to twisted bilayer cuprate systems, has generated considerable interest due to the prediction of $d+id'$ states close to a $45^\circ$ twist, as suggested by multiple experimental probes. Theoretical studies of these systems using a variety of models and many-body theoretical techniques have revealed multiple competing order parameters. The large variability in experimental results and theoretical predictions demands an accurate microscopic model for these systems, in particular a more realistic description of the interlayer tunneling. In this study, we fit the in-plane hopping and compute the interlayer tunneling amplitudes by calculating the orbital overlap between $d_{x^2-y^2}$ wave functions at each site of the twisted square-lattice model. We observe sign-changing tunneling amplitudes, a strong twist-angle dependence of the direct tunneling term, and long-range tunneling amplitudes that often dominate over direct tunneling. We investigate the band structure, Fermi surfaces, and spectral functions in comparison with those obtained from the phenomenological model and find a substantial redistribution of spectral weight that modifies the competition between different superconducting ground states. We determine the phase diagram as a function of twist angle, filling, and direct tunneling strength, which is controlled by the $c$-axis distance between the monolayers, and observe a reduction in the region of time-reversal-symmetry-breaking (TRSB) $d+id'$ states compared with the phenomenological model. Finally, we investigate the Josephson critical current across the phase diagram and find strongly suppressed critical currents close to a $45^\circ$ twist, consistent with experimental observations.



[164]. [*First-fracture kinetics in periodic chains: from free-energy landscapes and dynamical recrossing to absolute rates*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16944 "First-fracture kinetics in periodic chains: from free-energy landscapes and dynamical recrossing to absolute rates")

Aristotelis P. Sgouros

arXiv:2610.16944v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The nearly collinear configuration of highly stretched polymer chains before fracture motivates one-dimensional models. At fixed extension, stretching one bond redistributes length among the others, coupling rupture to the whole chain. In this work, I develop a framework for fracture-healing rates in periodic Morse chains, combining deterministic theories, equilibrium simulations and a quasi-deterministic approach using equilibrium fluxes and barrier-top trajectories. Constrained-partition calculations closely reproduce simulated populations and intact-state tensions. An intact-reservoir potential of mean force (PMF) supplies the first-fracture surface probability, while recrossing corrects the attempt flux without a rate-fitted prefactor. Independent rate tests identify an operational chain-length boundary for accurate two-state kinetics; failed-return durations decrease relative to population relaxation as chains grow. Kramers-Grote-Hynes (KGH), nonlinear reactive-flux and equilibrium rates show excellent agreement in this regime. KGH predicts rates away from coexistence and a chain-dependent local activation length dominated by the barrier response. Rates decrease approximately exponentially before declining more gently for longer chains: barrier growth dominates, additional fracture sites partly compensate, and transmission varies more weakly. Beyond coexistence, long chains can fracture practically irreversibly because healing becomes too rare to observe. Projected committor crossings, where the configuration-averaged probability of reaching the fractured core first is one half, lie near site-PMF maxima, although configurations at equal bond length retain different fracture probabilities. Multiple fractures remain negligible near coexistence through at least $N=50$. The framework could supply state-dependent rates to mesoscopic fracture models of polymer networks.



[165]. [*First-Principles Calculations of the Electron-Hole-Pair Creation Energy in Semiconductors and Dielectrics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16949 "First-Principles Calculations of the Electron-Hole-Pair Creation Energy in Semiconductors and Dielectrics")

Dallin O. Nielsen, Massimo V. Fischetti

arXiv:2610.16949v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We present first-principles calculations of the electron-hole-pair creation energy in semiconductors and dielectrics, focusing in particular on AlN, 3C-SiC, 4H-SiC, Si, and SiO2. We have employed density functional theory (DFT) to calculate the band structure, density-functional perturbation theory to obtain the phonon dispersion, and time-dependent DFT (TDDFT) to calculate the electron energy loss function (via the dielectric function). We performed full-band Monte Carlo simulations of electron thermalization for energies up to ~100 eV and calculated the average energy required to excite an electron-hole pair. We calculated pair-creation energies of approximately 12.2, 6.5, 7.3, 3.10, and 16.7 eV/pair for AlN, 3C-SiC, 4H-SiC, Si, and SiO2, respectively. These values are in good agreement with measured data, with the largest discrepancy being that of Si (about 14.9% lower than the accepted experimental value of ~3.6 eV/pair). To improve the agreement for Si, we used the GW approach and the Bethe-Salpeter equation (BSE) to calculate the quasiparticle shifts of the electronic band structure and the dielectric function, respectively. Finally, using the GW/BSE data, we calculated a creation energy of ~3.50 eV/pair. We find that the marked improvement in the accuracy of the calculated creation energy, when using the GW/BSE approach, is primarily attributable to the slight decrease in the magnitude of the electron energy-loss function when using the GW/BSE approach for energies below ~10-15 eV. Overall, it is clear that the competition between phonon emission and ionization near the intersection of the phonon scattering and ionization rates is largely responsible for determining the value of the creation energy, with the other major factor being the location and shape of the plasmon peak in the energy-loss function.



[166]. [*Emergent low-energy thermodynamics and universal $B/T$ scaling in $\alpha$-RuCl$_3$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16961 "Emergent low-energy thermodynamics and universal $B/T$ scaling in $\alpha$-RuCl$_3$")

A. Khansili, B. J. Ramshaw, A. Bangura, H. -Y. Kee, R. D. McDonald, F. Assaad, A. Rydh, A. Shekhter, K. A. Modic

arXiv:2610.16961v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Quantum spin liquids are exotic states of matter in which strongly interacting spins remain disordered down to zero temperature and host fractionalized excitations. This has motivated an extensive search for their realization in materials. Among candidate systems, $\alpha$-RuCl$_3$ has drawn particular attention as a platform for the quantum spin liquid proposed by Kitaev, in which the low-energy excitations in applied magnetic fields are interpreted as itinerant Majorana fermions and local flux excitations. However, conflicting experimental signatures have cast doubt on whether a Kitaev quantum spin liquid-like state is really the right starting point to interpret the low-energy states in $\alpha$-RuCl$_3$, motivating a closer look at the nature of these excitations. Here we report an extensive high field study of specific heat up to 35 T on $\alpha$-RuCl$_3$. We find that the low-energy thermodynamics exhibit universal $B/T$ scaling with a single effective energy scale proportional to the applied field as long as the magnetic field is not close to the $c$-axis. This behavior persists continuously from the antiferromagnetic phase boundary to the highest fields, suggesting that the perturbative Kitaev limit, with its Majorana and flux excitations, may not be an appropriate starting point for describing the low-energy thermodynamics of $\alpha$-RuCl$_3$ for fields away from the $c$-axis.



[167]. [*High-Efficiency Field-Free Superconducting Diodes in Mo$_2$N/FePt Bilayers*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16972 "High-Efficiency Field-Free Superconducting Diodes in Mo$_2$N/FePt Bilayers")

Juan S. Cruz, Emilio De Biasi, Juan C. Zapata, Yeonkyu Lee, Jeehoon Kim, Nestor Haberkorn

arXiv:2610.16972v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Near-ideal, field-free superconducting diode behavior is demonstrated in room-temperature-grown Mo$_2$N/FePt bilayers. The response is consistent with remanent FePt stripe domains producing polarity-dependent thresholds for flux entry and vortex motion. Micromagnetic simulations reveal a periodic stray-field modulation across the stripes and asymmetric field enhancement near their ends at the bridge edges. One current polarity remains nearly dissipationless up to a sizable fraction of the estimated depairing current, whereas the opposite polarity develops dissipation at much lower current, yielding diode efficiencies up to approximately 85% in the thinner bilayer. The nonreciprocity can be continuously tuned by the in-plane magnetic preparation angle and reversibly switched by reverse-field excursions through the hysteretic reversal of the FePt layer. These results support edge-controlled flux entry and vortex motion as a route to high-efficiency superconducting diodes.



[168]. [*Microscopic Theory of the Acoustic Faraday Effect in Spin-Orbit Coupled Magnets*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17010 "Microscopic Theory of the Acoustic Faraday Effect in Spin-Orbit Coupled Magnets")

Antoine Matar, Haoting Xu, Hae-Young Kee

arXiv:2610.17010v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The acoustic Faraday effect probes phonon Hall viscosity through the rotation of transverse acoustic polarization, conventionally interpreted in terms of circular birefringence. We show that magnetic order breaking rotational symmetry generates anisotropic phonon responses and elliptically polarized eigenmodes, so that Faraday rotation cannot be attributed to Hall viscosity alone. To elucidate their microscopic origin and consequences, we derive spin-phonon couplings from a generic $J$-$K$-$\Gamma$-$\Gamma'$ model with interlayer coupling, identifying transverse strain couplings for propagation along the $c$-axis arising from the interplay of Kitaev $(K)$ and $\Gamma$ interactions, along with strain-induced $g$-tensor contributions. Incorporating these couplings into our theory for the Kitaev spin-liquid candidate $\alpha$-RuCl$_3$, we find that symmetry breaking modifies the Faraday response, revealing a Hall viscosity approximately twice the value inferred assuming rotational symmetry. Our results establish a microscopic framework beyond circular birefringence, refining the connection between acoustic Faraday rotation and phonon Hall viscosity in symmetry-broken spin-orbit-coupled magnets.



[169]. [*Honeycomb Crystal in a Mass-Asymmetric Electron-Hole Bilayer*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17030 "Honeycomb Crystal in a Mass-Asymmetric Electron-Hole Bilayer")

Shengdu Chai, Wangqian Miao, Yuqiang Li, Lei Wang, Xi Dai, X. C. Xie

arXiv:2610.17030v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: A two-dimensional electron gas in a uniform neutralizing background forms a triangular Wigner crystal at low density. In a strongly mass-asymmetric electron-hole bilayer, localized heavy holes instead provide a nonuniform positive background that supports an electron crystal at densities two orders of magnitude higher than for a homogeneous electron gas in the same material. Here we show that correlated mobile electrons can overturn the preference for triangular order, stabilizing a honeycomb electron-hole crystal. Using the powerful real-space neural-network variational Monte Carlo in the Born-Oppenheimer limit with fixed holes, we find that increasing the density drives a transition from a triangular electron-hole crystal to a honeycomb crystal, which eventually melts into itinerant electrons coexisting with the honeycomb or the triangular hole lattice at fixed interlayer separation. Relative to triangular order, honeycomb order emerges when gains in electronic kinetic energy and electron-hole attraction outweigh its higher intralayer Coulomb repulsion, a threshold never reached in the rigid point-dipole model. Moreover, the honeycomb hole lattice supports paramagnetic, antiferromagnetic, and stripe-ordered spin states. Complex polarization indicates a metal-insulator transition, while the states on the honeycomb hole lattice are consistent with a stripe-ordered Dirac semimetal and paramagnetic or antiferromagnetic Mott insulators via mapping to the half-filled Hubbard model. We further propose a BLG/hBN/WS$_2$-WSe$_2$ heterostructure as a platform for the predicted honeycomb crystal, at an experimentally feasible density $n\sim10^{11}~\text{cm}^{-2}$.



[170]. [*Bifurcation Structure of Double Hysteresis and Isolated Branches in a Parametric Duffing Nanoresonator*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17052 "Bifurcation Structure of Double Hysteresis and Isolated Branches in a Parametric Duffing Nanoresonator")

Dingda Zhang, Maria Kallergi, Chanyoung So, Eva M. Weig, Ahmed A. Barakat

arXiv:2610.17052v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Direct and parametric driving together generate nonlinear response structures beyond conventional Duffing hysteresis, including multiple switching regions and isolated branches. Understanding how these structures are connected requires identifying the bifurcations that reorganize the response as the parametric excitation is varied. At a phase difference of 45 degrees between the direct and parametric excitations, we experimentally and analytically determine how the steady-state response changes as the parametric excitation is increased. A single resonance peak first splits into two while the response remains single valued. Two successive cusp bifurcations then create two saddle-node pairs and establish a double-hysteretic response. At the parametric-instability threshold, one saddle node of each pair is displaced towards unbounded amplitude and detuning, and an isolated-branch structure emerges above threshold. A parametric representation of the steady state distinguishes amplitude extrema from genuine saddle-node bifurcations and provides analytical conditions for these changes. Measurements on a dielectrically actuated pre-stressed SiN nanostring reproduce the predicted response and associated switching behavior. These results establish the bifurcation pathway connecting the different response structures at this phase configuration, providing a systematic basis for controlling multistability and switching in parametrically driven nonlinear resonators.



[171]. [*Capturing Coordination Core Topology in Metal--Organic Frameworks Using Persistent Mayer Homology*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17056 "Capturing Coordination Core Topology in Metal--Organic Frameworks Using Persistent Mayer Homology")

Caleb Simiyu Khaemba, Chun-Long Chen, Guo-Wei Wei

arXiv:2610.17056v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Local coordination cores are critically important in metal-organic frameworks (MOFs) and polyoxometalates (POMs), as they govern the fundamental chemical and structural properties of these materials. Existing methods, including element-resolved persistent homology (ERPH), have limited ability to translate local coordination cores into quantitative, scalable, and interpretable information. Here, we introduce persistent Mayer homology (PMH) to enable the detection of the geometric variability invisible by classical topology. By focusing on coordination cores formed by periodic metal-centered heavy-atom, carbon, and donor channels, PMH distinguishes different arrangements of the local coordination environment, linker framework, and potential coordinating atoms. We examine explainable PMH of orders $N=3$ and $N=5$ and separately combine each with ERPH, which characterizes the multiscale organization of chemically defined element groups across the extended framework, to predict eight nitrogen- and oxygen-related adsorption and self-diffusion properties. The resulting coordination-core PMH representations consistently improve predictive performance over ERPH alone, with the third-order Mayer representation providing the strongest overall performance. These results demonstrate that the inner coordination configurations captured by PMH contain essential structural information that is not fully represented by conventional element-resolved topological descriptors. This finding provides a quantitative interpretable topological framework for understanding local coordination environments and may facilitate the rational design of MOFs and POM-based materials.



[172]. [*Symmetry-constrained coexistence of charge-three and charge-one Weyl points in nonmagnetic crystals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17071 "Symmetry-constrained coexistence of charge-three and charge-one Weyl points in nonmagnetic crystals")

Ze-Xin Xue, Ke-Xin Pang, Yun-Yun Bai, Yanfeng Ge, Yan Gao

arXiv:2610.17071v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: High-charge Weyl semimetals (WSMs), particularly those hosting charge-three Weyl point (C-3 WPs), have attracted considerable interest owing to their unconventional topological properties and enhanced boundary phenomena. However, ideal C-3 WSMs remain rare, and the symmetry-constrained coexistence of charge-three and charge-one Weyl point (C-1 WPs) in nonmagnetic crystals remains largely unexplored. Here, we investigate a representative eight-node Weyl configuration, consisting of two C-3 WPs and six C-1 WPs with opposite chirality, corresponding to two identical $\{3,-1,-1,-1\}$ irreducible Weyl molecules. By combining topological charge conservation with space-group symmetry analysis, we systematically classify all 230 space groups and identify 12 and 23 candidate groups without and with spin--orbit coupling (SOC), respectively. First-principles calculations without SOC reveal that the boron allotropes TBR-B$_{48}$ and chiral OBD-B$_{48}$ realize this configuration, hosting hourglass-type and non-hourglass-type C-3 WPs, respectively. Surface-state calculations reveal six Fermi-arc branches at the shared projection of the charge-three pair and three at each individual projection. Reversing the structural chirality of OBD-B$_{48}$ reverses the signs of all Weyl charges, establishing a direct correspondence between crystal and momentum-space chirality. Furthermore, breaking the protecting $C_6$ symmetry in TBR-B$_{48}$ splits each C-3 WP into three C-1 WPs and reconstructs the surface Fermi arcs. Our work establishes symmetry-based guidelines for designing and searching for nonmagnetic WSMs hosting charge-three and charge-one Weyl-point coexistence.



[173]. [*Non-volatile Orbital magnetoelectric effect in a two-dimensional electron gas*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17073 "Non-volatile Orbital magnetoelectric effect in a two-dimensional electron gas")

Yu Chen, Martando Rath, Julien Brehin, Cinthia Piamonteze, Daniele Preziosi, Payal Wadhwa, Alessio Filippetti, Yuri Fukaya, Mario Cuoco, Filomena Forte, Daniela Stornaiuolo, Manuel Bibes, Marco Salluzzo

arXiv:2610.17073v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Electrical control of orbital magnetism is a central goal of orbitronics. While current-induced orbital polarization has recently been demonstrated in inversion-asymmetric systems, nonvolatile electrical control of an equilibrium orbital moment has remained elusive.
  Here, using x-ray absorption spectroscopy with \textit{in situ} electric-field control of the FE polarization, we demonstrate non-volatile switching of the equilibrium orbital magnetization of the oxide q2DEGs formed at the LaAlO$_3$/EuTiO$_3$/Ca-STO (001) interface. X-ray linear dichroism reveals a reversible modulation, driven by the FE-polarization direction, of the Ti-3$d$ crystal-field splitting and orbital occupancy, establishing a ferroelectric-like metallic state. X-ray magnetic circular dichroism shows a polarization-controlled switching of the Eu$^{2+}$ orbital magnetic moment induced by the orbital magnetization of the q2DEG projected onto Eu-5$d$ states and detected at the Eu-$M_{4,5}$ edge through Eu-5$d$/Eu-4$f$ hybridization in the inversion-symmetry-broken interface. These results provide spectroscopic evidence of ferroelectric control of orbital magnetism in an oxide q2DEG.



[174]. [*Spin-orbit torque effects in all-electrical spin-wave spectroscopy experiments*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17095 "Spin-orbit torque effects in all-electrical spin-wave spectroscopy experiments")

Jos\'e Solano C\'ordova, Jone Mencos, Richard Schlitz, Quentin Rossi, Denise Reustlen, Javed Ali, Sebastian Sailler, Michaela Lammel, Matthieu Bailleul, Daniel Stoeffler, Felix Casanova, Sebastian T. B. Goennenwein, Helena Reichlova

arXiv:2610.17095v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Electrical excitation and detection of propagating spin waves are key elements of magnonic devices. While conventional antennas consisting of non-magnetic metals such as Au or Cu excite spin waves predominantly through radio-frequency (RF) Oersted fields, antennas comprising Pt can additionally generate spin-orbit torques (SOTs) via the spin Hall effect. Recent theoretical work has proposed RF-SOT as an alternative mechanism for coherent spin-wave excitation, predicting distinct characteristics compared with conventional inductive excitation. Here, we investigate Pt antennas for the excitation of coherent spin waves in 105-nm-thick epitaxial $\text{Y}_3\text{Fe}_5\text{O}_{12}$ (YIG) films through two potential mechanisms: an oscillating Oersted field and an oscillating spin current. The raw experimental transmission spectra are strongly affected by fabrication-induced modifications to the YIG film associated with the etching process together with spatially inhomogeneous interface conditions induced by Pt. After removing these contributions, we find the remaining two wave propagation consistent with a combined excitation from Oersted fields and SOTs.



[175]. [*Skyrmion gears: translation-to-rotation conversion of skyrmion clusters along periodic substrates*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17102 "Skyrmion gears: translation-to-rotation conversion of skyrmion clusters along periodic substrates")

N. P. Vizarim, C. J. O. Reichhardt, C. Reichhardt

arXiv:2610.17102v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigate how the translational motion of a compact skyrmion cluster can be converted into collective rotation through interactions with a periodic obstacle substrate. Using an effective particle model, we consider a seven-skyrmion cluster driven along a one-dimensional array of repulsive obstacle sites. For suitable obstacle spacings and interaction strengths, repeated interactions with the substrate produce coherent clockwise or counterclockwise rotation, giving rise to gear-like cluster dynamics. We characterize these states using the translational velocity, Hall angle, angular velocity, structural deformation, and a dimensionless gear ratio that relates cluster rotation to motion over the substrate periodicity. Dynamical phase diagrams identify rotating, nonrotating, wall-guided, transverse, and deformed regimes. We further show that coherent rotation persists in the overdamped limit, demonstrating that the conversion mechanism does not require an intrinsic Magnus force and may therefore extend to other driven cluster systems, including colloidal assemblies. These results provide a simple route for controlling collective rotation through structured substrates.



[176]. [*H$_2$-Like Units and Pressure-Tunable High-$T_\mathrm{c}$ Superconductivity in NaH$_6$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17148 "H$_2$-Like Units and Pressure-Tunable High-$T_\mathrm{c}$ Superconductivity in NaH$_6$")

Tianran Chen, Taner Yildirim

arXiv:2610.17148v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Hydrogen-rich materials offer a route to high-temperature conventional superconductivity, but often require extreme pressures. Here we report a first-principles study of NaH$_6$, identified in our large-scale hydride search and independently predicted in a subsequent high-throughput study. Several phases compete, with $R\bar{3}m$ and $Pm\bar{3}m$ dominant over broad pressure ranges. The $R\bar{3}m$ phase remains dynamically stable down to 10 GPa, where its calculated $T_\mathrm{c}$ is 34 K, rising to 99 K at 50 GPa. Cubic $Pm\bar{3}m$ becomes thermodynamically competitive at higher pressures and reaches a calculated $T_\mathrm{c}$ of 230 K at 50 GPa, decreasing only weakly upon further compression. Molecular dynamics and phonon-renormalization calculations support structural persistence over the sampled timescale and indicate relatively small anharmonic effects. Despite their high $T_\mathrm{c}$, both phases contain elongated H$_2$-like units, often considered unfavorable for high-$T_\mathrm{c}$ hydrides. Mode-resolved electron-phonon coupling links Raman-active hydrogen vibrations to superconductivity. In $R\bar{3}m$, the linewidths of these modes increase with the pressure-induced tilt of H$_2$ units relative to the threefold axis, alongside the rise in $T_\mathrm{c}$. A minimal symmetry-based model of the vibration-induced second-moment tensor of the hydrogen sublattice shows that these trends are consistent with a significant contribution from response components perpendicular to the threefold axis. NaH$_6$ thus represents an unusual molecular-like hydride in which hydrogen-sublattice reorganization may enable high-$T_\mathrm{c}$ superconductivity at comparatively moderate pressures.



[177]. [*Comprehensive Workflow for Uncertainty-Aware Ionic Liquid Solvent Discovery*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17171 "Comprehensive Workflow for Uncertainty-Aware Ionic Liquid Solvent Discovery")

Alexander Lobo, Urvi Awasthi, Leonid Zhukov

arXiv:2610.17171v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Designing synthesizable materials with targeted properties remains one of the central objectives in materials sciences. Ionic liquids (ILs) are well suited to this goal due to their "tunability", where physical and chemical properties can be precisely customized by modifying their molecular structure, specifically by varying the combination of cations and anions. At the same time, current data-driven property models for ionic liquids often degrade in scarce, noisy, and uneven data regimes, where measurements cover only a small fraction of chemical space and a limited range of thermodynamic conditions. This makes out-of-distribution screening brittle: even when point predictions look plausible, it is difficult to compare candidates consistently, quantify downstream implications, or trust rankings enough to guide molecular discovery. We present a property-prediction and optimal candidate selection workflow that treats data processing, deep learning modeling, and physics-based calculations as a coupled system, linking property prediction with uncertainty estimation and physics-based filtering. By tightening the link between predicted properties and the criteria used for down-selection, the workflow supports reliable out-of-distribution ranking and enables prioritization of previously untested ionic liquid candidates with favorable predicted trade-offs. We demonstrate the application of our workflow by designing novel ionic liquids for CO2 capture from flue gas, predicting their properties and selecting Pareto-optimal candidates based on CO2 solubility, IL viscosity and working capacity.



[178]. [*Spatial Engineering of Topological Quantum Matter with Structured Light*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17181 "Spatial Engineering of Topological Quantum Matter with Structured Light")

Matias Mustonen, Ali G. Moghaddam, Teemu Ojanen, Marco Ornigotti

arXiv:2610.17181v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Structured light provides a powerful means of controlling matter while itself exhibiting nontrivial spatial topology. Here, we show that these spatial structures can be harnessed to locally engineer the topological properties of matter. In a paradigmatic example of a topological insulator with Dirac surface states driven by an optical skyrmion beam, we show that structured light spatially tailors the effective Hamiltonian, yielding position-dependent topological gaps and Dirac velocities. The resulting gap modulation can create topological and trivial regions separated by optically defined interfaces, without introducing material boundaries or chemical modifications. We further show that the geometry of these interfaces can be controlled through the spatial structure of the light, in particular its orbital angular momentum. Our results establish structured light as a route to dynamically pattern topological phases and open a broader perspective on the interplay between topology in light and matter, including the possibility of transferring topological characteristics between the two.



[179]. [*Revealing topological protection through unprotected operations in a Majorana parity qubit*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17198 "Revealing topological protection through unprotected operations in a Majorana parity qubit")

Sebastian Miles, Juan Daniel Torres Luna, Francesco Zatelli, Nick van Loo, Antonio Lombardi, Vincent P. M. Sietses, Florian J. Bennebroek Evertsz', Bart Roovers, Leo P. Kouwenhoven, Michael Wimmer

arXiv:2610.17198v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: A recent experiment reported coherent parity oscillations between minimal Kitaev chains, demonstrating a Majorana parity qubit (arXiv:2607.09511). Operations on this qubit are controlled by coupling two Kitaev chains and are intrinsically unprotected against noise in the coupling strength. Here we show how the exponential protection in the underlying chains can nevertheless be revealed through unprotected qubit operations. To this end, we develop an analytical theory of quasistatic dephasing for two coupled $N$-site Kitaev chains at the sweet spot. In Rabi experiments, protection can be resolved by adapting the operating point with chain length: depending on the coupling architecture, it appears as an exponentially increasing optimized number of coherent oscillations or as a longer coherence time. Ramsey experiments show an exponentially increasing coherence time, provided the chains can be sufficiently decoupled. Our analytical theory takes into account the non-Gaussian splitting statistics of the Kitaev chains, allowing us to quantitatively predict the scaling with chain length. Moreover, we determine how residual noise and imperfections limit the range of chain lengths over which this scaling can be observed. Our results show how qubit dynamics can be used to probe Majorana protection in experimentally realistic devices.



[180]. [*Berry curvature from plane-wave mixing: A three-band model with emergent symmetries*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17209 "Berry curvature from plane-wave mixing: A three-band model with emergent symmetries")

Avi Vadali, Tomohiro Soejima

arXiv:2610.17209v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Two-dimensional materials can host topological phases stabilized either by an intrinsic moir\'e potential or topological crystallization. Crucial to the topology of these phases is the interplay between the (self-consistently generated) potential and the spinor structure, which imbues the band-projected potential with momentum-dependence. Truncating this potential at linear-order in momentum, we obtain a three-band model that captures the low-energy band structure and Berry curvature of such topological bands. We analytically find that the Berry curvature strongly depends on momentum-dependence of the potential. Furthermore, we identify that the model can be tuned to have emergent symmetries, including $U(1)$ and anti-unitary symmetries. Emergent-symmetry breaking perturbations drive the system to regimes hosting topological flat bands, quadratic band touchings, and multiple Dirac cones. We recover many of these features when studying an un-truncated model of a $\lambda$-jellium spinor inside of a periodic potential. Our results reveal a rich interplay of spinor structure and a potential in determining the topology of the ground state.



[181]. [*Pairing from repulsive interactions in twisted WSe2 from functional renormalization*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17222 "Pairing from repulsive interactions in twisted WSe2 from functional renormalization")

Hannes Braun, Dumitru C\u{a}lug\u{a}ru, Pietro M. Bonetti, Haoyu Hu, Yan Zhang, Yi Jiang, Quansheng Wu, Kin Fai Mak, Jie Shan, B. Andrei Bernevig, Michael M. Scherer, Laura Classen

arXiv:2610.17222v1 Announce Type: new
