Abstract: Motivated by the observation of superconductivity in twisted bilayer WSe$_2$ over an extended range of displacement fields, reaching from the correlated insulator down to zero field, we analyze the correlated electron phases of this system at hole filling $\nu = -1$ and twist angle $3.65^\circ$ using Hartree--Fock, random phase approximation, and functional renormalization group calculations. We derive a continuum model with gate-screened Coulomb interaction from first principles and compare its instabilities to those of a realistic three-orbital Wannier model with a Hubbard interaction and an artificial single-orbital Hubbard model. In both cases, we find a superconducting instability of mixed $d$-wave singlet and $p$-wave triplet character, arising adjacent to inter-valley-coherent spin-density-wave order in the phase diagram spanned by displacement field and interaction strength. Our additional analysis of the pairing mechanism points to inter-valley coherent spin fluctuations as the pairing glue. The extent of the pairing regime and the associated critical temperatures depend on the model input, and we trace differences to the interaction range entering the projected interaction. Within the continuum model, the gate-screened Coulomb interaction yields pairing down to zero displacement field, bringing theory into agreement with experiment.



[182]. [*Turning a superconducting diode into a Feynman ratchet to realize a nanoscale heat engine*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17257 "Turning a superconducting diode into a Feynman ratchet to realize a nanoscale heat engine")

Konstantinos Kontogeorgiou, \.Inan\c{c} Adagideli, G\'eraldine Haack, Fabian Hassler

arXiv:2610.17257v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Feynman's ratchet illustrates how work can be extracted from thermal fluctuations in the presence of two heat baths at different temperatures. While some experimental realizations of the ratchet effect have been studied, most lack miniaturization, which is important for some applications. Here, we propose a physical implementation of Feynman's ratchet based on Josephson junctions exhibiting the superconducting diode effect. This setup functions as a heat engine that generates work solely from a temperature gradient via thermal fluctuations. We analyze its output power and efficiency, and, through the Seebeck coefficient, identify the conditions under which the device operates in reverse, functioning instead as a Peltier cooler.



[183]. [*Phase-Coherent Spin and Lattice Modulations in a Y-type Hexaferrite*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17292 "Phase-Coherent Spin and Lattice Modulations in a Y-type Hexaferrite")

Oskar Stepan\v{c}i\'c, Annika Stellhorn, Emma Campillo, Kun Zhai, Niklas Ekstr\"om, A. Corani, D. Alba Venero, R. Cubitt, E. Blackburn, L. Shen

arXiv:2610.17292v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Spin-driven multiferroics owe their electric polarization to lattice distortions that follow the magnetic order, yet in incommensurate magnets symmetry normally forbids such a distortion at the fundamental magnetic wavevector, and the distortions themselves are rarely observed. Here we use half-polarized neutron scattering to show that the Y-type hexaferrite Ba$_{0.4}$Sr$_{1.6}$Mg$_2$Fe$_{12}$O$_{22}$ hosts, in its saturated high-field state, a magnetic modulation and a longitudinal lattice modulation at one and the same wavevector, locked in phase. The signature is a large nuclear-magnetic interference asymmetry, $r$ ~ 0.8, of the same sign at the two conjugate satellites and persisting to 275 K. The zone contrast and the field dependence of the satellite indicate an internal, field-induced longitudinal distortion. The modulated state coexists with the transverse-conical magnetoelectric phase and outlives it, revealing a fundamental-wavevector spin-lattice coupling in a magnetic field regime previously regarded as a simple ferrimagnet.



[184]. [*Quantum skyrmion molecule: two quantum skyrmions bound by coherent magnon exchange*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17299 "Quantum skyrmion molecule: two quantum skyrmions bound by coherent magnon exchange")

Ricardo L. Silva

arXiv:2610.17299v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Quantum skyrmions in frustrated spin-$1/2$ magnets are bound states of a few flipped spins in a ferromagnetic background. Using exact diagonalization and density-matrix renormalization group calculations for the $J_1$--$J_2$ triangular-lattice model with easy-axis anisotropy $K$, we ask what happens when two such objects are brought together. The binding energy per flipped spin of a single skyrmion has a minimum at $\Nf^{*}=7$ for $K=0.02$--$0.04$, and skyrmions smaller than $\Nf^{*}$ do not exist as pairs: at any distance they coalesce into a single larger object, and pinning wells cannot prevent it. Two skyrmions of natural size, by contrast, form a bound molecule without pinning or external field, with binding energy $U\simeq-0.007J_1$ at $K=0.03$--$0.04$ and an equilibrium separation of $5.5$ lattice spacings that does not depend on $K$ or on the size of the simulation domain; at $K=0.02$, where the single skyrmion is itself marginally bound, the pair is unbound within our resolution. The two constituents keep their identity ($7+7$ flipped spins) and are coupled by a coherent exchange of magnons, visible as number fluctuations between the two halves of the domain in excess of those of two independent skyrmions and as in-plane spin correlations between the two skyrmion walls that reach $0.16$ across the gap. These correlations follow the vortex pattern of the two textures and lock their relative helicity, the quantum counterpart of the helicity-dependent interaction of classical skyrmions. We discuss the molecule as a self-assembled two-qubit element for skyrmion helicity qubits in a single layer.



[185]. [*Evidence of an anisotropic Griffiths phase in a two-dimensional van der Waals magnet*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17315 "Evidence of an anisotropic Griffiths phase in a two-dimensional van der Waals magnet")

Anupam K. Singh, Dhananjay Nair, Holger Meyerheim, Stuart S. P. Parkin, Banabir Pal

arXiv:2610.17315v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Over the last decade, the emerging field of two-dimensional van der Waals magnetism has focused mainly on long-range-ordered magnetic phases. Disordered magnetic phases, however, remain largely unexplored. Here, we report a highly anisotropic Griffiths phase driven by self-intercalation in the van der Waals magnet Fe5GeTe2. We find evidence of short-range-ordered ferromagnetic clusters embedded in a paramagnetic matrix, which dominate the magnetic response between the Curie temperature and a higher characteristic Griffiths temperature. An anomalous power-law divergence in the magnetic susceptibility, universal Lee Yang scaling, and distinct entropy anomalies provide further evidence for this phase. Strikingly, the non-analytic Griffiths response emerges exclusively under in-plane magnetic fields, whereas out-of-plane fields abruptly alter this behavior. This directional dependence indicates that the local magnetic interactions within nanoscale clusters are closely coupled to the layered lattice geometry. Our findings establish a regime of dimensionally constrained, disorder-driven magnetism and identify self-intercalated van der Waals magnets as a platform for investigating the interplay between magnetic disorder, anisotropy, and reduced dimensionality.



[186]. [*Machine-learned topological transport of magnetic colloids*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17344 "Machine-learned topological transport of magnetic colloids")

Sebastian Wohlrab, Aneena Rinu Perayil, Piotr Ku\'swik, Maciej Urbaniak, Feliks Stobiecki, Arne J. Vereijken, Arno Ehresmann, Thomas M. Fischer, Daniel de las Heras

arXiv:2610.17344v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: By modulating the orientation of a uniform magnetic field it is possible to transport magnetic microparticles above magnetic patterns. In periodic patterns, driving control loops that wind around special orientations, which are dictated by the symmetry of the pattern, induce transport by one unit cell. The transport is topologically protected with trajectories controlled by the winding numbers of the control loop around the special orientations. In non-periodic patterns, the special orientations are position dependent, precluding a global characterization of the transport using a small set of winding numbers. We use here a reinforcement learning agent trained with Brownian dynamics simulation data that uses the local environment of the magnetic pattern to decide an action over the control loop. The agent autonomously discovers topological transport protocols directly from the particle response without prior characterization of the magnetic potential landscape. An agent trained solely on a complex non-periodic pattern directly generalizes to completely unseen periodic, quasicrystalline, and disordered substrates. Moreover, the agent dynamically adjusts the control loop to transport particles across lattice defects and to counterbalance external drift forces that would cause static topological loops to fail. By adding a pathfinding algorithm to the model, we additionally achieve simultaneous, independent transport of multiple identical colloids along different on demand trajectories. We demonstrate that the control loops can be directly transferred to an experimental setup to successfully drive magnetic microparticles across a non-uniform magnetic thin film.



[187]. [*Can Universal Machine-Learning Interatomic Potentials Capture Small Polarons? A Geometry-Based Benchmark in Transition-Metal Oxides*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17352 "Can Universal Machine-Learning Interatomic Potentials Capture Small Polarons? A Geometry-Based Benchmark in Transition-Metal Oxides")

Vladislav Korostelev, Pjotrs \v{Z}guns, Konstantin Klyukin

arXiv:2610.17352v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Small polarons, formed when excess electrons become localized through lattice distortions, play a critical role in charge transport, catalysis, and degradation processes in transition-metal (TM) oxides. Whether universal machine-learning interatomic potentials (uMLIPs), which lack explicit electronic degrees of freedom, can describe them remains unclear. Here, we investigate H-induced electron localization across a range of TM oxides using a geometric descriptor based on TM-O bond-length expansion (Z-score) to identify polaronic distortions. We find that uMLIPs exhibit varying degrees of electron localization, from delocalized states to metastable small polarons, with their behavior strongly dependent on the training data and treatment of electronic correlations. In particular, the absence of Hubbard U corrections for specific TM cations in the training data leads to electron delocalization, in contrast to DFT+U predictions. Although some uMLIPs reproduce polaron hopping barriers during coupled proton-polaron transfer, all of them fail to stabilize spatially separated polaron-donor configurations, instead favoring localization near the H dopant.



[188]. [*Meso-scale modeling of cohesive cavitation in polymer networks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17360 "Meso-scale modeling of cohesive cavitation in polymer networks")

Saleh Assadi, Zachary T. White, Fay Salmon, Nitin Hansoge, Zhuonan Liu, Victor Crespo-Cuevas, Jason Clapper, Nikolaos Bouklas, Franck J. Vernerey

arXiv:2610.17360v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Cavitation influences the failure of elastomers and soft adhesives, but connecting its microscopic initiation to subsequent cavity growth remains challenging. Here, we develop a discrete mesoscale framework that combines finitely extensible, breakable strands with local van der Waals interactions to examine how cohesion and network heterogeneity transform distributed dilation into localized damage. In the simulations, local cohesive instability produces a network-scale precursor whose subsequent expansion is coupled to strand stretching and rupture. Cohesive strength and intrinsic defect size govern the critical hydrostatic tension, while cohesion also controls the number and spatial distribution of cavities. Under confined loading, decreasing the layer thickness promotes multiple cavities and distributed strand rupture, extending the load-bearing response and increasing the critical work density of rupture. An energetic interpretation based on the thickness dependence of the crack-driving force connects these observations to competition between additional cavity nucleation and propagation of existing damage. The results link local cohesive instability to the collective evolution of cavities and rupture in disordered polymer networks.



[189]. [*Collision Cascade Morphology and Defect Nucleation in Pure Tungsten and an Equimolar Refractory W-Ta-Cr-V Alloy*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17361 "Collision Cascade Morphology and Defect Nucleation in Pure Tungsten and an Equimolar Refractory W-Ta-Cr-V Alloy")

Mark Fedorov, Francisco Javier Dominguez-Gutierrez

arXiv:2610.17361v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Collision cascades and defect-formation mechanisms in pure W and the WTaCrV refractory high-entropy alloy were investigated using molecular dynamics simulations over primary knock-on atom (PKA) energies of 0.1-100 keV. Machine-learning tabulated Gaussian approximation potentials (tabGAP), complemented by short-range Ziegler-Biersack-Littmark (ZBL) interactions, were employed. Analysis of the heat spike lifetime, affected region, atomic relocations and surviving defects reveals an importance of subcascade formation and presence of light elements in the alloy. Cascade splitting into subcascades contributes to the distribution of the PKA energy over a larger region in the material, nucleating less dislocations and smaller voids - latter being present only in W and absent in WTaCrV. Since cascade splitting is more frequently observed in WTaCrV, the alloy shows better resistance to primary irradiation damage compared to pure W. Apart from cascade splitting, analysis of interstitials shows that Cr and to lesser extent V, being lighter and smaller elements, accommodate the damage in form of point defects, contributing to the mitigation of formation of the dislocation loops.



[190]. [*High-Resolution X-ray Spectro-Microscopy of Au Microcrystallites*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17417 "High-Resolution X-ray Spectro-Microscopy of Au Microcrystallites")

Chaitali Sow, Martin Seyrich, Johannes Hagemann, Giridhar U. Kulkarni, Thomas F. Keller, Christian G. Schroer, Andreas Schropp

arXiv:2610.17417v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Understanding the functionalities of heterogeneous catalysts requires high-resolution nanoscale chemical imaging tools. X-ray spectro-ptychography offers this capability by enabling X-ray absorption near edge structure (XANES) based amplitude and phase contrasts imaging with high spatial resolution. However, such demonstrations at energies above 10 keV, such as the Au L-edges, remain limited due to weak X-ray absorption cross-sections and correspondingly, low signal levels. Here, multimodal spectro-microscopy has been performed on Au microcrystallites that host both conventional face-centered cubic (fcc) and unconventional non-cubic lattices, particularly important for catalysis. Amplitude and phase contrasts across the $\mathrm{L}_3$ edge are resolved on thin Au crystallites (150 nm to 1.71 $\mu\mathrm{m}$) with 45 nm spatial resolution. The resulting XANES spectra exhibit excellent agreement with standard transmission and fluorescence contrasts, while providing complementary chemical insights with improved spatial resolution. Quantitative retrieval of anomalous phase variations ~12 keV further demonstrates the suitability of this method for high-resolution chemical imaging across a wide range of functional materials.



[191]. [*Crystal growth, magnetic, and transport properties of the candidate altermagnet Cr$_{1/4}$NbS$_{2}$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17452 "Crystal growth, magnetic, and transport properties of the candidate altermagnet Cr$_{1/4}$NbS$_{2}$")

Nashra Pistawala, Martin R. Lees, Geetha Balakrishnan, Daniel A. Mayoh

arXiv:2610.17452v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Altermagnetism is a rapidly emerging paradigm in condensed matter magnetism, yet the number of experimentally confirmed altermagnetic candidates remains remarkably limited. Cr$_{1/4}$NbS$_2$ has long been theoretically proposed as a promising altermagnet, but there are no reports on experimental investigation of single crystals. Here, we report the successful growth of high-quality single crystals of Cr$_{1/4}$NbS$_2$ and present its structural, thermal, magnetic, and transport properties. Single-crystal X-ray diffraction provides detailed crystallographic information and confirms the hexagonal crystal symmetry ($P6_3/mmc$), one of the criteria for altermagnetic behavior. Resistivity and heat capacity measurements unambiguously identify an antiferromagnetic N\'{e}el temperature of $T_N \sim 141$ K. Magnetotransport measurements reveal non-trivial features in the magnetoresistance and Hall response, indicative of quantum diffusive transport phenomena. Cr$_{1/4}$NbS$_2$, synthesized here for the first time in single-crystalline form, therefore serves as an experimental platform for probing the rich spectrum of phenomena predicted for altermagnetic materials.



[192]. [*Energy levels of screened charged impurity in gapped graphene under magnetic field*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17492 "Energy levels of screened charged impurity in gapped graphene under magnetic field")

Andrii Iurov, V. P. Gusynin

arXiv:2610.17492v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In this paper, we present a comprehensive theoretical study of the electronic states in gapped graphene in the presence of a charge impurity and a magnetic field. We focus on the impurity-induced modification of the Landau levels under the influence of an attractive or repulsive Coulomb potential. Since analytical solutions of the Dirac equation are not available in this case, the study is carried out using the Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin (WKB) approach. Special attention is paid to the screening of the Coulomb potential in the presence of metallic gates situated at some distances from graphene sheet, as is commonly the case in experiments. Using both analytical and numerical methods, we determine the energy spectrum as a function of the magnetic field strength and impurity charge for screened and unscreened Coulomb potentials. Our results reveal a significant dependence of the energy levels for sample-to-gate distances of less than 50 nm.



[193]. [*Electric Edelstein Effect in Two Dimensions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17535 "Electric Edelstein Effect in Two Dimensions")

Xiaoran Lv, Tianqi Zhang, Chaoxi Cui, Yaling Li, Yi Shen, Weikang Wu, Ziming Zhu, Yugui Yao, Zhi-Ming Yu

arXiv:2610.17535v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We propose the electric Edelstein effect (EEE) in two-dimensional (2D) systems, where crossed out-of-plane and in-plane electric fields generate a spin polarization. The gate field converts the Edelstein response from a material-fixed spin polarization into a gate-tunable one. A symmetry analysis over all magnetic layer point groups shows that the EEE is allowed in a wide range of magnetic and nonmagnetic systems, including ferromagnets, antiferromagnets, and altermagnets. The EEE plays two distinct roles: it generates spin polarization where the linear Edelstein effect (EE) is symmetry-forbidden, as in centrosymmetric materials; and it adds a new gate-tunable component, generally non-collinear with the conventional one, where the EE is already present. The two fields then act as independent knobs and raise the control dimension of the spin polarization from one to two. Our microscopic theory traces the EEE to band-geometric quantities, which are strongly enhanced near small band gaps. First-principles calculations predict pronounced effects in the centrosymmetric ferromagnet MnSe2, where the linear EE is symmetry-forbidden. In the nonmagnet SnSe where the EE and EEE coexist, the two knobs cover an extended region of the spin sphere. Our findings establish the EEE as a symmetry-guided, gate-tunable route to electrical spin manipulation and open avenues for multistate spintronics in 2D quantum materials.



[194]. [*A grand potential for molten-salt electrochemical phase diagrams from first principles*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17611 "A grand potential for molten-salt electrochemical phase diagrams from first principles")

Lorenzo Bastonero, Lucio Colombi Ciacchi, Nicola Marzari

arXiv:2610.17611v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The clean-energy transition is driving unprecedented demand for metals, whose extraction is among the largest industrial sources of carbon emissions. Molten-salt electrolysis offers a path toward carbon-free production of aluminum, iron, titanium, and critical metals. However, corrosive operating conditions and complex, multicomponent feedstocks make experimental development costly and challenging. Here, we introduce a first-principles framework for constructing electrochemical phase diagrams of multicomponent systems to accelerate electrode and electrolyte design and guide the selection of compatible materials and operating conditions for electrochemical cells. We derive a grand potential that generates these diagrams at scale, and supply free energies using first-principles data obtained through a fine-tuned foundation machine-learned interatomic potential. For silicon in molten CaCl$_2$, the construction not only recovers the experimental diagram when using the first-principles framework on a limited set of structures, but also predicts further domains when extended to a larger set of crystal structures. Finally, we demonstrate the predictive power of the approach by screening ten alkali and alkaline-earth halide electrolytes for aluminum electrowinning. Three thermodynamic criteria identify NaF, where cryolite emerges as the stable aluminum-bearing phase, recovering the chemistry that Hall and H\'eroult established empirically in 1886. The framework provides a first-principles basis for selecting compatible electrodes, electrolytes, and operating conditions toward low-carbon metal production from complex source materials.



[195]. [*A Parameter-Free Reference Hamiltonian for Tight-Binding Vanadium Oxides*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17687 "A Parameter-Free Reference Hamiltonian for Tight-Binding Vanadium Oxides")

Jun-qiang Lu

arXiv:2610.17687v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Density-functional tight binding has yet to yield a transferable description of vanadium oxides; for the standard second-order construction and the tested cells, we demonstrate that two of its approximations deviate by electron volts and offset each other in the band-index centroid separation between the V 3d and O 2p bands: the neutral two-centre reference omits electron-volt environmental terms, whereas the Mulliken charge model assigns the compact V 3d shell less than half of the self-consistent potential shift observed in PBE. Instead, we build a tight-binding model where the reference Hamiltonian derives from superposed neutral atoms, the full PBE exchange-correlation potential and frozen-core orthogonality, without adjustable terms, and where the orbital-resolved charge response employs three screening factors obtained from PBE linear response and an unscreened Coulomb range. Against the primary PAW reference, the reference energy at fixed geometry and the stated numerical settings matches the plane-wave Harris-Foulkes energy differences of VO2, exhibiting signed deviations of +2.6 meV/atom for M1 relative to rutile (an energy difference of 92 meV/atom) and +6.2 and -5.2 meV/atom under 3% volume compression and expansion; meanwhile, the charge response captures 21 of 22 fitted and all 8 held-out matched PBE responses within tolerance, providing the V 3d shell with nearly the same self-consistent shift as PBE (-3.89 versus -3.92 eV in rutile). The convexity of the spinless model at fixed geometry prevents it from spontaneously differentiating symmetry-equivalent sites under the symmetries respected by its implemented operators and sampling, so within this model such differentiation in the Magn\'eli phases must arise from electron-lattice coupling or an explicit spin or correlation term.



[196]. [*Observation of stacked vortex structure with number controllable Majorana zero mode in Bi$_{2}$Te$_{3}$/FeTe heterojunction*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17701 "Observation of stacked vortex structure with number controllable Majorana zero mode in Bi$_{2}$Te$_{3}$/FeTe heterojunction")

Xinru Han, Zhaokun Luo, Quanxin Hu, Xingjian Zhu, Xiaohong Pan, Dan Mu, Xinyu Liang, Zulin Xu, Qiheng Wang, Ryan L. Kong, Mingyuan Li, Zhengliang Zhang, Fazhi Yang, Mojun Pan, Xin Liu, Qiangqiang Gu, Baiqing Lv, Gan Wang, Hong Ding

arXiv:2610.17701v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Creation and control of Majorana zero modes (MZMs) in solid devices is the key challenge in realizing fault tolerant topological quantum computation. Here we report spectroscopic evidence for number controllable MZMs in stoichiometric one quintuple layer Bi$_{2}$Te$_{3}$/FeTe heterostructures, together with the observation of stacked vortices in superconducting islands. Low temperature scanning tunnelling microscopy and spectroscopy reveal a long period moire superlattice arising from lattice mismatch, which induces nanoscale modulation of superconducting gaps. Magnetic vortices exhibit a stacked core structure with two layer derived bound state branches: a dispersive branch evolving into an X-shaped pattern away from the vortex center, and a quantum-limit branch characterized by a spatially unsplit MZMs together with discrete integer quantized Caroli-de Gennes-Matricon states. Notably, MZMs are observed in all measured vortices, and their number can be tuned by magnetic field and the size of Bi$_{2}$Te$_{3}$ islands. These results establish stoichiometric 1QL-Bi$_{2}$Te$_{3}$/FeTe as a patterned superconducting interface that enables isolated and few-vortex Majorana configurations, providing a controlled platform for fuse MZMs.



[197]. [*A Full-Spectrum Theory of Moir\'e Superlattices*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17749 "A Full-Spectrum Theory of Moir\'e Superlattices")

Tong Liu (Shenzhen Institute for Quantum Science and Engineering, Southern University of Science and Technology, Shenzhen, China, Department of Physics, Hong Kong University of Science and Technology, Hong Kong, China), X. R. Wang (School of Science and Engineering, The Chinese University of Hong Kong, Shenzhen, China), Jiansheng Wu (Shenzhen Institute for Quantum Science and Engineering, Southern University of Science and Technology, Shenzhen, China)

arXiv:2610.17749v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Conventional theoretical descriptions of moir\'e superlattices, epitomized by the Bistritzer--MacDonald continuum model, are fundamentally constrained by low-energy Dirac cone approximations and first-shell interlayer coupling truncations. While successful for small-angle twisted bilayer graphene near charge neutrality, these frameworks fail to provide universal analytical solutions for strained lattices, high-energy band behaviors, or arbitrary commensurate geometries. Here, we establish a unified, approximation-free analytical formalism for generalized moir\'e systems based on three pillars: (i) a rigorous two-dimensional-to-one-dimensional supercell cyclic mapping, (ii) exact block-circulant diagonalization of intralayer Hamiltonians, and (iii) quasi-degenerate L\"owdin perturbation theory centered on arbitrary energy degeneracy points across the full spectrum. Unlike traditional schemes anchored to the Dirac point, our approach enables systematic perturbative expansions around any band crossing, unifying the characterization of low-energy flat bands with high-energy degeneracies. We demonstrate that this framework perfectly recovers the Bistritzer--MacDonald model in the low-energy limit while extending its reach to heterostrained systems and high-energy regimes. By incorporating high-order interlayer coupling shells, we reveal an intrinsic scale self-similarity in moir\'e lattices and predict the emergence of higher-order magic angles and tunable flat bands driven by secondary degeneracies. This work eliminates the long-standing lack of universal analytical tools for diverse moir\'e platforms, providing a full-spectrum theoretical foundation for band engineering and the exploration of exotic correlated and topological states in van der Waals heterostructures.



[198]. [*Hydrogen-Bond Density Drives the Order-Disorder Phase Transition from Orientational to Vibrational in Polyalcohol Plastic Crystals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17774 "Hydrogen-Bond Density Drives the Order-Disorder Phase Transition from Orientational to Vibrational in Polyalcohol Plastic Crystals")

Ares Sanuy, Carlos Escorihuela-Sayalero, Pol Lloveras, Mar\'ia del Barrio, Hassan Allouchi, Josep-Llu\'is Tamarit, Luis Carlos Pardo, Claudio Cazorla

arXiv:2610.17774v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Neopentane-derived plastic crystals undergo order-disorder phase transitions accompanied by exceptionally large entropy changes, making them attractive solid-state materials for thermal energy storage and barocaloric cooling. Yet it remains unclear whether the microscopic nature of this entropy change is an intrinsic property of each compound or can be understood generally and tuned systematically through molecular design. Here, we use classical molecular dynamics simulations of neopentyl glycol (NPG), pentaglycerine (PG), and pentaerythritol (PE), validated against experimental transition temperatures, enthalpy changes, and phase diagrams, to show that the character of the phase-transition entropy undergoes a complete inversion as the hydrogen-bond connectivity of the molecule increases: orientational disordering dominates in NPG, whereas lattice vibrations dominate in PE and become remarkably robust against pressure. PG occupies a balanced intermediate regime in which the orientational, conformational, and vibrational channels contribute comparably. This inversion reflects two coupled microscopic mechanisms: a growing low-frequency gap between the phonon densities of states of the ordered and disordered phases as the hydrogen-bond network densifies, and an increase in molecular symmetry that progressively suppresses orientational disordering. Because the vibrational channel is substantially less sensitive to pressure than the orientational one, hydrogen-bond density emerges as a practical, chemically accessible design handle for tailoring not only the magnitude but also the pressure robustness of the caloric response in hydrogen-bonded plastic crystals, with direct implications for the search for new barocaloric and thermal-energy-storage materials.



[199]. [*Skin phase transition of a skyrmion in a ferromagnetic superfluid*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17796 "Skin phase transition of a skyrmion in a ferromagnetic superfluid")

Wooyoung Yun, Se Kwon Kim, Hiromitsu Takeuchi

arXiv:2610.17796v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Compared with a scalar Bose-Einstein condensate (BEC), a spinor BEC supports richer phenomena because of its internal spin degrees of freedom, including topological solitons such as domain walls or skyrmions. Here, we theoretically reveal three distinct axisymmetric soliton structures formed as a circular domain wall in a spin-1 BEC, connected within fixed winding numbers. We refer to these structures as the broken-axisymmetry (BA)-skin, antiferromagnetic (AF)-skin and normal-core phases. Their occurrence is controlled by the two dimensionless parameters in the absence of linear Zeeman coefficient $p=0$: $q/\mu$, the ratio of the quadratic Zeeman coefficient to the chemical potential, and $c_s/c_n$, the ratio of spin interaction to the density interaction. The BA-skin and AF-skin phases exhibit local easy-plane magnetic order and local easy-plane spin-nematic order, respectively, while the normal-core phase is similar to a doubly quantized vortex analogous to that of a scalar BEC with a density depletion at the center. We obtain semi-analytical estimates of selected transition boundaries separating these phases by analyzing the Bogoliubov-de Gennes equation and also numerically confirm them by solving the Gross-Pitaevskii equation. We estimate the skyrmion radius using a hydrostatic approximation and compare the result with numerical solutions. We envision our results on skyrmions in a spin-1 BEC can trigger further investigation on novel soliton structures in a magnetic superfluid.



[200]. [*Proof of the exact correlation-length exponent $\nu = \frac{\sqrt[3]{2}}{2}$ and algebraic relations for $\beta$ and $\gamma$ in the 3D Ising model*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17797 "Proof of the exact correlation-length exponent $\nu = \frac{\sqrt[3]{2}}{2}$ and algebraic relations for $\beta$ and $\gamma$ in the 3D Ising model")

Aleksandr Liivand

arXiv:2610.17797v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Based on our previous results regarding critical exponent estimates for the 3D Ising model and scaling relations [1], we have proven that the exact value of the correlation-length exponent is $\frac{\sqrt[3]{2}}{2}$. This is in very close agreement with the most precise numerical estimates currently available. Using a group theoretical approach and some propositions of constructive geometry, we derived an exact construction of algebraic expressions for the critical exponents $\gamma$ and $\beta$. We proved that these expressions depend solely on the exact value of $\nu\,$.



[201]. [*Learning from fracture: Teaching language models to design through physical experience*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17804 "Learning from fracture: Teaching language models to design through physical experience")

Bo Ni

arXiv:2610.17804v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: How can language models learn to optimize physical designs through experience? We introduce FracLearn, an experience-abstraction-generalization framework evaluated through a deliberately unfamiliar, Tetris-inspired fracture game. The model first proposes architectures and examines simulated fracture responses to accumulate experience. Abstraction organizes these observations into conditional principles linking reinforcement placement, crack evolution and load transfer, with supporting evidence and failure conditions. Generalization challenges the model to combine these principles in new architectures and surpass the earlier design record. Campaigns demonstrate progressive refinement and recovery through hypothesis revision, yielding a best composite score 11.0% above the experience-stage record. Fracture analyses connect improved performance to crack deflection and temporary trapping as toughening mechanisms, alongside greater residual load capacity. Learning occurs through external knowledge and decisions, without model-weight updates. This approach offers a route to scientific agents that turn computational experiments into reusable physical knowledge for materials discovery and engineering design.



[202]. [*Polarons in Lead-Halide Perovskites: What the Structural Evidence Does and Does Not Establish*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17809 "Polarons in Lead-Halide Perovskites: What the Structural Evidence Does and Does Not Establish")

Inhee Maeng, Young Mi Lee, Jinwoo Park, Seung Jae Oh, Min-Cherl Jung

arXiv:2610.17809v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Photogenerated carriers in lead-halide perovskites are widely believed to move as large polarons, whose accompanying lattice distortion is thought to screen the carrier from charged defects. Three recent findings call this picture into question. First, time-resolved structural probes now measure the photoinduced lattice distortion directly; yet calculations show that thermal motion of the lattice alone can raise the carrier effective mass-an increase previously taken as evidence for polarons-so that it remains unclear whether the lattice distortion around a carrier is really a polaron. Second, the carrier-lattice coupling strengthens as the material is thinned from 3D to 2D. In the two main families of 2D perovskites, Ruddlesden-Popper and Dion-Jacobson, the carrier couples to the lattice through different channels, and their contrasting behavior is set by which coupling channel dominates rather than by lattice stiffness alone. Third, the first direct test of defect tolerance does not point to polaron screening; its authors attribute defect tolerance to the soft, disordered lattice. New transport data show that polaron screening reduces scattering between carriers, but not that it protects carriers from defects. Polaron formation, even if established, therefore does not establish polarons as the cause of defect tolerance. Separating the two requires measuring the lattice distortion as a function of excitation density, over a range spanning the low densities of a solar cell under sunlight and the much higher densities used in most ultrafast experiments. On this basis, we propose experiments that can clarify how photogenerated carriers behave in lead-halide perovskites.



[203]. [*Orthoexciton-to-paraexciton conversion rate in Cu$_2$O at sub-Kelvin temperatures*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17812 "Orthoexciton-to-paraexciton conversion rate in Cu$_2$O at sub-Kelvin temperatures")

Yusuke Morita, Yukihiro Akiyama, Yuki Haita, Yuga Hirota, Kosuke Yoshioka

arXiv:2610.17812v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We measured the conversion rate from 1s orthoexcitons to 1s paraexcitons in Cu$_2$O below 1 K using a dilution refrigerator. To determine the conversion rate, we performed time-resolved luminescence spectroscopy of 1s orthoexcitons and 1s paraexcitons confined in a strain-induced trap potential. From the time evolution of the ${\Gamma}$$_3$$^{-}$-LO-phonon-assisted luminescence of 1s orthoexcitons, we obtained a conversion rate of $5.9 \times 10^7$ s${^{-1}}$ at a mixing-chamber temperature of 64 mK under an applied stress of 2.2 kbar. Between 64 and 800 mK, the conversion rate depends only weakly on temperature, and it is less than half of the value of $1.6 \times 10^8$ s${^{-1}}$ reported at 2 K under the same applied stress. The conversion rate therefore continues to decrease below 2 K, contrary to the previous expectation that it becomes nearly constant below about 1.7 K. We discuss these results in terms of the momentum distribution of orthoexcitons, which does not reach equilibrium with the lattice during the conversion at these low temperatures. These findings call for a theory that accounts for both the small magnitude of the conversion rate and its weak temperature dependence below 1 K.



[204]. [*Colossal Spin--Lattice--Charge Coupling in Noncollinear Ferrimagnetic NbMnX$_2$ ($X$ = S, Se, Te) Monolayers*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17849 "Colossal Spin--Lattice--Charge Coupling in Noncollinear Ferrimagnetic NbMnX$_2$ ($X$ = S, Se, Te) Monolayers")

Peng Wang, Nanshu Liu, Hongkuan Yuan, Xue Jiang, Jijun Zhao

arXiv:2610.17849v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Noncollinear ferrimagnets combine a net magnetic moment with internal spin degrees of freedom, creating opportunities to couple magnetic, mechanical, thermal, and electronic responses. Using first-principles calculations, symmetry analysis, atomistic spin simulations, and transport modeling, we identify stable NbMnX$_2$ ($X$ = S, Se, Te) monolayers with noncollinear ferrimagnetic ground states and Curie temperatures up to 313 K. Competing exchange, Dzyaloshinskii--Moriya interactions, and magnetic anisotropy produce field-responsive canted states that couple strongly to the lattice and charge carriers. Magnetization rotation changes the elastic modulus of NbMnTe$_2$ by up to 36\%, while lattice entropy substantially enhances the near-room-temperature magnetocaloric response of NbMnS$_2$ and NbMnSe$_2$. In NbMnSe$_2$, rotation restores a mirror-protected crossing, continuously closes an approximately 0.13 eV gap, and produces a conductivity-based anisotropic magnetoresistance ratio up to 10,000\% at 100 K. The combined results establish noncollinear NbMnX$_2$ monolayers as a multifunctional platform for two-dimensional spintronics, nanomechanics, and thermal control.



[205]. [*ARES: a plane-wave density-functional-theory code in Rust*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17854 "ARES: a plane-wave density-functional-theory code in Rust")

Zehan Liu, Qian Wang, Xiangyu Zheng, Jiaxiang Li, Chuntian Wu, Yongshuo Chen, Xuecheng Shao, Qiang Xu, Yu Xie, Yanming Ma

arXiv:2610.17854v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: ARES (Ab initio atomic mateRial modEling Software at JLU) is a plane-wave density-functional-theory (DFT) code written entirely in Rust as a layered 22-crate workspace. It supports norm-conserving pseudopotentials and the projector augmented-wave (PAW) method with the local-density approximation (LDA) and Perdew-Burke-Ernzerhof (PBE) functionals, {\Gamma}-only and explicit k-point sampling, smearing and mixing, forces, stress, relaxation, band structures, densities of states (DOS) and dispersion corrections; execution is serial or MPI-distributed over k-point pools, band groups and plane-wave rows. The code's distinguishing engineering choice is its validation apparatus. Every numerical component is checked against Quantum ESPRESSO driven from the same physical and numerical inputs, and the comparison is organized as numbered fixtures kept in the repository whose reference data is regenerated by a script instead of being captured once. On six materials spanning a semiconductor, three metals and two two-dimensional systems, band energies within one electronvolt of the Fermi level agree with the reference to a median below 0.4 meV. The pseudopotentials the code reads are validated against all-electron reference data as well: the median delta factor is 0.696 meV/atom over all 65 paired elements. Restrictions are stated with the capabilities: two functionals, no spin-polarized or spin-orbit path, a stress implementation with an explicit scope, and no accelerator execution.



[206]. [*Imaging atomic-scale surface structure and superconducting gap of the misfit layered compound (SnS)1.17NbS2*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17879 "Imaging atomic-scale surface structure and superconducting gap of the misfit layered compound (SnS)1.17NbS2")

Dongming Zhao, Shamarita Deb, Lichen Ji, Souvik Das, Paul Simon, Ra\'ul Cardoso-Gil, Steffen Wirth, Walter Schnelle, Sushmita Chandra, Jian-Feng Ge, Claudia Felser

arXiv:2610.17879v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Superlattices of van der Waals-coupled two-dimensional materials offer a platform for engineering electronic band structure and fostering emergent quantum states. Misfit layered compounds provide naturally assembled superlattices, in which crystallographically distinct two-dimensional subsystems coexist within a single bulk crystal. Therein, the presence of both commensurate and incommensurate lattice directions introduces structural modulation, which might alter real-space electronic orders such as superconductivity. Here, we investigate the atomic-scale surface structure and local superconductivity of the misfit layered compound (SnS)1.17NbS2 using structural characterization, electrical transport, and low-temperature scanning tunnelling microscopy (STM). While X-ray diffraction and cross-sectional scanning transmission electron microscopy confirm the bulk misfit structure, STM resolves large SnS- and NbS2-terminated terraces, where the surface atomic registry differs from the bulk misfit structure. Furthermore, tunnelling spectroscopy at 2 K reveals nearly identical superconducting gaps on both terminations, and the spatial variation of the gap shows no visible modulation that corresponds to the misfit lattice. These results establish an atomic-scale picture of the surface structure and superconducting states of this naturally assembled misfit heterostructure.



[207]. [*Conformal soft modes at elastic instabilities*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17920 "Conformal soft modes at elastic instabilities")

Lars Franke, Nick Sander, Markus Garst

arXiv:2610.17920v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In case a crystal is close to a continuous elastic phase transition, pronounced critical fluctuations emerge. We show that on the level of linear elasticity theory the displacement vector for these critical modes obeys a condition reminiscent of the conformal Killing equation. For an isostructural instability, four critical modes arise corresponding to one dilation and three special conformal transformations. At crystal symmetry-breaking instabilities, the critical fluctuations include a continuum of modes even for crystals with finite size. For certain cases, these comprise the conformal transformations associated with two-dimensional Minkowski space. These results have implications for critical elastic phenomena as well as for extremal mechanical metamaterials.



[208]. [*WannierSTM: Efficient superconducting scanning-probe spectroscopy from maximally localized Wannier functions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17943 "WannierSTM: Efficient superconducting scanning-probe spectroscopy from maximally localized Wannier functions")

Oscar Moreno Segura, Dorye L. Esteras, Andrei Shumilin, Gonzalo Rivero-Carracedo, Riku Tuovinen, Jos\'e J. Baldov\'i

arXiv:2610.17943v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We present a Python-based program that combines maximally localized Wannier functions-based first-principles electronic structure with the nonequilibrium Green's function formalism to enable efficient simulations of scanning tunneling microscopy (STM). The workflow builds upon standard density functional theory packages to construct compact transport Hamiltonians that preserve the properties of realistic materials. Superconductivity is incorporated via an effective pairing potential within the Nambu-spinor formalism, while coupling to the STM tip and substrate electrodes is treated using the wide-band limit approximation. Within this formulation, transport quantities such as current-voltage characteristics and differential conductance are evaluated in a closed analytical form. This avoids explicit numerical energy integrations and enables rapid spectroscopic calculations over broad parameter spaces. The framework provides a unified and computationally efficient approach for modeling superconducting hybrid nanostructures with spin-orbit coupling, magnetic textures, and spin polarization. Representative applications in spin chains, two-dimensional and molecular magnets on a superconductor demonstrate its robust capabilities for both simple model systems and realistic ab initio molecular structures relevant to modern STM experiments.



[209]. [*Thermal stability and structural ordering of thick Co$_2$MnAl films grown on MgO and Si substrates*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17955 "Thermal stability and structural ordering of thick Co$_2$MnAl films grown on MgO and Si substrates")

David Robles-Cuenca, Juan Antonio Gonz\'alez, Juan Pedro Andr\'es, Miguel \'Angel Arranz, Mohamed Salaheldeen, Ricardo L\'opez Ant\'on

arXiv:2610.17955v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: While most studies on Co$2$MnAl (CMA) focus on the ultrathin regime for spintronic junctions, applications scaling with magnetic volume--such as Anomalous Nernst Effect (ANE) energy harvesters, integrated power inductors, and magnetic MEMS--require significantly thicker films. In this work, 750 nm thick CMA films were grown on MgO (001) and Si substrates to establish a roadmap for their thermal integration. Structural analysis reveals that B2 ordering is optimized on MgO at 600 $^\circ$C, which is important for functional devices. In contrast, samples on Si substrates exhibit a disorder transition toward the A2 phase, closely linked to diffusion-driven degradation processes. Above 500 $^\circ$C, Si interdiffusion into the Heusler layer triggers the formation of interfacial silicides and Kirkendall voids, leading to microcracking, electrical resistivity jumps, and a final fully disordered A2 phase. Despite the Mn-poor stoichiometry (Co$2.25$Mn$0.7$Al$1.05$), the films maintain a robust saturation magnetization of ~600 emu/cm$^3$, consistent with recent theoretical models for off-stoichiometric Heusler alloys. These results define the processing windows for integrating thick CMA layers into multifunctional CMOS-compatible platforms.



[210]. [*Field-free Magnetoacoustic Nonreciprocity via Exchange-bias Engineering*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17979 "Field-free Magnetoacoustic Nonreciprocity via Exchange-bias Engineering")

Weiqi Du, Mingyuan Ma, Zheng Sun, Chong Chen, Shixuan Liang, Deqi Tao, Shihai An, Yifan Fu, Sulei Fu, Feng Pan, Cheng Song

arXiv:2610.17979v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Nonreciprocal wave propagation is essential for directional signal processing and isolation. Magnetoacoustic systems are attractive platforms for nonreciprocal devices because of their small footprint and passive operation. However, pronounced nonreciprocity typically requires an external bias field,limiting device miniaturization and integration. Here, we demonstrate field-free magnetoacoustic nonreciprocity in an IrMn/FeGaB/Cu/FeGaB multilayer integrated with a shear-horizontal-wave delay line. Exchange-bias engineering stabilizes a remanent magnetic state with broken time-reversal and spatial-inversion symmetries while tailoring the spin-wave dispersion to achieve resonant magnetoacoustic coupling at zero field. The normalized isolation reaches 21 dB/mm, with its sign programmable by the field-sweep history. Further numerical optimization predicts a zero-field normalized isolation of 91 dB/mm by increasing the dynamical magnetic asymmetry. Our work establishes exchange-bias engineering as a promising route toward compact RF isolators and circulators and as a general strategy for realizing magnetoacoustic devices without external magnetic fields.



[211]. [*Direct view of exciton and carrier dynamics in twisted bilayer graphene*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17984 "Direct view of exciton and carrier dynamics in twisted bilayer graphene")

Gianmarco Gatti, Anders S. Mortensen, Zhihao Jiang, Alfred J. H. Jones, Gesa-R. Siemann, Thomas S. Nielsen, S{\o}ren Ulstrup, Naina Kushwaha, Bruce Weaver, Yu Zhang, Emma Springate, Charlotte E. Sanders, Vibha Reddy, Ulrich Starke, Philip Hofmann

arXiv:2610.17984v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Twisted bilayer graphene (tBLG) on H-intercalated SiC (twist angle of $9 \pm 1^{\circ}$) is studied by position\mbox{-}, time\mbox{-}, and angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES). Both graphene layers are hole doped, with a Dirac-point offset of 30~meV. Time-resolved ARPES near the van Hove singularity reveals a spectral feature within the hybridization-induced band gap, together with excited electrons in the conduction band. The in-gap feature exhibits distinct dynamics, and its binding energy and $k$-space location are consistent with the proposed non-hydrogenic ``ghost-Fano'' exciton arising from destructive interference between two nearly degenerate excitonic channels. Near the Dirac points, the twist separates the largely layer-polarized Dirac cones in momentum space, providing direct layer selectivity. We find markedly different peak electronic temperatures in the two graphene layers, even though their subsequent cooling dynamics are very similar. We attribute this to an ultrafast interlayer redistribution of electronic energy, driven by an interlayer electric field that arises from the different doping in the layers.



[212]. [*Knowledge-Guided Construction of Machine Learning Potentials for Reactive Behavior in Energetic Materials*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17987 "Knowledge-Guided Construction of Machine Learning Potentials for Reactive Behavior in Energetic Materials")

Guanchen Dong, Huanrong Liu, Lin Wang, Yuxuan Chen, Zhengyu Tu, Qing-an Li, Bin Xu, Jian Li, Shan Zhang, Xiaotong Li, Linghua Tan, Guangcheng Yang, Jun Kang, Rui Su, Pengfei Guan

arXiv:2610.17987v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Energetic materials involve multiple interactions, making reliable machine-learning potentials (MLPs) difficult to construct without extensive datasets. Here, we enhance the ability of the neural network interatomic potential (NNAP) to describe molecular systems using sphmulti, a refined spherical-harmonic basis set that combines extra radial basis with element-specific chemical weighting in each radial channel. We further integrate hyperactive learning, farthest-point sampling, and iterative sufficiency criteria with knowledge-guided key events, enabling the NNAP to retain essential information for describing the material system. For 3,3'-diamino-4,4'-azoxyfurazan, the resulting NNAP is trained on only 13,680 configurations yet captures key properties, including the isomerization barrier, pyrolysis activation energy, and shock Hugoniot behavior. NNAP-driven molecular dynamics simulations further reveal that pyrolysis proceeds through furazan-ring opening followed by chain reactions, whereas shock loading induces anisotropic hydrogen transfer driven by interlayer structural deformations. This work enables efficient and reliable MLPs for energetic materials.



[213]. [*Hydrogen partial pressure as a key parameter in the reactive sputtering of photochromic YHO thin films*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18068 "Hydrogen partial pressure as a key parameter in the reactive sputtering of photochromic YHO thin films")

E. Strods, M. Zubkins, E. Letko, V. Vibornijs, A. Sarakovskis, J. Purans

arXiv:2610.18068v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Oxygen-containing yttrium hydride (YHO) thin films were synthesised by reactive magnetron sputtering over a wide range of hydrogen-to-argon gas flow ratios (H2/Ar) during yttrium hydride deposition, followed by post-oxidation in air. The deposition process was monitored in situ by plasma optical emission spectroscopy. X-ray diffraction reveals a strong dependence of crystallographic texture on the H2/Ar ratio: highly <100>-textured films formed at low H2/Ar ratios progressively evolve into more polycrystalline microstructures with slightly reduced crystallite size as the hydrogen content increases. The optical transmittance gradually decreases from approximately 85 % to 70 % as the H2/Ar ratio is raised to 0.6, followed by a sharp drop at higher hydrogen contents. The photochromic contrast of 690-820 nm thick YHO films, measured after 10 min of illumination with a solar simulator, decreases from 14 % to 7 % as the H2/Ar ratio increases from 0.08 to 0.60. Thinner films (360-490 nm) exhibit a similar but less pronounced trend. Bleaching kinetics, analysed using a double-exponential model, indicate faster recovery, with effective bleaching time constants of approximately 1-15 min for films deposited at H2/Ar < 0.25. X-ray photoelectron spectroscopy reveals higher oxygen incorporation in films deposited at lower H2/Ar ratios, deviating from the commonly reported correlation between oxygen content and photochromic response in YHO. These results demonstrate that low hydrogen partial pressure during reactive sputtering is advantageous for achieving higher optical transparency, enhanced photochromic contrast, and faster bleaching kinetics in YHO thin films.



[214]. [*Evolution of dense liquid marbles under light tapping*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18074 "Evolution of dense liquid marbles under light tapping")

Amaelle Sprocq, Th\'eo Dhoudain, Alexis Duchesne, Guillaume Lagubeau

arXiv:2610.18074v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Liquid marbles interface is composed by a dense network of grains trapped by capillarity at their surface. The mix of capillarity and granular character make their interfaces remarkable, allowing non wetting, high mobility and strong robustness which are a basis for numerous applications. A canonical experiment showing out of equilibrium nature of granular material is the so called tapping experiment, where a dry granular medium under vertical vibration reorganize an evolve toward a denser final state function of the vibration amplitude In this paper we explore the out of equilibrium nature of densely covered liquid marbles by measuring their shape, compaction and stresses evolution under vertical vibration. We show that liquid marble do indeed reorganize when submitted to taps, leading to higher internal pressure and lower effective surface tension.



[215]. [*Tellurium trioxide as an n-type wide-gap semiconductor with ultrahigh work functions from first principles*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18076 "Tellurium trioxide as an n-type wide-gap semiconductor with ultrahigh work functions from first principles")

Tianwei Wang, Teruya Nagafuji, Kota Hanzawa, Hidenori Hiramatsu, Fumiyasu Oba

arXiv:2610.18076v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The exploration of next-generation wide-gap semiconductors with extreme work functions is highly desirable for the continued advancement of electronic and optoelectronic devices. By using first principles calculations, we show that \b{eta}-TeO3, with a wide band gap exceeding 3 eV, emerges as a promising n-type semiconductor exhibiting ultrahigh work functions of up to ~8 eV. Intentionally or unintentionally incorporated interstitial H, Li, Na, Cu, Mg, and Zn impurities and substitutional F impurities are identified as shallow donors, whereas O vacancies act as minor carrier-compensating defects that locally trap electrons at neighbouring Te sites. The work function is evaluated across a series of low-index nonpolar surfaces. The presence of ordered O vacancies is energetically favoured on these surfaces and tends to reduce the corresponding work functions. These findings underscore the significance of precise control over the O chemical potential to suppress surface O-vacancy formation, thereby preserving the exceptional electronic performance of \b{eta}-TeO3 associated with ultrahigh work functions. Collectively, the results highlight the potential of \b{eta}-TeO3 for hole injection/extraction/transport layers as well as transparent electrodes for applications such as light-emitting devices and photovoltaic cells, while providing useful guidance on carrier doping and surface tuning for these practical applications through elucidating the effects of the point-defect and surface configurations on the electronic properties.



[216]. [*Organisation and aggregation of calcium carbonate ions in water via molecular simulation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18128 "Organisation and aggregation of calcium carbonate ions in water via molecular simulation")

Elizaveta Sidler, Rita S Dias, Raffaela Cabriolu

arXiv:2610.18128v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Crystallisation of calcium carbonate (CaCO$_3$) in aqueous solutions is essential for carbon dioxide sequestration and the sustainable production of calcium carbonate materials, which are widely used in industry and increasingly explored for novel applications. While crystallisation is often analysed within the framework of classical nucleation theory, CaCO$_3$ and other ionic crystals often form through amorphous precursor phases, motivating the need for alternative descriptions. In this work, we investigate the relationship between the formation of the first CaCO$_3$ coordination sphere, a fundamental unit of local ordering, and ionic aggregation using two complementary simulation approaches: on-the-fly probability-enhanced sampling (OPES) and asynchronous and infinite-swap replica exchange transition interface sampling ($\infty$RETIS). OPES was used to explore free-energy landscapes, whereas $\infty$RETIS was used to determine reaction kinetics. We studied systems at two levels of supersaturation, between which the formation of the first coordination sphere exhibited distinct kinetic behaviour. These differences were found to be linked to concentration-dependent ionic aggregation. At lower concentration, aggregation remains limited, whereas higher concentration promotes extensive spinodal-like aggregation. For both concentrations, the first coordination sphere forms within the pre-formed clusters through one of two distinct pathways: either within a cluster close to the minimum size required for its formation, or within larger amorphous aggregates whose size and structure depend on concentration. The coexistence of these pathways at both levels of supersaturation reveals the multifaceted nature of early-stage CaCO$_3$ crystallisation and highlights the importance of differentiating ionic aggregation and organisation.



[217]. [*Local-resolvent formulation and assessment of linear-scaling Kohn-Sham calculations*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18137 "Local-resolvent formulation and assessment of linear-scaling Kohn-Sham calculations")

Albert Linda

arXiv:2610.18137v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Kohn-Sham density-functional theory is expensive because it needs orbitals that extend over the whole system. For insulators this can be avoided by using only the diagonal of the one-electron resolvent, $g(\mathbf{r},z)=\langle\mathbf{r}|(z-\hat h)^{-1}|\mathbf{r}\rangle$, at a few complex energies $z$. We call the scheme local-resolvent density-functional theory (LRes-DFT). The density and the band energy are linear in $g$, so neither orbitals nor a kinetic-energy functional are needed. The complex energies are $M$ equally spaced points on a circle that encloses the occupied levels, offset by half a spacing so that no point lies on the real axis. Summing the resolvent over them is equivalent to occupying each level with $1/(1+u^M)$, where $u$ is the level's energy measured from the circle's center in units of its radius. This is a smooth step that is near 1 inside the circle and near 0 outside, and it stays between 0 and 1 for every $M$. If the circle moves with the chemical potential, forces at fixed electron number are exact. Gauss-Legendre and Zolotarev rules need fewer points when the gap is small. In three dimensions we use eigenpairs of small windows around each molecule instead of resolvents. This is divide-and-conquer (DC) with a different occupation function, and it is as accurate as DC with Fermi-Dirac occupations. For H$_2$ molecular crystals of up to 1024 electrons it agrees with Kohn-Sham to $2.5\times10^{-7}$ Ha per electron. On one core it is slower than dense diagonalization below about 500 electrons and 3.5 times faster at 1024 electrons. It loses accuracy for disordered or compressed systems and for diffuse basis functions.



[218]. [*Effects of the choice of the exchange-correlation functional on high-temperature superconductivity predictions in covalent-cage M(BN)5 family*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18171 "Effects of the choice of the exchange-correlation functional on high-temperature superconductivity predictions in covalent-cage M(BN)5 family")

Zhongyu Wan, Guo-Hua Zhong, Cheng-wei Ju, Ruiqin Zhang, Hai-Qing Lin

arXiv:2610.18171v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The recent experimental synthesis of covalent cage superconductors has ignited hopes for ambient-pressure high-temperature superconductivity, while the computational strategy for discovering new materials poses challenges for accurately estimating superconducting properties. Here, we quantified the response of electron-phonon coupling (EPC) properties in M(BN)5 superconductors to different exchange-correlation (XC) effects using high-throughput first-principles calculations. We found that the metallic covalent bonds responsible for superconductivity exhibit anomalously high sensitivity to functional selection uncertainty, distinguishing them from conventional superconductors mediated by metallic bonding. The widely used PBE functional systematically overestimates lattice volumes, inducing phonon softening, enhanced EPC, and consequent Peierls instability. In contrast, PBEsol, which is more accurate through enhanced treatment of short-range interactions, eliminates imaginary frequencies in Rb(BN)5 that appear at the PBE level, yielding an outstanding superconducting critical temperature (Tc) of 113 K at ambient pressure and 138 K at 2 GPa. Enhanced electron localization and short-range correlation in covalent bonds manifest through dual mechanisms: phonon hardening at the lattice scale and band widening at the electronic scale, which collectively suppress Peierls distortion and delocalize antibonding states. Pressure-dependent analysis reveals that compression attenuates phonon discrepancies between XC functionals, whereas electronic properties remain notably sensitive to XC-induced perturbations of fine electronic bands near the Fermi level.



[219]. [*Non-Hermitian Statistical Geometry*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18175 "Non-Hermitian Statistical Geometry")

Xi Hu, Yan-Qiong Ma, Su-Peng Kou

arXiv:2610.18175v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Particle exchange in two dimensions gives rise to quantum statistics beyond the boson-fermion dichotomy, with central roles in fractional quantum Hall physics, topological quantum computation, and strongly correlated quantum matter. In conventional topological phases, microscopic exchange geometry is compressed into rigid braid and fusion data. We develop a theory of non-Hermitian statistical geometry in which relaxing Hermiticity softens these exchange data into a larger complex space of spectral and relative-geometric degrees of freedom. For two adjacent braid generators, a single projective exchange spectrum is insufficient: their relative eigenline geometry supplies an additional gauge-invariant modulus. On the generic irreducible two-channel branch, braid consistency locks these coordinates through an exact spectrum-geometry relation. Using Riesz projectors and Kato transport, we realize this geometry in a five-arm Kitaev-Majorana network and test spectral and relative-geometric responses in an independent two-dimensional four-vortex Kitaev model. Finite-time dynamics tests the exchange operation, microscopic fusion sewing tests its composition across fusion descriptions, and matched-spectrum controls isolate the role of relative geometry. Nonzero projective Kato curvature shows that braid-consistent, fusion-coherent exchange can depend on the control path. Non-Hermiticity thus exposes exchange geometry, braid consistency re-locks it, fusion coherence further constrains it, and projective flatness determines when geometric exchange becomes intrinsically topological.



[220]. [*High-throughput screening of Heusler alloys for thermoelectrics combining machine-learned densities of states and first-principles transport*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18179 "High-throughput screening of Heusler alloys for thermoelectrics combining machine-learned densities of states and first-principles transport")

Shivam Randive, Ayana Ghosh, Rahul Dixit, Himanshu Pandey

arXiv:2610.18179v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Thermoelectrics turn waste heat into electricity, but finding new ones requires transport calculations too costly for whole databases. Searches therefore choose what to calculate from the electronic density of states (DOS), increasingly predicted by machine learning, yet such choices are rarely tested. We test them on all 106,235 Heusler structures in the DXMag database, screening 51,257 on density-functional spectra and 54,978 on neural-network predictions, and judging both by 3,625 band-structure and transport calculations. A DOS-derived Seebeck indicator ranks most compounds passing every transport and stability test within the top 1% of their population, and ten of the eleven established half-Heusler thermoelectrics calculated pass. Predicted spectra lose most band gaps, unnoticed by spectral-similarity metrics, yet still rank passing compounds near the top. The screen certifies 80 computational candidates, 61 without reported measurements. Computed or predicted, the DOS can choose what to calculate; only a band structure shows whether a gap exists.



[221]. [*Orientation-Resolved Nanoindentation of Single Crystal Ti-15Mo: Inverted Elastic Anisotropy and Yield-Onset Behavior Beyond Simple CRSS Predictions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18181 "Orientation-Resolved Nanoindentation of Single Crystal Ti-15Mo: Inverted Elastic Anisotropy and Yield-Onset Behavior Beyond Simple CRSS Predictions")

Kidus Abebe, David Gaetano, Aurelia Moriyama-Gurish, Jana \v{S}milauerov\'a, Yanfei Gao, Udo D. Schwarz, Amit Datye

arXiv:2610.18181v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Ti-15Mo (wt%) is a well-established $\beta$-stabilized alloy whose mechanical response is strongly governed by phase stability and deformation mechanisms that operate at the single-grain scale. Because these intragranular mechanisms are intrinsically anisotropic, understanding the orientation dependence of the elastic and plastic response is critical to the texture-engineered processing of wrought components. Here, we quantify the nanomechanical anisotropy of single-crystal Ti-15Mo by combining EBSD with nanoindentation to correlate hardness, indentation modulus, strain rate sensitivity, and indentation yield response with the crystallographic surface normal near $\langle111\rangle,\langle124\rangle,\langle011\rangle,\langle023\rangle$, and $\langle013\rangle$ directions. While the modulus anisotropy is small (~10%), contrary to the prediction for a cubic crystal with A>1, orientations near $\langle111\rangle$ exhibit the lowest moduli. Yield onset is likewise softest near $\langle111\rangle$ and spans 41%, while hardness spans only 6.6% and follows neither the modulus nor the yield ordering. The alloy also exhibits yield behavior not captured by the simple CRSS/Schmid-factor models evaluated here. Strain-rate-jump tests yield V*=18-31b$^3$, consistent with kink-pair-nucleation-controlled plasticity; based on V*, the orientations separate into two groups suggestive of glide-dominated versus twin/transformation-participating response. We reveal an inversion of the elastic anisotropy expected for a homogeneous cubic $\beta$-phase and an orientation-dependent yield-onset response that is not reproduced by the simple Schmid-factor models examined here. Retained athermal $\omega$ is proposed as the contributor because $\beta\rightarrow\omega$ collapse occurs on {111}$_\beta$ planes and may provide anelastic compliance through the reversible $\omega\rightarrow\beta$ transformation.



[222]. [*Quantitative Impact of Occupation Smearing Methods on Conventional Superconductivity: from Electronic Structure through Lattice Dynamics to Electron-Phonon Coupling*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18185 "Quantitative Impact of Occupation Smearing Methods on Conventional Superconductivity: from Electronic Structure through Lattice Dynamics to Electron-Phonon Coupling")

Zhongyu Wan

arXiv:2610.18185v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: In first-principles calculations of metallic systems, the discrete electronic mesh in the Brillouin zone necessitates a smearing function and its width (e.g., the widely used 0.02 Ry Methfessel-Paxton scheme) to enforce continuity of energy and occupation at the Fermi surface. However, the quantitative impact of such empirical parameters on phonon-mediated superconductivity has long been overlooked, leading to their habitual selection by mere convention. Here, using hexahydride CsH6 as a benchmark, we systematically investigate the magnitude and propagation mechanism of smearing-induced errors against the parameter-free optimized tetrahedron method. Long-tailed smearing delocalizes the occupation window, causing substantial Fermi-level shifts, flattening of the density of states, and hardening phonon modes, thereby weakening electron-phonon coupling. Conversely, short-tailed smearing suffers from insufficient sampling, producing density-of-states oscillations and enhanced Fermi-surface nesting; via amplified electronic screening and Peierls instabilities, this softens acoustic modes and generates imaginary frequencies, causing CsH6 to be dynamically unstable and discarded. However, the optimized tetrahedron method reveals that CsH6 is dynamically stable under ambient pressure with a remarkable superconducting transition temperature of 121 K. To prevent subjective empirical parameters from obscuring theoretically promising high-temperature superconductors, we derive a simple scaling relation for the smearing width, applicable to different smearing functions, from the free-electron-gas model, offering an objective, precise, and efficient protocol for parameter selection in high-throughput screening.



[223]. [*Unexpected stabilization of a single-$q$ charge density wave in pristine 1T-TiSe$_2$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18211 "Unexpected stabilization of a single-$q$ charge density wave in pristine 1T-TiSe$_2$")

Kimoon Higashihira Han, Dongju Hwang, Dongsin Kim, Yeojin Ahn, Jaehun Cha, Seonggeon Gim, Gyubin Lee, Mingi Jho, Chan-young Lim, Sae Hee Ryu, Chris Jozwiak, Aaron Bostwick, Eli Rotenberg, Jonathan D. Denlinger, Makoto Hashimoto, Donghui Lu, Young Woo Choi, Gil Young Cho, Yeongkwan Kim

arXiv:2610.18211v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Over the past decade, studies revisiting the classical charge-density-wave system 1T-TiSe$_2$, long understood to host the $2 \times 2$ triple-$q$ order with equivalent subcomponents, have suggested that these subcomponents may not be identical, challenging a 50-year-old paradigm. Here, we demonstrate not only that these subcomponents are non-identical but also that they develop sequentially rather than simultaneously. By tracking folded-band features, angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy reveals that a unidirectional $2 \times 1$ single-$q$ order stabilizes first, while the other two components emerge sequentially at lower temperatures, completing the triple-$q$ phase. Our free-energy analysis explains this hierarchy through repulsive coupling among the three subcomponents, as supported by first-principles calculations incorporating phonon anharmonicity. Consequently, the three subcomponents are inequivalent in both phase and amplitude. These results reconcile reported symmetry breaking and differing characteristic temperatures and establish a framework in which ostensibly simple, high-symmetry charge-density order emerges from temperature-dependent competition and cooperation among inequivalent subcomponents.



[224]. [*MINFF: A Family of Classical Force Fields for Minerals with Tunable Angle Terms*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18220 "MINFF: A Family of Classical Force Fields for Minerals with Tunable Angle Terms")

Michael Holmboe, Nathan Lavauzelle, Rogers E. Swai

arXiv:2610.18220v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Atomistic simulations of minerals are limited by the accuracy and transferability of the force field. We introduce MINFF, a force field family for layered and non-layered oxides and hydroxides of M being Si, Al, Fe, Mg, Ca, Ti, Li and F. MINFF has no M-O bond terms. Instead M-O distances follow from electrostatics and dispersion, while a single harmonic angle term on all M-O triplets controls coordination geometry. Metal charges are DDEC6 charges from reference mineral structures, and oxygen charges are assigned by nearest-neighbour charge smearing, covering basal, apical, hydroxyl and edge sites and sites adjacent to isomorphous substitutions. The oxygen Lennard-Jones parameters are based on the OPC3 water model and change by less than 5% when re-optimised per mineral, so compatibility with OPC3 and related ion-pair potentials is measured rather than assumed. Metal Lennard-Jones parameters were fitted to unit-cell metrics, bond distances and angles, both synchronously across more than 30 reference minerals (general, GMINFF) and for individual minerals (tailored, TMINFF), each at four angle force constants (0, 250, 500 and 1500 kJ/mol/rad^2). With angle terms, MINFF reproduces experimental unit-cells metrics to within 0.5% and atom-type distances and angles to within 0.7-1.4% and 0.1-0.4%, with Global Instability Index values as low as 0.11, below those of CLAYFF (0.34) and INTERFACE (0.21). A moderate angle constant reproduces the stiff in-plane and soft shear elastic constants of phyllosilicates and improves the Kaolinite librational bands, and in hydrated Na-Montmorillonite MINFF gives the lowest residual factor against the 2W 00l pattern of the models tested. The general and tailored sets at four angle stiffnesses give direct control over the transferability-fidelity trade-off. Topologies can be generated with the atom (MATLAB) and atomipy (Python) libraries and at www.atomipy.io.



[225]. [*Zeeman spin quantum geometry and intrinsic gyrotropic spin current*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18224 "Zeeman spin quantum geometry and intrinsic gyrotropic spin current")

Srijan Chatterjee, Tarun Kanti Ghosh

arXiv:2610.18224v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We uncover an intrinsic gyrotropic spin current (IGSC) driven solely by an oscillating magnetic field, arising from the spin version of Zeeman quantum geometry. Using the density matrix formalism, we derive the IGSC in the low-frequency clean limit, in terms of the Zeeman spin quantum metric and the Zeeman spin Berry curvature. The effect is studied in a minimal Rashba-Dresselhaus model that also supports intrinsic gyrotropic charge currents from the Zeeman Berry curvature. The spin and charge currents exhibit a $\pi/2$ phase shift and are found to be perpendicular to each other for certain parameter ranges. This not only enables spin current detection but also opens pure spin current channels for energy-efficient spintronics. We further observe that these currents switch directions between longitudinal and transverse relative to the applied field, depending on the relative strength of Rashba and Dresselhaus interactions.



[226]. [*Molecular-Dynamics Verification of Effective Gravity under Heat Conduction*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18250 "Molecular-Dynamics Verification of Effective Gravity under Heat Conduction")

Satoshi Uruno, Naoko Nakagawa

arXiv:2610.18250v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We test the effective-gravity prediction of global thermodynamics using standard molecular dynamics of a two-dimensional Lennard-Jones fluid. Under weak heat conduction with the lower boundary hotter than the upper boundary, the transition between liquid below gas and liquid above gas shifts from zero to finite downward gravity. Using independently measured equilibrium saturation pressures, we calculate the shift predicted by global thermodynamics and find agreement with the observed transition shift. The transition occurs at zero effective gravity, where the hydrostatic pressure difference balances the saturation-pressure difference between the two boundaries. The hysteresis response under heat conduction also agrees with the equilibrium response when plotted against effective gravity. These results establish the predictive power of global thermodynamics for nonequilibrium structure formation.



[227]. [*Coherent Phonon Generation from Prethermal Carrier Populations in a Semiconductor*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18279 "Coherent Phonon Generation from Prethermal Carrier Populations in a Semiconductor")

Mingyu Shi, Matteo Savoini, Patrick Friebel, Andrea Annunziata, Thomas Metzger, Yunpei Deng, Laura Cattaneo, Steven L. Johnson

arXiv:2610.18279v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Ultrafast laser excitation of carriers in semiconductors often results in the creation of fully symmetric coherent optical phonons, typically understood in terms of a thermalized transient carrier population.
