We test this understanding with systematic measurements and first-principles calculations of the photon-energy dependence of ultrafast-laser-driven coherent phonon generation in tellurium, using excitation wavelengths around the electronic band gap. We observe that, contrary to the expectations of models assuming a thermalized carrier population, the coherent amplitude of the fully symmetric $A_1$ mode changes only weakly when tuning wavelength across the band gap. The measured spectral dependence is instead reproduced by a transition-resolved prethermal force weighted by optical matrix elements and band- and momentum-dependent $A_1$ deformation potentials. Fitting this model to the data yields an excited transition state lifetime of a few femtoseconds. These results identify transition selectivity, rather than a thermalized total carrier density, as a key factor controlling coherent-phonon generation in semiconductors excited near a band gap.



[228]. [*Phonon-Mediated Superconductivity near Phase Boundaries in Rhombohedral Multilayer Graphene*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18286 "Phonon-Mediated Superconductivity near Phase Boundaries in Rhombohedral Multilayer Graphene")

Konstantinos Sourounis, Peter M. Oppeneer

arXiv:2610.18286v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The recent observation of superconductivity in untwisted multilayer graphene has sparked intense interest in its microscopic origin. As the displacement field and carrier density are varied, superconductivity emerges in several distinct regions with different temperature and magnetic-field responses. This behavior has been attributed to the presence of distinct electronic phases, raising the possibility that different superconducting regions originate from different microscopic mechanisms. While purely electronic pairing mechanisms have been extensively explored, an in-depth examination of phonon-mediated superconductivity is still missing. Here, we solve the coupled selfconsistent Migdal-Eliashberg equations using input from first-principles calculations and incorporating interaction-driven symmetry breaking of the electronic ground state. Varying the carrier density and displacement field, we reproduce the experimentally observed superconducting regions with comparable superconducting properties such as gap and $T_c$ and predict additional, as-yet undiscovered superconducting phases. Our findings exemplify that several superconductivity regions in rhombohedral graphene can be satisfactorily explained by phonon-mediated pairing.



[229]. [*Role of dissipation dilution in achieving high mechanical quality factors in van der Waals resonators*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18288 "Role of dissipation dilution in achieving high mechanical quality factors in van der Waals resonators")

Lukas Schleicher, Leonard Geilen, Alexander Musta, Niklas Walter, Anne Rodriguez, Philipp Bredol, Alexander W. Holleitner, Eva M. Weig

arXiv:2610.18288v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Dissipation dilution has driven a revolution in the quality factors ($Q$) of top-down nanomechanical resonators. Yet it has not been leveraged for van der Waals (vdW) materials. Here we report quality factors up to $372{,}000\pm37{,}000$ in monolayer MoS$_2$ drums at 4 K, nearly an order of magnitude beyond the previous best for any vdW material at this temperature, enabled by a clean, polymer-free exfoliation technique. We develop a dissipation-dilution model for circular drums and analyze the contributing intrinsic and extrinsic dissipation mechanisms. The MoS$_2$ monolayer exhibits a dilution factor of order $1,000$, establishing sub-nanometer-thin resonators as ultimate dilution materials. The intrinsic quality factor, however, is limited by surface losses at atomic thickness. Conversely, multilayer drums show a lower dilution factor but a high intrinsic quality factor, on par with established top-down materials of similar dimensions, both resulting from the increased thickness. A two-level-system analysis of the temperature-dependent damping confirms this bulk-versus-surface picture and provides first insight into its microscopic origin. Our results identify dissipation dilution as the governing mechanism for high-$Q$ mechanical vdW resonators. Combined with strategies such as soft clamping or stress engineering, they establish atomically thin resonators as a platform for ultrahigh-$Q$ quantum transduction and sensing.



[230]. [*A Topological Spin on Racetrack Memory*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18297 "A Topological Spin on Racetrack Memory")

Benjamin Zingsem

arXiv:2610.18297v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Magnetic racetrack memory requires localized carriers that combine rapid motion, resistance to perturbations and a detectable signal. Here we show how a 360{\deg} domain wall in a hollow ferromagnetic tube combines these properties within a single continuous magnetic shell. Two successive magnetization reversals form a bound object whose surrounding domains point in the same direction. Analytical energetics and micromagnetic simulations establish its finite separation, recovery from tested perturbations and current driven transport without microscopic Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction or a maintaining field. The texture's winding resists unwinding while radial rotation carries an energy cost, providing a specific control over stability. Relative rotation of the constituent spins accounts quantitatively for additional displacement during current onset. The wall supports motion at hundreds of metres per second, with transient velocities approaching one kilometre per second at stronger drive. A prepared seven-bit pattern retains its carriers and local readout contrast after pulsed transport and recovery. Curved-track and differential-current tests identify radial stiffness and drive uniformity as controls for intact motion. These results establish a physical basis for fast, locally distinguishable tubular magnetic bits and identify a continuous switching pathway that can be targeted by tailored excitation.



[231]. [*Transition-Metal-Induced Uniaxial Anisotropy in YFe$_3$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18303 "Transition-Metal-Induced Uniaxial Anisotropy in YFe$_3$")

M. Nur Hasan, Bruno L. Fertig, Santa Pile, Rafael M. Vieira, Alena Vishina, Alexander Kovacs, Gotthard Rieger, Stefan Denneler, Samuel J. R. Holt, Martin Lang, Swapneel Pathak, Andrea Petrocchi, Hans Fangohr, Thomas George Woodcock, Thomas Schrefl, Heike C. Herper

arXiv:2610.18303v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Achieving uniaxial magnetocrystalline anisotropy while preserving high magnetization and thermal stability remains a central challenge in the development of Fe-rich permanent magnets. YFe$_3$ is a promising candidate owing to its saturation magnetization and Curie temperature, but its intrinsic easy-plane anisotropy limits its potential for permanent-magnet applications. Here, we show that selective transition-metal substitution drives an easy-plane-to-easy-axis transition in YFe$_3$. Systematic first-principles screening identifies V, Nb, and Ta as effective dopants, with YFe$_{2.33}$Ta$_{0.67}$ exhibiting a large uniaxial magnetocrystalline anisotropy energy of 2.3~MJ/m$^{3}$. Calculated exchange interactions and atomistic Monte Carlo simulations show that the enhanced anisotropy is achieved while preserving robust ferromagnetic order and high thermal stability. Micromagnetic simulations further reveal that YFe$_{2.33}$Ta$_{0.67}$ exhibits the highest coercivity and strongest resistance to irreversible demagnetization, whereas YFe$_{2.33}$V$_{0.67}$ achieves a comparable maximum energy product at 300~K. Experimental characterization of V-alloyed YFe$_3$ shows that the YFe$_3$ phase can be retained at low nominal V concentrations (1-3 at.\%) under the employed synthesis and heat-treatment conditions, whereas higher V contents lead to increasingly multiphase samples. Concentration-dependent MAE calculations reveal a sign reversal between approximately 11 and 14~at.\% V, marking the transition from easy-plane to uniaxial easy-axis anisotropy. Consistent with this concentration dependence, electron backscatter diffraction and Kerr microscopy reveal in-plane magnetic domain structures in the experimentally accessible low-V regime. Therefore, our work offers a promising approach to tuning the magnetocrystalline anisotropy of YFe$_3$ while retaining high Curie temperatures.



[232]. [*Accurately Generalizing Deep-Learning Hamiltonian Methods to Plane-Wave Density Functional Theory*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18317 "Accurately Generalizing Deep-Learning Hamiltonian Methods to Plane-Wave Density Functional Theory")

Chuntian Wu, Jiaxiang Li, Qiang Xu, Xuecheng Shao, Congwei Xie, Yu Xie, Yanming Ma

arXiv:2610.18317v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Deep neural networks that predict density functional theory (DFT) Hamiltonians from material structures offer a route to efficient electronic-structure calculations. However, extending localized atomic-orbital (AO) Hamiltonian learning to plane-wave (PW) calculations with hybrid functionals requires a compact AO representation that preserves the effects of nonlocal operators. Here we propose a projectability-guided construction of nonorthogonal AO Hamiltonians from a small, selected set of PW eigenstates and eigenvalues. High-projectability states are retained, and selected higher-energy states complete the AO subspace, avoiding an empirical shift and its arbitrary contribution to the learning targets. Because the construction uses PW eigenstates and eigenvalues, it incorporates nonlocal contributions present in the reference calculation without explicitly reconstructing their AO matrix elements. Benchmarks for MoS2 and sodium phases show accurate reproduction of PW bands with smaller AO bases than real-space reconstruction. The resulting PBE and HSE Hamiltonians can be learned by equivariant neural networks, connecting PW reference calculations with electronic-structure prediction. This approach broadens the range of PW DFT calculations available for AO Hamiltonian learning and enables compact, accurate training targets for developing more widely applicable electronic-structure models.



[233]. [*Charge-selective topological locking and ultraslow coarsening in magnetoelastic media*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18320 "Charge-selective topological locking and ultraslow coarsening in magnetoelastic media")

Tirthankar Banerjee, Amaresh Jaiswal

arXiv:2610.18320v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We demonstrate that the symmetry of magnetoelastic coupling governs the topological organization of composite defects and can qualitatively alter phase-ordering kinetics. In a two-dimensional theory of an in-plane magnetic vector coupled to elastic strain, we find that integer magnetic vortices bind pairs of elementary elastic half-defects. Isotropic magnetostriction produces a core-induced elastic winding independent of the magnetic charge, giving defect binding without charge locking and leaving the magnetic sector with essentially XY-like coarsening. In contrast, quadrupolar magnetoelastic coupling locks magnetic and elastic defect charges: continuous rotational symmetry favors $q_\phi=q_\theta$, whereas crystalline anisotropy can reverse the locking to $q_\phi=-q_\theta$. We reveal that both locking sectors display anomalously slow vortex annihilation: defect density shows a power-law decay $t^{-2/5}$, while the average defect-velocity scales as $t^{-4/5}$. These results are consistent with a dynamic exponent $z\simeq 5$. We show that this coarsening slowdown is associated with large anisotropic elastic friction. An adiabatic elimination of the elastic field reveals that isotropic magnetostriction merely renormalizes local magnetic interactions, whereas orientational coupling induces effective nonlocal interactions between magnetic spins. Our experimentally testable results thus establish magnetoelastic coupling symmetry as a means of controlling defect topology and ordering kinetics.



[234]. [*Ionic gradients shape the spatial organization and dynamics of supramolecular polymers*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18357 "Ionic gradients shape the spatial organization and dynamics of supramolecular polymers")

Claudio Perego, Annalisa Cardellini, Andrea Tagliabue, Giovanni M. Pavan

arXiv:2610.18357v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Living supramolecular materials such as microtubules and actin filaments assemble and function in dynamic, heterogeneous environments, subject to chemical concentration gradients. Their rapid and precise response to such gradients---via aggregation, disassembly, or autonomous motion---underlies the fine spatiotemporal control typical of living systems. Reproducing such responsiveness in synthetic materials is a central challenge in materials science and requires a molecular-level understanding of how gradients couple to supramolecular structure and dynamics, which remains difficult to access experimentally. To this end, we combine implicit-solvent coarse-grained molecular dynamics (MD) with the constant chemical potential MD (C${\mu}$MD) technique to investigate, at molecular resolution, the coupling of two prototypical supramolecular polymer models with externally imposed ionic concentration gradients. Despite their contrasting responses to homogeneous ionic conditions, both systems undergo pronounced spatial reorganization under the gradient, generating regions of locally enhanced supramolecular activity that are inaccessible to homogeneous systems with the same overall content of self-assembling species. Crucially, the local supramolecular dynamics is largely determined by the local concentrations, consistent with the formation of local quasi-equilibria: this direct connection between spatial composition and supramolecular activity emerges as a common governing principle across the two models. These findings shed light on the spatially complex supramolecular response that can be triggered by chemical gradients, and show how a single gradient simulation can encode this response across a continuous range of local chemical environments, thus providing a general strategy to investigate and design materials operating under spatially heterogeneous conditions.



[235]. [*Reversible Magneto-ionic Modification of Metallic Magnetic Thin Films*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18396 "Reversible Magneto-ionic Modification of Metallic Magnetic Thin Films")

Md Golam Hafiz, Hari Babu Vasili, Philippa Shepley, Mannan Ali, Andrew J. Britton, Oscar Cespedes, Weibin Li, Manuel Valvidares, Rohit Pachat, Wilfried Fotso, Mourad Cherif, Shimpei Ono, Yves Roussigne, Mohamed Belmeguenai, Gavin Burnell

arXiv:2610.18396v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Magnetic multilayers with structural inversion asymmetry, perpendicular magnetic anisotropy (PMA), and large Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction (DMI) are widely studied for magnetic domain wall- and skyrmion-based data processing applications. In this work, we report a controlled, nonvolatile, but reversible local modification of such a system by electrically driven oxygen migration through the metallic film structure by using ionic liquid gating. Our findings provide direct evidence for the modification of the oxidation state at the heavy metal (HM)/ferromagnet (FM) interface. As a result, we observed changes in fundamental magnetic properties, such as an increase in the effective anisotropy constant (Keff) and coercive field (Hc) with oxidation, and a decrease with the reduction of oxygen ions. Positive gate voltages relative to the thin film extract oxygen ions from the magnetic layer, significantly reducing the domain nucleation field. In contrast, negative voltages drive oxygen ions into the magnetic layer, leading to a reversible increase in the extent of domain wall pinning near saturation. We observe a corresponding decrease in the magnetic moment and DMI, with negative voltage reducing both. This magneto-ionic modulation in fully metallic structures is beneficial for spintronic device applications, particularly in field-programmable domain wall and skyrmion devices.



[236]. [*Curie-Weiss cell fluid model: Fitting to experimental critical-point data*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18411 "Curie-Weiss cell fluid model: Fitting to experimental critical-point data")

I. V. Pylyuk, O. A. Dobush, M. P. Kozlovskii, R. V. Romanik, M. A. Shpot

arXiv:2610.18411v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We examine how the Curie--Weiss cell fluid model can be quantitatively related to real fluids by determining its microscopic parameters from experimental critical-point data for water, carbon dioxide, and three polyol ester lubricants (POE5, POE7, and POE9). The microscopic parameters of the model are determined from the experimental values of the critical temperature and critical density: the global attraction coupling constant is fixed by the critical temperature, and the cell volume by the critical density. The critical pressure then follows as an independent model prediction, deviating from experimental values by 10--41$\%$ across the studied substances. Varying the ratio of local repulsion to global attraction $f=J_2/J_1$ over a wide range has only a weak effect on this deviation. For $f\geq1$, the model's critical compressibility factor lays within a narrow range $Z_c\approx0.382-0.386$, remaining close to the mean-field van der Waals value $Z_c=3/8=0.375$ and consistently overestimating experimental values. The best agreement with experiment is obtained for the polyol ester lubricants and improves along the POE5--POE7--POE9 series with increasing molar mass, suggesting that multiple-occupancy cell fluid models are well suited to systems of large molecules, particularly soft-matter systems with bounded interaction potentials. We further show that generalizing the model by introducing a temperature- and phase-dependent effective attraction allows it to describe a triple point and to reproduce the broad range of critical-to-triple temperature ratios $T_c/T_{\mathrm{tr}}$ characteristic of real fluids.



[237]. [*The role of dependency range in thermally coupled superconducting networks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18418 "The role of dependency range in thermally coupled superconducting networks")

Nahala Yadid, Bnaya Gross, Shlomo Havlin, Nadav M. Shnerb

arXiv:2610.18418v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Interdependent networks can be highly fragile because dependency links amplify local failures into cascades that trigger global collapse. Much of the theoretical understanding comes from geometric network models, where dependency range is imposed externally and coupling strength is distance independent, and it is unclear how these insights apply to real materials, where interdependence arises from physical transport. Here we study thermally coupled amorphous superconducting networks, in which dependencies emerge from current redistribution and heat-mediated interactions governed by thermal diffusivity. Using an experimentally calibrated model of resistively shunted Josephson junctions, we show the system reproduces the three canonical transition regimes: continuous, nucleation-dominated, and mixed-order. However, because heat-driven dependency simultaneously broadens and weakens with distance, long-range thermal coupling can stabilize the superconducting phase upon heating, in contrast with its destabilizing role in geometric models. Cascades are anisotropic, with one-dimensional front propagation. These results offer an experimentally testable perspective complementing the geometric framework.



[238]. [*Altermagnetic Ground States on the Ammann--Beenker Tiling*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18441 "Altermagnetic Ground States on the Ammann--Beenker Tiling")

SeungBeom Hong, YunHo Kim, Moon Jip Park, GiBaik Sim

arXiv:2610.18441v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We identify exact altermagnetic ground states of a classical Heisenberg model on a quasicrystal. Considering the Ammann--Beenker tiling as an example, we determine the ground states by exploiting the parity structure of its parent four-dimensional hypercubic lattice. The ground state hosts coplanar altermagnetic order characterized by the preserved spin-group symmetry $[C_{2x}\parallel C_{8z}]$. Without translational symmetry, the momentum-space spin splitting that usually identifies altermagnets is not directly accessible. We instead couple the spins to itinerant electrons and show spin-resolved transport with $g$-wave symmetry, confirming the altermagnetic nature in a nonperiodic system. Our results establish a geometric route to altermagnetic order in quasicrystals, connecting exact magnetic ground states to their spin-resolved transport signatures.



[239]. [*Substrate-controlled epitaxial-like growth and photochromic properties of yttrium oxyhydride thin films*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18448 "Substrate-controlled epitaxial-like growth and photochromic properties of yttrium oxyhydride thin films")

M. Zubkins, E. Strods, E. Letko, H. M\"andar, D. Moldarev, K. Mizohata, K. Sarakinos, V. Vibornijs, K. Kundzins, J. Purans

arXiv:2610.18448v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Yttrium oxyhydride (YHO) thin films are synthesised by reactive magnetron sputtering of \b{eta}-YHx precursor films onto single-crystalline yttria-stabilised zirconia (YSZ) (111) and (100) substrates at 200 {\deg}C, followed by post-deposition oxidation in air. For comparison, reference films are also prepared on amorphous SiO2 and soda-lime glass substrates at both 200 {\deg}C and room temperature (RT). The structural (X-ray diffraction and scanning electron microscopy), compositional (elastic recoil detection analysis), and optical properties, including photochromic behaviour, of the films are systematically investigated. The results reveal a strong influence of substrate orientation on the film microstructure and crystallographic texture, leading to epitaxial-like growth with a mosaic spread of ~3-4{\deg}. YHO deposited on YSZ(100) exhibits larger crystallite sizes (10-20 nm) than YHO grown on YSZ(111) (~8 nm) and exhibits residual in-plane compressive strain while remaining largely relaxed with respect to the substrate. Films deposited at 200 {\deg}C exhibit lower oxygen concentrations (O/Y = 0.71-0.82) than films deposited at RT (O/Y = 0.92-1.26). All films exhibit photochromic behaviour under solar simulator illumination, with relative transmittance changes ranging from 7 to 22 % after 10 min of illumination. Despite the pronounced structural differences, the epitaxial-like films show no systematic improvement in photochromic response, indicating that the effect is governed primarily by composition and the local O/H configuration rather than by long-range cation ordering. These findings provide new insights into the relationship between crystallographic texture, composition, and photochromic properties in YHO thin films, contributing to the understanding of this material for optical and optoelectronic applications.



[240]. [*Quantitative Modelling of Solvation in 2D Materials via Alchemical Decoupling and Potential Energy Surfaces*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18490 "Quantitative Modelling of Solvation in 2D Materials via Alchemical Decoupling and Potential Energy Surfaces")

Lindsey N. Ioos, Duncan A. Peterson, Scott C. Warren

arXiv:2610.18490v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Understanding why 2D materials remain suspended in solutions is essential to understanding the mechanisms of aggregation and exfoliation. These dynamics have been difficult to model because of the long timescales and large activation energies involved in these simulations. Here we describe an improved strategy for modelling the solvation and stability of 2D materials that uses the Gibbs free energy of solvation to accurately calibrate two disconnected potential energy surfaces for the fully desolvated and fully solvated 2D materials. By joining these surfaces using solvation energy, we obtain predictions for 2D material stability in various solvents that are qualitatively different than prior simulations and that have excellent agreement with experimental results. Our approach predicts a much higher barrier to full desolvation of the material, suggesting that materials that appear to be aggregated in fact still retain monolayers of solvent between adjacent layers of 2D materials. This work provides much needed understanding on the interactions between the solvent and 2D material that can guide future experimental work.



[241]. [*Macroscopic fluctuation theory of stochastic systems undergoing monitoring*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18500 "Macroscopic fluctuation theory of stochastic systems undergoing monitoring")

Jo\~ao Costa, Pedro Ribeiro, Andrea De Luca

arXiv:2610.18500v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We develop a macroscopic fluctuation theory for diffusive classical lattice gases subject to continuous coarse-grained monitoring. Treating measurements in the typical-outcome (quenched) regime, we show that observation adds a quadratic confining term to the MFT action that pins the density to its hydrodynamic profile. We derive sufficient conditions under which, in the presence of monitoring, the current large-deviation function remains convex, the additivity principle holds, and dynamical phase transitions are excluded. For particle-hole-symmetric one-dimensional models we find that monitoring suppresses dynamical phase transitions and can change their order from second to first, as we demonstrate in the Katz--Lebowitz--Spohn model. In the strong-measurement limit, we find that the current distribution becomes Gaussian and determine the scaling of its cumulants: they follow simple power laws when the mobility remains nonzero throughout the system, but exhibit anomalous logarithmic scaling when the mobility vanishes at the boundaries.



[242]. [*Flexoelectricity and Flexomagnetism*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18525 "Flexoelectricity and Flexomagnetism")

Alexander Edstr\"om, Massimiliano Stengel

arXiv:2610.18525v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Strain gradients, unlike homogeneous strain, are odd under space inversion and unlock couplings that are strictly forbidden in the parent crystal. At the nanoscale, and in curved low-dimensional systems in particular, such gradients are readily attainable and often unavoidable, so that curvature acts as a symmetry-breaking field written into the lattice itself. This chapter reviews the resulting couplings to electric polarization and magnetism. After establishing the geometric relation between strain gradients and curvature, we review flexoelectricity as the better-established reference case: the bulk, surface and dynamic contributions that complicate its definition, its microscopic mechanisms, its manifestation in two-dimensional materials, and the first-principles methodology now available for its calculation. Using the magnetoelectric effect as a conceptual bridge, we then develop flexomagnetism in a broad sense, covering any coupling phenomena between magnetism and strain gradients, and specifically discuss the linear magnetization response to curvature and curvature-induced Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interactions, beyond the established curvilinear treatment. Finally, we briefly describe some additional effects fitting under this broad umbrella of flexomagnetism, including nonlinear ones, inhomogeneous magnetoelectric and Rashba-type phenomena, and connections to altermagnetism.



[243]. [*Phases of a rotating Bose-Einstein condensate trapped in a hard-wall potential*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18527 "Phases of a rotating Bose-Einstein condensate trapped in a hard-wall potential")

H\'el\`ene Perrin, G. M. Kavoulakis

arXiv:2610.18527v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigate the rotational properties of a zero-temperature Bose-Einstein condensed gas, which is confined in a hard-wall potential. For weak interactions the gas carries its angular momentum via vortices of multiple quantization. As the coupling increases and for sufficiently slow rotational frequencies of the trap, the multiply-quantized vortex states become unstable against a mixed state. For stronger couplings, as the angular frequency of the trap increases, there is a transition from a vortex lattice, to a vortex lattice with a hole at the trap center, and finally to a giant vortex. For weak effective attractive interactions we still have vortices of multiple quantization. These states become unstable towards states which break the axial symmetry of the problem and resemble center-of-mass excitation, as the coupling becomes more negative. Finally, for even more attractive forces, the system becomes unstable against collapse.



[244]. [*Imaging Gate-Tunable Single-Layer Molecular Kagome Lattices*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18539 "Imaging Gate-Tunable Single-Layer Molecular Kagome Lattices")

Ziyi Wang, Rafal Zuzak, Weichen Tang, Haowen Pang, Yutang Qiao, Dhanvanth Balakrishnan, Yuqiao Yan, Aining Hu, Andrew Sun, Takashi Taniguchi, Kenji Watanabe, Feng Wang, Steven G. Louie, Michael F. Crommie

arXiv:2610.18539v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Kagome lattices provide a simple geometry for engineering electronic states that exhibit novel quantum behavior. In conventional kagome materials, however, the relevant states are often embedded in chemically complex three-dimensional crystals and are difficult to isolate or tune. Molecule-based kagome networks offer structural precision and tunability, but have largely been confined to metallic substrates incompatible with 2D devices. Here we report growth of single-layer Ni$_3$(HITP)$_2$ metal-organic frameworks (MOFs) on van der Waals substrates including graphene-based field-effect devices using metal-salt molecular beam epitaxy. Low-temperature scanning tunneling spectroscopy resolves kagome-derived flat-band and van Hove singularity-based spectral features consistent with first-principles calculations. Gating hybrid 2D MOF devices allows us to energetically shift kagome spectral features, thus establishing electrostatic control of chemically programmable 2D kagome electronic lattices.



[245]. [*A Transferable Machine-Learned Potential Enabling Large-Scale Copper-Carbon Simulations from Equilibrium to Extreme Conditions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18545 "A Transferable Machine-Learned Potential Enabling Large-Scale Copper-Carbon Simulations from Equilibrium to Extreme Conditions")

Feiyu Zhou, Wei Qiu, Yan Chen, Jiahui Liu, Yilun Liu, Shengying Yue, Penghua Ying

arXiv:2610.18545v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Cu-C materials and interfaces are central to diamond thermal management, metallization, graphene growth and carbon-coating technologies, but atomistic simulations across these applications require a potential that describes metallic Cu, diverse carbon bonding, heterogeneous Cu-C interactions and close atomic encounters. Here we develop NEP-CuC, a transferable neuroevolution potential trained on a dataset of 2,727 uniformly relabelled configurations selected from a heterogeneous Cu-C candidate pool. Disagreement within an ensemble of five independently trained models provides an internal indicator of model sensitivity. A Ziegler-Biersack-Littmark (ZBL) correction describes short-range nuclear repulsion, while a runtime D3(BJ) correction supplements dispersion-sensitive calculations. Tests on source-derived held-out and independent external datasets, together with structural, elastic, defect, interfacial, vibrational and finite-temperature transport benchmarks, establish broad but property-dependent physical fidelity across elemental Cu, diverse carbon environments and heterogeneous Cu-C configurations. Large-scale simulations of amorphous-carbon formation and orientation-dependent shock compression of Cu further test transferability under strongly non-equilibrium conditions. For Cu/diamond interfaces, the same potential describes deposition, annealing and tensile response: preparation history leaves persistent differences in interphase structure, while the mean peak nominal tensile stress at 500 K decreases from $12.35\pm0.71$ to $6.65\pm0.98$ GPa as the incident Cu energy during the initial deposition stage increases from 1 to 50 eV. NEP-CuC provides an atomistic framework for linking processing, interfacial structure and physical response across Cu-C systems, with direct relevance to Cu/diamond thermal-management interfaces, diamond metallization and non-equilibrium Cu-C processing.



[246]. [*Direct growth of crystalline spinel GaON films: A wide-bandgap mixed-anion semiconductor*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18550 "Direct growth of crystalline spinel GaON films: A wide-bandgap mixed-anion semiconductor")

Baikui Li, Zecheng Yang, Wenhao Yin, Zijing Wu, Haitao Zhao, Yunwei Sheng, Honglei Wu, Jiannong Wang

arXiv:2610.18550v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Mixed-anion semiconductors offer opportunities for bandgap engineering beyond conventional multication alloys, but competing phases and disorder complicate their synthesis and the identification of their electronic properties. Gallium oxynitride (GaON) could connect the technologically important GaN and Ga2O3 systems, yet establishing a clear relation between its crystal structure, composition and bandgap remains challenging. Here we report the direct growth of crystalline spinel gamma-GaON films by nitrogen-plasma-assisted pulsed-laser deposition. Varying the nitrogen and oxygen supply yields monoclinic beta-Ga2O3, predominantly disordered films and crystalline spinel GaON. Complementary diffraction, atomic-resolution microscopy and elemental analysis establish the spinel structure and nitrogen incorporation. Temperature-dependent photocurrent spectroscopy reveals a clear redshift of the crystalline gamma-GaON response, supporting assignment of a band-edge transition near 4.3 eV at room temperature. These results open opportunities to investigate mixed-anion wide-bandgap semiconductor physics and develop heterostructures.



[247]. [*Time-Resolved Phonon Dispersions of Thermally Excited Graphite at the Nanoscale*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18561 "Time-Resolved Phonon Dispersions of Thermally Excited Graphite at the Nanoscale")

Mairi McCauley (Department of Physics,Center for the Science of Materials Berlin, Humboldt-Universit\"at zu Berlin, Berlin, Germany), Christoph T. Koch (Department of Physics,Center for the Science of Materials Berlin, Humboldt-Universit\"at zu Berlin, Berlin, Germany), Benedikt Haas (Department of Physics,Center for the Science of Materials Berlin, Humboldt-Universit\"at zu Berlin, Berlin, Germany)

arXiv:2610.18561v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Lattice vibrations govern the propagation of heat in materials, and investigating their dynamics through time-resolved phonon dispersion measurements would provide insight into these excitations and their interactions. However, time-resolved measurements that directly resolve the phonon dispersion at the nanoscale have not been previously demonstrated. Momentum-resolved vibrational electron energy loss spectroscopy (EELS) provides access to phonon energies and momenta with nanoscale spatial resolution. Here, we combine momentum-resolved vibrational EELS with pulsed laser heating and synchronised gated electron detection to observe the phonon dispersion of graphite during controlled heating and cooling on the microsecond timescale. Recording both the gain and loss spectra at defined times relative to laser excitation, we determine the temperature at each time delay and follow changes to the phonon branches as a function of temperature. The momentum-resolved spectra reveal a decrease in energy of the optical phonon branch with increasing temperature accompanied by an increase in peak width. These changes are consistent with temperature-dependent phonon renormalisation and scattering associated with anharmonic interactions. The approach is compatible with detector gating on timescales down to 50 ns, providing a route towards extending these measurements to faster dynamics. This work demonstrates time-resolved phonon dispersion spectroscopy using vibrational EELS and provides a route towards probing temperature-dependent phonon dynamics at the nanoscale with simultaneous energy and momentum resolution.



[248]. [*Exact Microscopic One-Body Reduction in the Symmetric Exclusion Process*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18626 "Exact Microscopic One-Body Reduction in the Symmetric Exclusion Process")

Olivier B\'enichou, Aur\'elien Grabsch

arXiv:2610.18626v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The symmetric exclusion process (SEP) is a paradigmatic model of interacting particle transport. Its current--density correlations are governed by an infinite many-body hierarchy of equations. Only recently, this hierarchy was found to collapse at large scales onto a single closed equation. Is this remarkable simplification created by coarse graining, or does it reflect a deeper property already present in the microscopic dynamics? Here we show that the second scenario is realized. At arbitrary time on the lattice, the entire hierarchy collapses onto a single linear equation which is exactly equivalent to a persistence problem for a single auxiliary random walker. Thus an interacting many-body problem reduces exactly to a genuine one-body problem before any scaling limit is taken. This reduction determines the complete finite-time correlation profile, including during nonequilibrium relaxation. More broadly, the microscopic operator uncovered here opens a route to more general correlation functions in the SEP, and may point to a general organizing principle for nonequilibrium interacting particle systems.



[249]. [*Plasmon modes in triple-point semimetal, its quadratic cousin, and Hopf semimetal*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18629 "Plasmon modes in triple-point semimetal, its quadratic cousin, and Hopf semimetal")

Ipsita Mandal

arXiv:2610.18629v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We study collective charge excitations in pseudospin-1 triple-point semimetals within the random-phase approximation, considering both the linearised model with a flat-band and its quadratic-corrected counterpart. We analyse their density response, Drude weights, screening, plasmon dispersion and damping, particle-hole continua, and Friedel oscillations from the long-wavelength to the arbitrary-momentum regime. The quadratic correction renders the flat-band dispersive and endows it with a Fermi surface, thereby affecting the overall response. Comparisons with Weyl and Hopf semimetals reveal how bandstructure and quantum geometry jointly govern collective charge dynamics. While the Drude weights are determined by the Fermi surface and band-velocities, interband screening, plasmon dispersion, and Friedel oscillations reflect the quantum geometry of the eigenstates.



[250]. [*Momentum conservation for ab initio calculations with pseudopotentials via a phase space electronic structure formulation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18631 "Momentum conservation for ab initio calculations with pseudopotentials via a phase space electronic structure formulation")

Guy Ohad, Nadine C. Bradbury, Joseph E. Subotnik

arXiv:2610.18631v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We demonstrate how to build a robust phase space electronic Hamiltonian $\hat{H}_\text{PS}(\boldsymbol{R},\boldsymbol{P})$ that depends on nuclear position $\boldsymbol{R}$ and nuclear momentum $\boldsymbol{P}$ in the presence of ionic pseudopotentials. In particular, we show that in order to account for the non-local component of a pseudopotential while also conserving linear and angular momentum, one is forced to introduce a non-local one-electron operator $\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^\text{nl}$ that couples electronic and nuclear momenta together. To test the validity of our ansatz, we derive a generalized form of Nafie's exact equality (which relates electronic momentum and electronic position) so as to include the presence of pseudopotentials. We then benchmark our proposed phase space electronic structure theory against this generalized Nafie-like result for a diverse set of diatomic molecules spanning a wide range of elements. We find excellent agreement across this ab initio test set, highlighting the strength of our method in capturing meaningful electronic momentum beyond the Born-Oppenheimer framework.



[251]. [*Extending the mean first-passage-time formalism to the reconstruction of high free-energy barriers: A top-start approach*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18653 "Extending the mean first-passage-time formalism to the reconstruction of high free-energy barriers: A top-start approach")

Ying Jiang, Ivan Saika-Voivod

arXiv:2610.18653v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Rare events, such as crystal nucleation in mildly supercooled liquids, are characterized by waiting times that can be much longer than the timescale of the underlying microscopic dynamics. The mean first-passage time (MFPT) formalism [J. Wedekind and D. Reguera, J. Phys. Chem. B 112, 11060 (2008)] provides a route to reconstruct one-dimensional free-energy profiles from the MFPT and steady-state distribution measured along a reaction coordinate, but conventional implementations become inefficient for sufficiently high barriers because spontaneous crossings are rarely observed. We introduce a "top-start" extension that initiates trajectories near the barrier top and reconstructs the free-energy profile from their subsequent evolution away from the barrier toward lower-free-energy regions. We establish the equivalence between the top-start and two-region constructions using a Markov-chain transition-matrix formulation. We validate the method using one-dimensional random-walk simulations across low, medium, and high free-energy barriers, with transition-matrix calculations providing an exact benchmark for the corresponding discretized models. The top-start reconstruction remains accurate in the high-barrier regime where conventional MFPT sampling becomes prohibitively inefficient.



[252]. [*Dark state preparation in a zero-field-split molecular triplet*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18661 "Dark state preparation in a zero-field-split molecular triplet")

Maria Pfahler, Andrea Donarini

arXiv:2610.18661v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We propose a protocol to prepare and probe dark states in a single-molecule electron spin resonance based on atomic force microscopy (ESR-AFM), thus extending existing techniques developed to address Rabi oscillations. To this end, we simulate in analogy to atomic physics the coherent manipulation of a three-level system in the V-configuration using a multifrequency magnetic field. More specifically, we consider a neutral molecule with a zero-field-split (ZFS) first excited triplet and analyze within a pump-probe scheme the dynamics of the total triplet population. In the triplet subspace, the dark state is a coherent superposition formed by two ZFS states which does not evolve in time despite a continuous resonant driving towards the third one. We characterize the properties of this state comparing it to its fully incoherent counterpart. Within a density-matrix formalism for open systems, we then compute the expected dark state signatures both for ideal scenarios and for the realistic parameterization of ESR-AFM on pentacene.



[253]. [*Composition-controlled optical fingerprints and anisotropy of self-trapped excitons in double perovskites*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18665 "Composition-controlled optical fingerprints and anisotropy of self-trapped excitons in double perovskites")

Simone Grillo, Offir Zachs Maishlos, Liya Koren, Rotem Strassberg, Rachel Lifer, Edi Maizenberg, Umberto De Giovannini, Angel Rubio, Yehonadav Bekenstein, Ofer Neufeld

arXiv:2610.18665v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Double perovskites (DPs) offer a compositionally versatile platform for photocatalysis and optoelectronics, but their soft lattices favor strong exciton-phonon coupling and self-trapped excitons (STEs), which produce broad, red-shifted photoluminescence (PL). Here, we combine optical spectroscopy and ab initio calculations to study lead-free, Sb-doped Cs2BInCl6 nanocrystals with B-site alloying from Na to K. Alloying tunes the PL energy from about 2.48 to 2.70 eV, while absorption features near 3.8 eV remain nearly unchanged. Calculations reveal that Sb doping enables bright radiative recombination channels and delocalizes the excitonic wavefunction. We identify STEs responsible for the large PL red shift, associated with an upward shift of dopant-hybridized in-gap states. Na-to-K substitution selectively changes the STE lattice distortion, explaining why PL varies strongly with composition while absorption does not. Theory further predicts pronounced PL polarization anisotropy that reflects the STE wavefunction's spatial structure and depends on B-site composition. Time-resolved anisotropy measurements closely agree with these predictions, providing experimental fingerprints of the excitonic wavefunction and its evolution with alloying. Together, these results disentangle the roles of doping and alloying in lead-free DPs and establish polarization-resolved spectroscopy as a probe of STE wavefunctions.



[254]. [*Superexchange coupling between a dilute Gd3+ probe and orphan spins in a valence-bond glass*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18722 "Superexchange coupling between a dilute Gd3+ probe and orphan spins in a valence-bond glass")

Patrick Colgan, Artur Solodovnyk, Jeremy P. Carlo, Scott Dietrich

arXiv:2610.18722v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: The valence-bond-glass state of the 4d^1 double perovskite Ba2YMoO6 combines Mo^5+ singlets with residual paramagnetic "orphan" spins whose coupling is hard to reach with bulk probes. We probe them by electron paramagnetic resonance of a dilute Gd^3+ ion on the Y^3+ site via broadband coplanar-waveguide transmission at 5 K. The isostructural nonmagnetic niobate Ba2YNbO6, doped with the same Gd^3+ probe, is a fixed-lattice control. The Gd^3+ linewidth nearly doubles in the magnetic host. Dipolar coupling to the sparse orphans is nearly an order of magnitude too weak; the excess is instead reproduced by a transferred f-d superexchange of J ~ 0.2 K, so the width is a static measure of orphan-spin coupling. The half-field (Delta m_S = +/-2) satellite collapses in the magnetic host, a dynamical dephasing channel, while the unchanged g-factor marks a zero-mean random internal field. Together the two channels probe the coupling and dynamics of valence-bond-glass orphan spins.



[255]. [*Emergent Loop Geometry in Gauge-Equivariant Graph Neural Networks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18742 "Emergent Loop Geometry in Gauge-Equivariant Graph Neural Networks")

Ali Rayat, Gia-Wei Chern

arXiv:2610.18742v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Gauge-equivariant graph neural networks generate ordered products of gauge links through local message passing, but the geometry of these internal structures is not captured by the receptive field alone. We investigate the statistical ensemble of closed lattice walks generated by recursive node- and edge-feature operations, independently of network training. Analytical and numerical results reveal a separation between rapid growth of contour length and much slower spatial expansion: deeper compositions generate increasingly long paths through repeated traversal of relatively compact regions. Loop expansions of fermionic observables provide a physical interpretation of this hierarchy, showing how spatial locality remains compatible with high-order dependence on nearby gauge fields. These findings identify complementary roles of network depth in extending spatial reach and enriching the representations constructed within that reach, providing a geometrical perspective on learning lattice gauge observables.



[256]. [*Competing delocalisation and localisation induced by the dipolar tail in fragmented lattice gases*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18747 "Competing delocalisation and localisation induced by the dipolar tail in fragmented lattice gases")

Sabhyata Gupta, Adith Sai Aramthottil, Luis Santos

arXiv:2610.18747v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We investigate how the long-range tail of dipole-dipole interactions modifies the dynamics of strongly interacting hard-core bosons in a one-dimensional lattice. In the nearest-neighbour model, large interactions generate an emergent Hilbert-space fragmentation into manifolds labelled by the number of nearest-neighbour bonds \(N_{\mathrm{NN}}\). The dipolar tail has a non-monotonic effect on this fragmented dynamics as the interaction-to-tunnelling ratio \(V/t\) is increased. At intermediate \(V/t\), it enhances delocalisation relative to the nearest-neighbour model by reducing the energetic detuning between neighbouring \(N_{\mathrm{NN}}\) manifolds and sustaining their hybridisation by hopping. At larger \(V/t\), the same tail lifts degeneracies within each manifold, beginning with the splitting associated with the next-nearest-neighbour bond number \(N_{\mathrm{NNN}}\), and promotes intra-manifold localisation and Hilbert-space shattering. The competition between these two effects produces a crossover from a delocalising to a localising role of the dipolar tail, visible in the late-time spatial inhomogeneity. This crossover occurs for nearly all initial states considered and persists at different fillings. Transverse confinement strengthens the short-range part of the dipolar tail and shifts the crossover to lower \(V/t\), bringing it into the regime relevant to current experiments with magnetic atoms and polar molecules.



[257]. [*Spin textures at the Fermi surface of ferromagnets and associated torque*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18800 "Spin textures at the Fermi surface of ferromagnets and associated torque")

T. Valet, J. Li, H. Jaffr\`es, V. Cros, M. Chshiev, R. Raimondi

arXiv:2610.18800v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We establish theoretically that transverse spin textures are generically induced by the spin-orbit interaction at the Fermi surface of centrosymmetric ferromagnetic metals. We confirm this prediction with {\em ab initio} computations for Fe, Co and Ni. By symmetry, this cannot be associated with spin-orbit torques under a uniform current. However, a new {\em current-gradient-induced} torque emerges in nanoscale geometries. For a series of Ni-based bilayers, the computed Ni thickness dependence of the current-gradient-induced torque is in quantitative agreement with measurements previously attributed to orbital torques. We discuss how this opens a new avenue for the electrical control of magnetic orders, possibly also including the N\'eel vector of metallic altermagnets.



[258]. [*Towards synergy between theory and experiments: First-passage processes under resetting with stochastic return protocols*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18814 "Towards synergy between theory and experiments: First-passage processes under resetting with stochastic return protocols")

Ashutosh Dubey, Arnab Pal

arXiv:2610.18814v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Stochastic resetting with stochastic return (SR) has recently emerged as an effective and practically relevant strategy for controlling first-passage phenomena and optimizing search processes by significantly reducing completion times in a wide class of stochastic systems. While most theoretical studies assume instantaneous return (IR), in realistic systems the return to the resetting position typically occurs over a finite time. We consider a 1-d diffusing particle in a harmonic trap with stiffnesses $k_1$ and $k_2$ during search and return, respectively. The particle searches for the target and, upon resetting at rate $r$, enters the return phase, continuing until it reaches either the origin or the absorbing target at $L>0$. Notably, the model permits target detection throughout the return phase, distinguishing it from IR scenarios. We analytically derive the MFPT for our model and determine parameter regions where SR enhances or suppresses search efficiency over the underlying dynamics, constructing the corresponding phase diagram using the coefficient of variation criterion. We further identify regimes in which SR is more efficient than IR using the SR criterion. This behavior is tested across different parameter sets taken from experimental studies by Goerlich et al., Biroli et al., and Besga et al. We map the parameter values reported in existing experimental studies onto our theoretical phase diagram, thereby identifying the regimes in which SR outperforms IR. In particular, we find that the most relevant region of the phase space, where SR is most beneficial corresponds to larger values of the potential strength in the search phase and lower values of potential in the return phase. Our results provide experimentally testable predictions and bridge the gap between theoretical resetting models and optical trap mediated finite-time return dynamics.



[259]. [*Single anyons and anyon binding in a quantum Hall dot*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18817 "Single anyons and anyon binding in a quantum Hall dot")

Marco Valentini, Will Wang, Amir Shapour Mohammadi, Noah Samuelson, Chengxuan Li, Liam Cohen, Mark Zakharyan, Jiechao Feng, Taige Wang, Takashi Taniguchi, Kenji Watanabe, Michael P. Zaletel, Andrea F. Young

arXiv:2610.18817v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: Anyons are particles with quantized fractional statistics. In theory, a single anyon can be stabilized as a localized fractional charge within the bulk of a topologically ordered two-dimensional insulator. However, experiments to date have focused on probing anyons in structures in which many anyonic excitations coexist and interact. Here, we demonstrate the trapping of a single anyon in a device consisting of a graphene-based quantum point contact with an embedded tunable quantum Hall dot. Benchmarking our device at v = 1, we demonstrate backscattering between Fermi liquid edge states mediated by the whole-electron Coulomb blockade of the dot. The cleanliness and control available in our device allow depletion of the dot to the last quasihole, and we observe spin blockade for transitions to the opposite-spin Landau level. At v = 1/3 and 2/3, we observe backscattering resonances with three times the frequency, consistent with resonant transmission of e/3 charges via a single anyon transistor. We demonstrate controlled depletion to the last quasiparticle to form a single anyon quantum dot. Tracing the charge addition spectrum for quasiparticle number Nqp = 1-5, we observe highly irregular addition energies, consistent with DMRG modeling showing a complex orbital shell-filling behavior. At higher Nqp = 10-20, the addition energies show a striking pattern: quasiholes at 1/3 (and quasiparticles at 2/3) show a regular addition spectrum while quasiparticles at 1/3 (and quasiholes at 2/3) do not. We trace the origin of this irregularity to an attractive component of the inter-anyon interaction: by experimentally tuning the confining energy, we observe complete suppression of alternating charge sectors, after which the dot charges in increments of twice the fundamental charge. This observation indicates the emergence of an anyon binding energy as large as the charging energy.



[260]. [*A hierarchy of exact lower bounds on the Laughlin spectral gap*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18854 "A hierarchy of exact lower bounds on the Laughlin spectral gap")

Michael G. Scheer, Eslam Khalaf

arXiv:2610.18854v1 Announce Type: new 
Abstract: We develop a bootstrap approach to deriving lower bounds on the spectral gap of the first Haldane pseudopotential Hamiltonian on the sphere with $N_\Phi$ flux quanta for arbitrary $N_\Phi$, including in the thermodynamic limit $N_\Phi \to \infty$. Our approach can in principle be generalized to pseudopotentials describing other model wavefunctions such as the Moore-Read state or Read-Rezayi sequence. Our approach is based on Knabe's observation that if $H$ is a Hamiltonian with ground state energy $0$ then the spectral gap is at least $\epsilon > 0$ if and only if $H^2 \succeq \epsilon H$. To show this inequality, we split $H^2$ into two parts, one that can be analytically bounded and another that can be bounded using a finite semidefinite program (SDP). Crucially, the size of this program is determined only by cutoff parameters, and the program depends on $N_\Phi$ only parametrically, allowing us to obtain numerically exact (up to solver precision) lower bounds on the gap. In order to turn these numerical bounds into mathematically rigorous bounds, we introduce an algorithm that can produce nearly-optimal exact feasible points for large rational SDPs. We additionally provide an algorithm that establishes the rank stability of the semidefinite program in the limit $N_\Phi \to \infty$ which is needed to certify the bound in the thermodynamic limit. Beyond establishing the Laughlin gap and revealing a potential method for deriving spectral gap lower bounds for other pseudopotentials, our work provides a general method for turning numerical semidefinite programming bounds into rigorous mathematical proofs, which may have wide applicability.



[261]. [*Twofold Symmetry Observed in Bi$_{2}$Te$_{3}$/FeTe Interfacial Superconductor*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.05904 "Twofold Symmetry Observed in Bi$_{2}$Te$_{3}$/FeTe Interfacial Superconductor")

Xinru Han, Hailang Qin, Tianluo Pan, Bin Guo, Kaige Shi, Zijin Huang, Jie Jiang, Hangyu Yin, Hongtao He, Fei Ye, Wei-Qiang Chen, Jia-Wei Mei, Gan Wang

arXiv:2307.05904v2 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Superconducting pairing symmetry are crucial in understanding the microscopic superconducting mechanism of a superconductor. Here we report the observation of a twofold superconducting gap symmetry in an interfacial superconductor Bi$_{2}$Te$_{3}$/FeTe, by employing quasiparticle interference (QPI) technique in scanning tunneling microscopy and macroscopic magnetoresistance measurements. The QPI patterns corresponding to energies inside and outside the gap reveal a clear anisotropic superconducting gap. Furthermore, both the in-plane angle-dependent magnetoresistance and in-plane upper critical field exhibit a clear twofold symmetry. This twofold symmetry align with the Te-Te direction in FeTe, which weakens the possible generation by bi-collinear antiferromagnetism order. Our finding provides key information in further understanding of the topological properties in Bi$_{2}$Te$_{3}$/FeTe superconducting system and propels further theoretical interests in the paring mechanism in the system.



[262]. [*Revisiting magnetoelectric response in collinear antiferromagnetic zigzag chains: A downfolding approach beyond conventional low-energy models*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07118 "Revisiting magnetoelectric response in collinear antiferromagnetic zigzag chains: A downfolding approach beyond conventional low-energy models")

Shuhei Kanda, Satoru Hayami

arXiv:2605.07118v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Magnetoelectric (ME) effects in antiferromagnets provide a fertile platform for exploring symmetry-driven cross-correlated responses. However, their microscopic origin remains elusive and is often obscured in simplified low-energy descriptions. In this study, we revisit the microscopic mechanism of the ME effect in a collinear antiferromagnetic zigzag chain by employing a multi-orbital tight-binding model that explicitly includes both $s$- and $p$-orbital degrees of freedom. Using analytical and numerical calculations based on the Kubo formula, we demonstrate that the ME response is governed by orbital degrees of freedom activated through $s$--$p$ hybridization, while the spin contribution vanishes due to spin conservation. To elucidate the low-energy description, we derive an effective Hamiltonian projected onto the $s$-orbital subspace using the Schur complement. We show that a naive application of the Kubo formula within this effective model fails to capture the ME response. This issue is resolved by systematically incorporating vertex corrections in terms of orbital hybridization into the response functions. Furthermore, by introducing a quasiparticle renormalization scheme, we formulate a renormalized Kubo formula that preserves conservation laws and accurately reproduces the full multi-orbital results. Our analysis revisits the conventional low-energy perspective and reveals that the ME effect originates from virtual interorbital processes encoded in vertex corrections, rather than from the bare low-energy Hamiltonian. The effective framework developed here provides a unified microscopic understanding of orbital-driven ME responses and offers a systematic route to incorporate hybridization effects beyond simple low-energy models.



[263]. [*Tracking Coupled Granular Temperature and Entropy Dynamics in Granular Materials via Dielectric Spectroscopy*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19560 "Tracking Coupled Granular Temperature and Entropy Dynamics in Granular Materials via Dielectric Spectroscopy")

Sophia G. Krastana, Anthony N. Papathanassiou

arXiv:2605.19560v2 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: In glass-forming liquids, structural dynamics are governed by configurational entropy and temperature, with dielectric relaxation time scaling alongside structural relaxation time as described by the Adam-Gibbs (AG) model. Under Edwards's athermal statistical thermodynamics, a modified AG law similarly governs granular matter, provided that granular temperature and configurational entropy are appropriately defined. This study investigates whether variations in the structural relaxation of granular systems can be probed via thermally activated processes, specifically electric charge hopping and trapping. By progressively reducing the volume of graphite powder to vary its packing fraction, we estimated relative configurational entropy and granular temperature from volumetric data, while evaluating electrical conductivity and capacity via impedance spectroscopy. We demonstrate that the logarithm of the dielectric relaxation time, derived from complex impedance, scales with granular temperature and entropy across both loose and compact states. Consequently, changes in the complex impedance resulting from packing fraction variations are tuned by granule configuration, strictly adhering to an AG-like relationship for thermal systems. These findings establish dielectric spectroscopy as a viable, non-destructive tool for tracing configurational dynamics in granular matter, analogous to its established use in polymers and glass formers.



[264]. [*Composite Fermion Theory of Fractional Chern Insulators in Rhombohedral Graphene*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07174 "Composite Fermion Theory of Fractional Chern Insulators in Rhombohedral Graphene")

Peize Ding, Andrew J. Millis, Yongxin Zeng

arXiv:2610.07174v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: The experimental observation of the fractional quantum anomalous Hall effect in rhombohedral graphene multilayers has raised questions about the nature of the fractional Chern insulator (FCI) states and the role of the moir\'e potential. In this Letter we develop a composite fermion (CF) description and discuss how moir\'e modulation and Coulomb interactions stabilize the FCI ground states. Under a flux-attachment transformation and mean-field approximation, the CF spectrum forms Landau levels that are further split by the moir\'e potential. At moir\'e filling fractions in the Jain sequence, an integer number of CF bands are occupied, resulting in an incompressible ground state with fractionally quantized Hall response. Remarkably, when the attached flux opposes the Berry curvature of the conduction band of rhombohedral graphene, the CF Landau levels are widely spaced in energy and delocalized across layers, leading to a reduced density of states that helps protect the CF mean-field state and amplifying effects of the moir\'e potential acting on the layer away from the doped electrons. Interactions between CFs further enlarge the moir\'e-induced band gap and stabilize the FCI ground state. Our work sheds light on the nature of the FCI states in rhombohedral graphene and provides a new theoretical framework for further studies.



[265]. [*On the Fast Electrons-Optical Modes Interaction Problem Error*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13976 "On the Fast Electrons-Optical Modes Interaction Problem Error")

Svetlana Churochkina, Dmitry Churochkin

arXiv:2610.13976v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: A recently developed relativistic quantum optics approach for a fast electron beam-optical modes interaction problem (Optica 6, 1524--1534 (2019)) contains suggestion about a smallness of the error induced by replacement $\Psi_{\vec k}\approx\Psi_{\vec k_0}$ for noninteracting free electron Dirac spinors at such wave vector $\vec k$ that $\left|\vec k-\vec k_0 \right| \ll\left|\vec k_0\right|$ with $\vec k_0$ as a peaked wave vector of a focused electron beam. Here, based on quantum information approach we strictly investigated a degree of coincidence of spinors $\Psi_{\vec k}$ and $\Psi_{\vec k_0}$ by calculating fidelity for them. The analytical expression is obtained. It is shown that the fidelity inevitably depends on not only wave vectors $\vec k$ and $\vec k_0$ but also on mutual orientation of $[\vec k_0,\vec k]$ and $\hat{\vec{s}}^{\dagger}\vec{\sigma}\hat{\vec{s}}$ with $\vec{s}$ is a two-component spin unit vector and $\vec{\sigma}$ is a vector of Pauli matrices. This result can be conceived as a chirality-like effect in the fidelity and constraints spin arbitrariness when applying the developed relativistic quantum optics approach to the problems of interest. A geometrical configuration of $\vec k_0$, $\vec k-\vec k_0$, and $\hat{\vec{s}}^{\dag}\vec\sigma\hat{\vec{s}}$ that is optimal in terms of fidelity is established.



[266]. [*A semi-infinite hydraulic fracture propagating in a poroelastic medium*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14049 "A semi-infinite hydraulic fracture propagating in a poroelastic medium")

Evgenii Kanin, Andreas M\"ori, Dmitry Garagash, Brice Lecampion

arXiv:2610.14049v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We solve the near-tip coupled hydromechanical problem of a hydraulic fracture propagating in a permeable poroelastic medium, represented locally as a semi-infinite crack moving at constant velocity. In the moving tip frame, all fracture fields are steady. The porous solid obeys linear poroelasticity, fracture propagation follows LEFM, fluid flow within the tensile fracture is governed by lubrication theory, and the fracturing and pore fluids are treated as identical Newtonian liquids. The primary unknowns are the fracture opening and the fluid displacement function along the fracture, determined from the resulting non-linear system of equations. In a viscosity--toughness scaling, the problem depends on four dimensionless parameters: a dimensionless permeability $\varrho$ (associated with the intensity of fluid leak-in), a dimensionless effective confining stress $\Sigma_0'$ (measuring the relative importance of fluid leak-off and leak-in), the drained--undrained Poisson's ratio contrast $\beta$, and the poroelastic stress coefficient $\eta$. The solution evolves from a drained, toughness-dominated near-tip asymptote with uniform net pressure to an undrained, storage-viscosity-dominated far-field asymptote. Numerical solutions are obtained over parameter ranges representative of various rock types. The fully coupled model is compared with simplified models, namely uncoupled and one-way coupled formulations, over distances from the fracture front spanning laboratory-to-field scales. The resulting relative difference maps in the $(\varrho,\Sigma_0')$ parameter space identify four characteristic regimes: negligible poroelastic effects, dominance of the direct mechanical poroelastic response, importance of the poroelastic backstress, and a fully coupled regime. The latter occurs mainly at large $\varrho$ and small $\Sigma_0'$, corresponding to strong leak-in relative to leak-off.



[267]. [*A Gauge-Inspired Coupled Fokker--Planck System for Multistate Stochastic Dynamics: Formalism and 1-D Numerical Implementation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14052 "A Gauge-Inspired Coupled Fokker--Planck System for Multistate Stochastic Dynamics: Formalism and 1-D Numerical Implementation")

Francesco Pio De Cosmo, Gianluca Di Natale, Leandro Cieri

arXiv:2610.14052v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Extending the Fokker--Planck equation to a coupled system of equations through non-Abelian symmetry groups offers a powerful theoretical framework for coupled multi-state stochastic systems. However, translating this abstract gauge field formulation into viable numerical predictions remains an unaddressed challenge. Motivated by our previous work on ice crystal habit evolution, in this work we bridge this gap by presenting the first numerical solver for an SU(2) gauge-coupled Fokker--Planck system in (1+1) dimensions. Using an operator-splitting scheme combined with a non-oscillatory advection solver, our algorithm strictly ensures probability conservation, non-negativity, and numerical stability. We demonstrate that non-Abelian gauge coupling acts as a cross-reactive feedback loop that fundamentally alters system dynamics, driving continuous probability exchange between states, suppressing localised density peaks, and forcing long-time spatial delocalisation. Validated across linear and quadratic coupling regimes up to strong interactions (g <= 1.0), this work establishes the foundational computational infrastructure needed to integrate complex multi-state physics. Overall, while our approach provides a key step toward improving ice cloud representation in atmospheric and climate models, the underlying physical framework and computational engine remain fundamentally general and multidisciplinary, offering a versatile paradigm for any domain requiring coupled multi-state stochastic equations.



[268]. [*Categorifying Quantum Spin Systems*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14165 "Categorifying Quantum Spin Systems")

Matthew Yu

arXiv:2610.14165v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We develop a homotopical framework for understanding the space of quantum spin systems based on topological $n$-nets, which assign associative unital algebras to local regions in an $n$-dimensional space in a manner that captures the locality structure of the underlying lattice theories. The net-theoretic perspective admits a natural categorification, in which the category of topological $n$-nets carries the structure of a higher Morita category. Moreover, the nets of greatest physical interest form a Karoubi-complete subcategory that shares many of the characteristic structural features of the category of finite TQFTs. Our main result identifies the group of locality-preserving symmetries of (3+1)-dimensional quantum spin systems (i.e. quantum cellular automata modulo stabilization, finite-depth quantum circuits, and translations) with the full Witt group of nondegenerate braided fusion categories, thereby answering a question posed by Haah.



[269]. [*Active Neural Matter: A Finite-Window Response Framework for Physical Systems and Distributed AI Workflows*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14549 "Active Neural Matter: A Finite-Window Response Framework for Physical Systems and Distributed AI Workflows")

Tianchi Chen, Shihua Yu

arXiv:2610.14549v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Physical systems and distributed AI workflows can retain past information, receive delayed feedback and combine conflicting signals. These features pose a common modeling problem: determining what information about past interactions a state must retain to account for future responses. Active Neural Matter (ANM) addresses this problem through a finite-window response framework. Forward analysis uses each system's dynamics to predict state and output responses to perturbations, separating propagation from output sensitivity. Inverse analysis tests whether a candidate state retains enough information for these responses. With the system and future inputs or interaction rules fixed, distinguishable responses from histories assigned the same state identify distinctions the representation must preserve. Their distributional separation gives a lower bound on the worst-case error of approximating behavior from that state. Uncertainty bounds distinguish rejection, support within a fixed tolerance on tested comparisons, and unresolved cases. Delayed-consensus simulations check analytical predictions of response amplitude and phase over a narrow range of delays; flocking illustrates dependence on the chosen observable. An exact delayed-return construction establishes a sufficient state for a specified program. The AI studies identify distinctions in interaction history and evidence order that a representation must preserve to reproduce observed response distributions. Tests on new histories in a controlled feedback task provide bounded support for a state specified by the task instructions; the instructed rule predicts every observed action. ANM connects response prediction to measurable information requirements, separating behavioral fidelity from task correctness and initial-state adequacy from a closed evolution law for that state.



[270]. [*Maximal Axion Optical Chirality Enabled by Degenerate Quasibound States in the Continuum*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14611 "Maximal Axion Optical Chirality Enabled by Degenerate Quasibound States in the Continuum")

Chang-Yin Ji, Chong Wang, Jiafang Li, Yugui Yao

arXiv:2610.14611v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Axion quasiparticles introduce magnetoelectric coupling in topological materials, yet their optical signatures are intrinsically weak, severely limiting experimental detection and practical exploitation. Here, we demonstrate maximal optical chirality of axion quasiparticles by coupling them with degenerate quasibound states in the continuum (qBICs). By integrating three-dimensional topological insulators (3DTIs) with achiral photonic crystal slabs (PCSs), we reveal that axion quasiparticles induce an effective Zeeman effect that lifts the degeneracy of achiral qBICs in PCSs, splitting them into two nondegenerate high-Q chiral qBICs with maximally opposite chirality. These result in a more than 10^3- and 200-fold increase in the polarization conversion efficiency and Faraday rotation angle, respectively, compared to the intrinsic chiroptical response of bare 3DTI films. Moreover, circular dichroism and nonreciprocal optical responses are revealed as new key signatures for probing axion quasiparticles in 3DTI-PCS coupled systems. Our results provide a definitive route for observing axion quasiparticles and topological physics, while establishing a promising platform for controlling optical polarization, spin, phase, and nonreciprocal responses.



[271]. [*Lifts of symmetries to minimal nondegenerate extensions are unique up to fermion attachment*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14847 "Lifts of symmetries to minimal nondegenerate extensions are unique up to fermion attachment")

Ran Luo, Jiahua Tian

arXiv:2610.14847v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Gauging fermion parity in a $(2+1)$-dimensional fermionic topological order requires lifting its symmetries to the resulting bosonic theory. We give a direct tensor-categorical proof that, for a slightly degenerate braided fusion category $\mathcal{C}$ and a fixed minimal nondegenerate extension ${\cal E}$, the restriction homomorphism $r\colon\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathrm{LR}}(\mathcal{E})\to \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathrm{LR}}(\mathcal{C})$ on classes of complex-linear locality-respecting (LR) braided autoequivalences is surjective with central kernel $\mathbb{Z}/2$, generated by fermion attachment. Thus each symmetry class has exactly two LR lift classes, related by attaching the fermion to every vortex. We apply graded Morita theory to reduce the kernel to two module types on the vortex sector, corresponding to tensoring with the vacuum or the fermion. We then prove relative rigidity to show that each type determines a unique LR class, which are distinguished by fermionic self-braiding even when their actions on simple-object labels coincide. We prove surjectivity by extending graph algebras in $\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{rev}}\boxtimes\mathcal{C}$ to Lagrangian algebras in $\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{rev}}\boxtimes\mathcal{E}$. Our results require neither unitarity nor a ribbon structure. We illustrate the lifting ambiguity in the Laughlin state, the $SO(3)_3$ theory and the Ising topological order.



[272]. [*Full Lyapunov Spectra of Random Neural Networks, Part I: Finite-Time and Asymptotic Theory*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14922 "Full Lyapunov Spectra of Random Neural Networks, Part I: Finite-Time and Asymptotic Theory")

Haoyu Albert Wang, Wei P. Dai

arXiv:2610.14922v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We develop a dynamical mean-field framework for the full finite-time and stationary asymptotic Lyapunov spectra of random recurrent neural networks. At finite observation time, an exact log-determinant representation of tangent-propagator singular values leads to a closed-contour matrix Dyson equation for the smoothed spectral tail. In the stationary setting, a cocycle representation yields an operator equation for the asymptotic cumulative spectrum. Both formulations retain complete local gain histories and the repeated action of quenched connectivity. Direct singular-value calculations and network QR spectra support the predictions in the regimes examined, with sampling uncertainty and numerical cutoffs assessed separately. The stationary equation also recovers the known maximal exponent, and controlled gain-history replay illustrates how temporal correlations redistribute instability. This work was carried out in collaboration with ChatGPT 5.6 Sol. Toward the end of preparation we became aware of David G. Clark's contemporaneous work, \emph{Lyapunov spectrum of random neural networks}[arXiv:2610.12426], which reports overlapping mean-field results for the full Lyapunov spectrum using a cavity approach.



[273]. [*Sub-Micron Gate $\beta-Ga_{2}O_{3}$ FinFETs with Current Density $>$ 750 mA/mm and Ion/Ioff Ratio $>$ $10^{9}$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15155 "Sub-Micron Gate $\beta-Ga_{2}O_{3}$ FinFETs with Current Density $>$ 750 mA/mm and Ion/Ioff Ratio $>$ $10^{9}$")

Nabasindhu Das, Cameron Gorsak, Pushpanshu Tripathi, Ankita Kashyap, Noor Jahan Nipu, Advait Gilankar, Hari Nair, Uttam Singisetti, Trevor Thornton, Nidhin Kurian Kalarickal

arXiv:2610.15155v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: In this work, we demonstrate submicron-gate $\beta-Ga_{2}O_{3}$ FinFETs that integrate Si delta-doped channels with selectively regrown n++ source/drain contacts. The devices employ 100 nm-wide fins to improve electrostatic control of a high sheet charge density channel of $3.3\times 10^{13}$ cm-2. A 150 nm-gate-length FinFET exhibits a maximum drain current density of 764 mA/mm at VGS = 1 V and VDS = 10 V, an on-resistance of 9.1 $\Omega$.mm, and an Ion/Ioff ratio exceeding $10^9$. The device also shows a peak transconductance of 43 mS/mm and de-embedded $f_{T}/f_{MAX}$ values of 6.3/2.0 GHz. Pulsed I-V measurements indicate ~20 % current collapse, attributed primarily to trapping at etched fin sidewalls and the $Al_{2} O_{3}$/ $\beta-Ga_{2}O_{3}$ interface. These results show that delta-doped $\beta-Ga_{2}O_{3}$ FinFETs can simultaneously achieve high drive current, strong current modulation, and GHz-frequency operation, providing a scalable device platform for future power and RF electronics.
