[274]. [*The R\'enyi area law conjecture is false below $\alpha=1$ in high dimensions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15199 "The R\'enyi area law conjecture is false below $\alpha=1$ in high dimensions")

Tomotaka Kuwahara

arXiv:2610.15199v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Area laws express the expectation that entanglement in gapped quantum ground states scales with a boundary rather than a volume. In one dimension this holds for every R\'enyi entropy, and the R\'enyi area-law conjecture asserts the same in higher dimensions for each fixed $0<\alpha<1$. Here we disprove this conjecture in every spatial dimension two and higher. We construct finite-range Hamiltonians with fixed local dimension, bounded interaction strength, unique ground states and a uniform spectral gap, whose ground states exhibit volume-law R\'enyi entanglement while obeying a von Neumann area law and remaining connected to product states through uniformly gapped local paths. The mechanism is error-space complexity: locally identifiable components of arbitrarily small weight can encode states with high entanglement complexity. We also establish the worst-case rank-error trade-off for approximate ground-state projectors, a key tool in one-dimensional area-law proofs. At fixed gap, the optimal Schmidt rank at projection error $\epsilon$ across a planar cut of cross-section $A$ scales as $\exp[\tilde{\Theta}((A\log\epsilon^{-1})^{2/3})]$ when $\log\epsilon^{-1}\gtrsim A$. Thus $\alpha=1$ is the sharp threshold for a universal R\'enyi area law within this adiabatically connected class.



[275]. [*Monogamy of quantum steering in exciton--cavity optomechanical system*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15233 "Monogamy of quantum steering in exciton--cavity optomechanical system")

Hamza Harraf, Mohamed Amazioug, Rachid Ahl Laamara

arXiv:2610.15233v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: In this work, we study a theoretical scheme to investigate the monogamy of quantum steering in a hybrid exciton--cavity optomechanical (EOM) system. Within the Gaussian-state formalism, we quantify the steerability among the exciton, photon, and mechanical modes in both the steady-state and dynamical regimes. We establish and verify CKW-type monogamy inequalities for Gaussian steering and demonstrate that the distribution of bipartite steering among the three modes can be controlled through the system parameters. We further examine the influence of thermal noise and temporal evolution on the steering properties and the corresponding monogamy relations. Our results show that increasing the temperature generally suppresses the strength of Gaussian steering and modifies the degree of steering sharing imposed by the CKW-type monogamy relations. In contrast, in the dynamical regime, the steerability can increase with evolution time over suitable temporal windows, indicating that the transient dynamics can be exploited to enhance quantum correlations. These results highlight the potential of hybrid exciton--optomechanical platforms for controllable quantum correlations and provide a promising route toward the generation and manipulation of multipartite quantum resources.



[276]. [*Localized Joule heating and carbonization process optimization of 3D printed polymer structures for lab-on-CMOS applications*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15238 "Localized Joule heating and carbonization process optimization of 3D printed polymer structures for lab-on-CMOS applications")

Mohammad Aminul Haque, Andalib Nizam, Nickolay Lavrik, Nicole Mcfarlane

arXiv:2610.15238v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Use of carbon electrodes integrated with CMOS technology has great promise for lab-on-chip, biomedical, environmental, and industrial applications. One method of developing carbon electrodes is to 3D print polymer nanostructures on a CMOS chip, and use a thermal annealing process to pyrolyze them into carbon at high temperature, approximately 950 {\deg}C. Such high temperature is detrimental to the CMOS sensor circuits, causing severe degradation. We present a novel solution of localized Joule heating of these polymer nanostructures to achieve complete carbonization of the electrodes. Both analytical and COMSOL simulations guided the experiments to characterize the carbonization process conditions. Partial carbonization was achieved at 500-600 {\deg}C of global temperature, and complete carbonization was achieved by localized Joule heating. Pyrolized nanostructures monolithic samples on a silica wafer were fabricated using standard material processing, such as 2-Photon Polymerization. Pyrolysis was achieved by Rapid Thermal Processing (RTP). Observations using optical microscopy, scanning electron microscopy, and Raman spectroscopy demonstrated the feasibility of polymer carbonization within a CMOS compatible temperature window.



[277]. [*Holographic theory of non-invertible gravitational anomalies*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15358 "Holographic theory of non-invertible gravitational anomalies")

Xiao-Gang Wen

arXiv:2610.15358v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We review a many-body formulation of gravitational anomalies as obstructions to realizing the complete low-energy data by a local {\it bosonic} lattice quantum system of the same dimension. In this formulation, an anomalous theory can be realized at the boundary of a gapped {\it bosonic} lattice bulk, whose topological order gives a one-to-one classification of the anomaly. An invertible bulk reproduces conventional anomaly inflow; a bulk with non-trivial topological excitations yields a non-invertible generalization. To describe the holographic theory of anomalies, we explain the distinction between uniquely defined topological order and many versions of topological quantum field theory and discuss the boundary-to-bulk map. As an application of the holographic theory of anomalies, we describe morphisms between local quantum theories (such as quantum field theories). These morphisms are built from a bulk domain wall and an invertible boundary junction, which give rise to a sandwich picture. Chiral boson/fermion theory, Laughlin edges, and boundaries of $\mathbb{Z}_2$ higher gauge theory with bosonic or fermionic point-like excitations illustrate the different kinds of information about non-invertible anomalies contained in transport coefficients, superselection sectors, and condensation data. In particular, a fermionic theory can be viewed as a theory with a particular non-invertible gravitational anomaly. Those examples also reveal that {\it symmetry $\sim$ gravitational anomaly $\sim$ topological order in one higher dimension}.



[278]. [*Breakdown of thermalization in quantum spin systems with long-range interactions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15530 "Breakdown of thermalization in quantum spin systems with long-range interactions")

Filippo Caleca, Fabio Mezzacapo, Tommaso Roscilde

arXiv:2610.15530v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Quantum thermalization of closed systems -- i.e. the relaxation of local properties to their thermodynamic value along the Hamiltonian evolution -- is generically expected in non-integrable systems at finite energy density. In this work we show that quantum spin systems with at least one spin component coupled through long-range ferromagnetic interactions, and a second spin component coupled to a field, cannot in general thermalize to a paramagnetic state whenever they have a finite, negative energy per spin. The long-range interactions we consider decay as a power law of the distance, $\sim r^{-\alpha}$ with $\alpha < d$, where $d$ is the number of spatial dimensions of the system. The lack of thermalization is generic to an infinite family of Hamiltonians, and it is not associated to any form of integrability. It can instead be understood by the anomalous features of the thermodynamics of the system in the paramagnetic phase, which cannot be reflected in the properties of the eigenstates. This leads to a breakdown of eigenstate typicality and of the eigenstate thermalization hypothesis. Interestingly, the non-thermal states emerging at long times in the dynamics display long-range multipartite entanglement, elevating the breakdown of thermalization to a resource.



[279]. [*Tersoff-NN: Hybrid Machine-Learned Bond-Order Extension of the Tersoff Potential*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15591 "Tersoff-NN: Hybrid Machine-Learned Bond-Order Extension of the Tersoff Potential")

Yusuke Nishimura, Sho Kubota, Kotaro Takematsu, Kentaro Hirai, Takanobu Watanabe

arXiv:2610.15591v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Accurate, robust, and efficient interatomic potentials are highly desirable for large-scale molecular dynamics simulations. Although machine-learning interatomic potentials can reproduce first-principles potential-energy surfaces with high accuracy, they typically incur higher computational costs than conventional empirical potentials and may be less reliable outside their training domains. We developed Tersoff-NN, a hybrid machine-learning potential for Si, which retains the analytical radial interactions and cutoff structure of the Tersoff potential while representing its environment-dependent bond-order factor using compact neural networks (NNs). Benchmarks for 32,768-atom systems show that Tersoff-NN is 4-12 times faster than the Behler-Parrinello-type NN potentials tested in this study. Tersoff-NN improves the short-range structure of liquid Si relative to the conventional Tersoff potential, closely reproduces the ab initio radial and angular distributions, and reproduces the equilibrium structure of diamond Si and qualitative energetic trends among several crystalline phases. Furthermore, melt-quench simulations reveal a disordered solid with predominantly four-coordinated, tetrahedral-like local environments. Additionally, characteristic reconstruction motifs are retained on Si surfaces despite their exclusion from the training dataset. A direct comparison with an alternative atom-centered bond-order NN highlights the critical role of physical constraints in ensuring dynamic stability. Despite exhibiting comparable validation errors, the selected triplet-based architecture remains stable, while an alternative model exhibits unphysical bond-order growth and atomic collapse. These results confirm that learning only the bond-order term improves the description of diverse Si environments while retaining substantial computational efficiency and enhancing dynamical stability.



[280]. [*Intrinsic Orbital Nernst Effect in Real Photonic Bands*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15621 "Intrinsic Orbital Nernst Effect in Real Photonic Bands")

Tao Zhu

arXiv:2610.15621v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We predict an intrinsic orbital Nernst effect in reciprocal photonic lattices with real Bloch modes. In the collisionless regime, a thermal gradient generates a transverse angular-displacement response although band angular moments and ordinary Berry curvatures vanish. The response is carried by angular-density dipoles formed through virtual mixing of distinct spatial modes. Their correlation with band velocity survives thermal averaging and is independently recovered from the angular-density readout of unitarily evolved wave packets. Two controls expose the mechanism: a fixed-generator two-level system has zero antisymmetric intrinsic response, and flattening the dispersion suppresses transport without changing the eigenstates. A canonical scalar Maxwell calculation realizes the effect through symmetry-selected virtual channels, even when one band dominates the thermal population. These findings establish an orbital-dipole mechanism for intrinsic thermal angular transport in real photonic bands.



[281]. [*Financial Interactions and Collective States: Part III. Description of Dynamic Collective States*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15719 "Financial Interactions and Collective States: Part III. Description of Dynamic Collective States")

Pierre Gosselin (IF), A\"ileen Lotz

arXiv:2610.15719v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: In a series of papers, we analyzed the mechanisms of capital allocation and accumulation among firms, investors, and banks within the framework of Field Economics. At the macro level, we found that multiple collective states may emerge, each organized in groups of agents characterized by distinct levels of disposable capital, returns, and stakes across sectors. This multiplicity of collective states reflects the inherent instability and default-prone nature of financial markets. It also underscores the necessity of transitions among these states. These transitions were not fully accounted for in our previous description of collective states, where we only considered marginal fluctuations around equilibrium levels. To fill this gap, the present paper aims to characterize the possible dynamic mechanisms underlying transitions between these collective states. It reviews and extends our previous analysis by examining their probability and stability. By introducing the notion of dynamic sub-collective states, it explores potential patterns of transition-such as morphing, merging, or splitting-among collective states. This study thus provides the foundational tools and analytical framework that will be employed to develop a field-theoretic description of dynamic collective states.



[282]. [*Nonperturbative optomechanics with thin, weakly reflective intracavity dielectrics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15749 "Nonperturbative optomechanics with thin, weakly reflective intracavity dielectrics")

Andrew Laeuger, Nancy Aggarwal

arXiv:2610.15749v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Levitated dielectric sensors are often treated as perturbative probes of a pre-existing optical field. We show that this picture fails for a novel sensor geometry: a dielectric plate whose transverse extent captures the full optical mode. Even an optically thin sensor with modest refractive index can heavily restructure the intracavity fields, effectively partitioning a cavity into two subcavities with substantial cross-coupling and producing new optical resonances and trapping sites with properties which deviate from the standard intuition. Conventional perturbative expressions for the cavity frequency shift and optomechanical coupling depart from the exact solution at thicknesses as small as $\sim\lambda/(10n)$. Furthermore, cavity locking decreases the coupling and increases the trap stiffness by orders of magnitude, and the coupled subcavity structure permits appreciable optical cooling at zero detuning, a distinct behavior unique to this nonperturbative regime. These effects, sharing a common origin, establish a distinct regime of cavity optomechanics in which the sensor is no longer merely a probe of the cavity field, but an element that helps define it.



[283]. [*From Lyapunov Growth to Weak Quantum Chaos*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15880 "From Lyapunov Growth to Weak Quantum Chaos")

Sa\v{s}o Grozdanov, Toma\v{z} Prosen

arXiv:2610.15880v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Local out-of-time-order correlators (OTOCs) measure the growth of initially small commutators. For bounded local operators, this growth ends when the local commutator becomes of order one, but in an extended system the region over which it is large can continue to expand. We study this spatial proliferation using three related observables: the local squared commutator $C_r(t)$, its spatial sum $\bar C(t) = \sum_r C_r(t)$, and the symmetrically regularised connected dOTOC $D(t)$ of two extensive observables. We show that Lieb--Robinson locality gives $\bar C(t) \lesssim t^d$. We then prove that, in a Gibbs state with uniform exponential clustering, the full dOTOC obeys the same bound, $D(t) \lesssim t^d$, improving our earlier suboptimal $t^{3d}$ bound. We illustrate these results in two quantum many-body systems: a Brownian coupled-cluster model and a finite-temperature SYK chain. In both cases, an exact disorder symmetry relates $D(t)$ to $\bar C(t)$ and allows us to follow the same extensive observable from the pre-scrambling Lyapunov regime into the post-scrambling growth associated with an expanding saturated region. This late-time power-law growth is the concrete realisation of the weak quantum chaos introduced in our earlier work.



[284]. [*Transfer learning-aided, artificial intelligence approach to evaluate optical properties of materials for classical and quantum photonic applications*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15904 "Transfer learning-aided, artificial intelligence approach to evaluate optical properties of materials for classical and quantum photonic applications")

Mohammad Reza Habibi, Luca Sapienza, Juan C. Vasquez

arXiv:2610.15904v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: In this study, an artificial intelligence-assisted evaluation of the refractive indices of different materials is performed. The evaluation of the refractive index is necessary to design photonic devices for various classical and quantum photonic applications, and to theoretically analyse experimental results. The refractive index is one of the crucial parameters that affect the interaction of light with matter. Although the refractive index can be measured experimentally, its estimation via computational means provide the advantage of not requiring costly instrumentation, trained researchers and could avoid potential laboratory-based uncertainties. Therefore, in this study, artificial intelligence is implemented to estimate the optical constants of two types of materials, i.e., noble metals, and semiconductors. To this end, an artificial neural network is used to estimate the real and the imaginary parts of the refractive index of a specific material for various wavelengths of light. Then, the knowledge of the trained neural network is employed to initiate and train new neural networks to estimate the refractive indices of other materials. For the training of a neural network, various methods can be deployed. However, the application of the training can be a time-consuming process. A transfer learning approach is here implemented to provide a faster training solution for the problem under study. The implementation of transfer learning allows training the neural networks for a new material with higher speed or accuracy when compared to non-knowledge transfer-based training. Our results show the efficiency of the proposed technique in accurately evaluating the real and imaginary parts of the refractive index of different materials.



[285]. [*A clarification of Fermi's formula for the maximum of partial sums*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15935 "A clarification of Fermi's formula for the maximum of partial sums")

Alessandro Lodi

arXiv:2610.15935v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: In Fermi's 1926 note, the expression assigned to the probability that all partial sums remain below a threshold is the complementary crossing probability. We obtain the correct probability from the same diffusion equation and locate the inconsistency in the final integration. We then state the limit for independent increments under the Lindeberg condition. A counterexample with smooth densities shows that small individual variances do not suffice to justify this limit. The probabilistic results are classical; the purpose of this note is to reconcile the stated event with the printed calculation and to make the sufficient hypotheses for the discrete limit explicit.



[286]. [*Measuring higher-order bulk topology in a programmable quantum simulator*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16046 "Measuring higher-order bulk topology in a programmable quantum simulator")

Pratik Sathe, Niclas Heinsdorf, Majid Kheirkhah, Rahul Deshpande, Fabio Altomare, Andrew J. Berkley, Martin Dehn, Richard Harris, Emile Hoskinson, Stefan Paul, Gabriel Poulin-Lamarre, Jack Raymond, Chris Rich, Mohammad H. Amin, Marcel Franz, Andrew D. King

arXiv:2610.16046v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Topological insulators lie outside the standard Landau paradigm: Instead of being characterized by local order parameters and spontaneous symmetry breaking, their defining properties are encoded in the global structure of their quantum states and manifested as robust boundary states. Crystalline higher-order topological insulators can extend this principle by hosting localized corner modes in two and higher dimensions. Although these boundary modes can be detected spectroscopically through the local density of states, determining the underlying bulk topology, for example through nested Wilson loops, remains an outstanding experimental challenge. Here we use up to 728 qubits in a superconducting analogue-digital quantum simulator to realize and probe a hierarchy of one-, two-, and three-dimensional topological insulators governed by effective tight-binding Hamiltonians. By reconstructing complex propagators from real-space measurements, we obtain complementary boundary and bulk characterizations: local densities of states reveal localized boundary modes, whereas experimentally reconstructed band projectors yield bulk topological invariants, culminating in a three-dimensional octupole moment extracted through third nested Wilson loops. These results establish a route to realizing, characterizing, and manipulating topologically protected states in programmable three-dimensional quantum matter.



[287]. [*Permanent Thermodynamic Mpemba Inversion from Quantum-Jump Feedback*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16064 "Permanent Thermodynamic Mpemba Inversion from Quantum-Jump Feedback")

Jincheng Lu, Chen Wang

arXiv:2610.16064v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We show that a common quantum-jump feedback protocol can permanently reverse the nonequilibrium free-energy ordering of two prescribed initial states while preserving their Gibbs equilibrium. The construction exploits collective compensation between monitored channels: their feedback corrections cancel at equilibrium but remain active during relaxation. This balance preserves free-energy monotonicity while allowing the relaxation modes to be reshaped. We prove that eliminating the complete slow-sector contribution of the initially higher-free-energy state, while retaining it for the comparison state, produces distinct asymptotic decay exponents. Their free-energy ratio then vanishes at long times, guaranteeing a permanent thermodynamic quantum Mpemba inversion under the same controlled dynamics. A thermal qutrit realizes the mechanism through coherence interference and exhibits distinct detection thresholds for spectral selection and exact slow-sector cancellation under feedback retuning.



[288]. [*Fisher information and the geometry of memorization in neural networks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16161 "Fisher information and the geometry of memorization in neural networks")

Daniel T. Bernstein, Chase Goddard, Luca Di Carlo, Vudtiwat Ngampruetikorn, David J. Schwab

arXiv:2610.16161v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: A fruitful approach towards understanding generalization in machine learning involves characterizing how models separate generalizable structure from idiosyncratic or even corrupted features of training data. Fisher information has emerged as a diagnostic tool for this separation, leveraging loss geometry to distinguish generalization from memorization. Here we investigate this connection in vision models trained on CIFAR-10 with controlled noise that can be learned only through memorization. We show that projecting model weights onto the top Fisher eigenvectors decouples general task performance from noisy sample memorization, and we investigate how this decoupling presents differentially across layers. We show that the same phenomena are present in small transformers trained to solve a synthetic in-context learning task, and are reproduced in closed form in a simple, analytically solvable linear model. Our results reveal that Fisher information captures aspects of the geometric structure underlying how neural networks allocate capacity between generalization and memorization.



[289]. [*Probe Signatures of Quantum Chaos with Quantum Sensing*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16181 "Probe Signatures of Quantum Chaos with Quantum Sensing")

Isa\'ias Vallejo-Fabila, Jing Yang

arXiv:2610.16181v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: The spectral form factor (SFF), which is the Fourier transform of the two-point spectral correlation, is a fundamental diagnostics of quantum chaos. Nevertheless, its experimental detection in quantum many-body systems was less unexplored until recently. Here, we establish a relationship between the SFF with the quantum Fisher information (QFI) by means of quantum sensing protocols. Furthermore, when the eigenvalues and eigenstates of the disordered Hamiltonian are statistically independent, we prove that QFI is typical both in short and long timescales while numerics suggest that it is also typical at intermediate timescales, which ensures self-averaging of QFI. The self-averaging property renders the experimental measurement of QFI with our protocol less noisy in contrast with the direct measurement of SFF which is known to be non-self-averaging. Our results reveal an intriguing connection between quantum noise and the signatures of quantum chaos.



[290]. [*A Low-Temperature Tool for Acousto-electric Detection*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16262 "A Low-Temperature Tool for Acousto-electric Detection")

Rohit Mandlik, Sunil Nair

arXiv:2610.16262v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We have developed an apparatus for the measurement of the acousto-electric effect (AEE) from room temperature to liquid nitrogen temperatures. The AEE is excited by bulk acoustic waves (BAWs) for which a lithium niobate piezoelectric actuator is employed. A very low background noise level is realized through careful electrical and mechanical design of the setup. The validity of the setup is tested by measuring the AEE in heavily doped P-type and N-type silicon. The opposite sign of the AEE signal was seen for both samples, showing dependence of the signal on the type of charge carrier being dragged by acoustic waves. The signal showed quadratic dependence on the driving voltage, which is consistent with the AEE literature. Further, a temperature dependence of the AEE signal was measured, which exhibits a monotonic increase with a rise in temperature. The ability to probe the AEE in bulk samples at low temperatures makes this setup a promising tool to investigate the AEE and related phenomena in different materials at cryogenic temperatures.



[291]. [*Finite-time quantum equilibration from local correlations*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16351 "Finite-time quantum equilibration from local correlations")

Piotr \'Cwikli\'nski

arXiv:2610.16351v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: An isolated quantum system evolves reversibly, yet a small subsystem can lose nearly all information about its initial state. Conserved quantities may retain some of this information, depending on how strongly they overlap with local observables. Here we consider a subsystem in an arbitrary initial state coupled to a maximally mixed bath. We show that the worst-input mean-square equilibration error, maximised over window origins, is bounded from above and below by the mean square of a complete local autocorrelation, with the stationary part subtracted. A correlation record over a finite interval therefore controls the equilibration error in every interval of the same length. The result allows arbitrary degeneracies of energies and gaps. We apply it to two static Hamiltonian families. For fixed spin subsystems in the one-particle sector of a bounded-range periodic clock, fixed-accuracy thermalisation is guaranteed on windows that grow only slightly faster than linearly with system size, despite exponentially small positive energies. A random commuting model with logarithmic interaction range retains an extensive set of conserved observables. Its expected mean-square error is bounded by a finite-size term and an inverse-time term. The inverse-time dependence is unavoidable because of the initial transient. These examples show how local relaxation can coexist with exact conservation laws and a finely spaced spectrum.



[292]. [*Almost invariant subspaces in the topologically non-trivial setting: a pedestrian approach*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16393 "Almost invariant subspaces in the topologically non-trivial setting: a pedestrian approach")

Horia Cornean, Gabriele Peluso, Mikkel Hviid Thorn

arXiv:2610.16393v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We present a self-contained, algorithmic construction of almost invariant subspaces [Panati et al. (2003), De Nittis and Panati (2010), Freund and Teufel (2016)] for Hofstadter-like Hamiltonians $H_b $ acting on $\ell^2(\mathbb{Z}^2,\mathbb{C}^n)$. More precisely, for rational values of $b_0/(2\pi)$ we describe an iterative procedure that takes a ``locally gapped" projection $P_{0}$ (possibly topologically non-trivial) which commutes with $H_{b_0}$, into a projection $P_{\varepsilon}$ that commutes with $H_{b_0+\varepsilon}$ up to an error of order $\varepsilon^\infty$.
  Our construction insures that the Chern number of $P_{\varepsilon}$ equals the Chern number of $P_0$ as long as $P_\varepsilon$ exists. Moreover, its integrated density of states varies linearly with $\varepsilon$ (the ``St\v{r}eda formula"). We also show that any spatially localized Parseval frame of $P_{0}$ can be mapped into to a localized Parseval frame of $P_{\varepsilon}$.
  We choose to work with a discrete model in order to keep the technicalities to a minimum level, with no need for advanced pseudo-differential calculus. Nevertheless, we are confident that most of our results can be extended to (unbounded) magnetic (pseudo)differential operators, but the technical burden would be much heavier. This is left for future work.



[293]. [*PXRD-Bench: How Close Are We to Determining Crystal Structures from Powder Diffraction?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16480 "PXRD-Bench: How Close Are We to Determining Crystal Structures from Powder Diffraction?")

Dongyeop Woo, Nofit Segal, Nayoung Kim, Minkyu Kim, Rafael Gomez-Bombarelli, Sungsoo Ahn

arXiv:2610.16480v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Determining crystal structures from powder X-ray diffraction (PXRD) would enable routine characterization of newly synthesized materials, including those absent from reference databases. Generative models offer a promising approach, but existing evaluations remain fragmented and largely assume single-phase samples with known composition. To fill this gap, we introduce PXRD-Bench, a unified benchmark that extends evaluation to unknown per-element atom counts and recovery of both structures in two-phase mixtures. It provides approximately 4.1 million training structures with simulated PXRD, tests generalization to unseen compositions and structural prototypes, and evaluates recovery from experimental PXRD after excluding structurally matching training entries. To provide a strong reference baseline, we also introduce Bragglite, a PXRD-to-structure model developed through a systematic study of PXRD modeling choices. It achieves a 44.2\% Top-1 match rate on experimental RRUFF patterns with known composition, 24.6\% above the strongest prior baseline. Further analysis yields several key findings: some models fail to benefit from experimental PXRD, while generalization to unseen structural prototypes and recovery from two-phase mixtures remain challenging. Together, our benchmark, systematic studies, and Bragglite clarify the capabilities and limitations of current PXRD-to-structure models.



[294]. [*Asymmetric $q$-Gaussian Distributions from Tsallis Maximum Entropy*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16663 "Asymmetric $q$-Gaussian Distributions from Tsallis Maximum Entropy")

Francesco Cesarone, Massimiliano Corradini, Nicol\`o Giunta, Serena Rocci

arXiv:2610.16663v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Within the framework of non-extensive statistical mechanics, $q$-Gaussian distributions arise as maximum-entropy solutions of Tsallis entropy under suitable escort moment constraints. Although widely used in physics, biology, geology, finance, and related fields, standard $q$-Gaussians are intrinsically symmetric and cannot capture asymmetric fluctuations often observed in empirical data.
  We ask whether asymmetry can arise from the Tsallis maximum-entropy principle without externally skewing or joining distributions. We show that this is achieved by retaining the Tsallis entropy and imposing a global escort mean together with distinct downside and upside escort semi-variances.
  The key step is to reformulate the problem of maximum Tsallis entropy in terms of the escort density. The nonlinear escort constraints then become affine, while maximizing Tsallis entropy becomes equivalent to maximizing a strictly concave functional over a convex feasible set. Consequently, any admissible stationary solution is the unique global maximizer of the corresponding variational problem.
  The finite-sample approximation properties of the proposed asymmetric $q$-Gaussian are studied through Monte Carlo experiments using synthetic data generated from Azzalini skew-Student distributions. Compared with the classical symmetric $q$-Gaussian, the asymmetric specification systematically provides a closer approximation to synthetic asymmetric heavy-tailed distributions.
  Finally, we fit the model to return series from major equity indices, a commodity, and cryptocurrencies, and compare its in-sample goodness of fit with that of a symmetric $q$-Gaussian. The results show that the asymmetric model offers a closer in-sample description of unconditional return distributions, most clearly in the tails.



[295]. [*TheoPhysEval: Assessing AI agents at autonomous theoretical physics problem solving*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16733 "TheoPhysEval: Assessing AI agents at autonomous theoretical physics problem solving")

A. Ramesh Chandra, Aaghaz Mahajan, Ainesh Sanyal, Mianqi Wang, Shubhadeep Chakraborty, Sudip Chakraborty, Shailesh Chandrasekharan, Rama Govindarajan, Akshit Goyal, Jonathan J. Heckman, Yasir Iqbal, Divya Jaganathan, Saumav Kapoor, Andreas Karch, Pushkar Khandare, Sandeep Krishna, Anupam Kundu, Alok Laddha, Tongyan Lin, Swarnendu Maity, Nilmani Mathur, Shani Nadir Meynet, Alessandro Mininno, Abhijeet Mishra, Vidur Niranjan, Abhishodh Prakash, Pratik Rath, Durbar Roy, Debargha Sarkar, Srikanth Sastry, Gary Shiu, Aninda Sinha, Thomas Van Riet, Praneeth Netrapalli, Suvrat Raju

arXiv:2610.16733v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We present a suite of 50 unpublished research-level theoretical physics problems with verifiable answers. Without AI assistance, a typical problem in our collection could be solved by a world expert in about a day or less but would take considerably longer for experts in adjacent specialties. We test the ability of off-the-shelf AI agents to tackle these problems autonomously, without human hints. When given access to tools available in a realistic setting, including the ability to search the literature and write scripts for numerical and symbolic work, the best-performing agent, GPT-6-Astra harnessed in Codex, obtains the final answers with a near-perfect accuracy of 98% at an average cost under USD 3 per problem. On average, agents show only small changes in accuracy when denied web search and access to background research papers, suggesting significant memorization of the literature. These results suggest that state-of-the-art agents can accurately perform concrete calculations required across theoretical physics research. We comment on the broader implications of these findings and discuss possible explanations for the current lack of agent-led breakthroughs in theoretical physics despite their outstanding problem-solving abilities.



[296]. [*Iterated Graph Systems (III): Alexander-Orbach conjecture on hierarchical lattices*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16875 "Iterated Graph Systems (III): Alexander-Orbach conjecture on hierarchical lattices")

Ziyu Neroli

arXiv:2610.16875v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Can the Alexander-Orbach conjecture hold on hierarchical lattices? We construct Gromov-Hausdorff-Prokhorov scaling limits of critical percolation clusters on hierarchical lattices in the chemical metric and determine the typical spectral dimensions of the associated diffusions. These dimensions are dense in $(1,2)$; however, outside a narrow region in dimension space, we prove that the Alexander-Orbach conjecture fails on every hierarchical lattice under consideration.



[297]. [*Out-of-time-order hydrodynamics and scrambling*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16888 "Out-of-time-order hydrodynamics and scrambling")

Marta Bucca, Akash Jain, M\'ark Mezei, Alexey Milekhin

arXiv:2610.16888v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Hydrodynamics as an effective field theory (EFT) has been immensely successful for predicting the behaviour of time-ordered correlation functions in the long-time, large-distance regime. In this paper, we analyse the predictions of hydrodynamics for scrambling as probed by out-of-time order correlators (OTOCs). To do so, we consider an all-derivative order generalisation of the Schwinger-Keldysh EFT of hydrodynamics extended to a four-fold time contour required to capture OTOCs. A crucial ingredient of the construction is a local SL(2,R) symmetry, which has previously been suggested as a mechanism for scrambling. We also derive this exact EFT from the microscopic formulation of the maximally chaotic low-temperature limit of the SYK lattice, thereby determining all transport coefficients for this model. As a novel application, we compute the OTOC of energy currents, find that its exponential growth saturates the chaos bound, and match it to expectations based on a shockwave (or scramblon) effective description. Our work shows that the universality of hydrodynamics degrees of freedom extends beyond traditional time-ordered correlators to more complex observables such as OTOCs.



[298]. [*Photon-stimulated decay of $\chi$-matched ancillas causes unheralded bit flips in bosonic codes*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17017 "Photon-stimulated decay of $\chi$-matched ancillas causes unheralded bit flips in bosonic codes")

Aleksandr Gulkanov

arXiv:2610.17017v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Bosonic codes often read out their syndrome with a transmon ancilla whose computational levels $g$ and $f$ have equal parity and matched dispersive shifts: a decay $f\to e$ is then heralded and leaves the code intact, while the direct $f\to g$ is forbidden by parity. We show that the photons of the code open this decay at first order in the ancilla's decay rate. Dressing by the code mode lets the charge noise that limits the ancilla's $T_1$ drive a photon-stimulated Raman decay $|f,n\rangle\to|g,n+1\rangle$, at $(n+1)(\chi_{ge}/2g_{01})^2$ times the $f\to e$ rate for a smooth noise spectrum. When the matching is set by the detuning, the decay costs $\approx\Gamma_{fe}/\alpha$ per gate and photon, whatever the coupling strength; a deliberate mismatch or matching by a drive lowers this cost, and a qubit ancilla has no such channel. The opened decay is unheralded and flips a cat with probability $\approx 1/2$; being first order, it overtakes the second-order double decay $f\to e\to g$ as the ancilla improves. In a microscopic model of the Ocelot repetition cat code, it makes the bit-flip floor of the gates grow linearly with the cat size, by a factor 1.2-1.7 from four to ten photons, which would limit the noise bias to $\sim 10^3$. The published gate data end at four photons and do not yet test this growth.



[299]. [*Solvent Fluctuations Enable Photoionization of Aqueous Nitrite Microdroplets*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17021 "Solvent Fluctuations Enable Photoionization of Aqueous Nitrite Microdroplets")

Brandon J. Wallace, Eric R. Heller, Alex Prophet, Pranav Ballapuram, Ashok Ajoy, David T. Limmer, Kevin R. Wilson

arXiv:2610.17021v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Nitrite photochemistry in atmospheric aerosols and natural waters is a major source of hydroxyl radicals and reactive nitrogen oxides. Contrary to the prevailing view that in the environment aqueous nitrite photochemistry proceeds exclusively through photoexcitation followed by homolytic bond cleavage, we show that visible light directly ionizes aqueous nitrite in individual micron-sized droplets. Although the photoionization cross section is exceptionally small, this pathway is observable at the high photon flux densities accessible in single-droplet experiments. Molecular simulations reveal that this is because photoionization absorption has an exceptionally long wavelength tail due to the large solvent reorganization energy in aqueous solutions. Kinetic modeling of an aqueous-phase reaction mechanism quantitatively accounts for the observed chemistry and predicts the formation of nitrate, hydroxyl radicals, and solvated electrons. The overlap of the predicted broad photoionization continuum with the visible solar spectrum further suggests that visible-light photoionization is an overlooked source of nitrogen oxides, solvated electrons, and oxidants in atmospheric aerosols, snowpack, and natural waters. Under dilute conditions, where dissolved oxygen is the main sink for solvated electrons, photoionization is expected to be the dominant photochemical loss pathway for nitrite.



[300]. [*Entanglement Sudden Death in Gapped Ground States*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17038 "Entanglement Sudden Death in Gapped Ground States")

Robert Ferydouni, Thomas Jackson, Samuel O. Scalet

arXiv:2610.17038v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Spatial sudden death of entanglement refers to the disappearance of quantum correlations between subsystems after a certain distance called the entanglement length. Specifically, given a density matrix $\rho_{ABC}$ on a tripartite system $ABC$ with $B$ spatially separating $A$ and $C$, the entanglement length is the distance $\ell(A,C)$ after which the reduced density matrix $\rho_{AC}$, given by tracing out the $B$ subsystem, is separable. We show in the case of gapped ground states in any dimension that the entanglement length is finite on finite subsystems. Restricting to one-dimensional systems, we prove that normal matrix product states have a finite entanglement length independent of $A$ and $C$, and that a finite pure state with such uniform entanglement length has a matrix product state representation with bond dimension independent of $A$ and $C$. Consequently, in one dimension, entanglement length distinguishes matrix product states from all other pure states.



[301]. [*Fermionic non-Gaussianity via computable measures and experimentally accessible bounds*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17219 "Fermionic non-Gaussianity via computable measures and experimentally accessible bounds")

Leela Ganesh Chandra Lakkaraju, Naga Dileep Varikuti, Philipp Hauke

arXiv:2610.17219v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Interacting fermions owe both their non-trivial correlations and their computational power to their departure from Gaussianity. Yet, quantifying that departure geometrically faces two challenges: the Gaussian manifold is non-convex, and perturbative treatments are confined to weak interactions. Here, we introduce a geometric measure of fermionic non-Gaussianity, the fidelity distance of a target state to the manifold of fermionic Gaussian states. We construct an iteration formalism to numerically access it, applicable also at strong coupling. Further, we prove a sharpened operator Jensen inequality, of interest in its own right. We use it to derive a witness that involves only two- and four-point fermionic correlators. Numerical benchmarks on the one-dimensional Fermi--Hubbard model show that the quality of the witness improves with system size and, for strong coupling, is tighter than antiflatness. Our results open a scalable route to charting non-Gaussianity of strongly-interacting fermionic quantum simulators.



[302]. [*Whither quantum advantage? New classical lower bounds for Max-Cut on large-girth 3-regular graphs*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17236 "Whither quantum advantage? New classical lower bounds for Max-Cut on large-girth 3-regular graphs")

Flaviano Morone, Shivang Rawat, Dries Sels

arXiv:2610.17236v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Every 3-regular graph of girth at least $g$ has a cut containing at least a fraction $M_g$ of its edges, and the best rigorous bounds at large girth are $0.912\le \lim_{g\to\infty}M_g \le 0.92386$. Lower bounds on $M_g$ follow from the performance of local algorithms: on a graph of girth $g \ge 2p+2$ a depth-$p$ local algorithm sees the same tree around every edge, so its expected cut fraction on that tree bounds $M_g$ from below. We apply this strategy to the Variational Iterative Rotation Algorithm (VIRAL), a classical local algorithm, and evaluate it on the depth-$p$ tree for every even depth up to $p=32$. The resulting bounds exceed those recently obtained from the Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm (QAOA) for every $p\ge 4$, by $0.005$ to $0.011$, and they extend the range of girths covered from $g=36$ to $g=66$. Notably, at $g=66$ we obtain $M_{66} \ge 0.9127$ at $99.99999\%$ confidence level, which raises the asymptotic lower bound from $0.912$ to $0.9127$.



[303]. [*Krylov Complexity and Quantum Many-body Scars in Non-Hermitian Kinetically Constrained Models*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17328 "Krylov Complexity and Quantum Many-body Scars in Non-Hermitian Kinetically Constrained Models")

Arkaprava Sil, Rudraksh Kaushik, Souvik Kundu, Prasant Mallik, Sudipto Singha Roy

arXiv:2610.17328v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Thermalization in closed quantum systems and its breakdown are central to understanding the emergence of statistical behavior in isolated many-body systems. Kinetic constraints can hinder thermalization by restricting the allowed dynamics and giving rise to non-ergodic behavior. While various aspects of ergodicity breaking in kinetically constrained models have been extensively studied in Hermitian systems, their behavior in non-Hermitian kinetically constrained models remains less explored. In particular, the inequivalence of left and right eigenstate ensembles in non-Hermitian systems raises the question of whether they exhibit qualitatively different dynamical and eigenstate properties. In this work, we study non-Hermitian generalizations of the paradigmatic PXP and PPXPP models, focusing on their Krylov complexity, eigenstate structure, and dynamics. By exploiting the Arnoldi's algorithm, we find distinct behavior of Krylov complexity in the left and right eigenstate ensembles as non-Hermiticity is increased: in the right ensemble, it exhibits persistent oscillations with an increasing period, while in the left ensemble, it gradually saturates. The characteristic arch-shaped profile of the Arnoldi coefficients is also modified differently in the two ensembles, with a Hermitian-like arch re-emerging in the left ensemble at large non Hermiticity. Additionally, in the non-Hermitian PXP model, the right ensemble retains the characteristic scar structure, while the left ensemble develops additional non-scar eigenstates with anomalously low entanglement and large overlap with the vacuum state. These findings reveal that non-Hermiticity can affect Krylov dynamics and eigenstate structure differently in the left and right ensembles, demonstrating the need to treat them separately when characterizing non-Hermitian kinetically constrained systems.



[304]. [*Progress in Cavitated Front Gridline Ag Paste for PERC Solar Cell Architecture*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17341 "Progress in Cavitated Front Gridline Ag Paste for PERC Solar Cell Architecture")

Donald Intal, Sandra Huneycutt, Abasifreke Ebong, Dana Hankey, Marshall Tibbetts

arXiv:2610.17341v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: This paper reports on the progress made with the cavitated silver paste on passivated emitter rear contact cells (PERC) to reduce the series resistance. Thus, electrical and microscopy characterizations are used to examine these resistive losses. Fifteen 252 cm2 PERC cells were fabricated for this assessment. Under standard test conditions, these cells achieved a mean conversion efficiency of 21.87% and a maximum of 22.07%. The mean measured fill factor was 79.98%, compared with a pseudo-fill factor of 83.60%, giving a mean deficit of 3.62 percentage points. Fill factor decreased with increasing area-normalized series resistance, which ranged from 0.53 to 0.83 ohm-cm2, while open-circuit voltage and short-circuit current remained unvaried. These electrical trends support resistive current extraction as the principal contributor to fill-factor variation. Electroluminescence imaging revealed localized emission nonuniformity. Cross-sectional scanning transmission electron microscopy showed variation in the glass interfacial layer, while scanning electron microscopy identified micrometer and sub micrometer silver particles. Conductive atomic force microscopy after sequential removal of the bulk metal and glass revealed spatially heterogeneous current response. These observations are consistent with nonuniform electrical access at the contact, where restricted conducting pathways may concentrate on current and increase local voltage drops. The combined characterization identifies interfacial heterogeneity and the connectivity of conducting pathways as priorities for understanding and reducing extraction losses in cavitated silver metallization.



[305]. [*On the Statistical Mechanics of Attention*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17347 "On the Statistical Mechanics of Attention")

Jerry Yao-Chieh Hu, Yu-An Chen, Maojiang Su, Han Liu

arXiv:2610.17347v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We revisit the statistical mechanics of Transformer attention via modern Hopfield models. We present a free-energy formalism to characterize parameter regimes and critical thresholds for robust attention inference. The resulting free-energy landscape incorporates randomness in query states and attention weights. Attention then emerges as the equation of motion of the corresponding statistical-mechanical system (i.e., modern Hopfield model) through the standard free-energy minimization principle. This allows us to identify first-order phase transitions in attention between glassy and robust-inference phases. We further extend to attentions with stochastic queries and keys. Together, our results provide a rigorous statistical-mechanics framework for characterizing phase structure and inference reliability across a family of Transformer attention mechanisms.



[306]. [*Type I-II/II-I vortices from dimension-six self-interaction*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17437 "Type I-II/II-I vortices from dimension-six self-interaction")

A. Alonso-Izquierdo, A. Gonz\'{a}lez-Parra, K. Slawinska, A. Wereszczynski, T. Winyard

arXiv:2610.17437v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We show that adding a dimension-six self-interaction to the Abelian-Higgs or Ginzburg Landau model results in a remarkable and rich structure of multi-vortex solutions. Besides the standard type I (attractive) and type II (repulsive) regimes, separated by the Bogomol'nyi-Prasad-Sommerfeld limit (BPS), we identify two new possibilities: type I-II vortices, which attract at short distances and repel at long distances; and type II-I vortices, with the opposite behaviour. Specifically, within the type II-I regime, multi-vortex solutions form chunks of the Abrikosov lattice or clusters. Conversely, in the I-II regime, axially symmetric multi-vortices are stable; nevertheless, depending on the model parameters and topological charge (winding number), they can be either the ground state or not.



[307]. [*Imaging the topological sign of a Dirac mass with spin-polarized tunneling*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17464 "Imaging the topological sign of a Dirac mass with spin-polarized tunneling")

Payman Kazemikhah, Ruolun Zhang, Byunghoon Kim, Xiangyu Luo, Zachary Ducorsky, Yu-Peng Zhu, Md. Shafayat Hossain, M. Zahid Hasan

arXiv:2610.17464v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: A Dirac mass is characterized not only by its magnitude but also by its sign, which influences the Berry curvature, Hall response, and the propagation direction of chiral boundary modes. However, local spectroscopy of magnetic topological surfaces has so far accessed only the gap magnitude $|M|$. Here we show how the topological sign of $M$ can be determined locally. We first establish the sign information that spin-resolved tunneling can access and the reference required to obtain an absolute Hall sign. Although spin--orbital entanglement generally obscures the relation between measured spin and Dirac pseudospin, crystalline rotation symmetry alleviates this ambiguity at the band edge. On a $C_3$ axis, an axial tip couples to a fully spin-polarized state whose tunneling asymmetry directly reveals the sign of the occupied-band Berry curvature, independent of orbital composition. We further show that the zeroth Landau level provides an independent mass-sign measurement with an unpolarized tip, while opposite domains and their chiral domain-wall modes provide self-consistency checks. These results pave the way for imaging the locations where the topological character changes within a material, rather than merely identifying where a spectral gap appears.



[308]. [*Mathematical framework for simulation of non-perturbative cavity optomechanics with plate-like dielectrics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17475 "Mathematical framework for simulation of non-perturbative cavity optomechanics with plate-like dielectrics")

Andrew Laeuger, Nancy Aggarwal

arXiv:2610.17475v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Cavity optomechanical models typically treat a sensor either as a perturbative polarizable object in a pre-existing optical field, or solve the field at a fixed equilibrium and linearize in a fixed optical-mode basis. Neither self-consistently determines the spatially resolved cavity field, the optical force, the mechanical equilibrium, and the back-action when a finite-thickness dielectric plate intercepts the entire optical mode and restructures the intracavity field. In this companion paper to [1], we develop that treatment by extending the vacuum two-photon formalism to finite-thickness planar dielectrics, incorporating their Fresnel reflection, internal propagation, optical forces, and mechanical back-action directly into the field equations. We derive the carrier and sideband transfer functions needed to describe the coupled optical-mechanical system, including optomechanical cooling and the response to external perturbations. The result is a systematic starting point for calculating detector response and noise budgets for cavity experiments with intracavity dielectrics, including gravitational wave detectors deploying levitated sensors.



[309]. [*Elastic Properties of Titan Tholins Revealed by Brillouin Spectroscopy*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17564 "Elastic Properties of Titan Tholins Revealed by Brillouin Spectroscopy")

Mark A. Johnson, Jose R. Montes-Bojorquez, Adis Husic, Xinting Yu, Wenhao Su, Jia-Sen Hu, Ryan Blase, Edward Patrick, Alan Whittington, Jin S. Zhang

arXiv:2610.17564v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Titan tholins, complex organic solids synthesized by simulating photochemical processes in Titan's atmosphere, are widely regarded as analogs to organic surface materials on the Saturnian moon. Their mechanical properties are critical for understanding the evolution of Titan's geomorphology and crustal layering. Here, we report the first direct experimental determination of the complete set of aggregate elastic moduli of tholins using Brillouin spectroscopy. Measurements conducted on tholin powder samples yielded a bulk modulus of 11 (2) GPa and a shear modulus of 3.9 (5) GPa, from which we derive a Young's modulus of 11 (1) GPa and a Poisson's ratio of 0.34 (3). Thin-film samples synthesized under slightly different conditions yield similar results. Compared with other Titan-relevant organic compounds, tholins exhibit the highest elastic stiffness measured to date, exceeding even H2O ice in both bulk and shear moduli. These results support the interpretation that complex organics contribute significantly to the mechanical stability and transport behavior of Titan's equatorial dune sediments. Measurements following prolonged atmospheric exposure reveal persistent stiffening of tholins that remains after vacuum treatment, likely associated with minor chemical modification and physical reorganization, highlighting the importance of controlled sample handling for future laboratory investigations. This study also demonstrates the effectiveness of Brillouin spectroscopy as a non-destructive technique for characterizing planetary organic materials and provides new constraints relevant to future seismic and geophysical investigations by the Dragonfly mission.



[310]. [*Sampling from the Sherrington-Kirkpatrick Model up to Criticality*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17717 "Sampling from the Sherrington-Kirkpatrick Model up to Criticality")

Hang Du, Brice Huang, Sidhanth Mohanty, Amit Rajaraman

arXiv:2610.17717v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We prove that the simulated annealing algorithm efficiently samples from the Sherrington-Kirkpatrick model at inverse temperature $\beta < 1$ to an arbitrary inverse polynomial accuracy in total variation.



[311]. [*Theory of deeply subwavelength electromagnetic structuring of semiconductors due to thermal-ionization instability*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17847 "Theory of deeply subwavelength electromagnetic structuring of semiconductors due to thermal-ionization instability")

I. V. Oladyshkin, D. I. Kulshin, E. O. Smolina

arXiv:2610.17847v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We develop a theory of ionization-assisted instability that governs the reflection of intense electromagnetic pulses from a semiconductor surface. Local overheating of the material increases the free-carrier density and conductivity, which in turn enhances the absorption of the incident radiation and further amplifies local heating and avalanche ionization. At high pump repetition rates, the overheating accumulates from pulse to pulse, leading to a gradual rise of the local material temperature and free carrier density - a positive feedback loop that drives thermal runaway. However, this effect competes with diffraction of the pump field on the emerging perturbation of the dielectric permittivity or on the surface relief, which reduces the local electric field amplitude in the overheated regions, thus providing negative feedback. We show that the competition between heat diffusion and diffraction near-field weakening creates conditions for the exponential growth of temperature perturbations with a certain wavenumber determined by the pulse repetition rate, pump field, thermal conductivity and other material parameters. The derived analytical expressions accurately describe the spatial period of deeply subwavelength structures observed in recent experiments on silicon structuring by intense terahertz radiation, and reproduce qualitatively dependencies of their period on the pump intensity, the incident wavelength and the number of pump pulses.



[312]. [*Emergent Non-Biorthogonal Born Rule at Dynamical Quantum Phase Transitions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17855 "Emergent Non-Biorthogonal Born Rule at Dynamical Quantum Phase Transitions")

Wei-Wei Yang, Henning Schomerus, Jiangping Hu, Zhesen Yang

arXiv:2610.17855v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Non-Hermitian dynamics possesses distinct left and right eigenstates, but their normalization freedom obscures a direct probabilistic interpretation.
  Starting from a globally Hermitian system governed by the standard Born rule, we generalize dynamical quantum phase transitions (DQPTs) from a reference state to a physical subspace.
  This generalization provides a clear probabilistic interpretation of left and right states: when each state is normalized to itself, their mutual overlap determines a directly accessible dynamical transition rate.
  We discuss the new phenomena governed by this emergent non-biorthogonal Born rule and further identify a class of exceptional-point DQPTs (EP-DQPTs), at which the eigenstates of the critical propagator coalesce.
  Our theory places the probabilistic interpretation of non-Hermitian quantum mechanics on the firm footing of Hermitian quantum mechanics.



[313]. [*On-chip optical isolators*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17862 "On-chip optical isolators")

Abanoub Mikhail, Nikolay Solodovchenko, Sen Xu, Fan Zhang, Maksim Tumashov, Mingzhao Song, Sergei Makarov, Andrey Bogdanov

arXiv:2610.17862v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Optical isolation suppresses back-reflections that destabilize lasers, produce parasitic interference, and increase crosstalk in photonic systems. Although free-space and fibre-optic isolators are mature, no broadly adopted on-chip technology yet combines strong isolation, low insertion loss, broad bandwidth, compact footprint, and scalable fabrication. The difficulty is twofold: Lorentz reciprocity must be broken, and every relevant backward spatial, spectral, and polarization channel must be suppressed. This Review develops a common framework for assessing proposed solutions. We first distinguish pairwise nonreciprocity from intensity-asymmetric transmission and from true isolation using the constitutive relations and scattering matrix. We then compare three physical routes to an integrated nonreciprocal response: magneto-optical bias, externally driven space-time modulation, and nonlinear optical processes. Topological and non-Hermitian systems are treated as cross-cutting design frameworks that can route, protect, or enhance an underlying nonreciprocal response, but do not by themselves break reciprocity. Finally, we examine fundamental constraints and practical trade-offs among isolation, insertion loss, bandwidth, footprint, modal coverage, drive requirements, and fabrication compatibility. The resulting mechanism-based perspective connects reciprocity-breaking physics to the requirements of scalable photonic integrated circuits.



[314]. [*Deep Mesoscopic Dynamics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17885 "Deep Mesoscopic Dynamics")

Yue Zhao, Shiqi Wu, Yihang Ouyang, Sergey Grebenchuk, Magdalena Grzeszczyk, Xiaoxu Zhao, Kostya S. Novoselov, Maciej Koperski, Qianxiao Li

arXiv:2610.17885v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Microscopic simulations resolve spatial patterns but are feasible only over small domains and short timescales, whereas bulk descriptions erase those patterns through spatial averaging. Mesoscopic fields preserve this spatial organisation, but their effective equations are known only for selected microscopic systems and scaling limits. We introduce Deep Mesoscopic Dynamics (DeepMesoD), which uses neural networks to learn an effective mesoscopic field equation from microscopic trajectories at a prescribed coarse-graining scale. For the nonconserved dissipative dynamics studied here, DeepMesoD learns the free-energy functional with a spatial interaction kernel and a state-dependent dissipation operator constrained by the fluctuation-dissipation relation. We first validate the construction in the Ising-Kac model, where the learned mesoscopic equation recovers the known hydrodynamic limit and rollouts remain stable far beyond the training horizon. We then treat two systems without closed-form mesoscopic descriptions. For a two-dimensional Heisenberg ferromagnet governed by atomistic spin dynamics, DeepMesoD learns a mesoscopic model whose predictions match atomistic references up to 1 ns, and a rollout with an unseen initial condition remains bounded up to 25 ns. For layered CrBr$_3$, the learned kernel captures the suppression and sign reversal of interlayer coupling at a stacking fault whose location and strength are not supplied as inputs. Controlled changes to the learned interaction show how fault number and placement shape multistep magnetic responses, complementing our magneto-photoluminescence measurements. Across the two atomistic systems, DeepMesoD achieves a three-to-four-order-of-magnitude reduction in wall-clock time compared with atomistic spin dynamics.



[315]. [*All-electron machine-learned orbital-free density functional for periodic CHNO systems*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17975 "All-electron machine-learned orbital-free density functional for periodic CHNO systems")

Marc K. Ickler, Andreas Hermann, Fred A. Hamprecht

arXiv:2610.17975v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We introduce STRUCTURES-P, a machine-learned all-electron orbital-free density functional for periodic CHNO systems. Electrostatics are evaluated analytically in an atom-centered Gaussian basis, while an equivariant network learns the kinetic and exchange-correlation energy from energies and gradients of non-ground-state densities. Minimizing the functional from a superposition of atomic densities recovers the Kohn-Sham ground state: on held-out crystals, the density error falls from 6.7% to 0.4% of the electron count and the energy error reaches 0.25 mHa per atom, half that of a same-architecture interatomic potential. A single functional can be trained for both isolated molecules and periodic systems, and the model generalizes to larger unit cells. It largely preserves relative-energy rankings and reproduces convex-hull vertices on the tested H-N composition slice. Together, these results demonstrate the viability of machine-learned all-electron orbital-free DFT for variational density optimization and energy evaluation in fixed-geometry solids.



[316]. [*Supersymmetric Observables from Surface Defect Anomalies in 4d and 6d SCFTs*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18086 "Supersymmetric Observables from Surface Defect Anomalies in 4d and 6d SCFTs")

Zi-Xiao Huang, Ma-Ke Yuan, Yang Zhou

arXiv:2610.18086v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We study the contributions of half-BPS surface defects to supersymmetric Casimir energy and the universal logarithmic term of supersymmetric R\'enyi entropy in four- and six-dimensional SCFTs. Using equivariant integration of defect anomaly polynomials and its extension to supersymmetric R\'enyi backgrounds, we obtain closed expressions for these observables in terms of defect 't Hooft anomalies. For Gukov--Witten defects preserving transverse rotations in four-dimensional $\mathcal{N} = 4$ super Yang--Mills theories, both observables depend on a single R-symmetry anomaly. For $\mathcal{N} = (0, 4)$ defects in six-dimensional $\mathcal{N} = (1, 0)$ theories, additional terms arise from the gravitational anomaly. We provide complementary checks using chiral algebras, five-dimensional localization, and holographic probe branes at large $N$, while localized chiral fermions reproduce the gravitational-anomaly contributions. The framework also recovers Wilson-surface results in six-dimensional $\mathcal{N} = (2, 0)$ theories.



[317]. [*Chirality Inversion and Giant Optical Activity in Scalable Self-Assembled Metamaterials*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18116 "Chirality Inversion and Giant Optical Activity in Scalable Self-Assembled Metamaterials")

Antonio Carone (Max Planck Institute of Colloids and Interfaces), Aso Rahimzadegan (Max Planck Institute of Colloids and Interfaces), Birg\"ul Kalkan (Max Planck Institute of Colloids and Interfaces), Thomas G. Parton (Max Planck Institute of Colloids and Interfaces), Tony Jin (Max Planck Institute of Colloids and Interfaces), Zihao Lu (Department of Chemistry, University of Cambridge, Cambridge, UK), Clemens Schmitt (Max Planck Institute of Colloids and Interfaces), Emeline Raguin (Max Planck Institute of Colloids and Interfaces), Bruno Frka-Petesic (Department of Chemistry, University of Cambridge, Cambridge, UK, International Institute for Sustainability with Knotted Chiral Meta Matter), Kevin Vynck (Institute of Light and Matter, Claude Bernard University Lyon 1, CNRS, Villeurbanne, France), Silvia Vignolini (Max Planck Institute of Colloids and Interfaces, Department of Chemistry, University of Cambridge, Cambridge, UK)

arXiv:2610.18116v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Chiral materials manipulate light in ways that hold promise for sensing, imaging, and photonics, but producing such materials with strong and reproducible optical activity at large scale remains challenging. Here, we develop a scalable strategy to assemble hierarchical helicoidal metamaterials from metallic nanoparticles and chitin nanocrystals, a biosourced polysaccharide. Spontaneous co-assembly produces ordered chiral structures with strong plasmonic optical activity, reaching a dissymmetry factor of 0.55. The unprecedented ability to vary the helicoidal pitch allows control of the optical response, enabling inversion of optical handedness without changing the handedness of the template, effectively decoupling geometrical chirality from optical chirality. Remarkably, the gold nanoparticles can also be recovered from dry films and redispersed as colloids, introducing an appealing circularity concept for sustainable chiral plasmonic materials.



[318]. [*Local error correction prolongs time-crystalline memory*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18124 "Local error correction prolongs time-crystalline memory")

Benedikt Fauseweh

arXiv:2610.18124v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Discrete time crystals are non-equilibrium phases of matter with spatiotemporal order and a response that breaks the time-translation symmetry of a periodic external drive. Interactions, spatial disorder and correlated random driving can in theory stabilize this order. In experiments, however, coupling to the environment limits the time crystal lifetime. Here we use stochastic local bit-flip error correction to prolong the time-crystalline response of a driven, spatially disordered two-dimensional Ising model on a superconducting quantum processor, using a repetition code with distances three and five on a honeycomb lattice with up to 68 physical qubits. Even though ancilla qubits, midcircuit syndrome measurements and decoding increase the required quantum and classical resources, the stochastic drive increases the memory decay time by a factor of up to two. At late times we observe that recovery stabilizes spatial order across the lattice. The spin-configuration distributions explain this separation, as recovery suppresses disagreeing bonds while broadening occupies completely reversed states. Syndrome measurements without correction accelerate the memory decay, demonstrating the importance of conditional correction. Our results show that local bit-flip error correction can preserve time-crystalline memory over more drive cycles than the bare dynamics.



[319]. [*Large deviations, gradient flows and fluctuating hydrodynamics for fractional diffusion*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18182 "Large deviations, gradient flows and fluctuating hydrodynamics for fractional diffusion")

Julien Barre (IDP), Hiroshi Horii

arXiv:2610.18182v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: This work is a step towards a general understanding of fluctuations for systems of particles described at large scale by fractional diffusion. First, we derive formally a Large Deviation Principle for the empirical measure of mean-field interacting particles with a L{\'e}vy-type noise. In the case of independent particles, this unveils a generalized gradient flow structure at large scales. Second, we derive and solve numerically a stochastic Partial Differential Equation describing the fluctuating hydrodynamics of this system. The results compare well with direct numerical integration of the particles' dynamics.



[320]. [*Complex materials engineering via Provenance Action Graphs: potential, structure and demonstration*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18314 "Complex materials engineering via Provenance Action Graphs: potential, structure and demonstration")

Yevgeny Rakita, Paul Todd, James R. Neilson, Simon J. L. Billinge

arXiv:2610.18314v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: This work discusses and tests the potential of reaction graphs for material synthesis, in which events consist of reaction steps (actions) and their outputs (observations). The importance comes from the requirement to synthesize technologically useful materials, especially those that are kinetically stabilized. Often, it is a science that follows years of trial and error, culminating in (published) successful recipes, but is truly driven by far more (unpublished) failures. The entry into the era of AI provides an opportunity to use learning algorithms to encode rules from multi-dimensional synthesis pathways, regardless of their technological usefulness, and to decode both forward prediction and inverse learning modes. The main incentive is to accelerate material discovery by generating a global knowledge base that can be shared among data agents and contributed to by them. We will focus on several scenarios in the common synthesis process during solid-state synthesis, primarily because these reactions are kinetic in nature and require closer monitoring and control of the reaction history.



[321]. [*Free energy of non-convex multi-species Ising spin glasses with external fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18350 "Free energy of non-convex multi-species Ising spin glasses with external fields")

Jeanne Boursier, Jean-Christophe Mourrat

arXiv:2610.18350v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We identify the limit free energy of mean-field multi-species spin glasses with +/-1 spins, allowing for the presence of external fields, and without assuming any convexity property of the covariance function of the Hamiltonian. The limit is given by an "uninverted" variational formula over Brownian martingales. The proof consists in first identifying the limit free energy of configurations with a fixed magnetization. Once the magnetization is fixed, we can show the convergence of the enriched free energy to the solution of a Hamilton-Jacobi equation. The magnetization constraint is also used to show the convexity of the initial condition in this equation, and this yields a variational representation of the solution, as a Hopf formula. Convex duality then turns this formula into an optimization over martingales whose initial values are the prescribed magnetizations.



[322]. [*Directed percolation in higher-order social dilemmas*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18506 "Directed percolation in higher-order social dilemmas")

Pablo Villegas, Sandro Meloni

arXiv:2610.18506v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Multiplayer social dilemmas on hypergraphs show a variety of emergent phenomena, which has been attributed to group interactions. Here we test whether pairwise dynamics can reproduce them as well. We derive the mesoscopic Langevin equation of a testbed model and find the operator content of absorbing phase transitions: a directed-percolation line, a tricritical point, and a first-order region whose spinodal is the explosive transition. In our analysis, three-player games only set the values of the Landau coefficients. We construct a strictly pairwise model with short-lived memory, in which every event involves at most two entities, that reproduces these coefficients term by term and has exactly the same mean-field fixed points. Simulations confirm that the two dynamics share mean-field critical and tricritical scaling and the size of the critical mass, and that a purely three-player game on a two-dimensional triangular lattice lies in the directed-percolation class. For these games, the explosive transition and the critical mass therefore do not require group interactions, and the critical behavior is that of the pairwise theory.



[323]. [*Explicitly correlated ground states with a feedback-free variational hybrid algorithm*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18681 "Explicitly correlated ground states with a feedback-free variational hybrid algorithm")

Benjamin Corbett, Akimasa Miyake

arXiv:2610.18681v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: Explicitly correlated methods, which represent dominant short-range correlations by a diagonal Jastrow-type correlator, are designed to accurately describe systems of strongly correlated electrons, such as the fully periodic 2D Fermi--Hubbard model. However, as the reference state is compact in momentum space whereas the correlator acts naturally in real space, there is a fundamental mismatch of bases that classical algorithms resolve only by restricting the reference or the correlator. Here we propose a hybrid algorithm in which a quantum processor prepares a low-entanglement momentum-space matrix product state (MPS), precomputed classically by the transcorrelated method at the cost of a nonvariational energy, rotates it to real space, and measures in bases fixed by the Hamiltonian. Because the measurement bases are independent of the correlator, the correlator can be variationally optimized entirely in classical post-processing with no quantum-classical feedback loop. We demonstrate our algorithm on 32 qubits of Quantinuum's H2-2 with a bond-dimension-4 MPS for the half-filled $4 \times 4$ lattice and achieve a relative ground-state energy error of $16 \pm 4\%$ after postselection, reduced to $3.5 \pm 0.5\%$ by calibration using low-order moments from the MPS. Compared with other feedback-free hybrid algorithms, even under generous assumptions, our method requires fewer samples than quantum-selected configuration interaction and fewer two-qubit gates than quantum Krylov methods.



[324]. [*Finite temperature Fredholm determinants for dynamical correlation functions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18750 "Finite temperature Fredholm determinants for dynamical correlation functions")

Oleksandr Gamayun

arXiv:2610.18750v1 Announce Type: cross 
Abstract: We study the long-time, large-distance asymptotics of finite-temperature dynamical Fredholm determinants using effective form factors. The method expresses these asymptotics as a functional of an effective phase shift. We determine its two branches on either side of a quadratic stationary point and the transition profile joining them. The profile is expressed through parabolic-cylinder functions. Its connection to the high-temperature Gaussian $\beta$ ensemble fixes the finite normalization, in agreement with an independent Riemann--Hilbert analysis. We also show that the leading exponential asymptotics suffice to recover the Euler-scale prediction of generalized hydrodynamics for the dynamical structure factor of hard rods.



[325]. [*Fermion-fermion interaction driven phase transitions in rhombohedral trilayer graphene*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01877 "Fermion-fermion interaction driven phase transitions in rhombohedral trilayer graphene")

Qiao-Chu Zhang, Jing Wang

arXiv:2406.01877v4 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The effects of short-range fermion-fermion interactions on the low-energy properties of rhombohedral trilayer graphene are comprehensively investigated using the momentum-shell renormalization group method. We take into account all one-loop corrections and establish the energy-dependent coupled evolutions of independent fermionic couplings that carry the physical information stemming from the interplay of various fermion-fermion interactions. With detailed numerical analysis, we observe that the ferocious competition among all fermion-fermion interactions can drive fermionic couplings to four distinct fixed points, dubbed $\textrm{FP}_{1}$, $\textrm{FP}_{2}$, $\textrm{FP}_{3}$, and $\textrm{FP}_{4}$, in the interaction-parameter space.
