These fixed points primarily dictate the fate of the system in the low-energy regime and are always associated with some instabilities characterized by specific symmetry breakings, leading to certain phase transitions. To determine the favorable states arising from the potential phase transitions, we introduce a number of fermion-bilinear source terms to characterize the underlying candidate states. By comparing their related susceptibilities, we find that the dominant states correspond to spin-singlet superconductivity, spin-triplet pair-density-waves, and spin-triplet superconductivity for fixed points $\textrm{FP}_{1,3}$, $\textrm{FP}_{2}$, and $\textrm{FP}_{4}$, respectively. These provide valuable insights into the low-energy properties of rhombohedral trilayer graphene and analogous materials.



[326]. [*Ring states in topological materials*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03529 "Ring states in topological materials")

Raquel Queiroz, Roni Ilan, Zhida Song, B. Andrei Bernevig, Ady Stern

arXiv:2406.03529v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Ingap states in semiconductors are commonly characterized by hydrogenic models within the effective mass approximation. However, when impurities are strong, this description fails. The bound states are non-perturbative with energy pinned to a zero of the impurity-projected Green's function. We show that in the presence of Wannier obstructions, such zeros are irremovable. That is, no symmetric, gap-preserving deformation of the bulk can return the ingap state to the band continuum. We call these states ring states: their wavefunctions show a node at the impurity site, and their mixed band character is set by minimizing the level repulsion exerted by the bands above and below the gap. In Chern insulators, the band mixing endows occupied ring states with local circulating currents, remnant of a small edge mode surrounding the vacancies. Clusters of vacancies bind ring states that hybridize to form dispersive chiral edge modes, providing a real-space view of bulk-boundary correspondence.



[327]. [*Numerical methods and analytic results for one-dimensional strongly interacting spinor gases*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06060 "Numerical methods and analytic results for one-dimensional strongly interacting spinor gases")

Ovidiu I. Patu

arXiv:2408.06060v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: One of quantum physics' fundamental, but largely unsolved, problems is the computation of the correlation functions in many-body systems. In this paper we address this problem in the case of one-dimensional spinor gases with repulsive contact interactions in the presence of a confining potential. We take advantage of the fact that in the strong coupling limit, the wavefunction factorizes with the charge degrees of freedom expressed as a Slater determinant of spinless fermions and the spin sector described by a spin chain of Sutherland type with exchange coefficients that depend only on the trapping potential. This factorization is also present in the expressions for the correlation functions. Still, analytical and numerical investigations were hindered by the fact that the local exchange coefficients and the charge component of the correlators are expressed as $N-1$ multidimensional integrals, with $N$ the number of particles, which are notoriously hard to compute using conventional methods. We introduce a new approach to calculating these integrals that is extremely simple, scales polynomially with the number of particles, and is several orders of magnitude faster than the previous methods reported in the literature. This allows us to investigate the static and dynamic properties, temperature dependence, and nonequilibrium dynamics for systems with a larger number of particles than previously considered and discover new phenomena. We show that, contrary to natural expectations, the momentum distribution of strongly interacting trapped spinor gases becomes narrower as we increase the temperature and derive simple determinant representations for the correlators in the spin incoherent regime valid for both equilibrium and nonequilibrium situations.



[328]. [*Extreme Suppression of Thermal Conductivity in Monolayer Beryllene*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.05766 "Extreme Suppression of Thermal Conductivity in Monolayer Beryllene")

Sapta Sindhu Paul Chowdhury, Santosh Mogurampelly

arXiv:2409.05766v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We demonstrate an extreme suppression of thermal conductivity in monolayer beryllene through first-principles solutions of the phonon Boltzmann transport equation. While the fully converged three-phonon calculation predicts a room-temperature lattice thermal conductivity of 154.3 W/(m$\cdot$K), inclusion of four-phonon interactions suppresses it to 10.7 W/(m$\cdot$K), a reduction of more than 90\%. This exceptionally large reduction of the thermal conductivity is amongst the highest reported in two-dimensional materials. The microscopic origin of this breakdown is traced to the strong sensitivity of the low-frequency flexural ZA phonons to four-phonon scattering: mode-resolved scattering rates reveal a pronounced enhancement of ZA scattering, which substantially shortens their lifetimes and suppresses their dominant contribution to heat conduction. Moreover, the temperature dependence of the thermal conductivity changes from $\kappa\propto T^{-0.86}$ to $\kappa\propto T^{-1.38}$ upon inclusion of four-phonon processes. Our results establish $\alpha$-beryllene as an unusually strong case of four-phonon-controlled thermal transport amongst two-dimensional materials. These findings identify selective higher-order scattering of flexural phonons as a key mechanism for controlling thermal transport in strongly anharmonic low-dimensional systems.



[329]. [*Two-dimensional Topological Quantum Chemistry and Catalog of Topological Materials*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.08950 "Two-dimensional Topological Quantum Chemistry and Catalog of Topological Materials")

Urko Petralanda, Yi Jiang, Hanqi Pi, Maia G. Vergniory, B. Andrei Bernevig, Nicolas Regnault, Luis Elcoro

arXiv:2411.08950v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Two-dimensional topological materials host symmetry-protected, disorder-robust edge states cen- tral to quantum technologies. Yet, they remain scarce compared with their three-dimensional coun- terparts. We adapt the topological quantum chemistry formalism to layer groups, and study the band topology of 8,872 entries from the computational materials databases C2DB and MC2D. We find 4,073 topological or obstructed entries, including 905 topological insulators and 1,003 obstructed atomic insulators, greatly expanding the library of such materials in two dimensions. Detailed elec- tronic and topological properties of all materials are compiled into a a Topological 2D Materials Database (2D-TQCDB), and the edge states of selected new materials are studied in detail. Our methodology is implemented in new publicly available programs, designed to study the topology of any non-magnetic monolayer or multilayer material.



[330]. [*The benefit of ignorance for traffic through a random congestible network*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09684 "The benefit of ignorance for traffic through a random congestible network")

Alican Saray, Calvin Pozderac, Ari Josephson, Shravan Sripadhanallur Natarajan, Brian Skinner

arXiv:2503.09684v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: When traffic is routed through a network that is susceptible to congestion, the self-interested decisions made by individual users do not, in general, produce the optimal flow. This discrepancy is quantified by the so-called ``price of anarchy.'' Here we consider whether the traffic produced by self-interested users is made better or worse when users have uncertain knowledge about the cost functions of the links in the network, and we define a parallel concept that we call the ``price of ignorance.'' We introduce a simple model in which fast, congestible links and slow, incongestible links are mixed randomly in a large network and users plan their routes with finite uncertainty about which of the two cost functions describes each link. One of our key findings is that a small level of user ignorance universally improves traffic, regardless of the network composition. Further, there is an optimal level of ignorance which, in our model, causes the self-interested user behavior to coincide with the optimum. Many features of our model can be understood analytically, including the optimal level of user ignorance and the existence of critical scaling near the percolation threshold for fast links, where the potential benefit of user ignorance is greatest. We demonstrate that our qualitative conclusions apply also to networks with nonlinear cost functions, and we comment on the case of scale-free networks, where there is no direct analog of percolation physics.



[331]. [*Interplay of spin-phonon coupling in FeSe with pressure and isoelectronic (S/Te) substitution*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12043 "Interplay of spin-phonon coupling in FeSe with pressure and isoelectronic (S/Te) substitution")

Abyay Ghosh, Piotr Chudzinski, Myrta Gr\"uning

arXiv:2504.12043v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: In several studies, magnetism has been shown to strongly influence the properties of the iron chalcogenide compound \ce{FeSe}. In our previous work [Phys. Rev. Res. 6(4), 043154 (2024)], we demonstrated how the interplay between spin and orbital degrees of freedom governs the electronic structure and consequently shapes the magnetic phase diagram of \ce{FeSe} under external hydrostatic pressure. However, a comprehensive understanding of the phase diagram also requires investigating the role of lattice degrees of freedom (phonons) and their coupling to magnetism. In the present study, we examine how hydrostatic pressure and isoelectronic substitution affect the magnetic and lattice dynamical properties of \ce{FeSe} using Density Functional Theory calculations. Our lattice dynamics analysis reveals that different phonon modes exhibit distinct dependencies on the local magnetic moment across various magnetic phases. We further identify pressure- and magnetic-moment-dependent dynamical instabilities in specific magnetic configurations, highlighting the intricate coupling between structural and magnetic degrees of freedom in \ce{FeSe}. An investigation of spin-phonon coupling demonstrates the presence of strong magnetoelastic interactions affecting the prominent optical phonon modes: the Fe-derived $B_{1g}$ mode and the Se-derived $A_{1g}$ mode. Finally, we discuss the evolution of spin-phonon coupling under pressure and alloying in the context of its possible implications for emergent phases such as magnetism, nematicity, and superconductivity in \ce{FeSe}.



[332]. [*CoS++: Towards More General and Explicit Implementations for Sampling High-Order Feynman Diagrammatic Series*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12349 "CoS++: Towards More General and Explicit Implementations for Sampling High-Order Feynman Diagrammatic Series")

Boyuan Shi

arXiv:2507.12349v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We extend combinatorial summation (CoS) of connected Feynman diagrams to Hubbard mod- els with flavor-dependent propagators and counterterm shifts. The construction combines an interaction-occupancy mask with labels for the open fermion cycle, allowing equivalent endpoint his- tories to be accumulated while retaining the flavor and sublattice information needed by subsequent contractions. In the flavor-dependent version, denoted symmetry-broken CoS (SB-CoS), normal heads carry the cycle flavor and counterterm heads inherit the flavor at their interaction partner. We prove the correctness of the resulting recurrence and introduce terminal readouts that return all nonempty interaction-subset values from a shared circuit, as required by a many-configuration Markov-chain sampler. For three flavors, a second formulation uses determinant polynomials and a local interaction tensor, with connected coefficients extracted by subset recursion or Fourier interpo- lation and a support sieve. Applications to the two-spin honeycomb and three-flavor square-lattice Hubbard models illustrate the treatment of sublattice structure and non-uniform reference shifts.



[333]. [*peaks: a Python package for analysis of angle-resolved photoemission and related spectroscopies*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04803 "peaks: a Python package for analysis of angle-resolved photoemission and related spectroscopies")

Phil D. C. King, Brendan Edwards, Shu Mo, Tommaso Antonelli, Edgar Abarca Morales, Lewis Hart, Liam Trzaska

arXiv:2508.04803v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The electronic band structure, describing the motion and interactions of electrons in materials, dictates the electrical, optical, and thermodynamic properties of solids. Angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES) provides a direct experimental probe of such electronic band structures, and so is widely employed in the study of functional, quantum, and 2D materials. \texttt{peaks} (\textbf{P}ython \textbf{E}lectron spectroscopy \textbf{A}nalysis by \textbf{K}ing group @ \textbf{S}t Andrews) provides a Python package for advanced data analysis of ARPES and related spectroscopic data. It facilitates the fast visualisation and analysis of multi-dimensional datasets, allows for the complex data hierarchy typical to ARPES experiments, and supports lazy data loading and parallel processing, reflecting the ever-increasing data volumes used in ARPES. It is designed to be run in an interactive notebook environment, with extensive inline and pop-out GUI support for data visualisation.



[334]. [*Observation of Altermagnetic Spin Splitting in an Intercalated Transition Metal Dichalcogenide*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12985 "Observation of Altermagnetic Spin Splitting in an Intercalated Transition Metal Dichalcogenide")

Milo Sprague, Mazharul Islam Mondal, Anup Pradhan Sakhya, Resham Babu Regmi, Surasree Sadhukhan, Arun K. Kumay, Himanshu Sheokand, Igor I. Mazin, Nirmal J. Ghimire, Madhab Neupane

arXiv:2508.12985v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Altermagnetism is a novel magnetic phase combining characteristics of both antiferromagnetism and ferromagnetic ordering. Despite growing theoretical interest in altermagnetic materials, reports of experimentally verified high Neel temperature layered compounds are limited or remain to be firmly established. Here, we present an angle resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES) and density functional theory (DFT) study of Co1/4TaSe2, a compound we identify as a layered altermagnetic material. Magnetic susceptibility measurements confirm type A antiferromagnetic ordering with a Neel temperature of 178 K. Our ARPES measurements reveal an electronic band structure in excellent agreement with DFT calculations, demonstrating clear signatures of altermagnetic spin splitting at the Fermi surface. Furthermore, temperature dependent ARPES reveals a reconstructed valence band structure, with observable band shifts and the closing of energy gaps upon heating above the Neel temperature (TN), consistent with the suppression of altermagnetic order. These findings establish Co1/4TaSe2 as a promising platform for exploring altermagnetic phenomena.



[335]. [*Anyon polarons as a window into the competing phases of the Kitaev-Gamma-Gamma-prime model*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21129 "Anyon polarons as a window into the competing phases of the Kitaev-Gamma-Gamma-prime model")

Chuan Chen, Inti Sodemann Villadiego

arXiv:2508.21129v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We investigate the dispersions of anyon quasiparticles in the Kitaev honeycomb spin liquid perturbed by $\Gamma$ and $\Gamma'$ couplings in order to understand phase transitions into competing states through anyon gap-closing instabilities. We demonstrate how anyon gap closings provide a way to understand phase transitions into a plethora of previously identified competing phases---including zigzag, stripy, $120^\circ$, and incommensurate spiral phases---and are in agreement with numerical studies not only on the nature of the phases, but also on the specific critical values of $\Gamma$ and $\Gamma'$ couplings. Remarkably, when the antiferromagnetic Kitaev model is perturbed by a negative $\Gamma$ interaction, we find that the single-vison and fermion gaps remain open while the gap of a magnon-like local boson vanishes. This points to a plausible candidate coexistence regime where long-range magnetic order emerges while fractionalized excitations inherited from the Kitaev spin liquid may persist. The magnetic order can be either stripy antiferromagnetic or incommensurate spiral, depending on the sign of $\Gamma'$.



[336]. [*Pseudo-critical localization*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18943 "Pseudo-critical localization")

Hui-Qiang Liang, Linhu Li, Guo-Fu Xu

arXiv:2509.18943v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We uncover a novel localization phenomenon, termed pseudo-critical localization, in non-Hermitian critical systems. This phenomenon is induced by the synergistic interplay between weak localization and non-Hermitian skin effects. It exhibits critical-like behavior at finite scales and undergoes a crossover to Anderson localization as the system size increases, which is in sharp contrast to critical localization that universally exhibits persistent criticality in the thermodynamic limit. Concretely, we use the non-Hermitian quasiperiodic mosaic model and non-reciprocal Aubry--Andr\'e model as minimal models to present the general features of size-dependent critical localization, and use entanglement entropy analysis to confirm the size-dependent nature. This discovery challenges the established paradigm of critical localization, opening new avenues for understanding and controlling critical behavior in non-Hermitian systems.



[337]. [*Observation of a Novel Charge Density Wave Superstructure in Monolayer 1T-$VS_{2}$ at Room Temperature and its Evolution in Multilayers*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03800 "Observation of a Novel Charge Density Wave Superstructure in Monolayer 1T-$VS_{2}$ at Room Temperature and its Evolution in Multilayers")

Samanta Pal, Kaustuv Chatterjee, Jyotirmoy Sau, Biswarup Satpati, Manoranjan Kumar, A. K. Raychaudhuri, Prabir Pal

arXiv:2510.03800v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Spontaneous formation of charge density wave (CDW) superstructures in monolayers (MLs) of a two-dimensional (2D) crystal lattice is fundamental in understanding its complex quantum states. We report a successful top-down liquid phase exfoliation and stamp transfer process (LPESTP) to create ML VS\textsubscript{2}, undergoing a CDW transition at room temperature. Using high-resolution transmission electron microscopy (HRTEM) and electron diffraction (ED), we observed the coexistence of 1T and 2H polymorphic phases in VS\textsubscript{2} at room temperature, and only the 1T phase undergoes CDW transition. We discovered a novel incommensurate CDW superstructure ($\sqrt{7} \times \sqrt{7}$) R19.1\textsuperscript{o} in ML 1T-VS\textsubscript{2}. With an increase in the number of layers, the CDW order changes to a commensurate ($2 \times 2\times 1$) superstructure. Using angle-dependent photoelectron spectroscopy and TEM, we have shown that vanadium atoms self-intercalate as V\textsuperscript{3+} ions in multilayer VS\textsubscript{2} and are responsible for the evolution of the CDW superstructure from the incommensurate ($\sqrt{7} \times \sqrt{7}$) R 19.1\textsuperscript{o} to the commensurate ($2\times2\times1$) order. We also report the observation of novel Moir\'e superlattices in twisted bilayer 1T-VS\textsubscript{2} flakes with trapped CDW superstructure of the monolayer. The density functional theory (DFT) calculation performed on ML 1T-VS\textsubscript{2} show that the observed ($\sqrt{7} \times \sqrt{7}$) R 19.1\textsuperscript{o} CDW superstructure has lower energy compared to that of the pristine undistorted ML and the CDW instability is driven by formation of strong soft-phonon modes. Our findings provide an important platform for understanding the evolution of CDW superstructures in 1T-VS\textsubscript{2} with layer numbers and V self-intercalation.



[338]. [*Fock-space fragmentation in quenches of disordered interacting fermions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19510 "Fock-space fragmentation in quenches of disordered interacting fermions")

Ishita Modak, Rajesh Narayanan, Ferdinand Evers, Soumya Bera

arXiv:2510.19510v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Fock-space fragmentation, as it is currently investigated, primarily originates from specific kinematic constraints or emergent conservation laws in many-body systems with translation invariance. It leads to non-ergodic dynamics and possible breakdown of the eigenstate thermalization hypothesis. Here, we demonstrate that also in disordered systems, such as the XXZ model with random on-site fields, fragmentation appears as a natural concept offering fresh perspectives, for example, on many-body localization (MBL). Specifically, for a given quenched initial state, we identify a potential-energy shell in Fock space, defined by its energy and energy uncertainty, which represents the energetically accessible part of the many-body configuration space. For example, the drastic sample-to-sample fluctuations observed in the weak disorder regime, $W<W_{\mathrm{frag}}$, represent fluctuations of the mass of the shell. Upon increasing disorder, the potential-energy shell becomes increasingly fragmented. Its finite-size evolution exhibits a turnaround flow; extrapolating this flow to the disorder at which the turnaround vanishes provides a heuristic estimate of the strong-fragmentation scale $W_{\mathrm{frag}}$. We conjecture that the fragmentation identified here provides a geometric mechanism contributing to the finite-time slowing down of relaxation observed in studies of disordered interacting systems.



[339]. [*Anyonic time exchange in hierarchical Hall states: emergent Luttinger scaling and cross-correlations at finite temperature*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20592 "Anyonic time exchange in hierarchical Hall states: emergent Luttinger scaling and cross-correlations at finite temperature")

Aleksander Latyshev, Ines Safi

arXiv:2510.20592v4 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We address hierarchical fractional quantum Hall edges coupled by a spatially local quantum point contact within the Unified Nonequilibrium Perturbative (UNEP) framework. Without assuming a Tomonaga-Luttinger liquid (TLL) tunneling correlator, we impose a local anyonic time-exchange (ATE) constraint with phase $\bar{\theta}$, yielding a nonequilibrium fluctuation-dissipation integral equation relating DC tunneling current and noise. For thermal initial states, detailed balance closes this equation for the current. Using the Wiener-Hopf method, we find a unique physical solution of local TLL form, with scaling dimension $\delta$ determined by $\bar{\theta}=\pi\delta\pmod{\pi}$, emerging from the solution rather than being imposed via a TLL correlator. By contrast, ATE and inversion symmetry alone fix the exchange monodromy but not the full transport dynamics. We then study the "anyon collider" beyond Laughlin states at finite temperatures. Detailed balance is broken and noise becomes super-Poissonian, but the relation between nonequilibrium and equilibrium correlators determines current and noise. The output cross-correlation and balanced generalized Fano factor depend on both $\delta$ and statistical phase $\theta$, identical to $\bar{\theta}$ for co-propagating modes with short-range interactions. A temperature-dependent current scale separates the zero-temperature asymptotic regime from the low-current regime, where the balanced Fano factor saturates to a function of $\theta$ and $\delta$ distinct from its zero-temperature value. This latter regime is particularly relevant to ``anyon collider'' experiments. These complementary regimes constrain the statistical phase and scaling dimension, subject to the symmetry $\delta\mapsto1-\delta$ at fixed $\theta$. For independent statistical phase and scaling dimension, the thermal crossover can reverse the sign of output cross-correlations.



[340]. [*Collective dynamics of trail-interacting particles*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.03950 "Collective dynamics of trail-interacting particles")

Paul Pineau, Samuel Bell, Rapha\"el Voituriez, Ram M. Adar

arXiv:2512.03950v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Trail interactions occur when past particle trajectories bias future motion, rendering the system out of thermodynamic equilibrium. While such systems are abundant in nature, their understanding is limited to the single-particle level or phenomenological mean-field theories. Here, we introduce a minimal model of many trail-interacting particles, which extends this paradigm to the fluctuating collective level in arbitrary dimension. Particles diffuse while depositing long-lasting repelling/attracting trails that act as a shared memory field, coupling their dynamics across time and space. Using stochastic density functional theory, we derive fluctuating hydrodynamic equations and analyze analytically and numerically the resulting behaviors. We show that memory, coupled with fluctuations, fundamentally reshapes collective dynamics; In the repulsive case, the particle density displays superdiffusive spreading for $d=1$ and diffusive spreading for $d\geq2$, characterized by transient clustering and ballistic motion; In the attractive case, the system condensates in finite time into frozen, localized states. Our results establish general principles for trail-interacting systems and reveal how persistent fields generate novel instabilities and drive self-organization.



[341]. [*Field-angle-resolved heat transport in UTe$_2$: determination of nodal positions in the superconducting order parameter*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13877 "Field-angle-resolved heat transport in UTe$_2$: determination of nodal positions in the superconducting order parameter")

Ian M. Hayes, Elliot Fang, Shanta R. Saha, Vivek Mishra, P. J. Hirschfeld, Johnpierre Paglione

arXiv:2512.13877v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: One of the recurring hurdles in studying unconventional superconductivity is the challenge of efficiently and conclusively identifying the symmetry of the superconducting order parameter in a new material. Uranium ditelluride (UTe$_2$) exhibits an unprecedented number of superconducting phases as a function of pressure and magnetic field, each presumably characterized by a different symmetry of the superconducting gap function. None of these phases has had its symmetry conclusively identified so far. In this article, we report results of an extensive study of the thermal conductivity of UTe$_2$ in its low-field, low-temperature superconducting state as a function of the angle of an applied magnetic field rotated in the $b$-$c$ plane. We observe clear and substantial oscillations in the thermal conductivity as a function of field angle, which naturally suggests the existence of point nodes in the gap. Utilizing the experimentally determined Fermi surface, we are able to model this phenomenon for all the potential gap structures in UTe$_2$ and positively identify the location of these nodes as being along the crystallographic $b$-axis, implying that the superconducting order parameter belongs to the $B_{2u}$ irreducible representation of the crystal point group. The clarity of this result will accelerate the identification of other superconducting phases in UTe$_2$, and guide future studies through the use of high resolution field-angle-dependent measurements.



[342]. [*Earthquake Dynamics as a Benchmark for Scale-Invariant Behavior*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17615 "Earthquake Dynamics as a Benchmark for Scale-Invariant Behavior")

Kilian Duplat, German Varas, Osvanny Ramos

arXiv:2512.17615v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We analyze regional earthquake energy statistics from the Southern California and Japan seismic catalogs and find scale-invariant energy distributions characterized by an exponent $\tau \simeq 1.67$. To quantify how closely scale-invariant dynamics with different exponent values resemble real earthquakes, we generate synthetic energy distributions over a wide range of $\tau$ under conditions of constant activity. Earthquake-like behavior, in a broad sense, is obtained for $1.5 \leqslant \tau < 2.0$. When energy variations are further restricted to be within a factor of ten relative to real earthquakes, the admissible range narrows to $1.58 \leqslant \tau \leqslant 1.76$. We identify the physical mechanisms governing the dynamics in the different regimes: fault dynamics characterized by a balance between slow energy accumulation and release through scale-free events in the earthquake-like regime; externally supplied energy relative to a slowly driven fault for $\tau < 1.5$; and dominance of small events in the energy budget for $\tau > 2$



[343]. [*Fano profile in the resonance fluorescence spectrum of a solid-state quantum emitter coupled to phonons*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.19435 "Fano profile in the resonance fluorescence spectrum of a solid-state quantum emitter coupled to phonons")

Rafal A. Bogaczewicz, Pawel Machnikowski

arXiv:2512.19435v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We present a theory of resonance fluorescence (RF) of a solid-state quantum emitter in the regime of weak optical excitation. The emitter is coupled to the phonon modes of the surrounding bulk semiconductor, described by a super-Ohmic spectral density. We show that the RF spectrum of this system consists of a central elastic line, a broad phonon sideband known from other linear and non-linear spectra of such systems, and a narrow inelastic contribution characteristic of scattering spectra, stemming from noise-induced transient dynamics. At moderate phonon couplings or low temperatures, the interplay between the broad sideband and the inelastic feature leads to a Fano-like profile near the resonant energy with the Fano parameter determined by laser detuning. In the weak-coupling limit (where only single-phonon processes are included), the spectrum becomes an exact Fano shape, with the inelastic component vanishing completely at the exact laser frequency. The amplitude of this spectral feature grows linearly with temperature, while its width depends solely on the spontaneous emission rate of the emitter. Because the spectral line shape is extremely sensitive to dephasing, resonance fluorescence can serve as a direct, non-interferometric probe of coherence at dephasing rates critical for generating highly indistinguishable single photons. We relate the quantum character of the reservoir to the non-commutativity of noise observables and show that the Fano resonance persists in the classical (commutative) limit. We also discuss how the redistribution of optical coupling efficiency between the central line and the sidebands affects the total scattering rate under various excitation conditions.



[344]. [*Geometry-Enforced Topological Chiral Fermions in Amorphous Chiral Metals*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.19791 "Geometry-Enforced Topological Chiral Fermions in Amorphous Chiral Metals")

Justin Schirmann, Adolfo G. Grushin, Benjamin J. Wieder

arXiv:2512.19791v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Since the prediction and observation of topological Weyl semimetals (chiral TSMs), there have been enormous efforts to characterize further condensed matter realizations of chiral fermions. These efforts were dramatically accelerated by the subsequent discovery of a profound link between low-energy topological and lattice chirality in structurally chiral crystals. Though TSMs are well understood in the limit of perfect translation symmetry, real solid-state materials host defects and disorder, and may even be rendered amorphous down to all but the smallest system length scales. Previous theoretical studies have concluded that chiral TSMs transition into trivial diffusive metals at moderate disorder scales, raising concerns that chiral TSM states may only be accessible in highly crystalline samples. In this work, we in contrast identify large families of chiral TSMs that instead persist under strong structural disorder even into the amorphous regime. We show that amorphous chiral TSM phases can be stabilized in particular by the presence of long-range order in the local structural chirality. We present extensive analytic and numerical calculations demonstrating the existence of both Weyl and higher-charge chiral fermions in amorphous metals whose topology and spin and orbital angular momentum textures are tunable via the interplay of average symmetry and geometry. To distinguish and generate new realizations of strongly disordered chiral fermions, we introduce an analytic approach grounded in symmetry group theory. We then introduce an amorphous Wilson loop numerical method to identify chiral fermions in metals with 3D structural disorder. Our findings bridge the crystalline and strongly disordered regimes of chiral TSMs, and indicate a clear route toward engineering geometry-enforced topology in noncrystalline materials and metamaterials.



[345]. [*Sound Wave in the Backreaction Affected Spacetime in Analogue Gravity Based on Number-Conserving Approach*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24233 "Sound Wave in the Backreaction Affected Spacetime in Analogue Gravity Based on Number-Conserving Approach")

Sang-Shin Baak, Hyun-Yong Lee

arXiv:2512.24233v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Adopting a number-conserving approach, we show that the sound wave in the backreaction-affected dynamical spacetime obeys the equations of motion for a massive scalar field in an analogue spacetime. Even with backreaction, the analogue metric has the same form as that without backreaction, except that the sound velocity, fluid density, and fluid velocity incorporate small corrections that account for backreaction. Moreover, the modification of the classical fluid-dynamical equations by backreaction introduces a spacetime-dependent mass. We also present a linear form of the modified Bogoliubov-de Gennes equation with number conservation, which allows us to solve it numerically. Using a finite-size homogeneous quasi-one-dimensional Bose gas as an example, we find that backreaction enhances the canonically normal-ordered correlation function at the same point, by about $10\%$ in the bulk and up to about $20\%$ near the edges. The bulk enhancement persists when the finite imaging resolution of cold-atom experiments is taken into account.



[346]. [*Higher-order response theory in optimal stochastic thermodynamics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24540 "Higher-order response theory in optimal stochastic thermodynamics")

Samuel. H. DAmbrosia, Adrianne Zhong, Michael R. DeWeese

arXiv:2512.24540v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Linear response theory has found many applications in statistical physics. One of these is to compute minimal-work protocols that drive nonequilibrium systems between different thermodynamic states, which are useful for designing engineered nanoscale systems and understanding biomolecular machines. We clarify the relationships between linear-response-based approximations used to study optimal protocols in different driving regimes by demonstrating how they arise from a truncation of a causal response, or Volterra, series expansion. We then construct higher-order response terms and discuss the utility and drawbacks of their inclusion in the determination of work-minimizing protocols. We focus on the cases of an overdamped particle in a harmonic trap, and a double well system modeled as a two-state discrete system, ultimately showing that while the inclusion of higher-order response in work approximations can improve predictions of excess work and modestly improve the resulting higher-order optimal protocols, a significant computational expense is incurred while introducing the possibility of predicting arbitrarily low and unphysical negative excess work.



[347]. [*Geometry-Induced Skin Effect in Electron Hydrodynamics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17229 "Geometry-Induced Skin Effect in Electron Hydrodynamics")

Jaros{\l}aw Paw{\l}owski, Piotr Sur\'owka, Konstantin Zarembo

arXiv:2601.17229v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: In ultra-clean 2d materials electron viscosity is as important as Ohmic dissipation and electron transport exhibits hydrodynamic features. Using a simple framework of Brinkman equations we find that hydrodynamic electron flows give rise to a geometric skin effect: sharp obstacles locally enhance the current suppressing it far from the edges where the flow is unobstructed. This effect arises within hydrodynamic transport with finite momentum relaxation and does not rely on ballistic dynamics. Our results provide a natural hydrodynamic interpretation of edge-enhanced and double-bump current profiles observed in constricted geometries. By comparing with recent scanning NV magnetometry experiments on gated graphene, we demonstrate that such flow patterns are consistent with viscous hydrodynamics shaped by geometry, clarifying the role of geometric effects in the interpretation of electronic flow experiments.



[348]. [*Quantum Geometric Entropy Production and Entropy Hall Effect*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02040 "Quantum Geometric Entropy Production and Entropy Hall Effect")

Longjun Xiang, Jinxiong Jia, Jian Wang

arXiv:2602.02040v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Quantum geometry has been extensively linked to charge, spin, orbital, and thermal transport in crystalline solids, but its imprint on entropy transport remains largely unexplored. Here we develop a quantum theory of entropy transport for Bloch electrons under an electric field. {Within the relaxation-time approximation, we show that the leading and subleading contributions to entropy production reproduce the Joule heating relation, with the former arising from the linear Drude current and the latter from the nonlinear Drude current and the intrinsic nonlinear Ohmic current. Microscopically, near topological Dirac band crossings and anticrossings, these contributions are controlled by the quantum metric and the fully symmetrized quantum metric dipole, respectively. We further predict a Berry-curvature-induced entropy Hall effect, which, together with the local heat Hall current, provides a simple way to recover the modern theory of orbital magnetization.} Finally, we derive general relations between entropy and charge currents, offering a practical route to probe entropy flow experimentally.



[349]. [*Controlling inertial active Brownian motion via stochastic resetting*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.21134 "Controlling inertial active Brownian motion via stochastic resetting")

Manish Patel, Amir Shee

arXiv:2602.21134v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Inertia is intrinsic to many living and synthetic active systems, from animals and robotic agents to colloidal swimmers, and it strongly shapes transport. Many such systems employ intermittent restart protocols to regulate exploration. Stochastic resetting provides a theoretical framework for these strategies and a route to control nonequilibrium steady states, yet the role of inertia in reset-controlled active dynamics remains poorly understood. Here we study an inertial active Brownian particle in two dimensions under complete and partial stochastic resetting. We derive exact stationary second- and fourth-order scalar moments for the different protocols wherever their stationary limits exist, and obtain the full transient evolution of the mean-squared velocity and displacement under complete resetting. Under complete resetting, inertia strongly enhances localization: at large reset rates, the steady-state mean-squared displacement decays as $r^{-3}$, rather than following the overdamped scaling $r^{-1}$. Kurtosis-based phase diagrams reveal strongly non-Gaussian position and velocity statistics, including activity-induced re-entrant behavior as a function of reset rate and inertia. The transient solutions show that resetting shifts the velocity-relaxation rates, whereas the terminal relaxation of the displacement is governed solely by the mean resetting time $1/r$, with inertia controlling the faster transient modes. Comparing complete resetting with the partial protocols, we find that resetting the velocity together with the position substantially enhances localization. Resetting the orientation to a fixed or a random direction drives the mean-squared velocity toward opposite limits. These results establish inertia, reset frequency, and the choice of reset variables as coupled control parameters for localization, relaxation, and non-Gaussian fluctuations in active systems.



[350]. [*Chapman-Enskog expansion for chirally colliding disks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.21367 "Chapman-Enskog expansion for chirally colliding disks")

Ruben Lier, Pawe{\l} Matus

arXiv:2602.21367v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We study a two-dimensional gas of hard disks undergoing chiral, energy- and momentum-conserving collisions. We propose a simple chiral collision rule and show that it can effectively capture collisions of spinning noncircular particles without retaining spin or orientation as kinetic variables. Despite the microscopic breaking of time-reversal symmetry, the H-theorem is obeyed, guaranteeing relaxation towards equilibrium in the absence of external forces. A Chapman-Enskog expansion yields analytical expressions for the shear and odd viscosity and the thermal conductivity. Theoretical predictions are confirmed by nonequilibrium molecular dynamics simulations



[351]. [*Finite-temperature superfluid depletion of disordered Bose gases*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.21380 "Finite-temperature superfluid depletion of disordered Bose gases")

Cord A. M\"uller

arXiv:2602.21380v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: At zero temperature, homogeneous interacting Bose-condensed fluids are entirely superfluid, with remarkable transport properties. A non-superfluid, normal component is induced by finite temperatures and spatial inhomogeneity, the combined effects of which are rather intriguing, and difficult to describe quantitatively. By inhomogeneous Bogoliubov theory, applicable to weakly interacting condensed Bose gases in static external potentials with arbitrary spatial correlations, we calculate the normal fluid density via the transverse current-current correlation. We obtain finite-temperature disorder corrections to the normal fraction known since Laudau's seminal two-fluid theory, using diagrammatic perturbation theory for systems of any dimensionality, with closed analytical expressions to leading, quadratic order in disorder strength.



[352]. [*On the upper critical dimension of the KPZ universality class: KPZ and related equations on a fully connected graph*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02000 "On the upper critical dimension of the KPZ universality class: KPZ and related equations on a fully connected graph")

J. M. Marcos, J. J. Mel\'endez, R. Cuerno, J. J. Ruiz-Lorenzo

arXiv:2603.02000v4 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We investigate the infinite-dimensional limit of nonequilibrium surface growth by numerically integrating stochastic growth equations on a fully connected graph. In particular, we study the Edwards-Wilkinson (EW), Kardar-Parisi-Zhang (KPZ), and tensionless KPZ (TKPZ) equations. Using a network discretization adapted to the all-to-all interaction topology, we analyze the global roughness, height-fluctuation statistics, time power spectra, and two-time correlations. For the EW equation, we obtain an exact expression for the roughness that matches the numerical simulations and shows that the interface becomes flat as the number of nodes in the graph, $N$, tends to infinity. We also compute analytically the time power spectrum, show that height fluctuations are Gaussian, and derive an explicit expression for the two-time height autocorrelation function, indicating that the aging behavior is trivial. For the KPZ equation, finite-size and strong-coupling effects can cause deviations from EW behavior at moderate system sizes $N$, often accompanied by numerical instabilities; however, these differences disappear as $N$ increases. In the large-$N$ limit, KPZ dynamics converges to EW behavior, as the four observables analyzed exhibit identical scaling properties. Overall, our results indicate that on a fully connected graph the KPZ nonlinearity is irrelevant as $N\to\infty$, leading to EW-like dynamics with asymptotically flat interfaces. These findings are interpreted in the context of the upper critical dimension of the KPZ universality class.



[353]. [*Enhancing entanglement asymmetry in fragmented quantum systems*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02338 "Enhancing entanglement asymmetry in fragmented quantum systems")

Lorenzo Gotta, Filiberto Ares, Sara Murciano

arXiv:2603.02338v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Entanglement asymmetry provides a quantitative measure of symmetry breaking in many-body quantum states. Focusing on inhomogeneous $U(1)$ charges, such as dipole and multipole moments, we show that the typical asymmetry is bounded by a universal fraction of its maximal value. Multipole charges naturally arise in systems with Hilbert-space fragmentation, where the dynamics splits into exponentially many disconnected sectors. Using the commutant algebra formalism, we generalize entanglement asymmetry to account for fragmentation. While the asymmetry grows logarithmically for conventional symmetries, it can scale extensively in fragmented systems and distinguish classical from quantum fragmentation. We derive general upper bounds for the asymmetry and identify states that saturate them. To study the typical behavior of the asymmetry, we consider the ensemble of random matrix product states. By identifying the bond dimension with an effective time parameter, we qualitatively reproduce recent results on asymmetry dynamics in random quantum circuits, suggesting a universal behavior for the asymmetry of $U(1)$ charges in local ergodic systems.



[354]. [*Active fluctuations drive inertia-free absolute negative mobility in soft and living matter*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06070 "Active fluctuations drive inertia-free absolute negative mobility in soft and living matter")

K. Bia{\l}as, P. H\"anggi, J. Spiechowicz

arXiv:2603.06070v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Absolute negative mobility (ANM) is one of the most paradoxical transport phenomena in which a setup moves on average in a direction opposite to the applied force. The simplest system exhibiting this effect in minimal one-dimensional dynamics involves an inertial particle subjected to a constant bias while dwelling in a nonlinear, symmetric periodic potential in a nonequilibrium, nonstationary state generated by external driving. The necessity of inertia poses a significant conceptual barrier in predicting the ANM in a microscopic world of soft and living matter such as biological cells. In this work, we solve a two-decade old puzzle and demonstrate that none of the previously thought factors required for this effect are decisive. We show that the ANM may occur in a system composed of an overdamped particle in a piecewise linear symmetric periodic potential in an equilibrium state, provided that it is additionally driven by active fluctuations in the form of white Poisson shot noise. Our result may help to explain exotic transport behaviour emerging in biological cells where dynamics is typically overdamped and assisted by active fluctuations derived from various metabolic activities. It can also be exploited for effective separation strategies in a microscopic world, thus transforming fluctuations from a nuisance into a functional resource.



[355]. [*Gap structure and phase diagram of twisted bilayer cuprates from a microscopic perspective*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10865 "Gap structure and phase diagram of twisted bilayer cuprates from a microscopic perspective")

Siddhant Panda, Andreas Kreisel, Laura Fanfarillo, Peter Hirschfeld

arXiv:2603.10865v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Since the prediction of a time-reversal symmetry breaking (TRSB) $d+id^\prime$ state in twisted bilayer cuprate superconductors by Can et al. (Nat.Phys. 17,519(2021)), several experiments have attempted to detect this state, yielding conflicting results. At present, it is not clear which differences in samples or experimental conditions might explain these discrepancies. In this work, we perform a tight-binding lattice model calculation with phenomenological interlayer tunneling, examining the order parameter as a function of twist angle, interlayer tunneling, doping, and temperature. We observe the TRSB state to be correlated to the position of the Van Hove singularity in the normal state, which changes not only as a function of doping but also the tunneling strength. Two such phases are identified as nominally consistent with in-plane $d+id'$ and $d+is$ order, but with unexpected transformation properties under bilayer symmetry operations. We calculate the Josephson critical current, in particular examining the angle dependence for various tunneling strengths. Finally, we discuss the existing experiments in the context of our results.



[356]. [*Limitations of Avila-inspired theory in the diamond chain with quasiperiodic disorder*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12362 "Limitations of Avila-inspired theory in the diamond chain with quasiperiodic disorder")

Manish Kumar, Ivan M. Khaymovich, Auditya Sharma

arXiv:2603.12362v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The mobility edges (MEs) that separate localized, multifractal and ergodic states in energy are a central concept in understanding Anderson localization. In this work we study the effect of several mutually commensurate quasiperiodic frequencies on the mobility edge formation. We focus on the example of the addition of a constant offset to the quasiperiodic potential of the one-dimensional all-bands-flat diamond chain. We show that this additional offset can transform the anomalous mobility edges (AMEs), i.e. the energies, separating localized and multifractal states, into conventional mobility edges, separating localized from delocalized states. Also this appears to be the first example which shows the inability of Avila's global theory to analytically predict the ME location. We observe this both quantitatively, through the ME location mismatch, and qualitatively, via the formation of multiple MEs, not predicted by the theory.



[357]. [*Topologically quantized macroscopic attractor states in hydrated DNA*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26847 "Topologically quantized macroscopic attractor states in hydrated DNA")

Mariusz Pietruszka

arXiv:2603.26847v4 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Driven dissipative systems at ambient conditions typically exhibit continuous responses shaped by fluctuations and relaxation, with discrete macroscopic states arising only under specific dynamical constraints. Here, we report the emergence of discrete attractor states in a quasi-two-dimensional hydrated DNA sample under magnetic excitation. The transverse polarization voltage $V_{xy}$ displays telegraph switching between well-defined levels, indicating stochastic transitions between metastable macroscopic states. Statistical analysis of the voltage time series reveals bimodal distributions and strong Bayesian model selection in favor of multiple coexisting states. A phenomenological phase-field framework describes the observed metastability and switching in terms of a proposed collective $U(1)$ polarization phase. Within this interpretation, phase-slip dynamics and integer-labeled winding sectors provide a possible organizing principle for the discrete macroscopic response. The results suggest that macroscopic quantization can emerge in a classical system at ambient conditions as a consequence of dissipative dynamics constrained by phase topology.



[358]. [*Fundamental problems in Statistical Physics XIV: Lecture on Correlation and response functions in statistical physics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29481 "Fundamental problems in Statistical Physics XIV: Lecture on Correlation and response functions in statistical physics")

Thomas Franosch

arXiv:2603.29481v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: In the first part of these short lecture notes, we will present an introduction on (auto-)correlation functions and linear-response functions in the language of a physicist. In particular, the fluctuation-dissipation theorem in classical physics is presented underlining the central role of correlation functions. The fundamental importance of (auto-)correlation functions raises the natural question on how they are characterized in general without referring to the concrete underlying dynamical laws. Perhaps unexpectedly -- despite being elegant and long established in the mathematical literature (Bochner's theorem for correlations; Herglotz-Nevanlinna representations for response) -- this answer is not widely appreciated in physics, partly because the requisite tools lie outside the standard curriculum.
  In the second part we adopt a more rigorous viewpoint: we state the key structural properties of correlation functions and provide selected derivations of these results. Finally, we return to linear response and discuss general characterization results for response functions.



[359]. [*Unambiguous characterization of in-plane dielectric response in nanoconfined liquids: water as a case study*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00700 "Unambiguous characterization of in-plane dielectric response in nanoconfined liquids: water as a case study")

Jon Zubeltzu

arXiv:2604.00700v4 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The in-plane dielectric constant of nanoconfined water has attracted growing interest in recent years. Nevertheless, assigning a bulk-like dielectric constant to a molecularly thin film is convention dependent because it requires choosing an effective water width. We propose the in-plane 2D polarizability, $\alpha_{\parallel}$, as a macroscopic characterization of the in-plane dielectric response that avoids this choice. Using classical molecular dynamics simulations, we compute $\alpha_{\parallel}$ via two independent and consistent methods: based on fluctuation--dissipation theory, and from the induced dipole moment when water is placed in a capacitor. Our results provide the framework to quantify the in-plane dielectric response of polar liquids across simulations and experiments.



[360]. [*Fate of emergent Hilbert space fragmentation and information insulation upon softening of hard dual constraints at PXP-chain junctions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00732 "Fate of emergent Hilbert space fragmentation and information insulation upon softening of hard dual constraints at PXP-chain junctions")

Akshay Panda, Anwesha Chattopadhyay

arXiv:2604.00732v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We demonstrate emergent Hilbert-space fragmentation in a composite chain of PXP and dual-PXP subchains separated by junctions with simultaneous blockade and anti-blockade projection constraints. The Hilbert space shatters into disjoint Krylov fragments growing exponentially with junction number. Freezing does not require a unique allowed junction state: multiple configurations remain locally allowed, but the dual constraints remove dynamical links between them. Each fragment is labeled by frozen junction configurations and contains compatible basis states. This fragmentation produces dynamical caging of quantum information between neighboring junctions and novel states with partially thermal and athermal regions. Softening the junction by admitting other local states with variable nonzero diagonal amplitudes destroys the direct-sum block structure and fragmentation. Nevertheless, information remains dynamically insulated and inhomogeneous thermal properties persist up to an onset time scaling with the diagonal energy penalty of the previously restricted junction states, followed by longer prethermal behavior. Energy-level statistics in each symmetry-resolved sector evolve from Poissonian in the hard-junction limit toward Wigner-Dyson behavior upon softening, with finite-size non-monotonicity. This signals the breakdown of the tensor-sum structure yielding pairwise sums of chaotic subchain spectra, restoring chaotic thermalization. In the hard-junction case, we find a chirally protected zero-energy mode with density peaks at physical edges and near the junction, pinned to zero under chirality-preserving disorder. The tensor-product eigenfunction structure also enhances the number of nonzero-energy scar states. Finally, we discuss routes to these junction architectures in programmable Rydberg atom platforms using optical tweezers, local addressing, and facilitation techniques.



[361]. [*Quasicrystal Architected Nanomechanical Resonators via Data-Driven Design*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07379 "Quasicrystal Architected Nanomechanical Resonators via Data-Driven Design")

Kawen Li, Hangjin Cho, Richard Norte, Dongil Shin

arXiv:2604.07379v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: From butterfly wings to remnants of nuclear detonation, aperiodic order repeatedly emerges in nature, often exhibiting reduced sensitivity to boundaries and symmetry constraints. Inspired by this principle, a paradigm shift is introduced in nanomechanical resonator design from periodic to aperiodic structures, focusing on a special class: quasicrystals (QCs). Although soft clamping enabled by phononic stopbands has become a central strategy for achieving high-$Q_m$ nanomechanical resonators, its practical realization has been largely confined to periodic phononic crystals, where band structure engineering is well established. The potential of aperiodic architectures, however, has remained largely unexplored, owing to their intrinsic complexity and the lack of systematic approaches to identifying and exploiting stopband behavior. Here we demonstrate that soft clamping can be realized in quasicrystal architectures and that high-$Q_m$ nanomechanical resonators can be systematically predicted through a data-driven design framework. As a representative demonstration, the 12-fold QC-based resonator obtained numerically a quality factor $Q_m \sim 10^7$ and an effective mass of sub-nanograms at MHz frequencies, corresponding to an exceptional force sensitivity of $26.4$~aN/$\sqrt{\text{Hz}}$ compared to previous 2D phononic crystals. These results establish QCs as a robust platform for next-generation nanomechanical resonators and open a new design regime beyond periodic order.



[362]. [*Pressure-induced decoupling of the pseudogap and superconductivity in a bilayer cuprate*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10207 "Pressure-induced decoupling of the pseudogap and superconductivity in a bilayer cuprate")

Yanghao Meng, Wenjin Mao, Liucheng Chen, Elbert E. M. Chia, Yifeng Yang, Tao Xiang, Jianlin Luo, Lin Zhao, Xingjiang Zhou, Xiaohui Yu, Xinbo Wang

arXiv:2604.10207v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The physical origin of the pseudogap in cuprate superconductors, whether a precursor to superconductivity or a distinct intertwined order, remains an open question. Although hydrostatic pressure provides a clean tuning parameter, probing the relevant energy scales under compression has remained experimentally elusive. Here, we employ ultrafast optical spectroscopy to track the non-equilibrium quasiparticle dynamics of slightly underdoped Bi$_2$Sr$_2$CaCu$_2$O$_{8+{\delta}}$ up to 37 GPa. By disentangling the pseudogap and superconducting relaxation channels in the time domain, we uncover a contrasting pressure evolution: despite an increase in carrier density, the pseudogap thermal onset (T$^*$) rises monotonically, whereas its energy gap ($\Delta_{PG}$) is continuously suppressed. Conversely, the critical temperature (T$_C$) and the superconducting gap ($\Delta_{SC}$) exhibit a synchronized dome-like trajectory. The effective coupling ratio 2$\Delta_{SC}$/k$_B$T$_C$ collapses abruptly from ~10 toward the weak-coupling d-wave limit, signifying a crossover from a phase-fluctuating two-dimensional regime to a globally coherent three-dimensional state. Upon compression to 37 GPa, the superconducting condensate is completely quenched into an insulating-like state, yet the pseudogap persists. The extended phase diagram reveals an unambiguous decoupling between the pseudogap and superconductivity, imposing stringent constraints on microscopic theories of high-temperature superconductivity.



[363]. [*Double circular dichroism high harmonic spectroscopy: An ultrafast probe for topological photocurrents*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21121 "Double circular dichroism high harmonic spectroscopy: An ultrafast probe for topological photocurrents")

Osamah Sufyan, Ofer Neufeld

arXiv:2604.21121v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Understanding optical responses of topological matter is central to optoelectronic applications, photocurrent control, and spectroscopy. Yet schemes for sensing ultrafast photocurrents and separating bulk and surface contributions are lacking. We introduce double circular dichroism (DCD) harmonic spectroscopy as an all-optical probe of ultrafast dynamics in topological materials. Pump and probe pulses are circular with independently controlled helicities, yielding the circular dichroism of the circular dichroism: a time-resolved response that evaluates how probe-induced dichroism depends on pump helicity. While DCD vanishes in symmetric systems, it survives in broken time-reversal-symmetry materials, including Chern insulators. Simulations in a Haldane nanoflake, where a pump manipulates chiral current-carrying states and intense probe pulses drive harmonic emission, show that DCD arises from bulk and edge-localized states with opposite signs, similar magnitudes, and different amplitude scaling. Edge-associated DCD is connected to the outermost atomic layer of exponentially localized protected modes and is relatively robust to bulk-edge interference, enabling separation of bulk and edge photocurrents. Varying the electronic structure and laser parameters reveals harmonic anomalies that may probe topological attributes of photocurrents and material phase. In particular, HHG DCD oscillations under band-structure manipulation vary strongly across the phase transition and become stable and nonoscillatory in topologically trivial phases. DCD also oscillates with pump-probe delay on attosecond timescales, offering an ultrafast probe of topological photocurrents and their coherence. DCD could therefore disentangle bulk and boundary photoresponses in broken-symmetry quantum and topological matter and be implemented in photoelectron and absorption pump-probe spectroscopies and chiral systems.



[364]. [*Crystal-Field Symmetry Constraints in Layered Honeycomb ErBr$_3$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.24951 "Crystal-Field Symmetry Constraints in Layered Honeycomb ErBr$_3$")

Biaoyan Hu, Mingyuan Hu, Franz Demmel, Andrey A. Podlesnyak, Jiaqing He, Liusuo Wu

arXiv:2604.24951v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Crystal-field symmetry restricts the ground-state Kramers doublet of ErBr$_3$ to one of two classes. We show that the compressed octahedral environment selects the class with $\langle \psi_\pm | J^{\pm} | \psi_\mp \rangle = 0$, suppressing the lowest-order $J^{\pm}$-mediated exchange. Thermodynamic measurements reveal two zero-field anomalies at 0.375 and 0.200 K. Under an in-plane magnetic field, the thermodynamic response separates into a phase boundary and a broader crossover line. Inelastic neutron scattering measurements at 2 K reveal no well-defined low-energy dispersive magnetic modes. These results connect the ground-state symmetry with the field-dependent thermodynamic response of ErBr$_3$, providing a microscopic starting point for understanding its low-energy magnetic behavior.



[365]. [*Femtosecond tunneling spectroscopy of ultrafast band bending dynamics at the atomic limit*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25146 "Femtosecond tunneling spectroscopy of ultrafast band bending dynamics at the atomic limit")

Kaedon Cleland-Host, Vedran Jelic, Stefanie Adams, Mohamed A. Hassan, Jacob M. Conley, Austin Hayes, Sheng H. Lee, Tyler L. Cocker

arXiv:2604.25146v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Atomic-scale disorder shapes the potential energy landscape traversed by photocarriers, while the carriers themselves also dynamically reshape this landscape. However, resolving ultrafast photocarrier motion at atomic length scales has remained a central challenge in materials science. Here, we demonstrate that lightwave-driven terahertz scanning tunnelling microscopy (THz-STM) provides access to these dynamics by probing the ultrafast evolution of local electronic structure following resonant interband excitation. We introduce a generalized inversion protocol that reconstructs the time-dependent differential conductance directly from measured THz-STM data, enabling quantitative analysis of the photoexcited electronic structure without prior knowledge of the sample's properties. Applying this approach to the photoexcited GaAs(110) surface, we image the resulting femtosecond carrier dynamics by tracking the transient photocurrents produced by ultrafast shifts in the energy alignment of surface and bulk electronic states near individual surface defects. The reconstructed dynamics reveal time-dependent band bending induced by the photocarriers and highlight three key mechanisms that contribute to the ultrafast tunnel current response. Critically, terahertz time-domain sampling of the tip near-field, which both captures the waveform and demonstrates its invariance under sample photoexcitation, allows the intrinsic sample dynamics to be disentangled from the coherent sub-cycle dynamics induced by the terahertz driving field. We establish a new regime of ultrafast tunnelling spectroscopy that captures transient electronic structure and dynamic band alignment with unprecedented spatio-temporal resolution, which has significant implications for understanding carrier transport, defect-mediated processes, and the development of optoelectronic technologies based on dynamically tunable materials.



[366]. [*Discrete Lattice Models for Interface Growth on a Complete Graph*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27896 "Discrete Lattice Models for Interface Growth on a Complete Graph")

J. M. Marcos, J. J. Mel\'endez, R. Cuerno, J. J. Ruiz-Lorenzo

arXiv:2604.27896v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We investigate the behavior of discrete interface growth models belonging to the Edwards--Wilkinson (EW) and Kardar--Parisi--Zhang (KPZ) universality classes, when defined on a complete graph, a topology commonly used to probe the infinite-dimensional limit of statistical mechanical systems. Our aim is to assess to what extent discrete lattice models reproduce the behavior of their corresponding continuum equations in this highly connected setting. After assessing the trivial behavior shown by some well known cases (like random deposition with surface relaxation or the etching model), we focus on two paradigmatic models associated with the KPZ universality class, the Restricted Solid-on-Solid (RSOS) and Ballistic Deposition (BD) models, and assess non-trivial behavior through several observables including the roughness, height fluctuations, power spectra, and two-time autocorrelation functions. Still, despite similarities with continuum equations, important differences arise in the fluctuations and long-time dynamics. In both discrete models the rescaled height fluctuations display a pronounced left tail, indicating the presence of lagging nodes. While the RSOS model exhibits a saturation roughness that decreases with system size, similarly to the EW and KPZ equations, the BD model exhibits a saturation roughness that increases with system size and an additional ultrafast growth regime, placing it outside the KPZ universality class on a complete graph.



[367]. [*Entanglement dynamics after quenches with inhomogeneous Hamiltonians*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01595 "Entanglement dynamics after quenches with inhomogeneous Hamiltonians")

Andrea Di Pasquale, Federico Rottoli, Vincenzo Alba

arXiv:2605.01595v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We investigate entanglement dynamics in bipartite systems governed by inhomogeneous Hamiltonians of the form $H = H_L + H_R$, where $H_{L/R}$ acts only on the left or right region and is homogeneous within each region. Focusing on the XX chain and the transverse-field Ising chain, we derive analytical formulas for the entanglement entropy between the two regions in the hydrodynamic limit of long times. In this regime, fermions incident on the interface undergo scattering, generating entanglement between reflected and transmitted modes. The resulting quasiparticle picture is controlled by the transmission coefficient, which we obtain analytically by solving the stationary lattice Schr\"odinger equation. Due to the bounded dispersion, strong inhomogeneity suppresses both transport and entanglement growth. We benchmark our analytical predictions against numerical simulations in paradigmatic setups. Finally, we extend the analysis to the interacting XXZ chain using tDMRG. The numerical data show qualitative agreement with the quadratic case: entanglement growth remains suppressed in the strongly inhomogeneous limit. Notably, however, entanglement continues to increase even when transport is suppressed, at least at intermediate times.



[368]. [*Boundary-dependent topological degeneracy in an Ising chain*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09510 "Boundary-dependent topological degeneracy in an Ising chain")

E. S. Ma, Z. Song

arXiv:2605.09510v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The influence of boundary conditions on bulk properties is generally expected to vanish in the thermodynamic limit. Here we show that this expectation can fail in an open transverse field Ising chain with modified boundary fields. By removing only the transverse fields acting on the two edge spins while leaving the bulk Hamiltonian unchanged, we uncover a boundary-induced switching of the topological spectral degeneracy. In contrast to the conventional open chain, where the topological degeneracy occurs in the ferromagnetic phase ($\left\vert g\right\vert <1$), the modified boundary condition shifts it to the paramagnetic phase ($\left\vert g\right\vert >1$). We demonstrate that the underlying mechanism originates from two exact local conserved quantities associated with the edge spins, which partition the Hilbert space into invariant sectors. Based on this structure, we establish an exact description in terms of domain-wall excitations, under which the Hamiltonian decomposes into two independent Kitaev chains. Complementarily, the Majorana representation reveals that the spectral switching originates from different unit-cell conventions and boundary terminations of the corresponding effective SSH lattice, leading to distinct boundary zero-mode structures without altering the bulk Hamiltonian. Furthermore, we show that the boundary-induced topological degeneracy remains observable at finite temperatures through non-Hermitian quench dynamics, providing a dynamical manifestation of the bulk-boundary correspondence. These results demonstrate that boundary engineering, through the introduction of local conserved boundary degrees of freedom, provides an effective route for controlling topological degeneracy in quantum spin chains without modifying bulk interactions.



[369]. [*Disorder-driven symmetry suppression by van der Waals planar defects in a magnetic topological insulator*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15332 "Disorder-driven symmetry suppression by van der Waals planar defects in a magnetic topological insulator")

Rikkie Joris, Heyi Xia, Ana Beatriz Pedro Fontes, Sara Bey, Seul-Ki Bac, Jiaqi Zhou, Zviadi Zarkua, Ahmed Samir Lotfy, Muhammad Saad, Philippe Ohresser, Bence G. M\'arkus, L\'aszl\'o Forr\'o, Margriet van Bael, Clement Merckling, Xinyu Liu, Francisco Molina-Lopez, Jean-Christophe Charlier, Jin Won Seo, Badih A. Assaf, Lino M. C. Pereira

arXiv:2605.15332v3 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: Magnetic topological insulators offer a platform to control electronic topology through magnetic order, yet reliable routes to tune their properties remain limited. Here, we show that ion irradiation allows to modify the magnetic properties and the anomalous Hall response of the van der Waals magnetic topological insulator MnBi$_2$Te$_4$. Using noble gas ion beams, intrinsic defects are introduced via collision cascades without chemical doping. We identify two distinct regimes. At low fluence, cation antisite disorder leads to a near-complete redistribution of Bi over cation sites while preserving long-range crystallographic order, accompanied by a transition from $p$-type to $n$-type transport. At high fluence, cation-anion intermixing drives the formation of a previously unreported layer-disordered phase characterized by a high density of van der Waals-specific planar defects, including swapped bilayers. Despite significant structural disorder, the system retains partial periodic order up to high displacement levels. Magnetometry and X-ray spectroscopy show that the Mn high-spin state and intralayer antiferromagnetic interactions likely persist, while macroscopic magnetic anisotropy is strongly reduced. At the same time, the magnitude of the anomalous Hall conductivity is reduced by an order of magnitude, far exceeding the change in magnetization, and its sign is modified. The observations are consistent with a suppression of the Berry curvature of the occupied states. These results establish ion irradiation as a means to tune anomalous Hall conductivity in topological insulators through defect engineering and reveal a disorder-driven approach to control symmetry and electronic structure in van der Waals magnetic materials.



[370]. [*Lieb-Schultz-Mattis constraints for hyperbolic lattices*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15974 "Lieb-Schultz-Mattis constraints for hyperbolic lattices")

G. Shankar, Joseph Maciejko

arXiv:2605.15974v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The Lieb-Schultz-Mattis (LSM) theorem and its higher-dimensional extensions forbid the existence of a unique, symmetric, and gapped ground state at fractional fillings in quantum many-body systems with a conserved particle number (or spin angular momentum) and the conventional translation symmetry of Euclidean lattices. In this work, we propose a generalization of the LSM theorem to quantum many-body systems on hyperbolic lattices, i.e., regular tessellations of two-dimensional negatively curved space. By leveraging concepts from hyperbolic band theory in a many-body setting, we adapt Oshikawa's flux-threading argument to periodic hyperbolic lattices with a non-Euclidean (Fuchsian) translation symmetry and compute a lower-bound to the ground-state degeneracy as a function of filling and lattice geometry. We explore the consequences of LSM constraints for gapped phases of hyperbolic quantum matter and suggest frustrated spin models on hyperbolic analogs of the square and triangular lattices as promising platforms for realizing symmetric spin liquids in hyperbolic space.



[371]. [*Diagrammatic Monte Carlo for Fermionic R\'enyi Entanglement Entropy*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21632 "Diagrammatic Monte Carlo for Fermionic R\'enyi Entanglement Entropy")

Boyuan Shi

arXiv:2605.21632v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We develop a direct diagrammatic Monte Carlo method for the second R\'enyi entropy of inter- acting lattice fermions. Fermionic replica sewing yields a connected-determinant (CDet) expansion, which we sample with many-configuration Markov-chain Monte Carlo. On a 3 \times 3 Hubbard clus- ter, deterministic coefficients through third order agree with exact-diagonalization derivatives for three reference shifts, while sampling extends the series to tenth order. For a reference shift with a continuous stability region, conformal-Borel resummation also gives good agreement with the exact entropy. We further compute coefficients through ninth order on 40 \times 40 and 50 \times 50 periodic lat- tices, with subsystems of up to 676 sites. The resummed results from different reference shifts show good agreement at a common truncation order. Their shift and order dependence provide checks on the stability of the continuation. These calculations extend connected-diagram sampling to spatial R\'enyi observables on large finite fermionic systems.



[372]. [*Quantum critical collapse of a pinned vortex glass in high-field superconducting resonators*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23838 "Quantum critical collapse of a pinned vortex glass in high-field superconducting resonators")

David Perconte, Thibault Charpentier, Nikolaos Koutsopoulos, Kalpajit Roy, Nadjib Benchabane, Xiaoli Peng, Florent Blondelle, Fr\'ed\'eric Gay, Mikhail Feigel'man, Viktor Kabanov, Benjamin Sac\'ep\'e

arXiv:2605.23838v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: The interplay between disorder and vortex--vortex interactions in strongly disordered superconductors in a magnetic field can stabilize a vortex-glass state, characterized by strong pinning and the absence of positional order. Yet its role in the destruction of superconductivity at the field-driven superconductor--insulator transition has remained unresolved. Here we use plasmonic microwave spectroscopy of superconducting resonators patterned from amorphous indium oxide thin films to directly track the superfluid density up to the critical field $B_c$. We find an unexpected resilience of the superfluid density, which decreases only logarithmically over nearly three orders of magnitude in field, in stark contrast to the rapid power-law suppression expected for vortex lattices. We attribute this anomalously slow decay to a collective vortex-pinning mechanism counterintuitively enhanced by vortex--vortex interactions. The superfluid density then vanishes linearly at $B_c$, where independent magnetoresistance measurements identify a continuous quantum critical point, unlike the abrupt transition observed at zero field. We further uncover an exceptionally large nonlinear electromagnetic response of the vortex glass, manifested as a pronounced positive-Kerr effect with potential for quantum sensing. These results show how disorder controls the critical magnetic field and identify the vortex glass as the key intermediate state governing the magnetic-field-induced superconductor--insulator transition.



[373]. [*Dynamics of ring polymer melts: Memory function approach*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26905 "Dynamics of ring polymer melts: Memory function approach")

Nail Fatkullin, Carlos Mattea, Kevin Lindt, Siegfried Stapf, Margarita Kruteva

arXiv:2605.26905v2 Announce Type: replace 
Abstract: We investigated the static and dynamic properties of a Rouse ring polymer modified by introducing an effective, spherically symmetric, attractive potential of entropic nature and a memory function describing the effect of dynamic entanglement. Renormalized Rouse formalism is used to approximate the time dependence of the memory matrix. The results obtained are in good agreement with existing experimental data and the results of computer simulations of ring polymer ring with , , where N_e is the number of Kuhn segments in linear polymer melts between neighboring entanglements and , the number of Kuhn segments.
