arXiv:2504.18744v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We study the free energy of the classical square-lattice Ising model in a uniform real magnetic field $h$, for which no exact solution is known. We first conjecture a closed-form expression built from the one-dimensional Ising chain and Onsager's hypergeometric solution. Comparing with high-precision tensor renormalization group (TRG) data, we find that it deviates by up to $2.6\%$, has no spontaneous magnetization, and gives an effective critical-isotherm exponent $\delta \to 1$. However, using the Lee--Yang circle theorem, we show that the exact free energy is a superposition of one-dimensional Ising chains in the same field, weighted by a temperature-dependent (normalized, but not always positive) distribution of squared Lee--Yang gaps, of which our first expression is a single-chain approximation. Exact results (Onsager, Yang, the strong-field expansion and the Lee--Yang solution at $\beta h = i\pi/2$) become sum rules for this distribution. We propose a simple form for it, fixed by Onsager's free energy and the exact strong-field limit without any fit to numerical data. This \emph{improved} conjecture agrees with TRG to $0.15\%$ for all $\beta$ and $h$, reproduces Yang's spontaneous magnetization exactly, has a positive specific heat, and yields the critical isotherm $M\propto h^{1/\delta}$ with $\delta = 15.2$ and amplitude $1.055$, compared with the exact $\delta=15$ and $1.059$. A refined form, which builds in additional input from Ising field theory (including the exact critical isotherm, $\delta=15$) and a few constants adjusted to transfer-matrix and TRG data, matches the TRG free energy to $4\times 10^{-5}$.



[451]. [*Physics-Informed Graph Neural Networks to Reconstruct Local Fields Considering Finite Strain Hyperelasticity*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05291 "Physics-Informed Graph Neural Networks to Reconstruct Local Fields Considering Finite Strain Hyperelasticity")

Manuel Ricardo Guevara Garban, Yves Chemisky, \'Etienne Pruli\`ere, Micha\"el Cl\'ement

arXiv:2507.05291v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We propose a physics-informed machine learning framework called P-DivGNN to reconstruct local stress fields at the micro-scale, in the context of multi-scale simulation given a periodic micro-structure mesh and mean, macro-scale, stress values. This method is based in representing a periodic micro-structure as a graph, combined with a message passing graph neural network. We are able to retrieve local stress field distributions, providing average stress values produced by a mean field reduced order model (ROM) or Finite Element (FE) simulation at the macro-scale. The prediction of local stress fields are of utmost importance considering fracture analysis or the definition of local fatigue criteria. Our model incorporates physical constraints during training to constraint local stress field equilibrium state and employs a periodic graph representation to enforce periodic boundary conditions. The benefits of the proposed physics-informed GNN are evaluated considering linear and non linear hyperelastic responses applied to varying geometries. In the non-linear hyperelastic case, the proposed method achieves significant computational speed-ups compared to FE simulation, making it particularly attractive for large-scale applications.



[452]. [*Spin-Based Modeling of Perception as Emergent from contextualized Internal Evaluation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06041 "Spin-Based Modeling of Perception as Emergent from contextualized Internal Evaluation")

Laura Fanfarillo, Gustavo Diez, Victor G\'omez Mayordomo, Miguel Bosch, J. Ricardo Arias-Gonzalez, Bel\'en Valenzuela

arXiv:2507.06041v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We develop a microscopic model of perception of an interoceptive sensation in which spin-like variables encode an organism's internal evaluation of embodied vital norms associated with the sensation. Spins can take positive, negative, or neutral values. These local valorizations interact on a lattice embedded in the environmental context, and their collective configuration gives rise to a macroscopic perceptual state. By applying a coarse-graining procedure to a family of symmetric spin models, we derive a macroscopic Landau-type functional that makes explicit the mechanism by which key phenomenological features of perception, emerge from microscopic evaluative interactions. A central result is that the inclusion of a neutral evaluative state fundamentally alters the structure of the perceptual landscape, enhancing entropy, lowering the critical threshold, and increasing sensitivity to contextual input. These results establish a principled link between microscopic evaluative processes and large-scale perceptual organization, offering a flexible framework for integrating perceptual regulation and neurobiological modeling. The model integrates notions of neuroscience and cognitive science using the formalism of condensed matter field theory and providing novel theoretical insights and experimental predictions for conditions such as mental disorders and chronic pain.



[453]. [*Provably scalable learning from quantum many-body data via locality*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.13705 "Provably scalable learning from quantum many-body data via locality")

Koki Chinzei, Quoc Hoan Tran, Norifumi Matsumoto, Yasuhiro Endo, Hirotaka Oshima

arXiv:2509.13705v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Machine learning (ML) holds great promise for extracting insights from complex quantum many-body data obtained in quantum experiments, potentially solving certain classically intractable problems. However, addressing large-scale problems still requires significant amounts of data that exceed the capabilities of near-term quantum devices. We propose a scalable ML framework, the Geometrically Local Quantum Kernel (GLQK), that efficiently learns from quantum many-body data by leveraging the exponential decay of correlations prevalent in noncritical systems. For learning an unknown polynomial of quantum expectation values, we rigorously prove that the GLQK substantially improves the sample complexity in the system size $n$ over the existing shadow kernel, by constructing a feature space from local quantum information at the correlation length scale. This improvement is particularly notable when each term of the target polynomial involves few local subsystems. Remarkably, for translationally symmetric data, the GLQK achieves constant sample complexity, independent of $n$. Numerical experiments on two quantum many-body tasks corroborate these theoretical results, demonstrating the high scalability of the GLQK. Beyond the polynomial setting, we further show that it can efficiently learn certain non-polynomial functions. Our framework establishes new avenues for utilizing experimental data to advance the understanding of quantum many-body physics.



[454]. [*The Arf-Brown-Kervaire invariant on a lattice*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.11424 "The Arf-Brown-Kervaire invariant on a lattice")

Sho Araki, Hidenori Fukaya, Tetsuya Onogi, Satoshi Yamaguchi

arXiv:2512.11424v3 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We propose a lattice formulation of the Arf-Brown-Kervaire (ABK) invariant which takes values in $\mathbb{Z}_8$. Compared to the standard $\mathbb{Z}$-valued index, the ABK invariant is more involved in that it arises in Majorana fermion partition functions with reflection symmetry on two-dimensional non-orientable manifolds, and its definition contains an infinite sum over Dirac eigenvalues that requires proper regularization. We employ the massive Wilson Dirac operator, with and without domain-walls, on standard two-dimensional square lattices, and use its Pfaffian for the definition. Twisted boundary conditions and cross-caps, which reverse the orientation, are introduced to realize nontrivial topologies equipped with nontrivial $\mathrm{Pin}^{-}$ structures of Majorana fermions. We verify numerically (and partly analytically) that our formulation on a torus, Klein bottle, real projective plane (as well as its triple connected sum), and two types of M\"obius strip reproduces the known values in continuum theory.



[455]. [*Symmetric Mass Generation as a Multicritical Point with Enhanced Symmetry*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24836 "Symmetric Mass Generation as a Multicritical Point with Enhanced Symmetry")

Sandip Maiti, Debasish Banerjee, Shailesh Chandrasekharan, Marina K. Marinkovic

arXiv:2512.24836v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We explore the phase diagram of a lattice fermion model that exhibits three distinct phases: a massless fermion phase, a massive fermion phase with spontaneous symmetry breaking induced by a fermion bilinear condensate, and a massive fermion phase with symmetric mass generation. Using the fermion-bag Monte Carlo method on large cubical lattices, we find evidence for traditional second-order critical points separating the first two and the latter two phases. Remarkably, these critical points appear to merge at a multicritical point with enhanced symmetry when the symmetry breaking parameter is tuned to zero, giving rise to the recently discovered direct second-order transition between the massless and symmetric massive fermion phases.



[456]. [*Symmetric Gapped States and Symmetry-Enforced Gaplessness in 3-dimensions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12335 "Symmetric Gapped States and Symmetry-Enforced Gaplessness in 3-dimensions")

Arun Debray, Matthew Yu, Weicheng Ye

arXiv:2602.12335v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We establish a comprehensive framework for characterizing the infrared (IR) phases of a fermionic quantum theory in three spatial dimensions, based on its quantum anomalies associated with a finite symmetry. We uncover a fundamental dichotomy among these anomalies: the first class of anomalies can always be realized by symmetric gapped states, while the second class can never be realized by gapped states without breaking the given symmetry, establishing the phenomenon of symmetry-enforced gaplessness in these settings. Moreover, using the construction of symmetry extension, we construct the candidate gapped states that theories with the first class of anomalies can flow to in the IR. As an application, we provide concrete predictions of the candidate IR phases of (3+1)-dimensional gauge theories based on our results. Our results also suggest that systems with discrete chiral anomalies cannot be gapped out by adding arbitrary bosonic degrees of freedom.



[457]. [*Phase diagram of a lattice fermion model with symmetric mass generation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18360 "Phase diagram of a lattice fermion model with symmetric mass generation")

Sandip Maiti, Debasish Banerjee, Shailesh Chandrasekharan, Marina K. Marinkovic

arXiv:2602.18360v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We study the phase structure of a model containing two flavors of massless staggered fermions interacting through two independent four-fermion couplings, UI and UB, formulated on a three-dimensional Euclidean space-time lattice. At UB = 0, this model is known to exhibit a direct second-order quantum phase transition between a massless fermion (MF) phase and a phase in which fermions acquire masses through the mechanism commonly referred to as symmetric mass generation (SMG). We demonstrate that introducing a small nonzero value of UB qualitatively alters this structure: the single exotic transition at UB = 0 splits into two distinct, conventional transitions, separated by an intermediate phase in which fermion masses arise through the standard mechanism of spontaneous symmetry breaking (SSB). The first of these is a Gross-Neveu transition separating the MF phase from the SSB-induced massive phase, while the second is a three-dimensional XY transition between the SSB phase and the SMG phase. Using the fermion-bag Monte Carlo method, we verify that the critical exponents associated with both transitions are consistent with the literature, thereby yielding a quantitative characterization of the resulting phase structure of the model.



[458]. [*Differentiable Maximum Likelihood Noise Estimation for Quantum Error Correction*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.19722 "Differentiable Maximum Likelihood Noise Estimation for Quantum Error Correction")

Hanyan Cao, Dongyang Feng, Zixuan Lu, Cheng Ye, Feng Pan

arXiv:2602.19722v3 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Accurate noise estimation is essential for fault-tolerant quantum computing, since decoding per formance depends critically on the fidelity of circuit-level noise parameters. We introduce a differ entiable Maximum Likelihood Estimation (dMLE) framework that learns these parameters directly from experimental syndrome data using likelihood gradients, with no decoder in the training loop. Mapping syndrome likelihoods to statistical-mechanical models enables exact and efficient evalua tion via planar solvers for repetition codes and tensor networks (TNs) for surface codes, extending the exact treatment to code distance d = 5 with a small number of rounds, and, via spatial subsam pling combined with time-translation symmetry, to larger codes and larger round counts. Across four experimental superconducting datasets, dMLE reduces logical error rates by up to 30.6% for repetition codes. On the Sycamore d = 3 surface code, a TN decoder using the dMLE prior attains a logical error rate comparable to that reported for state-of-the-art AlphaQubit, using standard syndromes alone without auxiliary leakage data. On the larger d = 5 surface code the dMLE prior lowers the logical error rate of the TN and Tesseract decoders by 7.18% and 5.23% relative to a prior optimized by reinforcement learning; at d = 7 it lowers that of Tesseract by 4.56% and 4.83% in the X and Z bases.



[459]. [*Postselection-induced localization and coherence in quantum walks on heterogeneous networks*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17629 "Postselection-induced localization and coherence in quantum walks on heterogeneous networks")

Adithya L J, Suraj S Hegde, Chandrakala Meena

arXiv:2603.17629v3 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Postselection of quantum trajectories is known effectively introduce nonlinearity into dynamics of open quantum systems. We study the effect of such non-linearity in continuous-time quantum walks (CTQWs) on networks with homogeneous and heterogeneous degree distributions. Using the recently proposed nonlinear Lindblad master equation (NLME), we investigate the dynamics under two decoherence mechanisms: Haken-Strobl and quantum stochastic walk (QSW). Our analysis reveals a striking dichotomy: under Haken-Strobl decoherence the nonlinear contributions precisely cancel, yielding a uniform steady state independent of postselection details under imperfect detection. In stark contrast, QSW decoherence permits postselection in this regime to break dynamical balance on heterogeneous networks, inducing robust localization preferentially at low-degree (peripheral) nodes. Remarkably, this localized state maintains finite quantum coherence. Extending our results to many-body spin systems, we demonstrate that degree heterogeneity similarly stabilizes localization of spin-up excitations in spin-down backgrounds, enhancing entanglement preservation. These findings establish degree heterogeneity and postselection as joint control parameters for engineering quantum transport and localization in dissipative dynamics.



[460]. [*Entanglement in the $\theta$-vacuum*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29287 "Entanglement in the $\theta$-vacuum")

Sebastian Grieninger, Dmitri E. Kharzeev, Eliana Marroquin

arXiv:2603.29287v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We compute the entanglement entropy and the entanglement spectrum of the vacuum state in the massive Schwinger model at a finite $\theta$ angle. By computing two-point correlation functions we show that the correlation length diverges in the critical region. We clarify the physical origin of entanglement entropy enhancement at $\theta=\pi$ by relating it to the competition between distinct electric-flux vacuum branches. We show that the peak near $\theta=\pi$ persists across the range of masses studied and corresponds to the point of maximal competition between distinct vacuum branches with opposite electric-field orientation, where quantum fluctuations due to fermion pair creation are maximized. While this entropy enhancement is generic, a pronounced compression of the low-lying entanglement spectrum at $\theta=\pi$ distinguishes the near-critical case ($m/g\simeq0.33$). Using the Bisognano--Wichmann (BW) theorem, we construct a lattice BW entanglement Hamiltonian and compare it with the exact modular Hamiltonian obtained from the reduced density matrix. We observe agreement in the infrared sector, indicating that the entanglement Hamiltonian is well approximated by a spatially weighted Hamiltonian. Finally, we discuss possible applications to entanglement in topological insulators and quantum wires. In this work, we used the chirally rotated formulation of the Schwinger model Hamiltonian. We identify an additive vacuum-energy artifact induced by the discretization of the chirally rotated lattice and derive its exact free-lattice form to subtract it in the continuum limit.



[461]. [*Entanglement Growth from Structured Initial States in Many-Body Localized Systems*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20656 "Entanglement Growth from Structured Initial States in Many-Body Localized Systems")

Chen Xu, Pengfei Zhang

arXiv:2605.20656v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Understanding how complex entanglement structures emerge is a central problem in quantum many-body physics. Recent work by Zhang et al. [Phys. Rev. Lett. 137, 020404 (2026)] has considered structured initial states prepared by evolving a product state under a chaotic Hamiltonian for a finite time before quenching to the target Hamiltonian. In this setup, total entanglement entropy growth in many-body localized systems exhibits two distinct regimes, first increasing and then decreasing as the initial entanglement is tuned. In this work, we identify the physical origin of this behavior by analyzing the dynamics of both the R\'enyi entanglement entropy and the Wehrl-R\'enyi entropy in the random-field XXZ model, the latter of which characterizes multipartite entanglement. We show that a similar non-monotonic dependence on the initial entanglement also appears in the net growth of the Wehrl-R\'enyi entropy for product states polarized along the $z$-direction. The first regime is governed by a finite magnetization associated with local integrals of motion, while the second reflects inter-site correlations. In contrast, for product states in the $x/y$-direction, the entanglement growth exhibits a monotonic decay. Our results provide a more fine-grained picture of how distinct initial-state properties shape entanglement dynamics in many-body localized systems.



[462]. [*Local network evolution rules driving shortest path multiplicity*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25237 "Local network evolution rules driving shortest path multiplicity")

Alexei Vazquez

arXiv:2605.25237v4 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: The shortest path multiplicity, here denoted by $\mu$, is an important metric of complex networks. For real networks $\mu$ is high and it correlates with the network community structure. Since local network evolution induces network communities, it is possible that a high shortest path multiplicity is the natural expectation of local evolution rules. Here I demonstrate, by means of numerical simulations and exact results, that this is indeed the case. For random graphs with arbitrary degree distributions $p_k$, $\langle\mu\rangle\sim \langle k(k-1)\rangle / (\langle k\rangle e)$, growing with the network size when $p_k\sim k^{-\gamma}$ and $\gamma\leq3$. For networks generated by stochastic local rules, $\langle\mu\rangle$ increases with the network size and it does so faster than what is observed in their randomized versions. Furthermore, the number of communities increases with the network size and the correlation with $\langle \mu\rangle$ follows.



[463]. [*A thermodynamic analysis of nonsecular master equations, beyond strict energy conservation and Gibbs equilibrium state*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13504 "A thermodynamic analysis of nonsecular master equations, beyond strict energy conservation and Gibbs equilibrium state")

Mohamed Boubakour, Talia Szikman, Cyril Elouard

arXiv:2606.13504v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We present a systematic thermodynamic analysis of nonsecular GKLS master equations. When some energy transitions in the system are comparable with the dissipative rate, the secular approximation is not valid, and the positivity of the dynamics must be ensured via other approximations. The dynamics then presents features reminiscent from strong coupling to the environment: e.g. the equilibrium state with an environment at thermal equilibrium is not a Gibbs state of the local system Hamiltonian and the dynamics couples energy coherences and populations. Nonetheless, we build a unified, consistent thermodynamic framework for those dynamics. We focus on two approximations beyond the full secular approximation (the local and the more accurate geometric-arithmetic approximations), and then employ systematic perturbation theory on microscopic expressions for the first and second law of thermodynamics which relate irreversibility to the increase of system-environment correlations. We show that, in spite of the weak system-bath coupling, the system-bath interaction energy participates to the energy balance, and is directly linked to the Lamb-shift Hamiltonian. We compare this microscopic entropy production with the definition based on the contractivity of the reduced system dynamics (Spohn inequality). We show that, unlike for secular master equations, the two entropy production rates differ because of the presence of non-vanishing stationary coherences in the energy eigenbasis. However, in the case of a single thermal bath, the difference is purely transient, and no work can be cyclically extracted from the steady-state despite its non-Gibbs form. Finally, we illustrate our results with simple examples, clarifying and completing the thermodynamic picture of Markovian dynamics in the quantum regime.



[464]. [*Helical Dirac Current with Local Coupling to a Chiral Potential*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17618 "Helical Dirac Current with Local Coupling to a Chiral Potential")

Ju Gao, Fang Shen

arXiv:2606.17618v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We show that a propagating electron wave in the lowest cylindrical channel carries a spin-resolved helical conserved current, and connect this current texture to its local chiral coupling. In a weakly relativistic positive-energy Dirac projection, the two sectors have the same azimuthally symmetric charge density and opposite circulation superposed on longitudinal flow, even though their nonrelativistic orbital harmonic is $l=0$. The current is radially distributed and extends into the evanescent tail. A scalar screw potential couples these modes through a derived vertex expressed by the \emph{chiral current overlap}, $\mathcal J_\chi$. Both spatial textures determine the coupling: the environmental radial profile weights the electronic circulation, while the screw harmonic selects correlated changes of spin sector and longitudinal momentum. For a uniform static harmonic, elastic energy conservation makes the first-order spin-flip amplitude vanish within the same transverse mode. Environmental energy exchange is therefore required to activate these selected channels. The same current overlap supplies the spatial kernel for heliciton-assisted inelastic scattering in a companion study and for a subsequent transport treatment of chirality-induced spin selectivity (CISS).



[465]. [*Initial-state-dependent dephasing effect in non-Hermitian Su-Schrieffer-Heeger models*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24185 "Initial-state-dependent dephasing effect in non-Hermitian Su-Schrieffer-Heeger models")

Tao Min, Junjie Liu

arXiv:2606.24185v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Understanding the dynamical evolution of non-Hermitian systems under external dissipation is essential for devising realistic non-Hermitian quantum devices that surpass the capabilities of their Hermitian counterparts. Dephasing, a major realistic dissipation, has an impact on non-Hermitian systems that remains largely unexplored. Here, we focus on finite-sized non-Hermitian Su-Schrieffer-Heeger (SSH) lattice models with alternating gain and loss in real space and examine the dynamical evolution of the trace distance under pure dephasing. By tuning system parameters, this model supports phases with either parity-time or anti-parity-time symmetries, enabling us to explore the dephasing effect across different non-Hermitian symmetry regimes within a single platform. While the trace distance exhibits distinct dynamical behaviors across the different phases in the absence of dephasing, its response to dephasing is largely symmetry-independent but instead initial-state dependent. By varying the initial states, we find that dephasing affects the trace distance in two qualitatively different ways. For initial states that differ only in their coherences, dephasing accelerates the decay of the trace distance, destroying the phase information in much the same way as in Hermitian systems. In contrast, for initial states that differ only in their populations, strong dephasing can partially or completely stabilize the trace distance over a wide transient regime before its eventual decay. We attribute these metastable stabilization phenomena to the alternating gain and loss in the system and confirm their absence in Hermitian counterparts. Our findings reveal the intricate dynamical response of non-Hermitian SSH models to dephasing, thereby facilitating their potential applications.



[466]. [*Two-dimensional Hyperbolic RNN Neural Quantum State*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25600 "Two-dimensional Hyperbolic RNN Neural Quantum State")

H. L. Dao

arXiv:2606.25600v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: In the first part of this work, we construct the first type of two-dimensional (2D) hyperbolic neural quantum state (NQS) in the form of the Lorentz 2DRNN (Recurrent Neural Network) and benchmark its performance against the Euclidean 2DRNN in the paradigmatic $N \times N$ 2D Transverse Field Ising Model (2DTFIM) setting with different lattice sizes up to N = 12 at different transverse magnetic field strengths. We find that hyperbolic Lorentz 2DRNN NQS robustly outperforms Euclidean 2DRNN NQS in our experiments when the system is at the phase transition point, where the physics can be described by a conformal field theory (CFT) - known via the AdS/CFT holographic duality to possess an underlying hyperbolic geometry. In the second part of this work, we benchmark the performances of recently introduced one-dimensional (1D) hyperbolic NQS ansatzes (including Poincare RNN/GRU and Lorentz RNN/GRU) against their Euclidean versions in $N \times N$ 2DTFIM, converted to a one-dimensional sequence. The findings extend our previous results showing that 1D hyperbolic NQS robustly outperforms 1D Euclidean NQS, thanks to the combined effects of the induced 1st and Nth neighbor interaction hierarchy and the CFT physics at criticality. While studies on larger system sizes remain future work, this study serves as a proof-of-concept for the utility, effectiveness, and superior performance of 1D and 2D hyperbolic NQS ansatzes compared to conventional Euclidean NQS models in many-body quantum systems - particularly those exhibiting structural hierarchy, criticality, or a combination of both.



[467]. [*Neural Non-Equilibrium Hamiltonian Monte Carlo for Corrected Boltzmann Sampling*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15682 "Neural Non-Equilibrium Hamiltonian Monte Carlo for Corrected Boltzmann Sampling")

Moxian Qian

arXiv:2607.15682v4 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Learned dynamical proposals can generate configurations without providing a tractable endpoint density. Nonequilibrium path probabilities offer a way to correct such proposals, but correction alone does not determine their ability to connect separated regions. We introduce Neural Non-Equilibrium Hamiltonian Monte Carlo (NHMC), which combines conditional momentum distributions with reversible, volume preserving dynamics. The forward--reverse path ratio gives the work used for training, importance weighting, normalizer estimation, and Metropolis correction. We then construct a configuration space round-trip kernel whose reverse and forward paths share an intermediate configuration. Conditional on that configuration, its path record update is independence Metropolis--Hastings. We compare its stationary inter-region flow with the flow obtained under exact conditional matching and bound their difference by the conditional path mismatch. This separates errors in the conditional proposal from dependence between the endpoint region and the intermediate configuration. Many well calculations test normalizers and mode probabilities; a controlled lattice $\phi^4$ calculation compares ideal and accepted inter-sector flows and measures sector relaxation. In two dimensional $U(1)$, $\mathrm{SU}(2)$, and $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ gauge theories, corrected path weight estimates reproduce finite volume Wilson loops; $U(1)$ topological susceptibilities are also compared with analytic predictions across several lattice volumes.



[468]. [*The Lee-Yang property of the Blume-Capel model*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16958 "The Lee-Yang property of the Blume-Capel model")

Yuri Kozitsky, Yurij Holovatch

arXiv:2607.16958v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: The Lee-Yang theory is based on the theorem concerning a property of the ferromagnetic Ising model partition function proved by T. D. Lee and C. N. Yang in 1952. It provides powerful tools for understanding the very nature of phase transitions and critical phenomena in various spin and similar models. Sometimes, this theory is applied to models for which the theorem's validity has not been proved. By the main result of E. H. Lieb and A. D. Sokal, Commun. Math. Phys. 80, 153 (1981), for a given ferromagnetic model, this validity is guaranteed by the same property of the single-spin partition function. Due to the anisotropy term $\sum_i \Delta S_i^2$, the single-spin partition function of the Blume-Capel model fails to have the Lee-Yang property for $\beta \Delta> \ln 2$. In this article, we show that the ferromagnetic interaction in such a model can induce the Lee-Yang property, even for these values of $\beta \Delta$. To the best of our knowledge, it is the first result of this kind.



[469]. [*The Information Content of Krylov Observables: A Machine Learning Approach*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17346 "The Information Content of Krylov Observables: A Machine Learning Approach")

Ritam Basu

arXiv:2607.17346v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We employ machine learning techniques to estimate the amount of information contained in three Krylov-space observables: the spread complexity $C(t)$, the discrete Wigner negativity $N(t)$, and the normalized negativity $\chi(t)=N(t)/|S(t)|$, where $S(t)$ is the survival amplitude. We study thermofield-double evolutions on GUE, GOE and Poisson spectra, and on the integrable $SL(2,R)$ Krylov chain of a two-dimensional CFT primary, with Lanczos coefficients $b_n=\alpha\sqrt{n(n+2h-1)}$. To introduce chaos we add a GUE random matrix to the integrable chain, $H(\varepsilon)=H_{SL(2,R)}+\varepsilon R_0 W_{GUE}$, where $R_0$ is its spectral half-width. Either $C(t)$ or $N(t)$ alone determines the thermofield temperature, with $R^2\simeq0.999$. However, neither can reconstruct the fine structure of the spectral form factor. In the integrable sector all three observables carry the same information. When we turn on chaos an asymmetry appears: $\chi$ predicts $C$ better than $C$ predicts $\chi$. This gap grows from $+0.33$ to $+0.77$, while the same gap for the raw negativity decays to zero. The extra information comes from the survival amplitude, and $\chi(t)$ carries it while $C(t)$ can still be recovered from $\chi(t)$. We derive a lower bound on this gap, set by the fraction of the variance of $\chi$ that comes from $S$.



[470]. [*Integrable multi-species SSEP with reactive particle species*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18959 "Integrable multi-species SSEP with reactive particle species")

Mathieu Dabrowski, Lo\"ic Poulain d'Andecy, Eric Ragoucy

arXiv:2607.18959v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We investigate integrable exclusion Markov processes constructed from set-theoretical solutions of the Yang-Baxter Equation (YBE) that generalise the multi-species Symmetric Simple Exclusion Process (SSEP). We first introduce the process called the $ (p,1) $-SSEP that is analogous to the multi-species SSEP but with an extra particle species qualified as reactive. Reactive species are able to evaporate and condense by pairs or to transform by pairs depending on the interpretation. We provide a full combinatorial study of the sectors in the periodic case. We prove the integrability of the process in the periodic case and in the open case for two types of boundaries that we introduce using Baxterisations of solutions of the reflection equation. Next we move on to the process called $ (p,q) $-SSEP, i.e. the multi-species SSEP with an arbitrary number of reactive particle species. We prove its integrability in the periodic case and, for the open case, we introduce integrable boundaries generalising the ones considered for the $ (p,1) $-SSEP. Finally, we define physical quantities relevant to the models and we compute them in the non-equilibrium stationary state of the open $ (p,q) $-SSEP for one type of integrable boundaries.



[471]. [*Beyond Calabrese-Cardy Scaling: Exceptional-Point Sensitivity and the de Sitter RT Surface*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21536 "Beyond Calabrese-Cardy Scaling: Exceptional-Point Sensitivity and the de Sitter RT Surface")

Kuang-Hung Chou

arXiv:2607.21536v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Entanglement entropy at one-dimensional criticality typically follows
  the Calabrese--Cardy scaling.
  In the non-Hermitian critical free-fermion systems studied here,
  however, we find an additional interval-independent contribution
  $S_{\rm res}=\log(\Delta L)$, which allows the entropy to remain
  sensitive to energy gaps below the finite-size scale, $\Delta<1/L$.
  We refer to this term as the residual entropy.
  To understand its infrared origin, we examine finite-depth non-unitary continuous multiscale entanglement renormalization on a ring. Terminating the critical RG circuit before its singular final layer leaves a two-site infrared state. We calculate the entropy of this state directly from its correlation matrix and recover the same \(\log(\Delta L)\) dependence. The emergent de Sitter geometry provides a complementary geometric interpretation: extremal curves associated with different boundary intervals extend to a common infrared endpoint, suggesting how the entanglement retained there can contribute equally to their entropies.



[472]. [*Fluctuation-dissipation structure of quantum geometry*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25006 "Fluctuation-dissipation structure of quantum geometry")

Per Moosavi

arXiv:2608.25006v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Geometry of quantum states is a fundamental research area with applications ranging from topological materials in condensed matter to variational algorithms in quantum information. Due to their relative simplicity, pure states are often studied, while mixed states are generally needed, for instance to have nonzero temperatures or other statistical mixtures. Mixed-state geometry, however, is much more challenging and has so far lacked any overall structure that relates distance and curvature, like the K\"ahler structure does for pure states. This work introduces that missing structure. We show that it has the physical meaning of fluctuation-dissipation---by identifying distance with fluctuations and curvature with linear response---and becomes the known structure for pure states, but is non-K\"ahler otherwise. This paints a simple picture that unifies fluctuation-dissipation and geometry of quantum states. It also applies on parameter spaces, as encountered in condensed matter or estimation theory. Our picture implies that the geometry contains all linear responses and fluctuations, proves the usual fluctuation-dissipation relation, explains that quantum geometry is present in any sum rule, and allows one to geometrically characterize generic transport behaviors.



[473]. [*Integrable Chiral Quantum Field Theories with Spacetime-Dependent Interactions*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13377 "Integrable Chiral Quantum Field Theories with Spacetime-Dependent Interactions")

Pradip Kattel

arXiv:2609.13377v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We construct a class of integrable chiral fermionic quantum field theories with interactions that depend on both space and time. The construction starts from a unitary, regular, difference-form two-body scattering matrix satisfying braiding unitarity and the Yang--Baxter equation. Each freely moving right- or left-moving particle carries a characteristic coordinate that remains constant along its trajectory, and the scattering matrix depends on differences of these coordinates. The many-body amplitudes form a discrete connection over the coordinate-ordering sectors. The Yang--Baxter equation gives local path independence, while periodic winding on a spatial circle produces quantum Knizhnik--Zamolodchikov (qKZ) difference equations expressing global flatness. Once a reference amplitude satisfying the exchange and qKZ constraints is specified, these transport equations determine the exact wavefunction in every fixed-particle-number sector. Independent reparametrizations of the right- and left-moving characteristics give couplings that depend on both space and time. For the chiral $SU(2)$ Gross--Neveu realization, we solve the qKZ equations for this full characteristic family using off-shell Bethe vectors. The resulting amplitudes are written in the characteristic labels, and their pullbacks give exact many-body wavefunctions for the corresponding space-time-dependent couplings. The two-body amplitude is a ratio of Gamma functions that resums the phase shifts from successive scattering processes. Equal-slope affine maps give a spatially homogeneous specialization in which the coupling depends only on time, and the Bethe ansatz with constant coupling is recovered when the qKZ step vanishes.



[474]. [*Locally optimized variational evolution for quantum many-body systems*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20802 "Locally optimized variational evolution for quantum many-body systems")

Carolin Wille, Maximilian Marvell, Lauren Stewart, Maximillian Murphy, Vinul Wimalaweera, Lesley Gover, Andrew G. Green

arXiv:2609.20802v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Quantum many-body dynamics is difficult to simulate because the global wavefunction develops extensive complexity, whereas local observables may retain only an intrinsic, system-size-independent complexity. This distinction is especially relevant in thermalizing systems, where local subsystems lose microscopic information and relax towards equilibrium. We introduce a variational time-evolution principle that exploits this separation by replacing global-state fidelity with a cost function defined on local reduced density matrices. The resulting locally optimized matrix-product-state (LOMPS) algorithm retains coherent short-time dynamics while using the simplification generated by thermalization at later times, and admits closed-form equations of motion analogous to the time-dependent variational principle. The same principle yields a quantum-classical algorithm in which a quantum processor evaluates the local cost and a noise-robust classical procedure performs the optimization. Proof-of-principle implementations on Quantinuum H2 and IBM Heron processors recover the characteristic local dynamics.



[475]. [*Colored Weingarten Calculus for Block-Unitary Ensemble*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30385 "Colored Weingarten Calculus for Block-Unitary Ensemble")

Daniele Iannotti, Elisa Vallini

arXiv:2609.30385v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Haar randomness is the standard null model for quantum typicality, scrambling, and random-matrix formulations of thermalization. In many physical settings, however, randomness is naturally constrained to subspaces of Hilbert space. This structure arises, in particular, both in systems with symmetry-resolved sectors and in the Eigenstate Thermalization Hypothesis (ETH), where mesoscopic energy windows define subspaces in which nearby energy eigenstates are mixed. Motivated by these settings, we consider a Block-Unitary Ensemble consisting of independently Haar-random unitaries acting on each subspace. We develop a constructive Weingarten Calculus for this ensemble, providing a unified framework across quantum information and many-body physics. This construction reveals a symmetry-resolved Schur-Weyl duality, where the relevant commutant is not the ordinary permutation algebra, but the one of color-preserving permutations. We apply this framework to quantum states under symmetry constraints and to energy-resolved ensembles for ETH.



[476]. [*Genuine Multi-Entropy of Fully Symmetric Gaussian States*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30754 "Genuine Multi-Entropy of Fully Symmetric Gaussian States")

Hugo A. Camargo, Mitsuhiro Nishida

arXiv:2609.30754v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We study the tri-partite genuine R\'enyi multi-entropy of fully symmetric Gaussian states as a function of the squeezing parameter for different total number of bosonic modes under different subsystem configurations. Exact results for a few modes are obtained, and we conjecture an asymptotic formula at the limit of large squeezing parameter as a function of the replica index $n$, while also finding subleading subsystem-size dependent terms for $n=3$ under a specific tripartition. We analytically show that the genuine $n=2$ R\'enyi multi-entropy of any pure bosonic Gaussian states is zero for any number of modes and for any tripartition. Furthermore, to investigate the dependence on subsystem size, we plot the curve of genuine R\'enyi multi-entropy as a function of the number of modes in the subsystems while keeping the total number of modes fixed. We discuss differences and similarities between this result and studies of the multi-entropy curve for Haar random states. In particular, we identify a subsystem size configuration related with the multi-entropy time in an evaporating black hole for which the behavior of the genuine multi-entropy changes.



[477]. [*The equivalence of topological indices for $\mathrm{U}(1)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_2$-symmetric quantum spin chains*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34119 "The equivalence of topological indices for $\mathrm{U}(1)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_2$-symmetric quantum spin chains")

arXiv:2609.34119v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Symmetry-protected topological (SPT) phases in quantum spin chains are distinguished by topological indices. Ogata's operator-algebraic indices apply to a broad range of symmetry classes without requiring continuous symmetry, whereas the elementary twist index of Tasaki requires $\mathrm{U}(1)$ symmetry. We prove their equivalence in the common setting of integer-spin chains with on-site $\mathrm{U}(1)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry and a symmetric locally-unique gapped ground state, without assuming translation invariance or a matrix product representation. A charge-fluctuation estimate yields a strong-limit construction of half-chain rotations with their phases fixed. An exact group commutator identity then identifies the Ogata index with the limit of local twist expectations and gives an explicit error bound. This provides a computable formula for the index when a lower bound on the gap and the relevant ground-state expectations are available. Combining the equivalence with a recent boundary-field gap theorem of OpenAI and our earlier twist-index calculation, we obtain a spin-rotation-invariant locally-unique gapped ground state of the infinite $S=1$ antiferromagnetic Heisenberg chain with both indices equal to $-1$, without assuming infinite-volume uniqueness.
  The author formulated the problem and proposed the basic strategy, while ChatGPT Astra made substantial contributions to the development of the proof.



[478]. [*Directional Control of Droplet Motion via Geometric Gating*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38654 "Directional Control of Droplet Motion via Geometric Gating")

Manoj K. Chaudhury, Vanessa Elias, Michelle Fruge

arXiv:2609.38654v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We explore new methods for inducing the movement of water droplets on elastomeric substrates, such as polydimethylsiloxane (PDMS). Our approach builds upon the well-known phenomenon that water droplets move easily across these substrates when swelled w,ith silicone oil. While designing shallow open channels and traps on a PDMS film offers a simple way to control a droplet's trajectory by tilting the substrate in two orthogonal directions, this method is poorly suited for manipulating multiple droplets simultaneously. To address this limitation, we introduce two new methods. The first method utilizes a plano-concave PDMS platform with a radius of curvature significantly larger than the size of the droplet and its translation distance. When the system's configuration is altered, this curved substrate consistently guides the droplet along a curved geodesic. The second method incorporates diode-like gates on the PDMS. These gates are created on either flat or plano-concave PDMS films by embedding thin, rigid plates into the elastomer prior to crosslinking, followed by swelling in silicone oil. This process generates sigmoidal steps that restrict droplet motion to a single direction. Finally, we demonstrate how combining these two strategies enables various forms of directed motion for both single and multiple droplets.



[479]. [*A strategic roadmap for an atomistic machine-learning ecosystem*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39090 "A strategic roadmap for an atomistic machine-learning ecosystem")

J\"org Behler, Michele Ceriotti, Cecilia Clementi, G\'abor Cs\'anyi, Alin-Marin Elena, Aditi Krishnapriyan, Joseph W. Abbott, Fabio Affinito, Albert P. Bart\'ok, Ilyes Batatia, Filippo Bigi, Florian N. Br\"unig, Yannick Calvino Alonso, Giuseppe Carleo, Aur\'elie Champagne, Stefan Chmiela, Marc L. Descoteaux, Ralf Drautz, Alexandra Farcas, Meng Gao, Rohit Goswami, Michael F. Herbst, Christian Holm, James R. Kermode, Alexander L. M. Knoll, Tobias Kreiman, Marcel F. Langer, Hoang-Thien Luu, Yury Lysogorskiy, Mihai-Cosmin Marinica, Rocco Meli, Klaus-Robert M\"uller, Frank No\'e, Mohamadhosein Nosratjoo, Simon Olsson, Christoph Ortner, Aldo S. Pasos-Trejo, Anyang Peng, Eric Qu, Andrea Rizzi, Mariana Rossi, Bassem Sboui, Gregor N. C. Simm, Alexandre Tkatchenko, Jacopo Venturin, O. Anatole von Lilienfeld, William C. Witt, Brandon M. Wood, Tigany Zarrouk, Fabian Zills

arXiv:2609.39090v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Data-driven machine learning (ML) techniques have become an essential tool in many domains of science. Their application to atomistic simulations of matter is particularly widespread and impactful. This success is due largely to the existence of a well-developed and established physics-based modeling framework, ranging from first-principles electronic-structure calculations to molecular dynamics and statistical sampling, into which ML was integrated naturally to reshape long-standing trade-offs between accuracy, efficiency, and scale. Nevertheless, this integration raises both conceptual and practical challenges, from choosing between data-centric and physics-based modeling approaches to adapting established software stacks to modern hardware accelerators and ML libraries. As the field evolves rapidly, fueled in part by widespread enthusiasm but also by tangible impact, it seems appropriate to take a moment to consider the current state of the art and open challenges, and reflect on what can be done to better coordinate efforts across the community. With this goal in mind, several members of this community met in Lausanne in January 2026 at CECAM to discuss algorithms, models, software and hardware infrastructure, and the most promising scientific applications that have become possible thanks to the use of artificial intelligence in atomic-scale simulations. This strategic roadmap paper summarizes the outcomes of these discussions, suggesting some long-term goals, and some concrete actions, to establish a healthy, sustainable and impactful atomistic ML ecosystem.



[480]. [*Stationarity as a One-Mode Constraint on Quantum Correlation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02090 "Stationarity as a One-Mode Constraint on Quantum Correlation")

Itai Panas

arXiv:2610.02090v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: A stationary scalar correlation energy admits an exact one-mode terminal representation even when the microscopic correlated return is strongly multimode. We test predictive realizations built from specified stationary information carriers in two complementary two-electron systems with the same Coulomb repulsion but different confinement: He-like ions and harmonium (Hooke's atom). For the Coulomb sequence, canonical RHF supplies the symmetry-faithful information carrier. The repeated same-mode return gives a He correlation error of $+0.1164$ m$E_{\mathrm h}$ and no more than $0.0157$ m$E_{\mathrm h}$ for Li$^+$--Ar$^{16+}$; H$^-$ is nevertheless bound and $93.3\%$ of its conventional correlation energy is recovered even though the RHF carrier is qualitatively strained. The dimensionless confinement factor $R_{CC}=D_{CC}/Z$ tends to $1.092394$. For harmonic confinement, an independently constructed response-compressed one-mode return gives $R_H=D_H/\sqrt{\omega}$; for $\omega\ge0.1$ it agrees with reference-inferred values within $0.21\%$ and tends to the literature high-density limit $1.309283$. Cioslowski and Pernal locate the qualitative crossover to the radially localized strongly correlated harmonium regime at $\omega_{\rm crit}\simeq0.04012$, below the quantitative domain used here. The H$^-$ endpoint and $\omega_{\rm crit}$ are therefore complementary carrier-validity boundaries: they limit the predictive realization, not exact terminal one-mode representability. A broad helium Bethe--Goldstone spectrum confirms that this terminal statement is not microscopic one-pole dynamics.



[481]. [*Nematic order in monomer-dimer models of Heilmann and Lieb with lateral attraction in dimensions $d\ge 2$*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08600 "Nematic order in monomer-dimer models of Heilmann and Lieb with lateral attraction in dimensions $d\ge 2$")

Qidong He

arXiv:2610.08600v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: We study the $d$-dimensional generalization of Models II ($d=2$) and V ($d=3$) for liquid crystals introduced by Heilmann--Lieb (1979), which consists of dimers on the hypercubic lattice at chemical potential $\mu$, interacting via a hard-core repulsion and a lateral attraction between parallel dimers with coupling constant $b>0$. For $d\in\{2,3\}$, these models were conjectured to exhibit liquid-crystalline order at low temperatures provided that $\mu+2(d-1)b>0$. We establish nematic order for all $d\ge 2$ under the additional condition $(4d-6)b>\mu$, which corresponds to the physical regime in which misaligned dimers are rare on scales comparable to the correlation length of the one-dimensional reference system. While the treatment of orientational order here follows the approach of our recent work on Model I, the proof of the absence of translational order relies on a custom implementation of cluster swapping in the sense of Sheffield (2005). Consequently, we deduce uniqueness of Gibbs measure and anisotropic exponential decay of correlations within each oriented phase.



[482]. [*AtomWorld-Mirror: Macro-Step World Modeling of Critical Evolution Backbones for Materials Dynamics*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11527 "AtomWorld-Mirror: Macro-Step World Modeling of Critical Evolution Backbones for Materials Dynamics")

Ziming Pan, Ruge Zhang, Haozhi Han, Junkai Zhou, Xingyuan Chen, Yifeng Chen, Yunquan Zhang, Ting Cao, Yunxin Liu, Kun Li

arXiv:2610.11527v2 Announce Type: replace-cross 
Abstract: Atomistic simulation is a fundamental tool for studying long-term materials evolution, from diffusion and defect dynamics to interfacial reactions and fracture. Yet conventional simulators typically advance at microscopic resolution, spending substantial computation on low-impact local updates before reaching structurally consequential states, an evolutionary-resolution bottleneck that limits long-horizon simulation. We propose AtomWorld-Mirror, a time-aware macro-step world model for the critical evolution backbone of atomic systems. For Step-Wise atomistic simulation, AtomWorld-Mirror distills short micro-event segments into physically reachable transitions between key states, jointly predicting sparse structural edits and accumulated physical time through latent macro-step dynamics. Local reachability, inventory conservation, and continuous-time consistency constrain each transition. By amortizing local atomic physics into a reusable latent macro model and replacing explicit micro-event replay with macro-step inference, this formulation provides a path toward substantially faster prediction of long-term materials evolution while preserving structural validity and time semantics. Across five atomic systems, spanning Cu-rich RPV steel irradiation aging, Cu-Zr metallic glass, and Li$_3$N-based anti-perovskite solid electrolyte, macro-step inference delivers a speed up of $10^3$ to $10^4$ times over event-by-event simulation.
